---

## 1. Compatibility scope

**Contract [D]:** anything you do with a card or preset in ST works the same way here. Features only this harness has (e.g. summarization with the preset loaded) may need preset edits, and that's acceptable. Beyond this section, feature parity with ST is not a goal.

### 1.1 Cards
- **[D]** Import, create and distribute ST cards as easily as in ST. **Embedded lorebook editing built** (U19, 2026-10-29; ARCHITECTURE.md §14), through the card’s field drafts and revisions.
- **[P]** Formats: V1, V2 and V3, as JSON, PNG (`chara` and `ccv3` text chunks) or CHARX. Embedded `character_book` lorebooks. Alternate greetings. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §8).
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: Independent card forks and exportable card notes replace following variants/modules (§4 and §5.0, owner decisions). Card export is built (ARCHITECTURE §8, §11). Earlier wording: “Export flattens a chosen variant plus its exportable modules (§4) into a standard V2 or V3 card.”
- **[P]** A watched import folder: drop a card file in and it becomes available. Each imported card remembers its source file and hash, and offers an update when the file changes. Files are only import and export; the internal representation is the source of truth **[D]**. **Partial**: source paths/hashes and explicit re-import/update/merge are built (ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §11, §13); watched intake and filesystem change notifications remain open proposals.

### 1.2 Presets (chat completion only)
- **[D]** Import and export ST chat-completion presets so they give similar results. **U20 built (2026-10-28):** `pin_examples`, `strip_examples` and `collapse_newlines`, with ST differential tests and prompt-preset controls; implementation in ARCHITECTURE.md §9 and §15. This scoped requirement is obsolete as an implementation plan.
- **[D]** No text-completion presets, instruct templates or context templates. The importer lists them as unsupported.
- **[D]** Connection and model data in presets is ignored. There's an optional one-way binding the other way: a connection can switch to a chosen preset (§9).
- **[P]** Supported semantics: **Built** for the listed prompt semantics (ARCHITECTURE §9, §15); the sampler item is superseded by the owner’s model-settings split (§6.1). Unknown ST extensions are not promised.
  - `prompts[]` and `prompt_order`, markers (main, worldInfoBefore/After, charDescription, charPersonality, scenario, personaDescription, dialogueExamples, chatHistory, …)
  - prompt roles, in-chat injection at a depth with a role, injection order
  - squashing and post-processing modes
  - assistant prefill and continue prefill (the continue prefill is kept as data only: see the next item)
  - sampler values: **overtaken** by model/connection settings (§6.1); explicit model-settings import is built (ARCHITECTURE §6, §9)
  - macros (§5.6)
  - prompt-only regex scripts, plus display regex as display rules
- **[P]** **Regex scripts as ST 1.19 has them** (owner, 2026-10-31: "pretty fundamental"; built 2026-10-31, ARCHITECTURE §8–§10, §12 "Rewrites", §15). Three lists in ST's order: the account's global scripts, the preset's, then the character's (a card's `extensions.regex_scripts`, imported as data, edited in the card editor, exported back). The preset's and the card's run only once the user allows them; an imported file never allows itself (§11). Scripts with neither flag rewrite text as it's written (a reply as it finishes, the user's message as it's sent, an edit when the script runs on edits, reasoning). Each list and the rewrite are registered parts a plugin can wrap, disable or replace. Proposals not yet confirmed by the owner (coordinator decisions, fregex):
  - **[P]** The version's text is the rewritten text, so the display, copying, editing, summaries and later requests see what ST would have stored. The text as received is kept on the version, never sent anywhere; a message action shows it and restores it as a new version.
  - **[P]** Display regex runs by default once its list is allowed: the viewer's switch stays, on by default (the approval is ST's consent; a second default-off switch would surprise).
  - **[P]** A group has no current character when the user sends (as in ST), so a user's message gets character scripts only in a chat with one character; a reply gets its speaker's.
  - **[P]** Not rewritten: greetings (templates here, expanded on display), the first message `StartChat` sends, and an edit of a queued message.
  - **[P]** While a reply streams (owner, 2026-10-31: "must update *as the reply gets streamed*, not just get applied at the end"; built by fregexs, ARCHITECTURE §12 "Live rewrites"): every frame shows the text so far through the scripts its finish runs, as ST's streaming does, and the display scripts run on the live text and both reasoning panels. Proposals in it: nothing of a reply is shown until the scripts are known (they're prepared while the provider is on its first token); only text that went through the scripts is shown, so on a slow host the text arrives at the scripts' pace (fregexr dropped fregexs's raw tail after 24 ms of script time, which let text a script strips show between rewrites).
  - **[D] Rejected** (owner, 2026-11-02): hold back streamed text that a regex script will strip. A regex script has no "opening tag" to wait for, so streamed text gets no hold-back for scripts; only in-output thinking tags (§7.4) are held back.
  - **[P]** Reply sections ("Thinking in the reply") are reasoning for the scripts: placement 6 runs on each section's text as it's written and streams, and the prompt-only placement-6 scripts on it when it's sent back, as ST runs them on parsed reasoning. Scripts with placement 2 run on the body once the sections are out (ST runs AI-output scripts before it parses reasoning out, so a placement-2 script there could also touch the tagged block). The original text and Restore cover the sections.
- **[P]** The importer never runs code from a preset. It writes an import report listing anything it couldn't represent (pi-forge's ST importer is a good reference). **Built**, narrowed by the following owner decision to actionable reports (ARCHITECTURE §9, §13).
- **[D]** The import report holds only what a user must act on; nobody reads it, so nothing the user needs may live only there (owner, 2026-10-28).
- **[P]** Fidelity is tested by **differential testing against real ST**: a stock ST in a throwaway container, pointed at a mock endpoint that records its requests. Synthetic card, chat, preset and persona fixtures are fed to both, and the request bodies are diffed. This applies to the non-agentic assembler. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §7, §15).
- **[D]** Prefill must work where the provider supports it. Most frontier models don't. reference-harness's tool-schema prefill for GPT (`spec/OPENAI-PREFILL.md`) is worth porting as a separate capability.
- **[P]** Where the provider doesn't support prefill, it's dropped and the request preview says so. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §15). Continue on such a model: see the next item.
- **[D]** **Continue without prefill** (owner, 2026-10-31): on a model that doesn't take prefill, Continue doesn't refuse. It sends the reply so far as the model's own last message, and the final instruction (the end-of-request instruction) tells the model to write only the continuation of it, without repeating it. ST does the same with its continue nudge. The new text is appended to the reply.
- **[D]** **No "Continue by prefilling" switch** (owner, 2026-11-02): the preset editor's Prefill card doesn't show ST's continue prefill. The stored `prefill.continue` stays as it is: imported from ST's `continue_prefill` and exported back unchanged, so a round trip to ST keeps it. Nothing in generation reads it, and Continue behaves as decided on 2026-10-31 (above). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §9, §14.6).

### 1.3 Lorebooks / World Info
- **[D]** (owner brief, 2026-11-06) “New lorebook” from the Library, the lorebook switcher's list end and wherever lorebooks are attached: “a dialog with a name, and the same dialog everywhere.” It creates an empty named book, opens its editor and attaches it when started at an attach point. **Built** (newflows, ARCHITECTURE §14); today's attachment pickers are chats and presets, while cards author their own embedded book.
- **[D]** **Export-only header stays quiet** (owner, 2026-11-03): the collapsed Card export only "header must show NO dot and NO summary: just its title and arrow", because its options are inert until export. Other section headers retain their current markers and summaries pending a decision. **Built** (lorebookfix2; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** **Lorebook field explanations** (owner, 2026-11-03): "Remove that popover from the field (no popover on hover, focus or click). Put the same text in the standard "?" help button right beside the keys field (it has no label, so beside the field, aligned with its first line)." The same rule applies to every lorebook entry field: labelled fields keep help beside the label, unlabelled fields beside the field. **Built** (lorebookfix2; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** **Strategy and position stays visible** (owner, 2026-11-03): "Make it a plain, always-visible group, with no accordion: in the side panel editor it's always shown while the entry's options are open; in the large editor it's always shown." Strategy, Position, Depth, Order and Trigger % are not a boxed settings section. Other settings retain their grouping. This supersedes the lorebookeditor proposal to fold every section. **Built** (lorebookfix2; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) "implement three ST world-info features that are currently imported and kept but have no effect: **sticky**, **cooldown** and **character filters**." They use ST semantics, with state causal to the selected history and independent per fork; a message's swipes share their preceding state. Filters select the character writing the reply, including in multi-character chats. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[P]** (lorebooktimed, 2026-11-02) Admissions are immutable records of generated versions, folded on the selected path with shared prefix checkpoints; previews never record admissions. Imports resolve filter filenames to card identities and local tag IDs to names, retaining their export identities. An imported transcript has no historical admissions here: timers begin with the first generation that uses an entry. Automation ID (external automation), memo (editor/export metadata), export name/priority (card format metadata) and `use_regex` (ignored by ST's keyword matcher) are legitimately inapplicable to activation. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §8, §15).
- **[D]** Imported lorebooks must work. Matching ST's activation and placement exactly is **not** a goal; ST's implementation is considered poor.
- **[D]** Lorebooks usually wreck the KV cache, so they will work differently here.
- **[D]** Decision models (Jev and others) can decide whether an entry is included. This must stay optional.
- **[P]** Activation sources: keyword triggers as the fallback, an optional decision model asking whether each entry or group is relevant to the current scene, and an agent lookup tool for anything missed. **Partial**: keyword activation is built (ARCHITECTURE §15); decision-model activation and RP lookup tools remain Phase 4 work.
- **[D]** Keyword matching follows ST's convention for now (owner, 2026-10-12): `use_regex` is ignored, as ST never reads it, and a key is a regular expression only when written as `/pattern/flags`. Low priority to revisit.
- **[D]** A card's own lorebook (`character_book`) is used in new chats automatically, with no "import card lore" dialog. A chat imported from ST keeps what ST sent: the card's lorebook only if ST had imported it as a lorebook file. Each chat's settings can turn a card's lorebook on or off (owner, 2026-10-14).
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: The following owner decisions allow above-history placement when the default override is off, pin only above-history entries, and evaluate tail entries every turn. Built: ARCHITECTURE §15. Earlier wording: “Placement is cache-friendly: entries are never inserted above the history. They go in a block near the tail, where they stay until the next compaction boundary.”

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-10-27) Wherever lorebooks are selected there is a setting, **on by default**, that overrides entries' positions so lorebook entries are only placed **below the history**. What it does is made very clear, with a popover explaining why (entries that come and go above the history invalidate the cache for everything after them) and how, right where lorebooks are selected.
- **[D]** With that setting off, entries placed above the history stay activated ("pinned") until the history's start next moves; entries after the history are never pinned and are evaluated every turn, as in ST.
- **[D]** When the history's start moves, the cache should still not be lost entirely: the prefix before the history (preset, cards, high-level summaries) is preserved, through cache breakpoints where the provider has them (not every cache system does; with few breakpoints it may not always be possible).
- **[P]** (florebook, 2026-10-31) Standalone lorebook authoring matches ST's World Info editor. **Built**: ARCHITECTURE §14. The choices made, for the owner to confirm:
  - The entries are a table, one row per entry: switch, title (edited in place) with its keys at a glance, then strategy (constant / normal / vectorized), position (ST's short names: ↑Char, ↓AN, @D…), depth, order and trigger %, each edited in place. A row opens to everything else the entry holds. A card's embedded book uses the same table.
  - The default sort is ST's "Priority" (highest order first). The others are title, depth, trigger % and "recently changed". Entries keep no time of change, so "recently changed" puts entries with unsaved changes first, then the newest added. The order is fixed when the search, filter or sort changes, so a row never jumps away while it's edited. Added and duplicated entries lead the table, newest first, until then (florebookr).
  - Row actions: Duplicate, Copy to another lorebook, Move to another lorebook, Delete. Copy and Move save the entry in the other book at once, as its own revision, leaving that book's drafts alone. A move's removal here is a change saved with this book. A deleted entry can be restored until the book is saved. Order is set by its number, as in ST; there's no drag.
  - The book's name, description, scan depth, budget and recursion are folded under "Book settings", since they're rarely changed.
  - In the chat's Lorebooks tab, every book the chat reads is one list (the characters' own, those added, the global ones), each with its switch, **Open** (its editor over the chat, as the preset and card editors open) and ⋯ (Use in every chat; the separate attachment of a global book). The below-the-history setting stays visible after the list, as "Where entries go", because the owner's decision above puts it where lorebooks are selected. It's one setting for the chat, so it isn't under each book's ⋯ as the UI audit suggested. **Superseded** by the decisions below (owner, 2026-11-01): there are no global books, and a book opens in the tab itself.
- **[D]** **No global lorebooks** (owner, 2026-11-01): "The only utility I see would overlap with associating a lorebook with the preset. There's definitely a point in having that: dynamic prompts that get appended via trigger words and have to do with style, rules etc. Presets have no way to trigger prompts from words." A lorebook can be **attached to a prompt preset**, as a block of the preset with its own on/off switch, listed with the prompts in the Prompt preset tab and the large editor, and sent with the preset. Lore belongs to cards (a card's own book or one attached to the card); personal lore to the persona (a card here); ST's chat-bound books stay attached to their chat. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14.6, §15).
- **[D]** **Importing ST's global books** (owner, 2026-11-01): ST's globally selected World Info books are attached to the preset that was selected in ST at import time, and nowhere else. Attaching them to every imported preset was rejected: "a lorebook was its own state. You could turn it off and it would go off regardless of preset. Your idea locks it in for every preset... they remove it, switch to an old preset eventually, and don't realize the lorebook got dragged back in." "We'll associate it with the currently selected preset, and that's it." The import report says which books went to which preset. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[D]** The chat's Lorebooks tab lists the preset's books as rows "From the preset <name>", with a link into the preset, so nothing sent is invisible from the chat (owner, 2026-11-01). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** **The Lorebooks tab is one stable list** (owner, 2026-11-01: "I see lorebooks appearing, disappearing, being listed twice, as I enable/disable them, and there's considerable lag"): a row never disappears while the tab is open, switching a book off keeps its row with its switch off, a book taken off the chat stays listed, off, until it's removed explicitly, nothing is listed twice, and a switch shows at once, confirmed in the background and put back with a message if it fails. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** **Editing a book from the side panel** (owner, 2026-11-01): "You should be able to view the prompts and edit the lorebook directly from the column next to the chat." Not accordions in the list ("it shifts other lorebooks in the list all the way down"): picking a book's row opens it below the list, one header per entry with its title editable, its keys in small text under it ("usually the most important setting") and a few badges for the essentials, chosen with discretion; Expand all / Collapse all at the top; expanding an entry shows its text box, with its settings a further collapsible level. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14.6).
- **[D]** **The large lorebook editor** (owner, 2026-11-01): it's "incredibly hard to just read all the entries" because their settings sit between the texts; only an entry's content and primary keys show when it's opened (more compact), the rest folded away as a second level. Entry titles stay text boxes ("This is fine. Leave it."). The book's own title is renamed the standard way, and there's a discard (Revert) beside Save. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** **Lorebook expansion is per view** (coordinator decision, 2026-11-02): "expansion is per view. The side panel and the large editor each keep their own expanded set, so expanding or collapsing in one never changes the other." Each view's expansion remains restorable on reload. **Built** (entryclicks; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** **An opened entry is for skimming** (owner, 2026-11-02, comparing the side panel's preset editor with its Lorebooks tab): the preset's prompt, open with and without its options, "is a very good UI, not cluttered at all". The lorebook's entries "are fine in their non-expanded form, and the differences from the preset editor elements there are all justified (eg, header). But the expanded version could be improved to be more slick and less visually noisy when settings for that entry aren't open. Remember the point of this is having them all open (with settings collapsed) and scrolling many and reading the text, title, trigger words to parse skim and parse the lorebook visually." Also: the prompt header's buttons have "seemingly zero margin with the top once highlighted". **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[P]** (loreentry, 2026-11-02) How: the closed entry and its header are unchanged. Open, with its settings closed, an entry is its title, its keys and its text, and nothing else: no "Entry content" or "Primary keys" labels and no standing hint line. The keys stay on the header's quiet line under the title, as when closed (comma-separated, wrapping instead of cut off), beside the same badges; a click or focus turns the line into the box, one key per line, in place, and Escape or leaving it turns it back. Its hover explanation holds the "one per line; /pattern/flags is a regular expression" rule. The text box is as tall as its text (from two lines, at most 60% of the screen), with no fixed empty space. The settings open from a sliders button in the header, beside ⋯, as a preset prompt's options do, shown only while the entry is open; they open above the text, in a tinted panel, and a search match in a setting opens them. The large editor's open entries are the same component (the text now full width, since the keys moved to the header); its 2026-11-01 decisions stand (titles as text boxes, settings a second level). Whether an entry's settings are open isn't remembered across reloads (which entries are open still is). The header inset: a block header's row (preset prompts, notes, snippets) has 4 px above and below instead of 2, so a highlighted button no longer touches the block's edge.
- **[D]** **Search and replace** in the lorebook and preset editors goes through prompt text, trigger keys, or both, as chosen (owner, 2026-11-01). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** **Enable all / Disable all** switch every entry's own state at once ("it's not to disable the lorebook, it's to enable or disable all individual entries so the user can go and pick specific ones to make exception to"), with a way to undo it (owner, 2026-11-01). **Mass edits**: "there isn't even a way to say, switch all lorebook entries to a different position or switch them all to vector or constant"; copying one entry's settings to all "is too coarse because it would override every setting" (owner, 2026-11-01). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] A change to many entries is an ordinary unsaved change** (Enable all, Disable all, Change for selected…; owner, 2026-11-02): it's held like any other edit, Revert beside Save undoes it and Save keeps it. No revision is cut for it and its toast has no Undo of its own. The owner: "I don't understand the logic behind this. A revision isn't even really undoable, you can go back to a previous revision but it's harder than just pressing revert. Isn't that how you would undo it if they didn't save themselves?" **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6). Rejected, so it doesn't come back: the lorebooks job's [P] that each such change cut two revisions of its own ("Before: <the change>", then "<the change>"), undone by the toast restoring the revision before it.
- **[D] Search and replace's "Look in"** defaults to **Everything**, so nothing is missed by default, and the choice is remembered per editor kind (owner, 2026-11-02, confirming the lorebooks job's proposal). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] A preset's book in the chat's Lorebooks tab** ("From the preset …") switches on and off in the preset itself, affecting every chat that uses that preset (owner, 2026-11-02, confirming the lorebooks job's proposal), as the tab's text and tooltip say. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[O], superseded** by “Notes that replace prompts” (§4.2; owner, 2026-11-03): the 2026-11-02 possibility that “a note could override a specific preset field” is now decided for preset prompts and character card fields. The earlier example, switching a preset's lorebook off in one chat only, is not a prompt replacement specified by that decision.
- **[P]** (lorebooks, 2026-11-01, for the owner to confirm) The details of the above (the parts marked **[D]** are the owner's, 2026-11-02):
  - *Obsolete, replaced by "Edit N entries" below (owner, 2026-11-02):* **Change for selected…**: a Select toggle puts a checkbox on each entry; All, The N shown (what the filter leaves) and None select; the dialog picks one setting (strategy, position, depth, order, trigger %, secondary keys and their logic, case, whole words, scan depth, the six "also look for keys in" sources, group, prioritize, group weight, group scoring, the three recursion flags, ignore budget, sticky, cooldown, delay) and its value. **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) "Change for selected…" is in the side panel too: the toolbar wraps at that width.
  - **Search and replace scopes**: Content / Keys / Both / Everything for a book (Everything adds titles, outlet and group names and the book's name and description), Prompt text / Prompt names / Both / Everything for a preset (Everything adds the prefill, utility prompts and regex scripts). Everything as the default, remembered per editor kind, is now **[D]** (above); what each scope covers stays proposed.
  - **The header badges**: Constant or Vector (Normal, the usual, shows nothing); where it goes (↑Char, ↓Char, ↑EM, ↓EM, Outlet, or "@D 4" with the role when it isn't system); the trigger % when it's used and under 100; the secondary key logic with its keys when they're used. Order, matching, groups, recursion and timing are left to the entry's Settings. **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) On a phone the large editor uses the same compact entry headers as the side panel, the table's columns moving into each entry's Settings.
  - **The preset's books in the Lorebooks tab** have a switch, which switches the book in the preset (every chat using it), as the tab's text and tooltip say (now **[D]**, above). A book both added to the chat and sent by the preset is listed once, under the chat, saying when the preset sends it anyway.
  - Which entries are open is remembered per book in this browser, in both views.

- **[D] The lorebook editors' header** (owner, 2026-11-02): "grouping is random and uneven empty space looks cluttered"; elements moved badly when the panel shrank. The filter field belongs in the filter row with All/Enabled/Disabled and the sort, filling it up to a bounded width ("it currently expands far too much when wide"), the entry count staying with the filters; Save takes the filter field's old place beside Add entry, the editing actions together; the bulk and view actions (an expand/collapse toggle, ⋯ with Enable all and Disable all) are grouped deliberately, not orphaned right-aligned text. "Not the only sensible design, but better than the current one." **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[P]** (lorebookeditor, 2026-11-02) How: a title row (the name and its pencil, then **Large editor** as an icon button with its label on hover, and ×); an editing row (**Add entry**, the save cluster beside it, then **Search and replace**); a filter row (All/Enabled/Disabled, the sort picker, the filter field up to 24rem with the count inside its end: "3 entries", "2 of 3 entries"); then the list's own header inside its border: the select-all checkbox over the rows' checkboxes, the selection and its **Edit N entries** button when something is selected, and at its end the expand/collapse toggle and ⋯. Where the large editor's sticky bar has the save cluster and Search and replace, Add entry leads the filter row instead. **Collapse order**, narrowest last: the filter row puts the filter field on its own line below 46rem of width; Search and replace drops its label for its icon below 22rem; nothing else wraps. The selection shows in the list header's own row, so ticking a checkbox never moves the list.
- **[D] Search and replace** (owner, 2026-11-02): the magnifier beside the filter field was find-and-replace ("I would've NEVER guessed… it looks like a submit for it"): it's a labelled **Search and replace** button with a search-and-replace icon, away from the filter field, and named and drawn the same in every editor. Its name is **"Search and replace"** everywhere, labels, panel titles, hovers and accessible names alike, never "Find and replace" (owner, 2026-11-02, correcting an earlier brief). Its inline panel opens below its button ("If we have to layout-shift, it should go down because that doesn't make the button we just clicked on shift from under our cursor"), has a corner × and closes on Escape, and the button shows pressed while it's open. That holds for every inline panel a button opens. The panel is compact at any width: a bounded width, Find and Replace with close together (side by side when wide, stacked when narrow), the options inline with them and each action beside the field it acts on. Go to works for every kind of match, titles and names included. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] Selecting entries uses the standard header checkbox** (owner, 2026-11-02: "Everyone looks for a checkbox above the checkbox column... This is a very standard UI."): it selects all and none, indeterminate while some are selected, and replaces Select mode and All/None. **Built**. (No other multi-select list had All/None buttons to unify: preset prompts, the bin and the library have no multi-select.)
- **[D] Edit N entries** (owner, 2026-11-02, replacing "Change for selected…", which changed one setting at a time): the regular per-entry settings form with a checkbox beside every setting; unticked settings are greyed and left alone, showing the selection's shared value or "Mixed"; ticked ones apply, several at once, with one Apply. "Mixed" is styled so it never reads as a value, even for fields with few values: switches and checkboxes in their indeterminate state, selects, text and numbers empty with an italic muted "Mixed" placeholder (never an option in the list) and a small marker whose hover gives the breakdown ("170 on, 4 off"). A ticked empty field clears the value, which tells "unset this" from "leave it alone". **Built**.
  - **[P]** (lorebookeditor, 2026-11-02) The dialog is titled "Edit N entries" with no sentence under it; Apply counts the entries that will actually change ("Apply to 2 entries"; none: disabled). A cleared number that has no unset state (depth, order, group weight, trigger %) takes a new entry's value. Secondary keys, generation types, the character filter and the card-export name and priority aren't offered for several entries at once.
- **[D] Per-entry settings** (owner, 2026-11-02): expanding the settings "blows up the sidebar entry"; sections stacked vertically push the entry text far below. The owner raised accordions or tabs. The entry's text comes right after its keys, the settings below it; the export-only fields (automation ID, add memo, export name and priority, the regex flag) go together in one closed "Card export only" section with no per-field "kept for export" text. **Built**.
  - **[P]** (coordinator and lorebookeditor, 2026-11-02) Accordions with summaries: in the side panel every settings section except Strategy and position (superseded by the owner, 2026-11-03, above) is an accordion, closed by default, its header showing a one-line summary of what differs from the defaults ("Sticky 3 · Cooldown 2", or "Default settings") and a quiet dot when anything does. The old "Timing, filters and automation" fold is no longer an exception. The large editor keeps its two columns with every boxed section open (each with the same dot, except Card export only per the owner’s 2026-11-03 decision above). The settings stay behind the header's settings button, so opened entries remain skimmable. Sections and settings are registries (`lorebookEntrySections`, `lorebookEntrySettings`), so the single-entry form and Edit N entries are one form.
- **[D] Whole-header expansion for lorebook entries** (owner, 2026-11-02): the whole header opens and closes the entry, like a preset prompt's, while the title field, keys line, switch, ⋯ and checkbox keep their own clicks; the arrow stays the keyboard's toggle. Lorebook entries only: preset prompts are draggable, so their background doesn't expand. **Built**.
- **[D] The chat's Lorebooks rows** (owner, 2026-11-02): every row shows its source and its entry count, a book added to the chat included. **Built**: library books carry their count (`LibraryRef.lorebookEntries`).
  - **[P]** (lorebookeditor, 2026-11-02) A book added to the chat whose name (ignoring case and spaces) and entry count match an active card's own book says "Also active as <card>'s own lorebook", since its entries are sent twice. The "Artoria Pendragon 's Lorebook" space is the source's: the import copies a book's name as the file or card has it and never builds a name with "'s".
- **[D] The chat's placement setting** (owner, 2026-11-02): one switch, **Force all lorebook entries below the history**, the full explanation in its "?", and under it "Applies to this chat, for all lorebooks." **Built**.
- **[D] Hints don't explain the save model** (owner, 2026-11-02): the side panel's × says "Closes this lorebook." **Built**.
- **[D] Adding an item reveals and flashes it** (owner, 2026-11-02): an action that adds an item scrolls the new item fully into view, header included, and flashes its box a few times; buttons that add are "Add <thing>", their hover saying what's added and where. With reduced motion, a steady highlight fades instead. **Built** for Add entry and Duplicate (`ui/reveal.ts` `revealAdded`, shared for the preset editor's Add prompt).

### 1.4 ST data folder import

- **[D]** (owner brief, 2026-11-07): "The ST import does not turn settings.json's default_persona into the account's default persona. Add that to the import" and "make the dev seed set a default persona". **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (followups-ac, ARCHITECTURE §13). The dev sync imports the realistic fixture's existing `sam.png` default through the corrected importer.
- **[D] Import warning timing** (owner, 2026-11-03): "it makes no sense to show such a warning before the import is finished or terminated." Missing, not-imported, unmatched and skipped notices appear only after the import finishes, is cancelled, or fails/stops; progress and real errors remain visible during the run. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[D] Import result flow** (owner, 2026-11-02): "Where is the warning/error log?" The finished import page must show the grouped warnings and errors, with counts, affected-item links and Copy log; errors first, then warnings, then info. The layout is "a disaster": "four boxes in random order", the job listed twice, the import form in the middle. Show one flow: Result (status, when, duration, counts and live progress), Needs your action (only unresolved decisions, counted, each issue beside its controls), Warnings and errors, History, Start another import. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[D] Import actions and history** (owner, 2026-11-02): "Chats to link" must be an obvious action row, not a middle box far from "1 to look at". Resolved decisions disappear. "there's 'Remove from the list' and 'Clear finished imports' in two places?!" History has one Clear finished imports control in its header and a per-row ⋯ menu with Remove from history, and date, status, counts and a link to each result. Put the form last, behind Import ST data again, expanded when no import exists. Shorten the hints and "Using a browser on another computer…" copy. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[P] Result layout details** (importpage, 2026-11-03). Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal. Keep the existing narrow column, semantic surfaces and spacing. Default to the newest import, retaining `?job=` for history selections. Compact stats include zeroes; imported means added plus updated, with single/group chat outcomes combined. Keep the selected import in the one history list. All issue groups start collapsed, even singletons, with the issue message and affected-file count in the header; retain rarest-first order within each severity. Include failed transfers and job failures in the log and error count. With no issues show a brief empty state. Decision rows wrap on phones. The form trigger stays above the form, shows its pressed state and opens a panel with a corner close button. Existing registered import sections remain replaceable; history precedes the start section.
- **[D] Source modification and arrival dates** (owner, 2026-11-02): "it also seems like the 'edited' time is whenever they were imported for ST. For this and other things that we mass-import from ST in the job, we should make some effort to see if we can find out *when* they were actually last modified in ST, and import that value if possible. (eg, filetimes of internal files maybe)" Follow-up (same owner and date): "'Added' for characters has the same issue as what I said about imported presets. It counts the time we auto-imported characters from ST for everything. If these cards were manually imported one by one, it would be fine. But we should try to see if we can find out when a character was originally 'added' to ST and inherit that during the full import job."
- **[D] Preset and lorebook edit dates** (owner, 2026-11-02): "a preset's (and a standalone lorebook's) modified date is its own file's time." `settings.json` records ordinary ST use, so its time never counts as a preset/book edit, even when settings supply mapped fields. Personas remain the exception because their definitions live in settings. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §13); fresh imports only, with no repair of already-imported dates.
- **[P] Source date selection. Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §13). Prefer an actual modification timestamp inside the data, then the source file's modification time, then this import's start time. Creation timestamps are not edit timestamps. Browser folder uploads carry each `File.lastModified`; server folder reads carry `mtimeMs`. A folder ZIP is not an intake surface; a CHARX card can supply ZIP entry times.
  - Cards: positive CCv3 `data.modification_date` (Unix seconds; zero means unknown), else the latest consumed CHARX entry's modification time if applicable, else the card file time. ST's installation-local `create_date` dates arrival; Created prefers the CCv3 author's `creation_date`, with arrival as its fallback; `data.creation_date` and `date_last_chat` do not date edits. Embedded lorebooks, regex scripts and card notes share the containing revision's date.
  - Presets and standalone lorebooks: only their own file times, including when mapped fields come from `settings.json` (preset text switches/global-book attachments or lorebook author's-note placement). Settings time never dates these edits. No saved ST edit-date field exists for these files. Preset Created and Added remain the time the entity and upstream ref were first imported here; ST supplies no independent creation/arrival evidence for presets.
  - Personas: the later of avatar and `settings.json` times when settings define the persona, otherwise the avatar time. Their description notes share that date. Character author's notes from settings use the settings file's time.
  - Chats and group chats: messages/swipes retain ST `send_date`; a missing one uses the previous/header date, then the chat file time, then import time. Last activity remains message activity. Chat-owned author's notes use the chat file time (or settings time for a default note); each imported summary revision uses the message it was saved on, with the file/import fallback.
  - Groups supply names, casts and reply settings, not a separate versioned entity. ST derives `date_last_chat` from chat file activity; it cannot date group edits. Settings adoption and import-job bookkeeping are not historical revisions. There is no standalone regex-file import or global-regex import from settings; retained card/preset regex lists share their containing revision's date.
  - Imported revision timestamps and ref Modified dates use these source dates on initial import and changed re-imports. Unchanged content adds no revision. Local edits and real merge revisions keep their own current times; entities first created here without a source creation date keep their import-time Created date. File times are best effort: copying files can reset them, and one settings file cannot distinguish individual persona/note edit dates. ZIP DOS dates have no timezone and are interpreted as UTC; extended UTC timestamps take precedence.
- **[P] Full-folder Added dates. Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §13). Keep Added and Created separate. A card's top-level ST `create_date` is arrival metadata, not its author's creation date; a persona's timestamp-prefixed avatar filename and a group's millisecond `id` record their creation in ST (a group's saved `create_date` is the next metadata fallback). Otherwise use the earliest actual dated message attributed to that imported card/persona, or the earliest retained group-chat message/header for a group; otherwise the older available positive file birth/mtime, otherwise import start. Never use ctime, chat filename dates or regenerated thumbnails. ST sets imported-card `create_date` on every import, replacement imports included, so the original pre-replacement arrival can be unrecoverable; ordinary edits preserve the submitted date, while a fresh card's date may initially be synthesized from ctime by ST's listing endpoint. Metadata wins over archived chat dates because chat archives may predate arrival in this installation. Across our re-imports, retain the earliest equal-quality evidence and allow stronger evidence to replace weaker evidence. Every chat of an imported ST group shares its best arrival evidence. CCv3 positive `data.creation_date` (Unix seconds) remains Created; with no independent authorship date, earliest arrival is also the fallback evidence for Created, so both dates are the same. A card imported manually or written here keeps Added = now. Library personas use their ref's Added too.
- **[D]** Point the importer at an ST data folder. It finds the chats and their cards and adds them seamlessly. Exporting chats back to ST is secondary. **Chat export built and independently reviewed** (importexport1/review-importexport1, 2026-11-14; obsolete as an implementation plan): a branch or every branch as ST chat files with all versions, or plain text (ARCHITECTURE §13b).
- **[D]** ST has a single hidden flag. If a long consecutive run of hidden messages starts at message 0 (the manual-summary pattern), the importer offers to un-hide them so LCM can take over. Other hidden messages stay excluded from context.
- **[D]** Chats that have only the greeting and zero user turns are skipped (ST creates these just by opening a card). The import report gave the count until the owner cut the report to what a user must act on (2026-10-28, §1.2).
- **[P]** Mapping: **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §13).
  - single and group chats → chats with N participants
  - ST branch files (linked by metadata) → one message graph
  - swipes → versions
  - personas → cards in the persona folder, with their original images (ST's card-to-persona conversion blurs them)
  - global, chat-bound and embedded lorebooks → lorebooks
  - presets → presets
- **[P]** ST links chats to cards by folder name, so when a card was renamed the importer needs fallback matching. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[P]** **The import report says a repeated issue once** (importreport, 2026-11-02, after the owner: "This is too much spam… what is 66 more aren't listed?! … the warning drowns out anything else"). The same issue on many files is one entry with its count and every file listed under it, each linked to its chat; errors first, then the rarest first; nothing is cut silently (a report holds 20,000 issues and says when more didn't fit). Built (ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[P]** **An imported chat's date is when it started** (importreport, 2026-11-02): the chat's `created_at` (ST's header date, else its first message's), not the date in ST's file name, which can be years older (ST names a card's first chat after the card's `chat` field, which a downloaded card brings from its maker). Built (ARCHITECTURE §13). Its name: the next entry.
- **[D]** **An imported chat's title** (owner, 2026-11-02, confirming the proposal: "when ST's chat file name is only a timestamp, the title is the characters plus the chat's start date; when the user named the chat in ST, the title is that name"). ST's default names count as only a timestamp: a group chat's bare `humanizedDateTime()` stamp and a character chat's `<card name> - <stamp>` (ST's `script.js` and `group-chats.js`), in either stamp form, with or without ST's branch suffix. The start date is the chat's `created_at` (the entry above). Built (ARCHITECTURE §13).
  - **[P]** "<card name>" means one of the chat's characters' names, case aside; a card renamed since ST names no character, so its chats keep the old name before the stamp, with the start date. A name before a stamp that isn't a character's is the user's (a rename here keeps the stamp after the name).
  - **[D] Group titles** (owner, 2026-11-02: "ST's automatic group name counts as a default, not a user name"; "A stamp in a group chat's title is never kept raw"). A stamp-only file in a default-named group (`Group: ` plus the actual members' names, as ST's `createGroup` builds it) is titled by the characters plus the chat's start date (`created_at`), exactly like a character chat. A renamed group keeps its name plus that start date. A user-named chat file keeps that chat name. Fresh imports only; no migration or repair of existing data. Built (ARCHITECTURE §13).
- **[P]** An LCM backfill of a large imported chat runs lazily (for example when the chat is first opened, or in idle time), not as part of the import. **Built with explicit enable/adoption and backfill approval** (ARCHITECTURE §16); §8’s later owner decision makes approved catch-up up front. Import itself starts no summarization.

### 1.5 Out of scope
- **[D]** ST extensions and plugins, external ST integrations, text completion, connection profiles.

---

## 2. Chats and participants

### 2.1 A chat is its own object

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, branchrows) “At least showing the latest used branch's set means it's a set of characters that's actually currently in the chat.” A chat keeps its current branch's representation. Home's Group chats and the Chats tab's Group chats filter both require two or more characters on that branch. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D] Phone drawer and character sheet** (coordinator correction, 2026-11-04): “on phone, opening a character from the left menu drawer must close that drawer”, while “the new chat's character card sheet still opens as before”. The left drawer’s **Close the menu** and the character sheet’s **Close** are separate controls. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (nostartchat; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Phone new-chat arrival** (coordinator, 2026-11-04): “On phone, starting a new chat with a character must land the user on the new chat with its greeting visible, as the removed header picker did; a character sheet or drawer left open over it is the regression.” **Superseded by the coordinator’s drawer/sheet clarification below.**
- **[D] Header chat action** (owner, 2026-11-04): “This is making me think we should remove the "start a chat" affordance on the top header. It's tempting to keep, because it is a fundamental action, and having it always visible makes some sense. But visually it fits quite poorly on the header, and clicking it and getting this kind of little dropdown selector feels kind of awkward, even more so when the same thing exists in a bigger more permanent form right under it on the same column.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (nostartchat; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Card list versions** (coordinator, 2026-11-04): “Hide it in every card list/picker that shows it this way (left-column grid, home, library lists, pickers); keep it where a card's version is shown as a labelled field (editors).” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (nostartchat; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Chats with a cast member** (owner, 2026-11-02): “in a chat with several characters there's no obvious way to reach ‘all chats with this character’ for one of them.” The cast member menu offers **Chats with <name>**, immediately after **Write as <name>**; for the user's persona, **Chats as <name>**. The shared card header and card page offer **N chats** (singular **1 chat**), on its own muted row, hidden for a card with no chats. **Shared-header placement superseded by the 2026-11-03 four-row decision below:** its count now joins the final token line; the card page keeps its existing placement. These open the existing card-scoped Chats list in the left column, with its way back to All chats; the current chat stays in the centre. Scope survives Back/Forward and reload. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (chatswith; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Sidebar selection** (owner, 2026-11-02): “whenever the left column shows a list that contains the current chat, that chat is selected in the upper list, and the lower half's branch viewer shows its branches with the current branch selected, including a secondary branch.” If the current chat is filtered out, the upper list has no selection and the lower viewer still follows the current chat. Applies after navigation and in scoped and all-chat lists. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (chatswith; ARCHITECTURE §12).
  - **[P]** (chatswith, 2026-11-03): Reuse the URL's `character` scope and existing breadcrumb; clear the sidebar's search when following a Chats link. Reveal a hidden sidebar, or show the existing navigation drawer on phones, closing the phone's card panel/editor. The header's new registered `chats` placement precedes the dedicated Persona row, with token counts last; on the card page it precedes the Persona switch. Reuse the member menu for the persona's navigation and card actions, beside Switch.
- **[D] Chats tab filters and sorting** (owner, 2026-11-02): "the Chats tab in the left column has only "Find by name". Give it filters and sorting, modelled on the Characters tab." One compact row under search, with counted filters, a sort and the shared direction control; pinned chats stay above the list; choices persist per account and in the URL. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12, Chats tab browsing).
- **[P]** (chatsort, 2026-11-02) Options: All, Pinned, Group chats (two or more characters), One-on-one (one character), With branches (more than one live fork); Recent (existing recency, default), Started (chat start), Name, Messages (current path), Branches (live fork count). Dates start newest first, Name A–Z, counts most first; changing the sort resets its direction. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §12). The sidebar actually pages `GetShellForks`, so it owns server filtering/sorting rather than `ListChats`, which serves separate screens. Keep the existing independent pinned-branch section and its own sort, applying the chat filter to it; keep the existing per-branch ordering when scoped to one chat. Counts ignore the name search, as character counts do. Memory is per account in this browser, following character browsing. The owner clarified on 2026-11-03: "this is a cosmetic change." Recent therefore retains the implemented activity ordering, including Branch from here, and its existing browser regression stays unchanged.
- **[D] Workshop chats in the chat lists** (owner, 2026-11-13): “they absolutely need their own filter and entry style. They must be immediately recognizable as different.” **Built** (decisions1113; ARCHITECTURE §12 "Chats tab browsing", §16): the Chats filter has a **Workshops** choice, a workshop appears only under All and Workshops (the other choices and their counts read as story chats), and a workshop's row, wherever chats or their branches are listed with the shared row (including its compact branch variant), has the workshop's tint, an accent edge and its badge in the accent. The badge word stays, so the difference never rests on colour alone.
- **[D] A workshop has no “New chat with this cast”** (coordinator ruling from the workshop design, 2026-11-13): a workshop's cast is its assistant, so its ⋯ menu leaves the command out; story chats keep it. **Built** (decisions1113).
- **[D]** In ST you *message a card*: the card is the chat target, usually a whole scenario, and effectively the only chat with that character (older chats are history travel). Group chats are a special case created by irreversibly promoting a chat. Here, **a chat is a first-class object with any number of cards**. A single-card chat is just the trivial case.
- **[D]** Keep the convenience of ST's one click from the grid into a conversation, with the card definitions and the other participants in a side panel.
- **[P]** A card's page offers **Continue** (its latest chat), **New chat**, and the list of all its chats as peers. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** Opening a card must not create a chat.
- **[P]** Chats are created lazily: clicking a card with no chats opens a *pending* chat showing the greeting (alternate greetings selectable), saved only when the first message is sent. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] A pending chat looks and works like any other chat** (owner, 2026-11-02, after clicking a character with no chats: "the UI for 'no chat character' is shitty and inconsistent with the others. Lots of weird misaligned stuff too. It has its own un-resizeable sidebar with this weird button that has wrong spacing, for example (image 1). Image 2: what is this corner? The bot's name in the middle, below where the name already is, (why?) and then misaligned next to it an 'add character' UI?"): the same header, message column, composer and side panel, resizable, with the same tabs in the same places; what needs a saved chat is absent or says it comes once the chat is saved, not a different layout. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** How (pendingchat, 2026-11-02): the pending chat is a `PendingChat` the saved chat's parts read as a view with no chat. Persona, Reply from, Remove and Add characters move to the Cast tab (Add characters is the saved chat's grid); the greeting's alternates are stepped at its end, as a message's versions; the card's Full editor and New chat are in its character tab. The header shows the name the chat is saved under ("Aesc, Nov 2") with a "new" badge, and has the panel button but no pin or ⋯. Model and Prompt preset offer their pickers (the account's), Display and Snippets are the same; This chat, Notes, Lorebooks, Summaries and Search and replace say they open once the chat is saved. The first send replaces the URL with the saved chat's, its panel at the same tab, and the saved page opens on the chat as the send left it (path and cast read in the same action), so nothing flashes or jumps: no loading skeleton, no placeholder title.
- **[D] A chat not sent yet shows its context size and the request its first send would make** (owner, 2026-11-12, asked "Should a new chat that hasn't been sent yet show the context size and the request it would send, as a saved chat does? It would apply to every new chat"): "Yes. Make sure this doesn't cause anything to be sent to inference providers." Every pending chat, from a local card, a group or a card source.
  - **[P]**, **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12, "The first request of a chat not sent yet") (cardex3, 2026-11-12) How: the server builds the first send's request through the real assembler over sources that stand in for the chat not made yet (the cast's joins and persona, the greetings as picked, the typed message, and the reply after it), reading cards, notes, lorebooks, the preset and the model as the first send would. Nothing is written, and the path has no provider call, no remote token count and no model-list fetch: counts come from the local tokenizers (an approximation where a model has none, labelled as an estimate). The next-reply strip gets a **Context** chip ("~1.2k tokens"), whose popover says what it counts and has **View request**, which opens the request inspector on that request in a dialog (its state in the URL, `request=first`). With text in the box the request is Send's (that message, then the reply); with an empty box, Generate's.
  - **[P]** What the dialog shows is the request as the assembler maps it for the model's API (the inspector's message list, sources and samplers), not bytes captured from a provider, since nothing is sent; a provider plugin's own last-moment transforms aren't in it.
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: Message-activity ordering is built (ARCHITECTURE §12); the separate portrait Inspect action is superseded by the owner’s one-click chat plus definitions rule below. Earlier wording: “Recents are sorted by last message, never by last opened. A separate *inspect* action shows a card without entering a chat.”
- **[D]** Cards can be filtered by "never used".
- **[P]** Chats have editable titles (auto-generated by default, never just the first character's name). The thumbnail is an avatar collage by default, and can be overridden. **Partial/overtaken**: editable names and cast portraits are built (ARCHITECTURE §12); model-generated titles are explicitly rejected below. Arbitrary per-chat thumbnail overrides remain unbuilt/open. Built (families1, 2026-11-15): a chat can have its own picture, chosen in This chat; see ARCHITECTURE §12. Still [P].
- **[P], rejected as the home screen (see below)** The main surfaces are a **chat list** (recent first, also the mobile home screen) and a **library** (cards, personas, presets, lorebooks). **Overtaken** by the owner’s rejection and the shell/home navigation (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** Chats imported from ST must be easy to recognize and find. Most users' chats, the owner's included, will come from ST (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D]** One click on a portrait gives both the chat and the card's definitions, as in ST. No flow may take more steps than ST's or add clutter where it makes no sense: separate "open chat" and "open card" buttons on a portrait are a regression (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D] Rejected:** a list of the most recent chats as the home screen (the first UI). Rows titled like "Jane Doe - 2026-04-23@6:00 AM", with forks of the same chat flattened among them, can't be recognized or picked out. The owner judged that design not worth improving (owner, 2026-10-14; restated 2026-10-16).
- **[D]** The character grid with group tiles (redesign of 2026-10-14, R2-A) mirrors ST's data model, individual cards plus "group chats" with a 2×2 portrait of the first four members, and has the wrong concepts for this harness, though it does make imported chats easy to find (owner, 2026-10-16).
- For reference, ST's layout (owner, 2026-10-16): the main screen shows the three most recent chats; the character and group grid is always in a side panel, never in the center. That panel picks and searches characters and groups; clicking one replaces the grid with that card's definitions, while the main view becomes the current chat with that character or group (there is only one current chat).
- **[D]** Characters are added to and removed from a chat seamlessly. The natural way to get a chat with several characters is to start with one and add more; adding more on the first turn is a special case. So there's no multi-card picker or multi-step creation, and creation must not reintroduce a distinction between "group" and single-card chats (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D] Rejected:** titles generated from the story by a model. It assumes a separately configured cheap model, and chats with the same card all start the same way, so it's a more expensive way of naming them after the card (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[O]** What a usable UX is on data made entirely in this app, with nothing imported (owner, 2026-10-16). **Implementation concern addressed** by New card, New preset and pending-chat creation (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14); owner daily-use acceptance remains open.
- **[O]** **Overtaken**: The owner’s “Each fork is effectively its own chat” and current/peer rule (§10.3) supply the model. Stable fork IDs, checkpoints, pins, browsing and switcher are built (ARCHITECTURE §3.8, §12); owner UX acceptance remains unverified. Earlier wording: “How forks of a chat are treated: part of the same chat or separate chats, how they're browsed and chosen, where they're switched. ST could treat everything with the same character or group as one more chat, new or forked, because its forks are copies (owner, 2026-10-16).”
- **[O]** **Overtaken**: New chat creates a new peer chat through the pending flow (ARCHITECTURE §12), consistent with §10.3’s independent-chat model; it does not create another root in an existing chat. Earlier wording: “Does "start over" with a card create a new peer chat (proposed), or a new root inside the same chat?”
- **[D] Rejected:** per-participant roles (character vs. narrator) in each chat. They force a separation, and muting plus the card's own text already cover it. Per-card preset overlays replace them (§6.3).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, groupgrid) "make the gaps between portraits equal horizontally and vertically, at every panel width, with any number of members." Portrait/card grids use one CSS `gap` for both axes. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).


- **[D] Card archiving** (owner, 2026-11-06): “We currently have nothing for organizing cards, besides favourites... I am thinking we should add an 'archive' button which is essentially the opposite of a favourite. We want a way to remove cards from normal search results and listings, without having to delete them, since our frontend avoids making users delete anything permanently. This would add an 'archived' tab to the homepage for example, and it would be another filter option. They'd go out of 'All', so they won't be listed there. The issue is it's called 'All', so this annoys me a little. We could add another toggle like the persona one under 'All' to make it technically be all if that is enabled ('Show archived') but I am not sure of the utility of this. I also want a UI somewhere to 'mass archive' cards based on some common sense criteria. For example, it should be able to archive all cards that were never used and were added at least so much time ago: cards that were downloaded and never ended up being used. Or, cards that were used but haven't been in a long time (I wouldn't archive those, someone might).” **Built (cardarchive); obsolete as implementation requirements**; ARCHITECTURE §3.9 and §12.
- **[P]** (cardarchive, 2026-11-06) Keep the favourite star when archiving: it expresses preference independently of visibility, and unarchiving restores Favourites membership. Every ordinary card listing and picker excludes archived entries, including personas. Home and the sidebar have an explicit Archived filter. Archived matches appear only under the All filter, after the ordinary results in a collapsed **Archived (N)** group; no Show archived toggle. Archived includes personas without a second toggle, so every hidden card can be found there. Existing chats keep their cast and definitions. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** The archive toggle on a tile (owner, 2026-11-06): "Is this for real? How is ONE button messed up in so many different ways?" and "The archive button is always visible, it's in a really ugly position style wise (why is it under a different button instead of its own corner?)" The archive toggle has its own corner, the top right (Open beside's, now removed), and the star keeps the top left. Both behave alike: hidden at rest unless on (starred, archived), shown while the tile is hovered or holds focus, always shown on touch screens, the same size, one backing on the button itself. **Built** (archivebtn; ARCHITECTURE §5 "Characters").
- **[P]** (archivebtn, 2026-11-06) Add-to-chat pickers show no archive toggle: their top right holds the chat-membership mark, picking a cast isn't where cards are tidied, and pickers list no archived cards anyway. The toggle's hover names the action and its effect: "Click to archive Mira: hidden from All and searches, found under Archived" / "Click to unarchive Mira: back in All and searches"; a menu's Archive/Unarchive says "Hides Mira from All and searches; find Mira under Archived" / "Shows Mira in All and searches again". Like the star, it shows its new state at once, and while hidden it takes no clicks (a click there opens the tile). Headers and the side panel's card tab keep star and archive together as one group, so a wrapping toolbar moves them as a pair. Card ⋯ menus (library, side panel) drop their Archive entry, since a visible archive control sits beside each, as the star has no menu entry; a tile's right-click menu keeps it. The layout audit checks controls over pictures at rest as well as hovered (one backing on the control, no space kept by a hidden sibling, the same corner inset on every tile of a grid, only pressed toggles shown at rest). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5).
- **[P]** (cardarchive, 2026-11-06) Bulk archive uses either checked registered criterion: never used and added more than 30 days ago (on initially), or last used more than 6 calendar months ago (off initially); thresholds are adjustable. Used means a human message or a nonempty model reply while the card participated, any retained swipe or branch, or a card field edit (drafts included) or fork (both the original and new fork). A greeting alone, an empty failed generation, opening a card or joining a cast does not count; imported initial model messages are treated as greetings. Binned chat history still records use until purged. Favourites never qualify. The reader previews names, portraits, added and last-use dates; the user can exclude individual matches. One writer transaction rechecks eligibility and archives the selected matches. One Undo restores just that operation’s unchanged archive states. Criteria descriptors and predicates are registered for the later cardex “Only tried” rule. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §3.9).

### 2.2 Personas, speakers and writers

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16, on the Write for me composer draft writer) "THAT IS WHAT ST DOES AND IT SUCKS. YOU CANNOT SWIPE IT, YOU CAN ONLY DELETE THE TEXT AND REDO IT. WE ALREADY HAVE GENERATE AND GUIDANCE FOR THIS." The composer draft writer (writeforme1) is rejected and removed. The user's turn is written by Generate as the persona, which makes a real message with swipeable versions, steered by Guidance. The editable user-turn preset instruction ("When your card speaks") stays: Generate as a persona uses it.

- **[P]** (coordinator brief, 2026-11-06; not the owner's words) “New persona” from persona lists and the persona switcher's list end: “A persona form like New card, with name, picture and description, using the existing card creation where the data model shares it.” Built by newflows; **superseded** by the [D] below.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-07, on the persona form newflows built) "No, quite literally every single thing about it is wrong. There's nothing correct here. This is not our card editor, which is what a persona is, for one. No way to select display name, version, nickname. No way to do all the other definitions. It doesn't use our avatar cutout UI that was developed. And most importantly the description field is a plain field and not our code editor. Every single thing here needs to go". A persona is a card; New persona is New card's flow and our card editor: the same form and fields as New card (one component, no persona fork of the form), only titled "New persona" and created in the persona folder, then opened in the card editor, where the portrait is set with the avatar cutout UI and the description and every other definition in the code editor. **Built** (personaform, ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **[P]** (coordinator brief, 2026-11-07) From a chat's persona selector, the new persona also becomes the chat's persona and opens in the side panel's card editor; from the library lists it opens in the full editor, as New card does. **Built** (personaform, ARCHITECTURE §14).

- **[D] 2026-11-05 (casttabscroll)** Owner: "Clicking on cast tabs scrolls to the description for some reason". Ordinary cast-tab opening starts at the top; explicit reveal and search Go to retain their requested destination. Implemented and obsolete as an implementation requirement; see ARCHITECTURE §12.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): "The new add to chat button in add characters is approved." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).

- **[D]** **Add-grid insets and metadata** (coordinator review, 2026-11-04): "The grid must sit inside the same horizontal insets as the Find by name field above it." "There is a large empty gap between the filter row and the grid; match the spacing used between the other rows." "Every tile shows a bare '1' line under the name, which says nothing to the user. Find what it is: drop it if it is a version or internal value, or label it if it is meaningful." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12): the bare line is the character version and is omitted in this grid; search and grid align and use the same row gap.
- **[D]** **Add belongs inside the tile** (owner, 2026-11-04): "This fails in a very basic way. The buttons are not part of the cards or connected to them. Nothing says what they control". The control is the tile's own footer inside its shared border and background, below the name/version/chats lines and separated by a divider. Labels are "Add to chat" and disabled "In this chat", with the unavailability explained on hover; the rest of the tile opens the editor. Supersedes the external-button placement and labels in the addchars [P] below. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** **Inspect before adding** (owner, 2026-11-04): "Currently, this UI adds characters immediately when they're clicked. There's two issues with this: 1. it goes against what clicking on a character in such a grid does everywhere else, which is opening it 2. It makes it impossible to inspect a character you find first. I'm thinking that we need to add a control to each card that actually adds the character, and it should be very obvious, and also fit well in the layout. Clicking on a character would instead open it in the big editor". **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (addchars; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** Implementation (addchars, 2026-11-04): reuse the chat's center-column editor and its existing Close control. Place a full-width "+ Add" control below each tile, replaced in place by disabled "In chat" for members and the selected persona; explain the action or unavailability on hover/focus. Keep portraits fully openable. Implemented in the replaceable `cast.add-characters` section; see ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04, focusring): “fix it. This should go without saying”. Centre keyboard focus evenly around the bare Persona icon and the enabled badge, preserving header layout and text alignment; check other bare icon buttons for the same offset. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** (focusring, 2026-11-04): draw the aligned off-state ring in a positioned pseudo-element centred on the icon; preserve the existing hit area and badge ring. Implemented in ARCHITECTURE §12.

- **[D] Header counts order** (owner, 2026-11-04): “in the persona cluster, chats should come after tokens/permanent on that line, not before, because it's the only one that might be missing.” Owner correction (2026-11-04): “I mean character cluster”. The rule applies to the character tab’s card header (and personas shown there): “~tokens · ~permanent · N chats”, omitting the last separator when chats are absent. Supersedes the counts order in the four-row decision below. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (countsorder; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04, personabtn follow-up): “The icon in the deactivated state can just not have a border/button styling. It becomes highlighted on hover and does give the cursor a pointer. It turns into a badge when enabled. That solves it, since we can align the two states differently.” Off is a bare icon with colour-only hover and pointer cursor; on uses the padded icon-and-Persona badge. The off icon and on box align with the text; both retain focus rings and explanations. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04, personabtn follow-up): “Also I'm currently thinking that the button belongs above the chats/tokens line after all. But the chats/tokens line should be aligned with the foot of the picture, and the persona toggle in the middle of the space this leaves.” Keep name and nickname at the top, counts at the portrait bottom, and centre Persona between nickname and counts; retain at least 4 px gaps when space is short. Supersedes the smallfixes4 placement below counts and the earlier personabtn shared-edge geometry. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** (personabtn follow-up, 2026-11-04): stretch the panel text stack beside the portrait; let the Persona row absorb free height, with a centred 28 px control and a 16 px counts line below. Keep full-editor geometry unchanged. Implemented in ARCHITECTURE §12.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04, personabtn): production Persona button: “This isn't good either.” Superseded by the follow-up above. In every side-panel state, align the visible left edge with the name, nickname and counts; keep fill, hover and focus inside the text column and retain the row gaps. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** (personabtn, 2026-11-04): replace the negative row offset with a flush button box, place the icon stroke at its left edge, and draw keyboard focus inside the box; preserve the other header geometry. Implemented in ARCHITECTURE §12.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “Controls must never overlap.” Fix the character identity row “at the minimum panel width and every width above it, without changing the layout at normal widths”; “the pencils stay whole and clickable.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** (identityoverlap, 2026-11-04): reserve the name/version pencils’ combined minimum width and allow flex wrapping only when the identity controls cannot fit. Implemented in ARCHITECTURE §12.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): Side-panel card header: “the spacing looks messed up again here”. Align the Persona icon's visible left edge with the text column and use consistent row spacing, preserving control placement, size and hit areas. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** (headerspacing, 2026-11-04): give the side-panel counts row the same 28 px minimum height as nickname and Persona; retain 4 px row gaps. The original negative Persona offset is superseded by the personabtn decision above. The persona-card variant shares this layout; the group header uses a separate component.


- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “The 'Full Editor' button is buried in the action list for the card. It's not an action.” Match the other sidebar editors' full-editor control and position. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P]** (smallfixes5, 2026-11-04): render the existing registered `card.edit` contribution as an expand icon after Save in the card identity row; retain its menu presentation elsewhere.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): **Placement superseded by the personabtn follow-up above.** In the side panel character header, “the persona button needs to be at the bottom of this cluster”, below the chats/token counts. Remove the portrait's Persona badge here: “entirely redundant”; keep You and badges elsewhere. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-03, personadefault) "we'll never automatically switch someone's persona for a chat once the chat started. It stays whatever the user selected." Changing the default never touches existing chats. "we do need a user-selected default for new chats." One account default persona, or none, starts new single and group chats only if the card is still a live persona. Picking another persona on the pending page affects only that chat. Persona lists/pickers and persona-card menus offer **Set as default persona** / **Clear default persona**, with a small **Default** badge. Hover: "New chats start with this persona. Existing chats keep theirs." / "New chats start with no persona." The pending chat's Playing as name adds **(default)** when selected. With no default, no persona; no first-folder-card fallback or browser-local last-persona setting. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-03, personadefault) Preset and model "are universal settings": account-wide. A per-chat preset or model is "an exception, not the rule". The pickers opened from the existing chips get a switch row **Lock for this chat**, hover "This chat keeps this preset when you change it elsewhere." / "This chat keeps this model when you change it elsewhere." A locked chip shows a small lock icon as a state marker, not a button. Changing the selection in a locked chat changes only that chat's lock; elsewhere it changes the account setting. The picker originally stated **This chat only** / **Every chat**; lockline2 below supersedes that scope text. No separate lock buttons in the row: the owner did not want two more affordances there. **Built 2026-11-04 (lockchat); obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, lockline) “The spacing here is also messed up, moreover, why does it say "Every chat"” (Prompt preset picker).
- **[P]** (lockline; superseded by lockline2 below) Replace the bare scope line with “Picking a preset here changes it for every chat.” when unlocked and “Picking a preset here changes it for this chat only.” when locked; use “a model” in the Model picker. Use the shared Switch description, aligned below its normal-size, normal-colour label, with smaller muted description text, standard spacing and padding before the divider. **Built 2026-11-05; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, lockline2) On “Picking a preset here changes it for every chat.” under “Lock for this chat”: “That looks like it's explaining what "Lock for this chat" means.” The coordinator directs removing the line entirely in both states and both pickers: the row contains only **Lock for this chat** with its existing hover explanation. The picker no longer states the scope in text; the locked chip’s lock icon shows the state. **Built 2026-11-05; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[O]** (owner, 2026-11-03, personadefault; **not built**) Model and preset are "often, but not always, tied": presets are made with a model in mind. Whether/how to tie them remains open.
