- **[O]** (owner, 2026-11-03, personadefault; **not built**) Per-character persona lock is undecided: it "would make it implicit"; ST's made sense because its chats are "to a character" and it has a separate persona selector.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, personausage) "A persona counts as used in every chat where it was the selected persona, or where any message was written as it (imported or new). Its chats list shows those chats. ‘Last used’ follows the latest such message." "For the persona (the user's own card), writing a message as it counts as presence." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.8). The character presence rule remains in §3.5.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, composerrow) "Composer controls: spacing here feels uneven. Also why was 'Generate' replaced with Write? Generate was unambiguous." Restore **Generate**, including its accessible name and explanations, and even the composer controls' spacing. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** (owner follow-up, 2026-11-02, composerrow) With guidance on, keep "the same gap between every control in that row in every state"; explicitly check and photograph the gap between the guidance toggle and the highlighted Generate button. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, splitconfirm) About the speaker picker and the model, preset and Cache chips under the message box: “We're putting a rectangular portrait inside a circular-sided element. This looks really bad. All these dropdowns need to have rectangular with rounded corners elements. That's our design language everywhere else too, so this is just inconsistent anyways.” **[P]** They use the buttons' `rounded-md` radius; other pill-shaped controls elsewhere are left as they are and listed in the splitconfirm report. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** Personas aren't a special entity. **A persona is a card in the "persona folder"**, and any card can be added to that folder. Cards in it get special UI treatment.
- **[D]** **Personas apart in card lists** (owner, 2026-11-02: "if they're dumped together with no distinction, especially from import, they're going to confuse users"). The home page and every other card list (the library, card pickers such as Add characters, Start a chat, anything listing cards) have a **Personas** tab or filter showing only the persona folder's cards, in the pattern that surface already has. On the home page, any other tab listing characters has an **Include personas** checkbox: off by default, personas hidden from that tab unless it's ticked; the choice is remembered per user as the other list preferences are, and restored from the URL as home's other filters are. Other lists take the checkbox where mixing makes sense. Personas inside a mixed list keep a visible "Persona" badge (owner correction below). The persona switcher keeps Members / Personas / All cards. Hover explanations follow the copy rules. **Built** (personalists; ARCHITECTURE §12, "Personas in the card lists").
  - **[P]** Which lists take Include personas (personalists): the home page and the left panel's character grid (the same browser, narrow), yes; Start a chat, no: its Personas filter is enough to pick from, and a picker's list stays short (Add characters: yes, by the owner's [D] below). The library page keeps All / Characters / Personas, its All marked by the shared "Persona" badge. Add characters' separate persona rows under All are replaced by its Personas filter. The badge is on the picture's lower left, as in the card header, explained "In the persona folder: a card you can play in chats."
- **[D] Persona marking across surfaces** (owner correction, 2026-11-02): “the "You" badge is only meaningful inside a chat, where it marks the card you play in this chat.” Outside a chat (home, left panel grid, library, Start a chat, Add characters, any card list or picker), a persona is marked **Persona**, never **You**. The app calls them personas everywhere. Inside the chat's cast, character/persona tab and composer speaker/persona switcher, **You** marks only the persona selected in that chat, including a selected card outside the persona folder. A standalone card page or full editor uses **Persona** for a folder card; the editor opened in a chat uses **You** for that chat's selected persona. All markers share one component and style. The explanations are exactly "In the persona folder: a card you can play in chats." and "You play this card in this chat." This supersedes personalists' mixed-list **You** badge and its proposal to use **You** throughout the library. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (personabadge; ARCHITECTURE §12, §14.6).
  - **[D]** Persona-only lists (owner decision via coordinator, 2026-11-03): a list that shows only personas (the Personas filter or tab in any picker, grid or the library) shows no persona badges, as the persona-only home and left-panel grids already do; badges appear only where personas are mixed with other cards.
- **[P] Persona-folder controls** (personalists audit, 2026-11-02; not yet confirmed). The marker above is decided; these additional controls remain proposed.
  - How a card enters or leaves the persona folder today: an ST folder import tags its personas (a re-import leaves tags alone); a card file import never does; the only control is **Persona**, a tick in the library item's ⋯ menu. There is no control in the editor, the card page or the card's chat tab, no folder view to drag to, and picking a non-folder card in the persona control plays it without adding it. Discoverability is low: the control is in one menu on one page and its label doesn't say it's a folder.
  - Proposal (superseded by the [D] below): one control, **Persona folder**, a switch with the explanation "Offers this card when you pick who you play, and keeps it under Personas", in the same place on every surface that edits a card: the editor's header beside the favourite star (full editor and side panel alike), the card page's actions and the library item menu (where it is now, renamed). The persona control's All cards offers "Add to persona folder" beside a non-folder card it plays. No drag and drop until the library has folders.
  - Conflicts with [D] entries: none found. The [D] persona switcher entry (its tabs open on Personas) and the speaker switcher entry (cast only) are unchanged.
- **[D] Four-row shared card header** (owner, 2026-11-03, saveflow follow-up): to the right of the portrait, exactly four rows, all within its height at every panel width: (1) display name ✎, version in muted text or “Add a version ✎” on the **same row**, with the save cluster at the right end; (2) in-chat nickname ✎ or “Add an in-chat nickname ✎”, keeping its existing explanations; (3) Persona control; (4) “19 chats · ~91 tokens · ~91 permanent”, with the chats count remaining a link and the token line last, all muted. No additional header row. Applies to the character side-panel tab and full editor. Supersedes separate version/save/chats rows, two-line names and permission for the text to extend below the portrait. An e2e must assert the exact four direct rows, their order and height no greater than the portrait at 390/1024/1440 in both views. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[P]** (saveflow follow-up, 2026-11-03): fit the fixed rows with one-line ellipses for all three names. Keep the existing portrait sizes and 4px row gaps; compact identity controls to 24px below 25rem and 20px below 18rem of header width. The Persona control uses 32px off / 88px on width and a 150ms transition. These sizing details implement the owner's structure, rather than an additional owner decision.
- **[D] Expanding Persona icon toggle** (owner, 2026-11-03): replace the Persona switch with one shared `ui/` component everywhere the switch appears, including the card page. Off: muted person icon with no visible text. On: pressed accent button, growing horizontally to reveal “Persona” after the icon inside the button; clicking again shrinks it. Brief width animation respects reduced motion, and `aria-pressed` reflects the state. Off hover exactly “Mark this card as a persona: a card you play as, listed under Personas.”; on hover exactly “Remove the persona mark: this becomes a regular card again.” The library item menu's Persona entry stays a menu item. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Remove persona switching from card actions** (owner, 2026-11-03): remove “Switch persona” from the card actions row, leaving its other actions unchanged: “It makes no sense logically for it to be there, and it's also entirely redundant given the switcher is right under the composer”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Superseded by the 2026-11-03 Persona toggle decision below.** **A visible Persona switch** (owner, 2026-11-02). A switch labelled "Persona" adds the card to the persona folder or removes it, in the card editor's header (the side panel's character tab and the full editor, the shared card header) and on the card page; the library item menu's "Persona" tick stays, and all three run the same action. Its hover explanation: "Keeps this card in the persona folder, so you can play it in chats." Its original placement rule is superseded by the dedicated-row decision below. It updates live everywhere (lists, filters, badges). **Built** (personaswitch; ARCHITECTURE §12, "The Persona switch").
  - **[P]** **Superseded by personarow below.** Placement (personaswitch): on the token counts' row, beside the counts where the width allows, else under them, within the portrait's height except with a two-line name at the narrowest panel or a phone's width (one row taller there). On the card page, under the usage line. The library tick takes the same hover text. Its explanation is a tooltip only, never a line under it on a phone, so the header keeps its rows.
  - **[D] Superseded by the 2026-11-03 four-row header and Persona toggle below.** Persona row (owner, 2026-11-02): "Give the Persona switch its own row directly above the tokens line. The tokens line stays the last row." The switch precedes its vertically centred label, with the same muted text size and colour as the token counts. Keep the hover explanation and behaviour, and the existing top-stacked row spacing. The portrait defines the minimum height; text can extend below it at narrow widths. Applies to character/persona side-panel tabs, the full editor and the card page. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (personarow; ARCHITECTURE §12, "The Persona switch").
- **[D]** **Add characters gets Include personas** (owner, 2026-11-02), as the home page and the left panel have it: off by default, remembered per account, personas marked when included; it keeps its Personas filter. This supersedes the personalists [P] that Add characters takes no checkbox. **Built** (personaswitch; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** **Include personas is an icon toggle** (owner, 2026-11-02, personatoggle): "Make it an **icon toggle button in that filter row**". On home, the left panel grid and Add characters, use the existing persona/user icon after the sort-direction button, with the same size and style, `aria-pressed` and a visible pressed state. Hover: "Include personas" off, "Personas included" on. Remove the checkbox row; keep account memory and URL state. The compact filter shrinks and truncates before the row could wrap. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).

- **[D]** You can switch to writing as any card, on any turn.
- **[D]** The model can write for "your" character, because you may not be playing it.
- **[P]** Each turn has a **speaker** (any participant) and a **writer** (human or model). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12, §15).
- **[P]** A per-chat "played by me" set (state on the graph, §3.4) drives: **Built state foundation** (ARCHITECTURE §3.8), but the macro claim is **overtaken** by the following owner decisions: `{{user}}` is the selected single persona or pinned text, separate from speaker; it never joins the set.
  - the composer's default speaker
  - what `{{user}}` resolves to
  - which characters the "don't write for" instructions protect
- **[D]** `{{user}}` is the persona currently selected in the chat, or the text the user pinned in its place; never a list of several cards (owner, 2026-10-29). An ST chat that used several personas imports with only the last one selected.
- **[D] Speaker switcher:**
  - The current speaker is **always visible** next to the composer.
  - Clicking it opens a panel listing **only the chat's cast**: the persona and the cards in the chat. To speak as another card, add it to the chat first (owner, 2026-10-28; this replaces the tabs Members / Personas / All cards and "switching to a card that isn't in the chat adds it"). The persona control keeps Members / Personas / All cards.
- **[P]** Up to three one-tap avatars next to the switcher for the speakers used most recently in this chat. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12). Compact phone controls use the speaker picker.
- **[P]** Composer actions: **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12).
  - **Send as X**: you wrote it.
  - **Generate X**: the model writes X's turn, as a real generation. Not a fake turn inserted into the history, and no command to remember.
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: Version-owned one-turn direction is built (ARCHITECTURE §12); the guidance-toggle and guidance-on-swipes owner decisions below define reuse, clearing and edit targeting. Earlier wording: “**Composer text used with Generate becomes a one-turn direction**, stored on that generated turn. Swiping reuses it, going back restores it, and it never leaks into later turns. This replaces "put next-turn guidance in the author's note and remember to remove it".”
- **[D]** `{{user}}` (who the persona is) and the speaker (who is writing this turn) are separate (owner, 2026-10-27). `{{user}}` is relatively fixed and only changes sometimes. The persona can be any card; persona cards sit in their own folder, whose tab opens by default when switching persona. The persona role exists so the model maps intuitively for ST users. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12). Pending-chat cast members remain selectable as personas after refreshing the picker (suitefix2, 2026-11-12).
- **[D]** Roleplaying as any other character is as easy as writing as yourself and never invalidates the cache: selecting a speaker means writing as it (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** Guidance instead of a written turn (owner, 2026-10-27): the owner usually doesn't write the turn but a `[Next: …]` direction (in ST, in the Author's Note, or with the Guided Generations Quick Reply set, which injects `[<text>]` at chat depth 0 and generates). Here: type in the composer and Generate, and the text is a prompt for that generation, not a sent message. (A popover on Generate was asked for first, then made unnecessary by the guidance toggle below.)
- **[D]** Impersonation, the model generating as `{{user}}`, must work as seamlessly as possible, with or without a direction, and the model must be able to take the user's role or another role in the RP. Presets often have strong anti-impersonation prompts; the owner adds an exception ("unless the prompt explicitly says you may speak for {{user}} next") and states it in the `[Next: …]` (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** Roles: the last turns being user matters most. Presets can have system sections near the end too (Anthropic supports system content there), so the end of the context isn't one block (owner, 2026-10-27).

- **[D]** A **guidance toggle** on the composer (owner, 2026-10-27): on, the composer's contents are used as guidance wherever applicable (Generate, a new swipe, Continue); off, they never are, Generate included. Rejected: using composer text as guidance on a swipe implicitly (too non-obvious: the composer may hold your own next message), and a "new version with direction" box on every swipe control (redundant surfaces, more annoying).
- **[D]** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27) Consequences: with guidance on, the box reads as guidance (its own look and placeholder) and Send is replaced by the generate actions, so guidance is never sent as a message by mistake; (2026-10-29: the text staying in the box and "with guidance off nothing is reused" are replaced by the item below.) The popover on Generate becomes unnecessary while the toggle shows the mode. The per-account scope is superseded by the 2026-11-04 decision below. **Enter submits as Generate while guidance is on**, the same key that sends a message while it's off (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04) “Is the composer history/text state per chat? (it should be)”; on being told Guidance was per account: “huh? Why?” Guidance belongs to the composer, per chat and fork like speaker and reply pick. A new fork inherits its source once; an existing composer keeps its own switch. Existing composers migrate from their account's current value. A pending new-chat composer inherits the composer it was opened from, otherwise starts off. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10, §12).
- **[D]** **Guidance on swipes** (owner, 2026-10-29; replaces the box keeping its text). Submitting guidance clears the box, and the reply shows the guidance under it, as now. A swipe reuses the guidance of the version it re-rolls, whether the guidance switch is on or off; everything else (edited messages, notes, cards) is read fresh, as in ST. To fix the guidance, the guidance shown under a reply has an **Edit** button: it puts that guidance in the composer's box, with a bar above the composer naming the reply ("Editing guidance for Aria's reply", ✕). While the bar is there, any submit (Enter, the write button) makes a new version of that reply with the box's text, never a new turn, and the text stays in the box between those versions, so fixing is edit, submit, edit, submit. The box's text applies live: that reply's swipe button also uses the text as it is in the box, unsaved edits included; each new version records the guidance it was written with. Swiping any other reply uses that reply's own guidance. ✕ on the bar clears the box and returns it to new turns, giving back whatever draft was there before. No feature depends on a key combination: shortcuts may be added only beside a visible control. **Built** (W2; ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** Guidance changes only *who writes*, never *which character* (owner, 2026-10-28). Off: Enter sends your text as the selected speaker's message. On: Enter makes the model write the **selected speaker's** message following your text as the direction, persona or card alike; never the Reply-from or next-in-turn character's.

- **[D]** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27) Directions and "who writes next" are ordinary prompts in the preset: the macros `{{speaker}}` (who the model writes as) and `{{direction}}` (the composer text); every prompt has a condition (always / only when the model writes as a character / only when it writes as `{{user}}` / only when there's a direction). Default direction prompt, user role: `[The user has written a direction for {{speaker}}'s next message. It is not part of the story; follow it closely: {{direction}}]`. "Who writes next" is one prompt (`Write {{speaker}}'s next message.`); impersonation needs nothing extra. Anti-impersonation prompts get "not when writing as `{{user}}`". ST's group nudge and impersonation prompt map onto conditions on import. ST's per-prompt generation-type triggers (normal, swipe, regenerate, continue, impersonate, quiet; none set = always) are kept **in addition** to the conditions, as a normal per-prompt option in the preset editor, imported from and exported to ST (owner, 2026-10-28; this replaces mapping the triggers onto conditions). There's no regenerate separate from a new version, so ST's `regenerate` and `swipe` triggers both mean "on a new version" (owner, 2026-10-29); ST's `quiet` is a summary request. (Placement of these prompts and the closing user turn: replaced by the end-of-request instruction below, owner 2026-10-29.)
- **[D]** **End-of-request instruction** (owner, 2026-10-29; provisional, to be judged in use). The prompts that say who writes next and carry the direction are always the request's last message, one user message, after everything else (post-history instructions, other prompts after the history, notes at the end of the tail). The preset editor shows them in a fixed **End of request** section, not in the ordering list, as three editable texts: **when a character speaks** (ST's group nudge imports here, `{{char}}` read as `{{speaker}}`), **when your card speaks** (ST's impersonation prompt imports here), and **when you give a direction**, appended after the turn text. Defaults: `[Write {{speaker}}'s next message. Write only for {{speaker}}.`, the same for your card, and `What happens next: {{direction}}]`. Who else the model may write for is only this wording, with no switch anywhere: edit the text to let other characters act; a one-turn exception goes in the direction. The composer is unchanged. The closing user turn is removed; the only fallback is a bare `[Write {{speaker}}'s next message.]` when the request would otherwise end on assistant and the model has no prefill, decided on the whole request. With prefill (Continue or a prefill on a model that supports it) the instruction goes right before the partial message. The wording is per preset, so strict and loose chats need two presets. In a summary request the same last place holds the summary instruction instead of the turn text. The ST fidelity gate lists the placement as a documented difference. **Built** (W1; ARCHITECTURE §9, §15).
- **[D] Overtaken** (2026-10-27 import rule, replaced by the owner’s end-of-request instruction, 2026-10-29). **Built W1** (ARCHITECTURE §9, §15): import stores the direction text as `directionPrompt`, outside the ordering list, with the default supplied by the end-of-request conversion. It appends no default direction prompt to the order. ST has no corresponding field, so this text is not exported.
- **[D] Overtaken in placement** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27; superseded by the end-of-request decision, 2026-10-29). **Built W1** (ARCHITECTURE §9, §15): ST’s group nudge and impersonation text become the two end-of-request turn texts, outside the ordering list, with defaults when empty; their speaker macros are converted accordingly. They no longer map to ordered conditioned prompts. The first-impersonation notice about anti-impersonation conditions remains built, with an editor link.

- **[D]** Changing persona swaps the persona definitions, as ST does: the cache is rebuilt, and the history is untouched (old messages keep their speaker's name). Several personas means adding cards to the cast and switching the speaker (owner, 2026-10-28).
- **[D]** The group nudge is sent whenever the model writes as a character: there's no group / one-on-one distinction, and the persona is a cast card (owner, 2026-10-28). ST sends it only in group chats; the ST fidelity gate lists it as a documented difference. It's the "when a character speaks" text of the end-of-request instruction.
- **[P]** Impersonation is just Generate for a character you play. The output lands as a real turn with versions. A "draft into composer" option is available. **Partial**: Generate commits a real version (ARCHITECTURE §12); a separate “draft into composer” action remains an unbuilt proposal.
- **[D]** Sending into a chat that's still generating **queues** the message (owner, 2026-10-12). It shows as pending under the streaming reply and posts as soon as the reply finishes. If the reply is stopped, it posts right after the stopped version. Typing and sending are never blocked; to interject, press Stop first.
- **[P]** The queued message is held by the server, so it survives closing the tab. It attaches to the reply's slot, not to a version, so it posts after whichever version ends up selected, including a failed one (which stays out of the context). Posting it starts the next reply as a normal Send does. A queued message can be edited or withdrawn until it posts. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12).

### 2.3 Group-chat orchestration (see §7)

- **[D]** **Reply settings share available width** (owner, 2026-11-03: "They must share one row when there's space"; "the switch wraps below, left-aligned with the select"). Who replies keeps its label above the select; One reply at a time sits to the right, vertically centred on the select. Use flex-wrap and existing gap tokens, without fixed breakpoints. Built, obsolete as an implementation requirement (whoreplies); see ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** The owner only uses group chats. Single-card chats are a special case of the general system.
- **[P]** **Auto follows ST's natural order** (owner, 2026-11-01: "There's also no 'talkativeness in group chats' setting"). The composer's automatic reply pick (`replyPolicy.ts`, policy `natural`) replaces the round-robin: a character named by a whole word of their name in the text being sent replies first; else each present character passes a roll with their card's `extensions.talkativeness` (0..1, ST's default 0.5 when unset); when no roll passes, one character with talkativeness above 0 is picked at random. Round-robin stays registered as a replaceable policy. Built (ARCHITECTURE §12, Next).
- **[D]** **Rounds, as ST, with a switch for one reply** (owner, 2026-11-02: "The chained replies thing can be annoying. Sometimes I want to stop it and edit the message before letting the next one continue. We should match ST but have a separate switch on the cast tab to turn this particular behaviour off and limit to 1 turn per user action."). Replaces the [P] "One reply per send". After a user action (a send, Reply now, an empty Send), everyone the chat's reply order activates replies in turn, as ST's `generateGroupWrapper` does: natural order, those named, then each whose roll passes; list order, every character in the cast's order; pooled order, one; manual, none to a message you send and one to an empty send. A separate switch in the Cast tab, **One reply at a time**, beside Who replies, limits it to one reply per user action (the behaviour before); off by default, per chat, and ST groups import with it off. Built (ARCHITECTURE §12, Next, rounds and the Cast tab).
- **[P]** **How a round runs** (replychain, 2026-11-02). The composer plans the whole round when it sends (the same seeded plan the Next chip shows: "Next: Mira, Wren"), and the server queues the replies after the first as round entries of the send queue (§2.2), each posting when the one before ends, so a round goes on with the tab closed, and a reload or a second tab shows it. A pick under Next, Speak now, Write for and a new version (swipe) are one reply, as ST's force talk, impersonate and swipe are. The round is planned once, as ST's is: muting or removing someone mid-round doesn't change it (End round does).
- **[P]** **Stopping and going on.** Stop ends the reply being written and holds the rest of its round, as does a reply that fails (ST's round ends on an error too) or a server restart. Above the composer, the round shows who's still to reply, with **Continue round** (the rest reply in turn, after the last message as it is then: edited, deleted, swiped or not) and **End round** (drop the rest; also offered while the round runs, letting the current reply finish). ST has no Continue: its Stop ends the round for good.
- **[P]** **What else does to a round.** Any turn added at the fork's tip (a send, queued or not, Reply now, Generate, Speak now) is a new user action: the round there, running or held, ends, and the action's own round, if any, replaces it; a send queued mid-round posts after the reply being written, not after the whole round. A new version of a reply mid-round is that reply again (the round waits for it); of an earlier reply, the round goes on after the current one. Rounds belong to their branch: one keeps running in its branch when you go to another or branch off elsewhere, and its controls show only on its branch. A second tab sees the same round and controls; Continue twice continues once.
- **[P]** **The rolls are seeded**, not `Math.random()`: a mulberry32 PRNG seeded by a hash of the branch tip's version id and the candidates with their talkativeness, so the Next preview, what Send submits, a reload, a second tab and a queued send agree, and the pick changes only when the chat moves on. The text being sent decides only the mentions, so typing doesn't reshuffle the rolls.
- **[P]** **With nothing typed** (Reply now, an empty Send), the mentions come from the tip's message, and when a character wrote it they're skipped (ST's `bannedUser`); unlike ST's fallback, they're picked only when no one else can reply. With text typed, the last speaker can reply again, as in ST.
- **[P]** **The chat's reply order, in the Cast tab** (importreport, 2026-11-02; owner, 2026-11-02: "if it does [have a switch], we probably should have it in the cast tab"). ST's group "reply strategy" (`activation_strategy`: natural order, list order, manual, pooled order) is a per-chat choice, **Who replies**, under the cast, each option explained beside it, imported from each ST group for its chats; natural order stays the default. Rounds as in ST (above); with One reply at a time on, each is one reply a send: **list order** is the first in the cast's order after your message, then, each time you ask for another reply with nothing typed, the next after the last to reply since your turn, wrapping to the first; **pooled order** is someone who hasn't spoken since your message, at random, else anyone but the last to speak (as ST); **manual** is no reply to a message you send unless you pick someone under Next, and someone at random for Reply now or an empty Send (as ST's empty send). Built (ARCHITECTURE §12, Next and the Cast tab). The former Open (whether list order writes every character's turn after one send) is settled by the rounds decision above: it does, unless One reply at a time is on.
- **[P]** **Auto replies, bounded** (orchestr1, 2026-11-14; ST's auto mode). From the composer's "Auto…" chip (moved to the Cast tab, below), the characters reply in turn with nothing sent from the user, for a number of replies chosen before anything is sent (and an optional limit on tokens written). Each turn is Reply now's request; the run belongs to the tab that started it and ends on anything it didn't do itself (typing, a send, a stop, a failure, a tool approval, a change to the last message, another branch, leaving the chat), so it never goes on with nobody able to stop it. Unlike a round, it isn't queued on the server. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12, Auto replies). **[O]** whether a run should show, or be resumable, in other tabs or after a reload.
- **[D]** **Auto replies live in the Cast tab, not the composer** (owner, 2026-11-17: "Don't remove it then. Just move it to the cast tab in a sufficiently less conspicuous spot. It has no place in such an important place."). The composer's strip has no Auto chip. An **Auto replies** button sits in the Cast tab's Who replies row, beside One reply at a time, with its explanation on hover; it opens the same dialog, and the run's row with its count and Stop stays above the message box. Shown only where two or more characters could take turns (or while a run is shown). The label carries no "…", which read as a cut-off label in the strip.
- **[D]** **No Reply now control** (owner, 2026-11-17, of the ▶ beside Next: "It seems redundant with the submit button"). Send with nothing typed already asks for the reply alone, ST's force talk, and names who replies ("Mira replies", "Get a reply" while the decision model chooses), so the ▶ is removed. Earlier items' "Reply now" now means the empty Send.
- **[P]** **The empty Send with guidance on** (replynow1, 2026-11-17). With guidance on and nothing typed, the Send button still asks for the reply alone, as the ▶ did: there's no guidance to lose. With text, Generate stays the only way to send guidance, Enter stays Generate, and while a reply's guidance is edited Send stays disabled.
- **[D]** **No one under Next** (owner, 2026-11-06, about the composer's "Who replies next": "I think we should have an extra option for 'No one' for when the user wants to disable replies being generated automatically."). Built (nooneauto; ARCHITECTURE §12, Next).
- **[D]** **The decision model can choose who replies next** (owner, 2026-11-06: "the way it works in ST without any kind of real context feels stupid. Two characters cannot talk without a third one who left the scene 3 messages before not butting in. Perhaps the decision model should just choose who speaks next, although the 'who has left' decision and state is useful since it can be decided more 'locally'."). An opt-in decision point beside presence, which stays. Built (nextspeaker; ARCHITECTURE §12, Next; spec/AGENTIC.md §7).
- **[P]** **How "Who replies next" works** (nextspeaker, 2026-11-06). The `next` point, off by default on the decision connection's Decisions card. It runs before a reply whose speaker Auto picked is written (Send, an empty Send, ▶ Reply now with Auto's pick, queued sends, and each reply of a round at its turn), in a chat with two or more characters the user doesn't play; never under No one, for a pick under Next, Generate, Speak now or a new version. Candidates: present characters (not away, not muted), not played, and not whoever wrote the latest message, Auto's pick first; fewer than two, nothing is asked. One `choice` question over the last 6 messages (each its last 1,200 characters), the candidates' names and the latest speaker; the chosen option's probability must reach the threshold (default 50%). Below it, on a timeout (3 s), an error or a malformed answer, Auto's pick replies. Until the model has chosen, the reply names no one: its header says "Choosing who replies…" with a blank portrait, never Auto's pick (coordinator, 2026-11-06: a visible wrong state otherwise), then shows the speaker chosen, the model's or, when it didn't choose, Auto's. In a round, the This round strip names the turns already chosen ("Ash so far.", "Choosing who replies now." while one is being chosen) and only counts the rest ("Then 2 more, each chosen when it's their turn"), since Auto's plan for them is only the fallback. The pick is recorded on the version (`speakerPick`), so the inspector says "The decision model picked Mira (91%)" and the Next pill's hover says "The decision model picked Mira for the last reply (91%)". No "nobody; the user should speak" option: Auto answers a request for a reply, and not replying is the user's own choice (No one, End round, One reply at a time); a round's length is planned once at send, as before. Asked at send, not after the previous reply: the user's message is the strongest signal of who answers, and a guess made before it would be wasted on most sends.
- **[P]** **The Next pill while the decision model chooses** (nextspeaker, 2026-11-06; coordinator: with the point on and Auto selected, the pill must not name Auto's plan). With "Who replies next" on, Auto selected and two or more who could reply, the pill reads **"Next: chosen when you send"**, its hover "When you send, the decision model reads the chat and chooses who replies; if it can't decide, the chat's reply order does.", and its Auto row is plain "Auto" ("Chosen by the decision model when you send"). Send's hover says "…, then the decision model chooses who replies"; with nothing typed, Send reads "Get a reply" and ▶ "Reply now" ("A reply now, with nothing sent from you; the decision model chooses who"). A pick under Next, No one, the point off, or fewer than two to choose from: the pill names who replies, as before.
- **[P]** **How No one works** (nooneauto, 2026-11-06). A row **No one** between Auto and the characters ("No reply to what you send, until you choose otherwise"). While it's chosen, a message sent (typed, or images only) gets no reply and no round, whoever it names, under every reply order; it stays chosen through sends, reloads and other tabs until Auto is chosen. It's kept where the pick is, with the fork's composer draft, so it's per branch, and a new branch starts with its source's, as the pick and speaker do. Getting a reply on demand: a character picked under Next replies to the next send, one reply with no round, and then No one is back, not Auto (the owner's intent is to stop automatic replies, so a one-off ask doesn't undo it; the footer says "then No one again"); asking with nothing typed (Send with an empty box, ▶ Reply now) gets Auto's pick, one reply with no round; Generate, Continue, new versions and Cast → Speak now work as always. The chip reads "Next: no one" with a struck-through speech bubble, its hover "No one replies to what you send, until you choose otherwise here."; the plain "Next: no one" without the icon stays for a chat with no one able to reply, where the chip is disabled. The chip is now enabled with a single character (Auto, No one and that character), so No one works in single-character chats; ▶ still needs two. Agent modes and decision models need nothing of their own: No one only decides whether a send asks for a reply, and the presence point still runs after each reply that is asked for.

---

## 3. The message graph: versions, branches, threads, state

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "replace the database CHECK … IN (…) lists that plugins or new features will extend ... with validation in the writer against a registry"; "Each registry is a registered, replaceable part (plugins can add values)." Keep CHECKs for truly closed data-model lists. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.7).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "remove the leftovers of the never-built ‘parallel threads in one chat’ design"; "mark the old lane design as removed, not decided against: a future threads design will get its own schema." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.7).


### 3.1 Slots and versions

- **[D]** Remove the message action to generate with another model and the refusal-specific "Retry with…" picker; choose the model below the composer and swipe instead (owner, 2026-11-02: "I don't get it. There's a model switch right below the composer. What's the point of having a dedicated button for something you can do by switching and then swiping? That's what everyone does. Same with refusals with another model."). Per-generation model recording stays. **Built; obsolete as an implementation plan** (nomodelretry, 2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §12).

**[O]** **How the model is presented to users** (owner, 2026-10-14): "the UX for how we translate our immutable and version controlled data model to something that users understand and which doesn't cause surprises for them still hasn't been worked out or discussed properly." The rules below (generated text frozen, edits of it as new versions, the user's own text amended with undo history, branches as forks, layered cards, re-imports merged) are decided; how each shows up in the UI, and what the user is told when one applies, was the open question at that date. **Largely overtaken** by the later owner decisions on edit/amend, per-note fork inheritance, editor shape and independent forks (§3.4, §6.2a, §10.3), whose concrete UI is built (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14). Owner acceptance of the resulting UX remains unverified.

- **[D]** **Versions belong to a message slot, and the continuation belongs to the slot**, not to a version. Swapping, regenerating or editing a version at *any* depth replaces that message in place and keeps what follows. This is a version, not a branch.
- **[D]** Any message can get a new version, including the user's own messages and messages in the middle of the history.
- **[P], rejected (owner, 2026-10-30)** Regenerating in the middle of the history can optionally show the model what follows ("write a replacement consistent with what comes after"). Rejected by the decision below: messages are in causal order.
- **[D] A message generated in the middle of the history never sees what follows it** (owner, 2026-10-30, "Messages are inherently in causal order"). A regenerate, and a message generated at an insertion point, is assembled from the fork's history up to the message before it, and the model is never shown anything after it. There is no option, checkbox or stage for it.
- **[D] No mark** on later messages that were generated against an older version of an earlier slot (owner, 2026-10-30; replaces the earlier [P]): it would flag every message after the edited one, a stream of warnings with no practical use. The request inspector already shows what each reply was generated from.
- **[D] Messages can be inserted anywhere in the history** (owner, 2026-10-30, non-negotiable): written as any cast member or generated by the model at that point, in the middle of a chat as well as at its end. ST's "Copy this message" (a duplicate right after the original) is one use of it; the duplicate itself matters less than being able to insert. Design (proposed): [MESSAGE-INSERT.md](MESSAGE-INSERT.md).
  - **[D]** Cancel in "Insert below/above" sends the typed text to the bin's recovered texts, like every other editor's Cancel (owner, 2026-11-02). MESSAGE-INSERT §4.2 said Cancel keeps the draft; built as decided (`InsertEditor.tsx`), with no separate Discard.
- **[D] Messages can be moved up or down within a branch** (owner, 2026-11-16): one shown message at a time, in that branch only; branches made from it later inherit the order, branches made before or beside it keep theirs. Undo restores the previous order. Summaries written over the old order are marked stale there. **Built** (schemafinal; ARCHITECTURE §3.7 "Moves", §16). Builder choices, **[P]**: Move up/Move down sit in a message's More actions with an Undo toast; deleted neighbours are skipped; what was moved after the message, or inserted below it, stays where it was; a message inserted below a moved one is shown right after it.
- **[D] No automatic continue** of short replies (owner, 2026-10-30): Continue stays a manual action.
- **[D]** **"Write your own version"** on any message, without a sacrificial swipe.
- **[D]** **Saving an edit of a generated version creates a new version**, recording its source ("edited from #7"). Editing a version the user created **amends** it, with its earlier states kept in its undo history.
- **[D]** A **composition scratchpad**: the owner generates 10–50 versions of a turn and merges the best parts. Select text in any version, assemble it in the scratchpad, commit it as a new version. The scratchpad persists like a draft. **Built 2026-11-06 (scratchpad); obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12 "The composition scratchpad". How, as proposals:
  - **[P]** (scratchpad, 2026-11-06) The turn's scratchpad is its "Write your own version" draft (your version). While it's open the version shown stays above it, in a box with a bar over its text: the version arrows (moved there from the footer while composing, so stepping never moves them) and the text actions for that version: **Add selection to your version** and **Add to your version** (the whole version), beside Send selection/text to snippet. Text goes to the end after a blank line, briefly highlighted; focus stays where it was. A selection keeps its formatting: one that starts or ends inside italics (or bold) gets the marks it cut through, so a piece never leaves a mark open. Nothing overrides the browser's menu over the text.
  - **[P]** (scratchpad, 2026-11-06) Snippets are the second scratchpad: a snippet's own editor has the same two buttons, which add to the open composition used last (as Send text to snippet picks its snippet), with a notice saying which message; with none open the button stays in place, disabled, saying to open Write your own version first. So a snippet becomes a new version in two clicks, reviewed in between.
  - **[P]** (scratchpad, 2026-11-06) Add as a new version makes it the message's next version, selected, with "Saved as version N"; it's undone as any version is (the arrows step back, Delete sends it to the bin). An already saved version of your own is changed with Edit (amended, with its history), not by the composition.
  - **[P]** (scratchpad, 2026-11-06) Two views of one chat (workbench frames) show the same composition: text added or typed in one appears in the other once saved, and adding it as a version or cancelling closes it in both.
Snippets and shared named notes: **implemented, obsolete as a proposal** (2026-10-28), [ARCHITECTURE.md §14](../ARCHITECTURE.md). The owner's 2026-10-27 confirmation in UX-REWORK supersedes the earlier separate-store wording and the open question about named notes: they are one store, global or chat-scoped, with a right-panel editor and selection transfers in both directions. Server persistence and same-origin cross-tab updates from the earlier **[P]** are used for continuous drafts. The broader composition-scratchpad workflow remains a proposal. **Superseded in part** (owner, 2026-11-01, §4.1): Insert is gone (copy and paste), and a chat's snippet is the whole chat's, every branch of it.

- **[P]** The version picker is built for dozens of versions: compact previews, star, hide, delete, side-by-side comparison. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12). Delete is on the adjacent version action surface.
- **[D]** Pressing swipe while a generation is running starts another one. You can move freely between versions that are still generating and finished ones. **The stop button belongs to the version**, not the composer. There's no dedicated "generate ×N" action.
- **[D]** **Failed generations are versions** with a clear failure style showing the cause: HTTP error, context length, classifier refusal (with its category), or a length cutoff. They never enter the context. **Built** (U08, 2026-10-28; ARCHITECTURE.md §12).
- **[P]** A stream that dies midway keeps its partial text and offers Continue (§7.5). Refusals keep ordinary retry through the version arrows; to use another model, change the composer's model first (**[D]**, owner, 2026-11-02; §3.1 supersedes the earlier "Retry with…" picker proposal). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12). Text Continue is distinct from agent transcript recovery (§7.5).
- **[D]** Generating with different models and upstreams at the same time is required. The model is chosen per generation and recorded on the version **[P]**. **Built** per-generation model recording (ARCHITECTURE §12, §15).

### 3.2 Branches

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-13, branchundo1) "I don't understand why it would apply to a different branch you're not looking at. What is the purpose? Did I not say again and again that branches should be independent?" An action on one branch never changes what another branch shows or what its characters know, even when they share messages. Undo of a scene check corrects only the branch in the tab; children made from it afterwards inherit the correction, existing branches retain their own view. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §3.8 "Undo of a scene check's away".

- **[P]** (forkdesign, 2026-11-05; owner asked 2026-11-05 “whether or not forks are O(1). Does forking a particularly large and complex chat structure cost more now? For example, with many turns, swipes and forks.”) Making a fork costs the same whatever its history, swipes, overrides or genealogy, and reads in a fork don't depend on its depth or the chat's fork count: each fork gets a label in a nested-interval order of the fork tree, and per-fork choices are found by one index probe at that label, replacing the 64-entry lineage and its compaction copy. The readers' page cache goes to 256 MB. Measured on a prototype and specified in [FORK-COST.md](FORK-COST.md). **Built** (forkbuild, 2026-11-05), ARCHITECTURE §3.8 "Fork labels".

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, splitlink) “Every branch other than the one the chat started on has a start point, so the link must show for all of them, including imported branches and branches of branches, and must scroll to the split message.” Hover explanation stays as it is. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.


- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, branchrows) “I do think showing new characters is useful. The issue is though, added relative to what?” … “It should be relative to the split point, that actually identifies a branch.” … “I don't know about crossing someone out. I think it would look bad … In some sense characters only really get added.” Branch rows show characters with participation after their split who were absent from the parent's cast at the split. Small portraits follow a leading “+”, two fully visible with the overflow count tucked behind; hover names the additions. No removal portraits, no portraits without additions, none for the original line. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §3.8 and §12.
- **[P]** (branchrows, 2026-11-05; implemented) Store the added ids with shell fork summaries, using the inherited bounded event view for the baseline and retained swipe participation after the base. Summary format 6 recomputes earlier rows; existing stamps and lineage invalidation apply.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, branchmenu) The header's branch trigger "looks great" and stays as it is; the issue "is entirely in the opened selector", which "looked quite bad and sloppy from a UI perspective, especially visually". **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 "The branch control").
- **[P]** (branchmenu, 2026-11-05; implemented) The opened menu uses the left panel's branch rows: bordered rounded boxes, a one-line truncated title with a small pencil after it and the "opens here" chip at its end, the preview in a fixed two-line slot, "Last message <time>" under it, the shown branch with the accent border, pin in the upper corner and delete in the lower corner (where the panel's selected row has Split off). Every row keeps its pencil, since renaming any branch was already the menu's job. Rows follow the panel's order and headings (Pinned branches, Named branches, Branches; headings only when more than one group is present), and the shown row is scrolled into view on opening. The footer becomes a column of icon-first ghost buttons with their existing labels; ones that don't apply stay in place, greyed, with the reason on hover, instead of appearing and disappearing. Pinning from the menu is new here (the same action and explanations as the panel's pin).
- **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-05, branchmenu follow-up) The rows' open area is a link to the branch ("Openers are links", §10.1): a plain click switches in place, a middle or modified click opens that branch in a new browser tab. The menu takes the height available below the trigger, less a small margin, and never flips beside it; at 1440×900 about six rows show. The phone sheet keeps the sheets' image button.
- **[D]** A branch is an explicit fork of the path. It's what you choose when you want the continuation to differ, and it never happens as a side effect of swiping.
- **[D]** A named branch is one the user promoted into its own identity; an ST file's name never names one (owner, 2026-11-01). An unnamed branch, imported or made here, is labelled by where it splits off, the time first ("Split 12 Jan 2025, 21:40 at #14"), with its last message's time and preview beside it; the chat's own name is its group's or its root chat's, never a branch file's. An imported chat opens on the branch ST last had open, else the one with the latest message. The left panel lists each chat once, "⑂ 12" listing its branches in the panel (crumbs back), named and pinned first, then by last message; the open chat's branches sit at the panel's foot, collapsible, for switching without leaving the list; no accordion rows. Implemented in ARCHITECTURE §12 and §13.

### 3.3 Threads and merges (the architecture must allow them now; the UX comes later)
- **[D]** Parallel threads in one conversation that fork and **merge**, with the ability to send one message to several threads at once (same or different speaker, and the speaker can be any card). Design: [THREADS.md](THREADS.md) (Proposed, 2026-11-12).
- **[D]** Concurrent threads and alternate branches are different concepts and must look different in the UI. Proposed UI: [THREADS.md](THREADS.md) §13.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-12; THREADS §16.2 question 1, answer (a)) Starting threads at an earlier message makes the messages already played after it one of the threads: they continue as the first thread ("This branch"), and the characters who go to the other threads are recorded as not present for those messages in this branch only (a per-branch correction of who knew them, never a change to the messages other branches show). The other mode, both threads starting empty at that message with the played messages left as an alternate, is struck as redundant: "whoever wants both threads to start empty at #40 makes a branch at #40 first and starts threads there, where #40 is the newest message."
- **[P]** Messages form a **graph from day one**: slots point to their parents, and a merge is a slot with several parents. Threads are named lanes. **Every view is a selection of a path through the graph**, and context assembly always works on a path. **Former foundation removed** (owner, 2026-11-06): "remove the leftovers of the never-built ‘parallel threads in one chat’ design." Lanes, multi-parent slots and placement groups were unused scaffolding and are gone (ARCHITECTURE §3.7). This proposal is obsolete as an implementation foundation; future in-chat threads need their own schema. The product requirements above remain future work, as do the next two items.
- **[P]** Sending to several threads creates one placement per thread, linked by a group ID. Editing one asks whether to edit the whole group. Proposed as `thread_sends`: [THREADS.md](THREADS.md) §6.
- **[O]** How two merged threads become one sequence: ~~interleave by time~~, or the merge node carries a summary of the other thread. A merge is an explicit event that sets the merged state **[P]**. Proposed for in-chat threads: [THREADS.md](THREADS.md) §7.2–§7.3.
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-08) "History cannot be 'interleaved by time'. Messages do not have a real 'time' to them." The interleave option is struck. For chat merges this was already answered below (owner, 2026-11-05).

#### Merging another chat into this one (chatmerge, 2026-11-05)

Design: [CHAT-MERGE.md](CHAT-MERGE.md).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) "the reverse, and also the complement, of branching, merging a conversation into the current one. ... you should be able to "merge" another arbitrary chat into the current one. The two chats would not need to be branches of the same chat, but if they are, the common prefix would be considered in merging: only the post-divergence part would need to be handled by it."
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) "The branch or other convo continue to exist in this version, and have their own identity. It's not really one conversation with parallel threads, and more so conversation forks that may be intentionally merged into again later." In-chat parallel threads moved by agents remain the separate Phase 7 idea above (threads themselves: [THREADS.md](THREADS.md), Proposed, 2026-11-12).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) "there's one conversation you're merging *into* which is THE conversation, and the other one is simply being read from and merged, and you initiate the merging from the destination, and the destination's chat settings are always the ones that apply whenever we need to choose/do something with both conversations"
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) "I don't think interleaving messages would be the correct choice." A summary of the source posted as a chat message: "pretty lame, and doesn't feel like a feature." This answers the **[O]** above for chat merges: neither interleaving nor a summary message.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-08) "Also, the chat merge should be possible with the other way where the timelines remain separate for the characters from chat A and chat B, as discussed before. That's arguably the most straightforward timeline merge." A merge option alongside the built one (one shared transcript of the source): after the join, characters who came from chat A keep A's history and characters from chat B keep B's, separate contexts by origin. "As discussed before": the owner's 2026-11-06 answer on two separate 1 on 1 storylines that merge (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.1, §2.2: a merge join's section filtered by the source versions' own casts, no gap line for a storyline the character was never part of) and his earlier mention of merging summaries while keeping recent chat separated as a way to merge external chats in (§2.4). Those stay **[P]** designs for separate contexts; this entry decides that the merge offers the option. Its place in the merge dialog and how it combines with the shared past (§2.4, Open) are not designed yet. Proposed dialog placement and view rules: [THREADS.md](THREADS.md) §7.1, §7.3. **Built** (merge2, 2026-11-14; ARCHITECTURE §12 "Merges kept separate by origin"): the thread joins' per-join choice in the merge dialog, for chats with one shared context; the shared past and the narrator's layout stay Open.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) Merge position: "I agree that a user should only merge into the latest message, and they likely will, but I don't see a technical or UX reason against not allowing it for any message." It invalidates later messages no more than editing a past message does. So: any message of the destination, defaulting to the newest.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) Cast: "The final cast would *need* to be a union of the final cast members of both chats at the merge point, in almost all cases. How are you going to merge a chat with a character without including it?"
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) On one framed section at the merge point holding the source's post-divergence messages (the coordinator's first proposal): "it basically adds a huge interruption between messages, distancing our own chat's recent history, adding a huge block from elsewhere to it. It's kind of uneven, context wise. It means our continuation might take more from the merged chat than our own, for example. It's also not really putting the two chats in parallel. But I don't have a better solution, either, and we could always change it later, perhaps."
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05) "if I had to design a *multi-chatroom bot*, which is analogous, I would probably have the summary layer carry all chatrooms' history together as a unified history (potentially with each summary block having sections per-room), and recent context grouped by chatroom (since interleaving actual messages is problematic). The problem is this is much harder to map to our case. In our case the merge happens both after the fact (summaries would need to be regenerated or united somehow), and there's no clear unified timeline to make things like summaries overlap correctly."
- **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-05) A merge is a join message in the destination fork at the chosen message, with a recorded second parent: the source chat/fork pinned at its tip as it stands at merge time, and the merge base (the common prefix when the chats share history; else none). The source keeps going; merging it again brings only what's new since the last merge. Delete, restore and branch it like any message; a fork made before it doesn't contain it.
- **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-05) Default context layout "parallel span": the source's post-base messages as one framed section before the destination's own span that ran in parallel ("Meanwhile, in parallel with what follows: <title>, with <cast>"), a short join marker at the merge point; sized relative to the destination's parallel span, verbatim up to the budget and the older part from the source's own LCM summaries; missing summaries are offered for generation only on the user's explicit action. Second registered layout "block at the join", selectable per chat. Framing and markers are preset templates with editable defaults; the destination's preset, model, cards and settings apply; lorebooks scan the merged text; later, only agents whose character was in the source see its section.
- **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-05, mapping the multi-room addition) The alignment unit is the whole parallel span: within it there is no finer order to get wrong. Recent context: the parallel layout groups it by line. Summary layer: once the span falls behind the verbatim window, its summary blocks carry both lines, one section per line (destination first, then the source), built from both lines' existing leaf summaries (the source's own LCM output reused), condensed upward like any tier. A registered strategy alongside the layouts. Finer alignment inside a span by wall-clock time: an idea, not built.
- **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-05) Cast after the merge is the union of both casts at the merge point; the source's other state is listed and adopted only when ticked. UI: "Merge another chat…" in the chat's ⋯ menu and "Merge a chat here…" among a message's actions; a dialog that picks the source, shows what comes in, the position, the span start for unrelated chats, the title and framing line, the cast union, the state to adopt and a preview of the exact context; in the timeline one collapsible "Merged <title> · N messages" entry with the read-only source messages and a link to the source, numbering unchanged; the context inspector shows the section where it is sent.
- **[P]** (chatmerge) How it's built (CHAT-MERGE.md): the join is an ordinary slot (appended at the tip, else inserted below the chosen message) whose one version, origin `merge`, holds the transcript the model reads; its second parent lives in `chat_merges`, with soft source references (the unused multi-parent slot scaffolding was removed in schemaclean, 2026-11-06), so the destination's summaries cover the join like any message and a source in another chat needs no cross-chat edge. All references to the source are soft: deleting it never touches the destination. The budget is the destination's parallel span in tokens, at least 4,000 and at most 50,000; what neither fits nor has a source summary is left out with its count, or included as written when the user ticks it.
- **[P]** (chatmerge, built 2026-11-05) Left for later: the source's chat notes aren't offered for copying yet, and the join takes a message number like any message (a merge in the middle of the history renumbers the messages after it by one; the default merge after the newest renumbers nothing). The section and the join marker are sent as system messages without a name, as summaries are. The merged chat's entry offers branch, insert, hide, copy link and delete, not edit or versions.

### 3.4 Chat state lives on the graph
- **[D] Problem:** in ST the author's note state is not tied to branch points, so the owner branches at the latest message as a checkpoint before every author's-note edit.
- **[D]** Following the messages has a cost (owner, 2026-10-16): branching from an earlier point resets everything that follows the chat's messages. That's less intuitive than it seems, and it's a problem for everything that works this way: not every change is specific to that exact chat state, and many are wanted even in a fork that goes back earlier.
- **[O]** **Overtaken**: Core scopes are implemented (ARCHITECTURE §3.8): branch-owned card swaps; positional presence, played-by-me and legacy bindings; ordinary model/preset selection is account-owned (§6.1). Human notes use the owner’s per-note choice below. Agent/tool scopes remain Phase 4 work. Earlier wording: “So each kind of chat state has to be classed as either following the messages (causal: it belongs to what happened at that point) or belonging to the branch (kept by new forks made from any earlier point, while existing forks keep what they had). Cards are classed below (§4); human notes are decided below. Preset switches and toggles, presence and the "played by me" set remain open.”
- **[D]** For notes it depends on the note (owner, 2026-10-16). Notes often hold background or higher-level, story-driven changes that aren't meant to apply only from the message they were last edited at, so forking back to an earlier message doesn't necessarily mean reverting every note. This is harder than for cards: a note isn't a change to something that already existed, and there can be many of them. It might be a per-note setting, but that needs care not to become confusing.
- **[D]** Human note inheritance on fork (owner, 2026-10-28 23:30 and 23:33; implemented, this proposal is obsolete: ARCHITECTURE §3.8). When forking before human note edits, ask per changed note: **As it was then** / **As it is now**, including its text and on/off state. **Then is the default**. A top-level then/now switch sets all notes at once; individual rows can differ afterwards. Each accordion row expands to a side-by-side CodeMirror diff. A button opening the same diff is available behind a development-only toggle for comparison. This supersedes the earlier proposed default-now mode. Notes the agent changes during a turn always follow the messages (§4.1a).
- **[P]** Card-note activation in the chat follows the same per-note fork choice (coordinator fill-in, 2026-10-28; implemented). Card-owned text remains owned by the card, as in §4.1. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §3.8); remains [P], not an owner decision.
- **[P]** **Every change to chat state is an event at a position in the graph.** The state at any message is the result of replaying the events on the path to it. Chat state covers: **Partial/overtaken**: event replay is built (ARCHITECTURE §3.8), but branch-owned swaps survive earlier forks and ordinary preset selection is global (§4, §6.1); not every setting is a positional event.
  - notes and which notes are active
  - card overrides
  - preset switches and toggles
  - presence
  - the "played by me" set
  - variables
- **[P]** As a result, branching restores state automatically, edits after a branch point don't leak into other branches, and the timeline can show small markers for state changes. **Built isolation foundation** (ARCHITECTURE §3.8); **Built markers** (markers1, 2026-11-15; ARCHITECTURE §3.8 "Change markers in the timeline"): quiet lines for the changes recorded with a message position; summary settings and card swaps, which have none, stay unmarked.
- **[P]** State events produced by a rollout are **attached to the version that produced them**, so switching versions switches their effects too. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.8). General RP tool production of these events remains Phase 4 work.
- **[P]** A deleted message's state events stop applying while it's in the recycle bin, and restoring the message brings them back (built, ARCHITECTURE.md §3.8; found 2026-10-11: before, they kept applying from the bin).
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: History edits are always this chat only (§10.3, owner); shared-note text has explicit global sharing (§4.1), and new forks use the per-note choice above. No blanket apply-to-all-forks option is built. Earlier wording: “Default edit scope is "from here on, on this branch", with "apply to all branches" as an option.”
- **[O]** **Overtaken**: The blanket scope option is superseded by §10.3’s owner decision; named shared notes (§4.1) have explicit identity and global text sharing (ARCHITECTURE §14). Earlier wording: “That option assumes the note has a stable, recognizable identity, which may not be clear to the user.”

### 3.5 Presence
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) "Does adding and removing a character to a chat right away leave any permanent connection? I would say that a character is not *truly* added to a chat until a message or swipe is generated with that character being connected. If there's any kind of 'was in chat X' connection/feature, it should not consider a character that was added and removed right away. This is a design principle."
- **[P]** (castpresence, 2026-11-02) Keep join/leave events as current and historical cast state; record historical participation with authored message versions. Each version pins its cast, with its speaker also counted, rather than deriving past participation from today's events. A historical swipe counts the cast actually used at its position; a tip-only join does not change an earlier swipe. A branch without a new version inherits cast state without inventing participation. Retained versions keep their evidence across selection changes and cast undo; purged versions cease to count. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §3.8).
- **[D]** Characters can be marked present or absent per branch. Presence can be set:
  - manually (like muting in ST)
  - by a tool call
  - by a required `send_message` argument
  - by a decision model reading the message
- **[P]** All of these produce the same presence event. **Built manual Mute event** (ARCHITECTURE §3.8, §12); autonomous tool/decision-model producers remain Phase 4 work.
- **[D]** Muting is not presence (owner, 2026-10-26). **Muting** is a concrete user action with no fixed RP meaning: the character doesn't talk, whether it's away or you're just having a long one-on-one with someone else while it stays in the scene. It's shown as an explicit option, the one that isn't confusing for users. **Presence** was meant for agentic RP: the model or a decision model marks that a character *isn't there*, so it won't talk. The two overlap (an absent character can't talk). **[O], answered for plain RP below:** whether presence stays separate or is redundant with muting. **Overtaken for plain RP** by PHASE1-DECISIONS §2 (owner, 2026-10-28): “Muting and presence … one thing, shown as Mute. Keep.” The Mute control is built (ARCHITECTURE §12); future agent semantics remain later work.
- **[D]** Muting only stops the character talking: it is never picked to reply automatically (it can still be made to speak explicitly). It **doesn't remove its card from the context**: taking cards out or swapping them busts the KV cache every turn, the worst possible default (the owner rejected it in the first design session; §5.2). Cards are only swapped in the opt-in card-swap layout, at the tail (owner, 2026-10-26; "at the tail" superseded 2026-11-06, §7.1: the swap rotates the speaker's card into the preset's definition positions). **[P]** Per fork, switched from the cast. **Built** per-fork cast switch (ARCHITECTURE §3.8, §12); explicit speaker choice can still make a muted card speak.

---

## 4. Layered, versioned entities (cards, presets, lorebooks)

- **[D] Problems:**
  - Reusing a character for a different premise means changing the card without breaking existing chats, and ST forces duplication.
  - Duplicates can't be renamed during duplication, and the card name *is* the in-chat name.
  - Opening a card reorders the list, so you can't tell duplicates apart.
  - Users also expect editing a card to change it everywhere.

  The UI must make edit scope clear. The owner expects this design to be hard.
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: The following owner-confirmed CCv3 mapping uses `name`, `nickname` and `character_version`; built (ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §14). Earlier wording: “Three names, kept separate: **identity** (internal ID), **library label** (title plus a free-text subtitle), **in-chat name** (what `{{char}}` resolves to).”
- **[D]** A card has three names (owner, 2026-10-16):
  - the **in-character name**: what `{{char}}` resolves to and what the model sees
  - a **display name**: the in-character name by default, but it can be set to something else, and then it's the name the UI shows the card by, while the in-character name stays the one sent to the model and used in templates
  - a **subtitle**: the version string. It is the card's own version field, ST's `character_version`, a real card field that ST can show as the subtitle: so it's part of the card's content, with its revisions, and goes in and out with the card file. It's not a separate label the app keeps beside the card (owner, 2026-10-16). That earlier separate-library-subtitle build is obsolete: the standard-field mapping below is built (ARCHITECTURE §3.9).
- **[D]** These names use the standard card metadata, not a scheme of the app's own (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[D]** Home's cards show the card's version (its subtitle, `character_version`) under the name (owner, as dictated earlier; reconfirmed 2026-11-02). Only a card source's Show option ("cardex", §12; renamed from "Archive tab", owner 2026-11-06), which lists cards that aren't the user's, shows different card content on its tiles. Built for the home grid (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Characters"); CARD-FORKS surface 5 and CHATS.md's card-variant entry are settled by this for the grid.
- **[D]** The mapping CCv3 gives (owner confirmed, 2026-10-26): the **display name** is the card's `name`; the **in-character name** is `nickname`, which CCv3 says `{{char}}` uses, falling back to `name` when it's empty; the **subtitle** is `character_version`. So "display name defaults to the in-character name" holds the other way round in the file: a card with one name sets only `name`, and setting a different in-character name fills `nickname`. ST 1.19 ignores `nickname` (it appears nowhere in its source, checked 2026-10-26): its cards have only `name`, which imports as both names, and a card whose two names differ sends its display name to the model in ST.
- **[P]** Other standard fields cover things planned or open elsewhere: `source` (CCv3: IDs or URLs of where the card came from, only appended to, not user-editable) records an archive card's origin and recognizes duplicates (§12); `creation_date` and `modification_date`; `group_only_greetings` (greetings used only in chats with several characters; **[D]** offered as greetings when a new chat has more than one character, owner 2026-10-30). Card lineage (forks) has no standard field, so it stays the app's own relationship outside the card's content. **Partial**: all listed fields round-trip (ARCHITECTURE §8); **[D] group-only greeting selection built** (fgreet, 2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12). Archive duplicate/update recognition and automatic timestamp maintenance remain later work; preservation is not archive integration.
- **[P]** (cardeditor, 2026-11-01, not yet confirmed) The card's Metadata shows a read-only **Sources** list of links to the archives and sites it was uploaded to, each site a replaceable recognizer: **Chub** from `extensions.chub.full_path` (written by chub's API, copied by ST) → `https://chub.ai/characters/<full_path>`, plus the creator's `https://chub.ai/users/<creator>`, and `chub.ai`/`characterhub.org` URLs in `source`; **RisuRealm** `https://realm.risuai.net/character/<id>` URLs in `source` or RisuAI's `extensions.risuai.source`; **JanitorAI** (`janitorai.com`), **JannyAI** (`jannyai.com`), **Pygmalion** (`pygmalion.chat`), **AI Character Cards** (`aicharactercards.com`) and **Perchance** (`perchance.org`) URLs in `source`, named by host (the sites ST imports from by URL). Other URLs show as plain links named by host; ids no recognizer claims show as text. cardex has no recognizer yet: its card fields and page URLs weren't found in public documentation; a recognizer is added when they are. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6), in the card's Metadata tab.
- **[D] Superseded for non-card forks by the 2026-11-01 decision below.** Card forks are a model for cards only: cards and presets can't work the same way, notes even less (owner, 2026-10-26). Presets, lorebooks and notes get their own designs.
- **[D]** A card's own notes (its Character's Note) are forked with the card: there is no other coherent way (owner, 2026-10-26). **[O], answered** by the owner’s global named-note store (§4.1), built with linked note slots and snippets (ARCHITECTURE §14). Card-owned text copies on fork; linked shared text remains linked.
- **[D]** Who wrote the earlier messages after a fork or a rename has no single answer. As in ST, which asks on renaming a character whether to replace its name in all past chats, renaming offers to rewrite the name in past chats (§10.3; built in U18, ARCHITECTURE §3.13). That still leaves a gap: using the renamed fork in another chat that has the previous name. A mechanism for wide-range edits on chat history, replacing strings and names, is needed (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[D]** A card's names in its editor (owner, 2026-11-01): the pencil rename flow (pencil → field → confirm or cancel, Enter or Escape) for the display name, the version and the nickname, in the header of the full editor and at the top of the chat's character tab, the same component. The display name is the title, the version a small badge beside it, the nickname small muted text under the title phrased by what it does ("In chat: Mio"), or a faint "Add an in-chat nickname" when there's none (wording updated by the owner, 2026-11-02); no "Display name:" labels on screen, but each has a tooltip saying exactly what it is. The full editor's separate identity section goes away. History replacement is offered only when the effective nickname (nickname, else display name) changes, asked right there on confirm, not after a later Save: a display name changed with a nickname set asks nothing; a nickname changed asks "Replace <old> with <new> in the history: No / this chat / all N chats"; a nickname set for the first time asks only if it differs from the display name, then as above; a nickname cleared asks only if the display name differs, then as above; a display name changed with no nickname offers three choices: keep the old name in chats (the old display name becomes the nickname, history untouched), rename everywhere (replace in history: this chat / all N chats), or just change it (the new name from now on, old messages untouched). The rename-history machinery moves to this point, and the after-save rename offer goes. **Built** (cardeditor; ARCHITECTURE §3.13, §14.6).
- **[P]** (cardeditor, 2026-11-01, not yet confirmed) Details of the rename flow the owner's design leaves open: closing the question (Escape, or the dialog's close) cancels the rename and keeps the field open, so a name never changes without an answer; the names are written as live text once answered (sent at once, as typing is), so Save never asks; a version change asks nothing; a display name or version another card already has is refused on confirm with the clash sentence; a history replacement that fails still renames, and the failure is kept for **Resume history replacement**.
