- **[D]** The card editor's structure (owner, 2026-11-01: "basically everything should be editable directly in the side column tab"): the side panel's character tab holds every field the full editor has, and both views divide the fields into tabs (sub-tabs in the panel): Definition (every field that goes into the prompt directly), Greetings (first message, alternate and group-only greetings), Metadata (everything that isn't prompt text: creator's notes, tags, creator, version info, links and sources), Lorebook (the card's own book in the side panel's lorebook editor), Notes (the card's notes, inline, with "Add a note"), Extra (regex scripts and anything later). The name header sits above the tabs in both. "New chat with <name>" is a visible button; the More menu goes; Export… is in the full editor's header and the tab's actions; the card's total and permanent token counts show at the top of both, with per-field counts in Definition, live as you type; a persona's tab shows the same tabs and fields as any character. **Built** (cardeditor; ARCHITECTURE §12, §14.6).
  - Notes replacing individual fields, their links in both directions, and the proposed display and editing of a replaced field in a chat are specified in [§4.2](#42-notes-that-replace-prompts) (owner decisions and proposals, 2026-11-03; unbuilt).
- **[P]** (cardeditor, 2026-11-01, not yet confirmed) Where things went within that structure: the Character's Note is a card note here (§4.1a), so it's in Notes, not Definition, and Definition says so; the picture is beside the names in both headers (click to change), and the full editor's Metadata also has the picture with its drop zone; Talkativeness is a 0–100% slider (5% steps, ST's 50% when the card has none) under "In group chats" in Metadata; the file's creation and modification dates show read-only in Metadata; the Lorebook tab is the side panel's book editor in the side panel (its **Large editor** opens the book's own view over the chat) and the table editor in the full editor, whose width suits it; every field's explanation is a tooltip on its label. Token counts and Export… as proposed here are **superseded** by the owner's decisions of 2026-11-02 below (a real tokenizer; Export with unsaved changes asks first). The persona's tab has Full editor, Export… and Switch persona, without Fork for this chat or Use another version.
- **[D], built** (exportsubmenu, owner, 2026-11-02): “Export… becomes a real submenu”, opening “on hover and on click or Enter/→”, with PNG, V2 JSON, V3 JSON and CHARX using the existing unsaved-changes question. Every export control shares the choices. Hover text: “Exports this card as a file.” Obsolete as an implementation proposal; see ARCHITECTURE §5 (Submenus) and §11.4 (Export with unsaved changes).
- **[D] The card header's layout** (owner, 2026-11-02, on the persona's tab in the side panel): "'You play' at the top: this shifts the entire layout below for a persona card, which looks bad. It needs to be repositioned/redesigned to not do that. Use a badge-like UI. The other thing is that the entire top cluster looks misaligned no matter what. The card thumbnail there is far too small to make sense, and looks disproportionately small to the title cluster next to it. The bottom controls on it also look misaligned to the bottom of the text next to it. Make it significantly larger, put the image controls on the image as overlays that are semi-transparent until you hover them. Make sure the height of elements align." **Built** (charheader; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[D] Row placement and two-line name superseded by the 2026-11-03 four-row header (§2.2); pencils still follow their text.** (owner, 2026-11-02): "each pencil sits directly after the text it edits, inline: right after the display name's last line, right after the version badge, right after the nickname (or its placeholder)"; "Stack them with normal, consistent spacing from the top." Keep the token counts, the display name at most two lines, and the version and nickname one line each with an ellipsis; a clamped name keeps its pencil at the visible end, in view and on the same line. **Built** (charheader2; ARCHITECTURE §14.6), superseding the earlier evenly distributed rows and shared pencil axis.
  - **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02): the card field is called "in-chat nickname" / "nickname", never "chat name". Its field label is "In-chat nickname", its empty placeholder "Add an in-chat nickname". Nickname explanation, exactly: "The nickname used in chats: this is what's seen by the LLM, and what {{char}} is set to in prompts. If empty, the display name is used." Display-name explanation, exactly: 'The display name for this card: if a separate "nickname" is set, the display name is not shown in prompts and is not used for {{char}}. Otherwise, the display name doubles as the nickname.' **Built** (charheader2; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[P] Superseded by the 2026-11-03 four-row header decision (§2.2).** (charheader, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed): the version is on its own row under the display name, changing the 2026-11-01 "badge beside it" placement. The owner has left this undecided; charheader2 keeps the separate row.
  - **[P]** (charheader, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) Other implemented details: one header component for every character tab and the full editor (`CardHeader`). "You play" is a small accent badge on the portrait's bottom-left corner, so a persona's header has the same shape as any other. The portrait is 2:3: in the panel 136 px high at its narrowest, 144 px from the default width, 160 px when dragged wide; in the full editor 144 px, 176 px on a wide page (it was 60 px). Adjust cutout and View full image sit over the portrait's top-right corner, faint until the portrait or a control is hovered or focused, always shown on a touch screen; nothing takes room under the portrait. The full editor's favourite star stays at the end of the name's row.
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) "By the way, the big version's header has the same annoyances as the side bar one." The full editor's header is the same header, with the same picture controls over the portrait (Adjust cutout too, which it lacked). **Built** (charheader).
- **[D] The full editor's Picture** (owner, 2026-11-02, on Metadata's label, small portrait, Adjust cutout button and full-width drop zone): "These are misaligned and also this entire 3-element UI looks awful in so many ways, and not really made for a wide screen, despite being exclusive to the big version of the card editor." **Built** (charheader; ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[P]** (charheader, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) One large 2:3 portrait (288 × 192 px) that is its own drop target: a click or **Replace the picture** picks a new image, **Adjust cutout** sits beside Replace over its top-right corner (faint until hovered or focused, as in the header); with no picture, a dashed box of the same size, **Add a picture**. The accepted formats and "the whole image is kept" are in the explanations, not a standing line. On a wide page the Picture stands beside Creator's notes, Tags and Creator, tops aligned; on a narrow one above them. Every card header's portrait (side panel and full editor) also takes a dropped image, so the side panel changes the picture as fully as the full editor (click, drop, Adjust cutout, View full image).
- **[D] Tags** (owner, 2026-11-02, on the "A tag" field and "Add the tag" button under the chips): "Misaligned. Button spells out *three words* when a + icon with a tooltip would've been enough (and really the + would've explained it all, maybe a +-with-tag icon?) The tag input bar is hilariously wide." **Built** (charheader).
  - **[P]** (charheader, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) The last chip in the row is a dashed chip holding a short field ("New tag", as wide as what's typed, at most 12rem) and a + icon button ("Add the tag", its explanation says Enter adds too), the same height and on the same line as the tags; Enter or + adds. The same in the side panel's Metadata.
- **[D] Export with unsaved changes asks first** (owner, 2026-11-02: "If someone is exporting after edits it's likely they forgot to save and they can't even check after export"). An export only ever writes a saved revision. If the card has unsaved fields, Export opens a dialog listing them by name, with **Save and export** (cuts a revision, then exports it), **Export the last revision** (without the unsaved changes) and Cancel. With nothing unsaved, Export works as before, with no dialog. Everywhere a card or persona can be exported: the full editor's Export…, the character and persona tabs' Export…, the library's item menu. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §11.4, §14.6).
  - **[P]** The library's item menu asks for presets and lorebooks too, through the same check: the owner's reason holds for them as much, and one export path stays one behaviour. A preset's held changes (§6.2) count as unsaved and are named as one line.
  - **[D]** A card's notes are part of what it exports, so the rule covers them too (coordinator, 2026-11-02, applying the owner's rule to every unsaved field): the dialog also lists unsaved typing in the card's exportable notes ("Note “Lamp oil”: Content"; a linked note's shared note), **Save and export** saves those notes as well (each a revision of its own) and **Export the last revision** takes their last revisions. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §11.4). The earlier [P] that only the card's own fields were asked about is superseded.
- **[D] Token counts use a real tokenizer** (owner, 2026-11-02: "We should at least use a real tokenizer if it's not prohibitive from a performance perspective… ideally you'd always use the tokenizer of the selected model"). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §6 "Text tokens", §14.6). **History fitting and cache estimates built** (tokencut, 2026-11-07; ARCHITECTURE §15), superseding the original display-only performance gate. How, for the owner to confirm:
  - **[D] No provider counting by default** (owner, 2026-11-02: "I think this isn't a good idea to do behind the user's back, especially given varying retention policies. A user could send a card prompt it never intended to an API it only selected for something else. We probably should support it but not do it by default."). Counting with a provider's endpoint (Anthropic's, Google's, OpenAI's) is an explicit per-connection opt-in, off by default, in the connection's settings ("Token counts"), whose explanation says plainly that the text being counted is sent to that provider. Without it, OpenAI's models stay local and exact, and Claude and Gemini are counted with the local `o200k_base`, marked "~" and approximate in the hover. Anthropic's old public Claude tokenizer is not used: it undercounts current models by 30–50% (owner). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §6 "Text tokens"). Rejected, so it doesn't come back: counting through the providers' endpoints by default (this job's first version). Governed by the rule in §11 ("Nothing goes to a provider the user didn't choose").
  - **[D] A separate token counting endpoint** (owner, 2026-11-02: "It should also be possible to specify a separate token counting endpoint for a connection"). See §11. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §6 "Text tokens").
  - **[P]** (providersend, 2026-11-02) How: the connection's **Token counts** setting is one picker, "Count with": **Local estimate** (the default), **This connection's provider**, or **Another: <connection>**, one of the user's other saved connections, used only for counting. Its explanation names the provider and address that receive the text and images being counted. The same choice governs the editors' text counts and the next request's image counts (§10.2a). Through another connection, the chat model is asked for by that connection's name for it: the same key if it lists one, else the key without a router's `vendor/` prefix, matched with dots and dashes alike (OpenRouter's `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5` → Anthropic's `claude-sonnet-4-5`). Stored per user, by connection (`user_prefs` `token_counting`, `{ connections: { id: viaId } }`; the earlier on/off list reads back as nothing chosen). A counting connection that's deleted puts the choice back to local.
  - **[P]** The tokenizer is the one of the model chats generate with (the account's current model: a chat binds no other). OpenAI's models (any host, OpenRouter's `openai/…` too) are counted locally with their own encodings; with the connection opted in (above), Claude through Anthropic's free counting endpoint, Gemini through its countTokens, other OpenAI models on api.openai.com through OpenAI's input-token counting, one request per text, cached. The opt-in is stored per user, by connection (user-scoped `settings`), so no schema change was needed. Published open-model families now have local tokenizers (tokenizers, 2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §6); unsupported models use o200k_base as an approximation, marked "~" and explained in the count's tooltip, as is a failed count; nothing is counted at four characters a token any more, except when the tokenizer can't run at all. Adding a model's own tokenizer is one registration (the provider registry's text counters).
  - **[P]** The counts that say what's sent use it: the card editor's total, permanent and per-field counts, and a preset prompt's own text. The context inspector and displayed next-request total now use local tokenizers and per-model calibration (tokenizers, 2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §15). The budget cut, cache effects and preset markers retain the fast estimate: scale measurements exceeded the owner's performance allowance for budget tokenization. The inspector compares reported total input/cache tokens to the raw local request estimate; calibration uses complete reported text-only requests and remains separate per owner, connection, model and tokenizer. These implementation choices remain **[P]**.
  - **[P]** As ST's character panel (public/scripts/RossAscends-mods.js `RA_CountCharTokens`, public/index.html's `data-token-counter`/`data-token-permanent` counters): each field counted on its own and summed; permanent is the name, description, personality, scenario and the Character's Note, which here is the card's notes on by default; total adds the first message, example messages, system prompt and post-history instructions.
  - **[D] The card's counts include exactly what's sent** (owner, 2026-11-02, asking about the linked notes left out above: "Why not? Is it sent as a prompt or not?"). A linked note that's on by default is sent like any of the card's notes, with its shared note's text, so it counts, with that text. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6). The earlier [P] that a linked shared note isn't counted is superseded. How, for the owner to confirm:
    - **[P]** Checked against the assembler, three more places counted differently, now fixed: a note's text is counted as typed (its live text, as a reply sends it), not as last saved; a link to a shared note in the bin counts nothing, since it sends nothing; and each text is trimmed, as the assembler trims every card field and note. Left as they are: a note with a condition (on its card's turn, while it isn't muted) counts as permanent although it comes and goes, as ST counts its Character's Note; the preset's wrappers around the fields (`{{char}}'s personality: …`) aren't the card's and aren't counted, as in ST.
  - **[D]** Token counts substitute macros before counting, as ST does (`substituteParams` in `RA_CountCharTokens`): `{{char}}`, `{{user}}` and the rest, using the current chat's names where there is one (coordinator, 2026-11-02). `{{char}}` is the card's name for chats, `{{user}}` who the user plays in the open chat, else "User" as in ST; `{{description}}` and the like read the card's own fields. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6). The earlier [P] counting the text as typed is superseded.
- **[D]** Existing card variants and "only in this chat" overrides are not migrated to forks: no data has any (ST imports never create them). The owner clarified on 2026-10-28 that deployment starts fresh from the single edited baseline: no card-specific startup refusal or migration (unit-4 brief).
- **[D]** Making a variant is called **forking** the card, with the semantics that go with it: a fork is the child of the card it was forked from, its parent (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D]** There must be an easy way to find all the variants of a card (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D]** No two cards may have both the same display name and the same version: one of the two must differ. The UI enforces it when making forks or new cards, where it's required. It isn't a data-model rule, because imported cards won't respect it (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[P]** The same check applies when an existing card's display name or version is changed. Imported cards that collide are left as they are. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §14). This is UI validation, not database uniqueness.
- **[D]** ST's problems with this (owner, 2026-10-16): cards have a `character_version` field that ST can show as a subtitle, but it's hidden until enabled in settings, it's settable only in the advanced definitions, and cloning sets neither it nor the display name, so a clone is an identical card and the original can only be told apart by the chats tied to it.
- **[P]** Three layers, the same mechanism for cards, presets and lorebooks: **Overtaken for cards** by the owner’s independent forks and fork-and-swap below. Explicit re-import merges are built (ARCHITECTURE §3.9); chat overrides and non-card variant refs were replaced by the one fork model (libfork, 2026-11-06, below).
  - **Base:** the imported or upstream version. Re-importing a new version adds a base revision.
  - **Variant:** a named fork, stored as changes on top of the base, so upstream updates flow through. *(Rejected for forks, below.)*
  - **Chat-local override:** changes for one chat only, with no library entry. It can be **promoted** to a variant.
- **[D] Explicit merges for every library fork** (owner, 2026-11-01): "Should presets and lorebooks work like cards? Yes, do this. But they should work like git branches, still connected to the parent. You explicitly merge git branches, you don't push to master and then see every branch change on its own. We don't want that anywhere." The parent offers versions since the fork/last merge, with inspection, selection and conflict resolution; "Also every parent and child would have a way to see the full genealogy tree with links to each of them you'd be able to click or open in new tabs." Cards also get import Merge (owner, 2026-11-01: "the former is the same as presets"). **Server foundation built 2026-11-02 (ARCHITECTURE §3.9); the UI built 2026-11-05 (forkmergeui, ARCHITECTURE §14.6). Obsolete as an implementation proposal.**
  - **[P]** (libfork, 2026-11-06; spec/LIBRARY-FORKS.md) **One fork model: every library fork is an entity of its own**, for presets, lorebooks and notes as for cards (owner, 2026-11-06: "If it doesn't make a product difference, we shouldn't have two models, but make sure the unified one is the best option"). Compared with forks as refs of one entity and with folding refs into entities, this one keeps every behaviour the owner relies on, fixes archive and bin acting on the original and every sibling, makes the family one indexed walk, and lets a plugin kind fork with nothing of its own. Visible changes: a fork's History runs on through its original's (the [D] below; first built starting at the fork); archiving a fork archives that fork alone; a card fork's note copies can merge the original notes' changes; chat overrides (never reachable from the UI) are removed. Kept: the "variant" badge and "Make a variant", "update available" for a variant whose original moved, "Update from file…" on originals only. **Built 2026-11-06 (ARCHITECTURE §3.9).**
  - **[D] A fork's History is a git branch's log** (owner, 2026-11-06, via the coordinator). On "a preset or lorebook variant's History now starts at the variant", the owner's reaction was "???": library forks work like git branches, and a branch's log includes its ancestors' commits up to the fork point. So every kind's fork, cards included (which never did this), lists its own revisions, then a clear "forked here from <original>" marker, then the original's revisions up to the fork point; nested forks are followed all the way back. Restore of an inherited revision acts on the fork (makes it the fork's new revision), never on the original. **Built 2026-11-06 (libfork; ARCHITECTURE §3.9, spec/LIBRARY-FORKS.md §4).**
    - **[P]** Revision numbers run on across the fork point (a fork of revision 3 starts at revision 4). Restoring an inherited revision keeps what the fork step changed (a card fork's name, nickname and version; a copied note's card and condition). An inherited revision's message shows on the fork but is edited only in the original's History.
  - **[P]** (forkmerge, 2026-11-02; **overtaken** by the one fork model above: there are no chat overrides and no second refs any more) Retain non-card ref identities and parent links, including chat overrides; none takes automatic updates. Existing retained refs simply stop following content; their last merge base stays where it was. New refs additionally retain their original fork revision. The latest brief authorizes baseline edits and daily re-import rather than migrations; databases created before this baseline need rebuilding, not an in-place conversion of already-applied changes.
  - **[P]** Merges refuse active field drafts; Save or Discard first, then preview. A preview names a historical parent revision, the current child row version and its merge base. Later parent revisions do not invalidate that historical selection; changes to the child/base do.
  - **[P]** Select per keyed prompt/entry, scalar field and array order. Conflicts retain base/ours/theirs, with an absent value representing deletion. Exclusion retains ours. An included conflict requires an explicit resolution. Schema-invalid combinations are conflicts or a refused commit. Advancing the base accepts the whole selected parent revision, including excluded changes: later merges do not offer those excluded changes again unless the parent changes them again. Undo restores the earlier base.
  - **[P]** Every accepted explicit merge writes a two-parent revision and a merge record, even if the content stays identical. Undo at that head writes a compensating revision and restores the earlier base; after later edits, use normal history restore (which restores content without rewinding merge bookkeeping).
  - **[P]** Card file Merge stages a revision on the existing import line; it changes neither the card head nor its own note entities. Embedded lorebook and other card fields use the common merge. Exported card-note sidecars are excluded from this operation: they have independent histories and need their own update review. A fork's first own import takes over as its update source; its genealogy still points to the original parent.
  - **[P]** Genealogy reuses CardFamily for cards, including purged tombstones; other kinds use connected ref nodes (since libfork, every kind uses the same entity family, tombstones included). Library labels, tags and archive state do not merge. Manually linked card parents have no known fork revision and therefore no merge preview; relinking clears a former parent merge base, while an own import line keeps its base. The policy is registered as `library.merge` in each database worker, so it can be replaced or disabled.
  - **[P]** (forkmergeui, 2026-11-05) The UI. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6):
    - **Where merge is offered.** A notice under the editor's actions, where the old "update available" alert was, in the full editor and in every side-panel editor (preset, lorebook, character tab): "The parent has N new revisions" (or "A newer imported version is waiting" for an entry updated from its own files) with **Review and merge**. It replaces the old one-step Update dialog. A read failure shows with Retry, never as "up to date". A card marked as a fork by hand gets the notice without the button, saying there's no shared starting revision. Card forks get it too, from their parent's later revisions. The card import dialog (Import a new version…) gets **Merge…** beside Replace, opening the same review on the file just staged.
    - **The review** is one dialog at every width: the source (the parent, linked), a parent-revision picker when there are several (the newest by default; an older one leaves the later ones for another merge), then one row per change the parent made (a prompt, an entry, a field, an order), conflicts first, each with a **Take** switch, what the parent changed (the History comparison components), and Take all / Leave all out. Changes made only in the fork aren't rows; one line counts them. Each included conflict shows the current and the parent's version, how each side changed it, and needs an explicit pick (Keep current version, Take parent's version, Edit it); a deletion shows as "(removed)". An order conflict is picked by side, shown as numbered names; content changes inside it merge separately. Leaving changes out says they won't be offered again for that revision. Before Merge, "What merging changes in <name>" shows the result against the current content, computed with the registered `library.merge` policy; Merge stays disabled, with the reason, while a conflict is unpicked or the result wouldn't be valid.
    - **Picks are a private user draft** keyed by the fork and the three revisions, so a reload keeps them; a stale comparison (the fork was saved elsewhere meanwhile) keeps the picks and offers Compare again.
    - **Unsaved fields** refuse the review: the dialog says how many and embeds the editor's own Save/Revert controls; once they're saved or reverted the comparison appears.
    - **Undo merge** sits beside Restore on a merge revision in History, enabled only while that merge is the latest revision and nothing is unsaved; otherwise disabled, saying why.
    - **Family** (presets and lorebooks): a button beside History in the full and side-panel editors opens the family tree: the original, every fork and fork of a fork, each a link (new tab works; in the chat's panel a plain click opens it in the panel), this one marked, fork and last-merge dates, bin/archive state, forks foldable. A card's family stays its existing Forks tree.
- **[D]** Customizing a card for one chat is the typical case, and it's done by **forking and swapping** ("Fork for this chat" or similar): it makes a concrete card, a fork, and the chat uses it instead. Forking the chat from anywhere earlier keeps the changed card; *existing* forks of the chat keep whatever they had. This covers refining a card for a chat and then going back a few messages, or redoing much of the story (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D], superseded — DROPPED** (owner, 2026-11-03; §4.2): timeline card edits. Earlier decision (owner, 2026-10-16): “Card changes that follow the messages (made at a point in the timeline, reset by branching before it) are the specific case, not the default, and users wouldn't find them intuitive. They also invalidate the cache in a default configuration. Their main purpose is tool-based autonomous card rewriting, which must always work this way: the model can't change the card for anyone else as part of a chat turn without losing every property of chat turns and swipes, and this keeps swipes redoable and reversible. They can make sense manually for the same reason, character progression.” Notes replacing prompts now supply those chat-state semantics; tools create notes rather than timeline edits to the card object.
- **[D]** Character progression needs a more convenient path that's typically used instead: not editing the card, but adding and changing other things connected to it, tied to the card *and* the chat, such as author's notes for characters (owner, 2026-10-16).
- **[D] Rejected:** forks that follow their parent, with the parent's later changes merged into them (owner, 2026-10-16). There's no way to keep that from overwriting the changes the user made, nor for the user to make sure the result in the downstream cards is coherent; it's an effect hidden from the user and surprising; and the point of forking is to replace duplicating cards, which this doesn't allow. (That older card-variant implementation has been removed; independent card forks are built, ARCHITECTURE §3.9.)
- **[P]** So a fork is a card of its own from the moment it's made: a full copy whose only tie to its parent is the recorded relationship. Nothing done to the parent changes it. The same holds for a chat's own changes to a card. Re-importing a new version of the *same* card or preset (the preset case below) is different: it's an explicit act on that one entity, merged into the user's edits there with conflicts shown, and it doesn't reach the entity's forks. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.9). For cards “this chat’s changes” means fork-and-swap, not a following override.
- **Import a new version of a card, replacing it wholesale** (owner, 2026-11-01: "we need a way to import and merge/import and replace a new version of a card. The latter is the one that's unique to cards ... Cards participate in chats, so it can make sense to replace them with a new version wholesale everywhere"). Replace and Merge are built (ARCHITECTURE §11.1, §14.6). Choices made while building it, each **[P]** unless the owner confirmed it:
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) The portrait cutout is kept only while the picture is the same one (the file has none, or the same image); a new picture starts from the file's own cutout or the default, since a cutout is bound to its image (§14.5c).
  - **[P]** Fields typed since the last save take the file's text, as a restore does, with what was typed one state back in each field's edit history, so live text never overrides the new version unseen.
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) Replace is refused when the card changed since the preview (another revision, a reply's checkpoint): the user sees the comparison again rather than replacing a version they didn't see.
  - **[P]** The replacing revision has two parents (the card's head and the file on the import line), origin `merge` (History: "Took an update") and the message "Replaced with a new version from <file>". A card with no import line (a fork, a card made here) can take one; the file starts its import line.
  - **[P]** "M chats on a fixed revision" counts the chats bound to a revision where they stand now (each chat's active leaf); the rest of "used by N chats" follow the card.
  - **[P]** It replaces "Update from file…" for cards everywhere (library menu); presets and lorebooks keep the merging update. It's offered in the card editor's More menu, the card page, a chat's character tab and a library card's menu.
  - **[P]** The preview shows the file's picture from the upload itself (its bytes in the preview's answer, up to 16 MiB, else "too large to show"), so a preview stores no file.
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: Card chats bind an independent card’s latest upstream head or a fixed revision (ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §15); the following-variant noun is obsolete (§4 owner decisions). Earlier wording: “Chats point to a variant and follow its latest version by default, or are pinned to a fixed revision.”
- **[D]** Cards often need **non-additive changes as a story progresses**. You can't substitute a duplicate for the card in the current chat. **[P], overtaken**: the owner’s fork-and-swap rule above replaces card-local overrides; built (ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §12).
- **[D]** **Preset re-import must keep user edits.** Concrete case: the owner added a `{{summary}}` prompt and a summarization instruction, and softened "do not impersonate the user". A new upstream version currently means redoing all of it in ST's poor editor.
- **[P]** Additions survive trivially. Edits to upstream prompt text are reapplied with a three-way merge, and you're only asked when upstream changed the same passage. Real conflicts get a side-by-side resolver. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §14).
- **[P]** Edit scope depends on where you edit from: **Built/overtaken**: library edits affect that card everywhere; Fork for this chat creates/swaps an independent card (ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §12, §14), replacing the old local-override scope.
  - in the library, the variant changes everywhere, and the screen always shows "used by N chats"
  - from inside a chat, you choose "this chat only" or "the card everywhere", with the count visible
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: Independent card forks and fork-and-swap (§4), plus card notes (§5.0), replace this distinction. Usage, revisions and family comparisons are built (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14). Earlier wording: “The UI shows two concepts, **variants** and **this chat's changes**. Revisions sit behind a History button, and modules appear as sections of the card.”
- **[P]** Library: variants are grouped under one character entry. Sorting is explicit (last chatted or last edited, never "last viewed"). Any two variants can be diffed. **Partial**: family tree/filter, explicit sorting and saved family-card comparisons are built (ARCHITECTURE §14); collapsing each family into one grid entry remains an unbuilt proposal. Built (families1, 2026-11-15): an optional “Group forks” view on the Characters lists, off by default; see ARCHITECTURE §12. Still [P].

### 4.0a Card galleries and embedded images

- **[D] Card gallery** (owner, 2026-11-03, gallery brief): “Gallery” in Extra, in both the side panel and full editor, groups stored images under Portraits, Expressions, Backgrounds and Other, retaining their names. Multiple-file picking and dropping add Other images; thumbnail actions rename, change kind, remove to the restorable bin, and use an alternative icon as the main portrait, retaining the old portrait as an alternative in a new card revision. Every kind toggles in the image viewer. Fresh ST folder imports copy the card's gallery folder; CHARX preserves embedded asset kinds and names. PNG/JSON cannot carry image files. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5d).
- **[D] Alternative portraits must be usable, not just stored** (owner, 2026-11-03): “Alternative portraits are meant to actually be used, in different ways. Although it's a niche feature, we'll need to have it.” **Decided to build later:** using an alternative as the main portrait is built; contextual portrait choices remain future work.
  - **Open:** settle the uses with the owner: per-chat portrait choice, per-branch choice, switching per message/swipe, an outfit/state variant chosen from the chat, expression sprites by mood.
- **[D] Expressions and backgrounds** (owner, 2026-11-03, gallery addendum): display and preserve these asset kinds in the Gallery now. **Decided to build later:** expression-sprite behavior and chat-background behavior; their triggers, scope and controls remain **Open**.
- **[P]** (gallery, 2026-11-03): keep gallery membership and order in the existing revisioned CCv3 `assets` array and its media mapping; use card-asset bin entries to restore removed membership without reverting unrelated edits. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5d).

### 4.1 Card modules and character notes
- **[D]** (owner brief, 2026-11-06) “New note (a library note, not tied to a chat): from the Library and the note switcher's list end.” **Built** (newflows, ARCHITECTURE §14).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-03): A note the user creates new starts expanded, with its settings (placement, depth, role, conditions and other options) expanded too, then scrolls into view and flashes. “The user must be able to discover these options exist.” Existing and reopened notes retain their remembered state; copies made by Fork for this chat are excluded. Collapsing a newly created note or its settings is remembered normally. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).

- **[D] Wanted:**
  - something like author's notes, but **several, and per character**, placed at the tail or earlier
  - **only on that character's turn** in group chats
  - **stashable** (switched off without being lost)
  - **traveling with the character between chats, but not activated in other chats automatically**
  - example: outfits, which today must either live permanently in the card definition, be lorebook entries (which doesn't really work), or be described in chat
- **[D]** Extended prompts attached to a card, swapped per chat or situation, possibly **private** (excluded from export).
- **[P]** **Notes as a first-class object:** **Partial**: card/chat ownership, placement, activation, privacy and turn/mute conditions are built (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14, §15). Persona/global ownership is superseded by the owner’s card/chat slot rule below; “while present” by the later mute condition. Decision-model conditions remain Phase 4 work; automatic universal notes remain open below.
  - owner: a character, a chat, a persona or global
  - placement: tail/D0, depth N, or a fixed section
  - condition: always, on this character's turn, while this character is present, or decided by a decision model
  - activation per chat (as state events)
  - **[D]** a note created in a chat starts on, from the message shown (owner, 2026-10-13)
  - an exportable or private flag

- **[D]** There are no personas apart from cards: a card can be played by the user and roles can be swapped, so a "persona note" is just a card's note (owner, 2026-10-26). A note's owner is a card or a chat (global notes: open). Ownership decides where a note is found and edited, which chats it can be switched on in, and whether it travels when the card is exported (unless private) or forked (copied); a chat's note is inherited by a fork as it is at that point, then independent.
- **[D]** Every note has an on/off switch, not a number to type (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[D]** Positions (owner confirmed 2026-10-26), relative to the preset's own sections (§5.0): start of context; with the definitions (after the main prompt); at depth N in the history; start of the tail; end of the tail (default: the last thing before the newest message, or the very end when the preset puts the newest message earlier). Each note has a role. **[D]** A **card's** note placed "with the definitions" goes inside that card's own part (owner, 2026-10-28); a chat's goes after the main prompt.
- **[D]** **No hidden note kinds** (owner, 2026-10-28): notes are plain things users create and edit, and a note's behaviour is fully described by what the note shows. Nothing about a note's kind, title or origin changes what it does beyond its visible settings: the `persona_description` kind (which removed the persona's description from the persona's place, even with the note off) is gone. (The chat's author's note as a marked note, which notes at its top or bottom joined, is overtaken by the next item.)
- **[D]** **No author's note** (owner, 2026-11-01): a note has no special property, and nothing is placed relative to another note. "It doesn't even make sense at all anymore once you have multiple notes." ST's "before/after the author's note" is used **only during import**: anything ST placed there (lorebook entries, a persona, a character's own author's note) is translated into a concrete position and keeps it, "NOT keep itself relative to a moving target", which "brings ST specific concepts to this harness". The ST chat's Author's Note becomes an ordinary chat note (its depth, role and position; on only if it was on in ST). An ST chat can only have one author's note, so a chat imported from an ST branch family has one such note, whose text in each fork is the one that fork's ST file had (owner, 2026-11-01: "ST can only possibly have one"; another fork's note showing, switched off, in this one was a bug).
- **[D]** **ST personas on import** (owner, 2026-10-28): ST had the persona at the preset's persona description → the text is the persona card's description. ST had it at the author's note's top or bottom, at a depth, or nowhere → the importer writes the text into an **ordinary** note on the persona card at that place and leaves the card's description empty (one copy, nothing hidden). At the author's note's top or bottom: **overtaken** by "No author's note" above, a concrete place (earlier wording: "each imported chat places it from that chat's own author's note: at its depth and role, immediately before (top) or after (bottom) it"). New chats: nothing special; a persona-card note goes where its placement says. ST 1.19.0 still sends the persona with an empty author's note that's due, and sends none of it with frequency 0: a chat imported with frequency 0 has the persona's note switched off.
- **[D]** ST's per-character author's note is imported as a card note on that card; the chat's author's note becomes a chat note; "replace" never replaces anything (owner, 2026-10-28).

- **[D]** Any note can be named, since there is more than one, and the notes need a collapsed view (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[D]** A global "database" of named notes, usable from anywhere: when adding a note anywhere, instead of writing one you can pick an existing shared note, filterable by name, which fills that note slot; changes to a shared note are global (owner, 2026-10-26). ("Save as local note" is the unlink below.)
- **[O]** A note that applies in every chat whatever the preset: it extends a preset without modifying it and stays when the preset changes, which makes it somewhat useful, but it's close to being a preset itself and its UX is unclear (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[P]** So a note *slot* belongs to a chat or a card (the only two owners), and its content is either its own text or a link to a shared named note. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §14).

- **[D]** When a note applies: always, or only on a given character's turn. "While X is present in the chat" was dropped: cards are rarely removed and re-added (owner, 2026-10-26). **[D]** "While X isn't muted" is a condition too; both it and "on X's turn" are the user's choice, and placed sensibly (not above the cached part of the context) neither invalidates the cache (owner, 2026-10-26). **[D]** A conditioned note above the history is allowed: a user choice; the editor shows the cache warning and nothing is restricted (owner, 2026-10-28).
- **[D]** Each note has a switch for whether lorebook entries can trigger on its text (ST's "Include in World Info Scanning") (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[D]** Importing ST's notes (owner, 2026-10-26): the chat's Author's Note becomes a chat note, on only if it was on in ST (non-empty, frequency not 0); the card's Character's Note (`depth_prompt`) a card note, on in every chat as ST always applies it; the private Character Author's Note a private card note, on where ST had "Use character author's note" ticked.
- **[D]** A note slot linked to a shared note can be unlinked, which turns it into its own copy in that slot (owner, 2026-10-26).

**Implemented; obsolete as a proposal** (owner, 2026-10-26; built 2026-10-28): private/off notes and Save as shared note now join the snippet store; see [ARCHITECTURE.md §14](../ARCHITECTURE.md).

- **[D]** No insertion frequency (ST's "every Nth user message" for the Author's Note): a note that comes and goes breaks the cache and serves no real purpose (owner, 2026-10-26). On import, ST's 0 becomes off, any other value on.

- **[D] The notes UI** (owner, 2026-11-01: "the same problem the preset UI had", structurally identical blocks with every option always shown, and only one open at a time). Notes are blocks as the preset's prompts are: several can be open at once, with Expand all and Collapse all at the top; with fewer than 5 they're all open by default; each note's open state is remembered. Opening a note shows its text, not its settings: the settings are a collapsible section inside it. The name is renamed in place in the always-visible header, as elsewhere, and under it the header shows where the note goes and when it applies (lorebook trigger and "the agent may edit it" are minor, advanced settings). The settings form puts settings side by side while they fit. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 Notes, §14.6).
  - **[D]** **Section headers with the add buttons** (owner, 2026-11-02): note order isn't meaningful, so adding belongs in each section's header, not below the notes. The header row has the section's name on the left and, on the right, **Add note** and **Link shared note** as compact buttons, an icon and a short label where there's room, the icon alone with its label on hover when narrow; the same in the chat's Notes panel and a card's Notes tab; the buttons under the notes are gone. A new note scrolls fully into view and flashes. Hover texts: "Adds a new note to this chat." / "Adds a new note to <card name>.", and "Adds a shared note here. Editing it changes it everywhere it's used." **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 Notes).
    - **[P]** (notesheader, 2026-11-02) How: the labels show from 320 px of row width (`NoteSectionHeader`); the card's Notes tab's header names the card. The scroll and flash use the shared `revealAdded` (`ui/reveal.ts`: scroll only if hidden, header included; follow the editor's growth for one second and flash the box three times).
  - **[D]** **Narrow note headers** (coordinator, 2026-11-03): "Row 1: expand arrow, then the title, then the rename pencil right after the title text, then the right-aligned controls" in switch, save cluster, settings, menu order. The title takes the remaining width, with min-width 0, ellipsis and its full text on hover when truncated. Row 2 is always below the title, aligned to its left edge: the ellipsized placement/role summary with its full text on hover, then badges. Header behavior uses its own container width, not the viewport. Below 22rem the save actions are icons with their labels and existing explanations on hover; actions stay visible and widths stay stable. Applies to the card Notes tab too. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 Notes, §14.6); supersedes the earlier header arrangement below.
    - **[P]** (notesheader follow-up, 2026-11-03) `@container/note-header` on the header; the existing icon-only save cluster stays icon-only, with 24px save buttons below 22rem and 32px above. The summary has its own line outside the expansion button. `ui/useTruncated` measures CSS ellipsis to enable full-text explanations only when needed.
  - **[D]** **The rename pencil sits on the note title's line** (owner, 2026-11-02), centred with it. **Built**.
  - **[D]** **Settings open from the header** (owner, 2026-11-02): the "Settings" accordion at the bottom of an open note is replaced by the settings toggle (the sliders button, as preset prompts have) in the header, which opens the settings in place; on a closed note it opens the note and its settings together. The short summary ("as system", depth, …) stays under the title. **Built**.
    - **[P]** (notesheader, 2026-11-02) How: the settings open under the header, above the text, as a prompt's options do, with a corner X; the header's end group is the save cluster (while open), then the settings button, then ⋯, 4 px apart and centred on the title's line, so the settings button doesn't move when opening the note adds Save. The line under the title adds the role, lorebook scan and agent editing to where and when ("End of tail · always · as system").
  - **[D]** **The note header's save cluster** (owner, 2026-11-02): grouped at the right end next to ⋯, vertically centred with the title row, with even gaps. Revert is always shown in `ui/SaveControls`, disabled and greyed out while nothing changed, everywhere the cluster is used, instead of an invisible reserved slot. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[D]** **The Notes panel's top spacing is even, and Expand all / Collapse all have one alignment** (owner, 2026-11-02): they sit at the right at every width, with the page-level controls, in the Notes panel and a card's Notes tab, instead of flipping from left to right as the column widens. **Built**.
    - **[P]** (notesheader, 2026-11-02) Two buttons kept: the merged expand/collapse toggle isn't on master yet.
- **[D]** A note's settings are plain values, with no per-setting Undo, History or Revert, never JSON in the bin; no confirmation on save ("It should not take MORE EFFORT than it does in ST… Saving just happens"); its history and the diff from the previous revision are available on demand (the note's ⋯ menu), settings in words (owner, 2026-11-01). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** The note editor's "From here on, on this branch / Saving applies from the displayed message on" box is removed (owner, 2026-11-01: "totally confusing, un-actionable"). **Rejected** (owner, 2026-11-03: "What the fuck added this message? Where was it ever requested? It doesn't even fit."; removed): the coordinator's 2026-11-01 proposal, built without confirmation, was one short line under the header only when it matters: when another branch has its own version, "Other branches have their own version", with an explanation popover. (Its other case, "Changes apply from message N on" at an earlier message, went with the earlier-message edits, below.) History says where each change took effect ("Changed at message 42, this branch"). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 Notes).
- **[P]** A chat's own note keeps its unsaved changes per branch (coordinator, 2026-11-01, consistent with live text and the note's branch-tied behaviour): what's typed on one branch is that branch's live text, and a reply on another doesn't send it. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10, §14.2a).
- **[D]** A note edit is always at the chat's current state (owner, 2026-11-02, relayed by the coordinator): "When you edit a note, the edits are inherently tied to the current chat state, so later you can revert to where a note was at say, message X. That's how they work." The earlier proposal, an edit at an earlier message applying from it up to the next one with its own unsaved text there (implementation choice, 2026-11-01, [P]), is withdrawn: no user path reached it (the Notes tab always names the branch's latest message), and an editor naming an earlier one (a stale tab's) now edits at the latest. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 Notes).
- **[D]** **Unsaved note text goes with a fork** (owner, 2026-11-02): "If a user modifies a note, doesn't save it, then makes a fork, he probably decided those edits should *specifically* go into the new fork. Maybe he doesn't even *want* them to be saved in the previous one." As git carries uncommitted changes onto a new branch: the new fork gets the unsaved text, still unsaved; the branch (or card) it was made from goes back to its last revision; that field's edit history there keeps the moved text, so one pick restores it. No dialog and no extra step, for every way a fork is made. This replaces the earlier "a new fork starts without the parent's unsaved note text" (notesux, [P]). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10, Unsaved note text goes with a fork).
  - **[P]** (forknotes, 2026-11-02) The ways covered: Branch from here, a branch send or generate at an earlier message, a branch queued onto a reply being written, and a card's Fork and Fork for this chat (its notes' unsaved text goes to their copies). Opening a line no fork follows and restoring from the bin create fork rows without the user branching, so nothing moves.
  - **[D]** A card's fork ("Fork card", "Fork for this chat") takes the card's own unsaved field edits too, without asking, as its notes' (owner, 2026-11-02): the "bring unsaved changes" checkbox is gone; the original card goes back to its last revision, and each field's edit history there keeps the moved text. The names stay out of it: the fork's are the ones set in its dialog, and the original keeps its own unsaved names (forknotes, [P]). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §3.9, §3.10).
  - **[P]** (forknotes, 2026-11-02) With the per-note fork choice (§3.4): unsaved text is the latest state, so the dialog shows it on the "now" side, with "Unsaved changes" on the row. **As it is now** takes it to the new branch, still unsaved; **As it was then** leaves it on the branch it was typed on, still unsaved (nothing goes back there, since nothing moved). **Then** stays the default (owner, 2026-10-28). A note whose only change since that point is unsaved text isn't listed: its saved version is the same then and now, and its unsaved text moves as at the latest message, with no dialog. A branch made without choices (an API call) is positional: text moves only for notes unchanged since the point.
- **[D] Snippets** need a plain explanation at the top and on every control; "This chat and future forks" is confusing; Insert goes ("Have you ever heard of copy and paste?") (owner, 2026-11-01). **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-01) A snippet is for "This chat" (every branch of it, not inherited by forks) or "All chats", by a small switch replacing Make global; each has a one-click Copy; its Undo and Earlier text become the edit-history control; the list and editor are blocks as notes and prompts are (header with inline rename, text box); its text saves as every library field's does (live, revisions on Save). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.2, Snippets). **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) **Snippets are shared notes**: a note can link to a snippet and show its text, and that is the only way a snippet's text reaches a prompt (as built: a linked note slot reads the shared note, ARCHITECTURE §14.2 Snippets).
- **[D] Dismissible Snippets explanation** (owner, 2026-11-03): "Build a shared ui/ component, a dismissable info box" with a subtle border and corner X, "Hide this explanation". Dismissal is remembered per account through the existing settings RPCs, keyed by box id. Once dismissed, the same text is available from the standard "?" beside Search snippets; before dismissal, no "?" is shown. The text describes the target as "marked with a dot in its corner". **Built, obsolete as an implementation requirement** (snippetsui; ARCHITECTURE §14.2).
- **[D] Snippet target corner badge** (owner, 2026-11-03; supersedes the header marker below): "a larger accent dot (about 10–12 px) placed in the snippet box's top-left CORNER, sitting on the box's border like a badge". It is not a button, has no pointer cursor, click behaviour or focus ring, exposes its state accessibly on the card, and on hover says exactly: `This snippet is currently the default destination for "Send text to snippet" actions.` Remove the old dot position; header controls never shift when the target changes. **Built, obsolete as an implementation requirement** (snippetsui; ARCHITECTURE §14.2).
- **[D] Sending text to a snippet never navigates** (owner, 2026-11-02): "Sending must not navigate." Send text to snippet and Send selection to snippet keep the user in the source editor with focus and pending edits intact. A notice says which snippet received the text and offers **Open**; only that action (or Open in Snippets) opens it. **Built, obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.2).
- **[D] Where sent text goes** (owner, 2026-11-02; supersedes "the snippet open last"): a click with no explicit choice adds only to a snippet open on screen. The owner keeps a most-recently-used order of the snippets the user opened, clicked or typed into; the target is the most recent one that is **still expanded** in a shown Snippets panel or sheet (scrolled out of view within it still counts, and it scrolls into view when text lands). "The user clicks A, then B, then C, then closes B and C. A is still expanded, so A is the target." With a target, one click appends the whole text or the selection to its end, briefly highlighted and scrolled to, with no notice, focus staying in the source editor; the target carries a small, quiet marker on its header ("Text sent to a snippet goes here."), which never shifts the header and follows the target. With no snippet expanded, Snippets not shown, or on a phone or touch screen, the click opens a small menu at the button: the snippets, the one last picked from it on top, then **New snippet**; picking adds there without changing the side panel or navigating, then shows the notice ("Added to “<title>”", with Open). A right-click (long-press on touch) on either button always opens that menu, even with a target: a context menu on a button, not over text. Hover explanations: with a target "Adds this text to “<title>”. Right-click to choose a different snippet." (selection: "Adds the selected text to …"); without "Choose a snippet to add this text to." / "… the selected text to." Ctrl+Shift+S follows the same rule. In the editor's strip the conditional Selection to snippet button appears without moving any other button, and no other conditional strip control shifts its neighbours. **Built** (snippetpick; ARCHITECTURE §14.2).
  - **[P]** (snippetpick, 2026-11-02) How: a snippet never used but expanded still counts when none of the used ones is (so the menu opens only when none is expanded or Snippets isn't shown). Phone or touch is `(max-width: 639.98px), (pointer: coarse)`; there every tap opens the menu, so a long-press is the same (Android's long-press fires the right-click). The marker is an accent dot in a room the header always keeps, hidden on a phone. The menu's order is kept per user in this browser. Any snippet editor surface registers itself while shown (`useSnippetEditorSurface`), so a later large editor in the main column counts with no change to the rule. The edit-history control keeps its room in a box's strip before there is any history, and the selection's actions come first in the end-aligned strip, so nothing moves when either appears. The strip's buttons are exempt from the layout audit's 8 px inset against the box's own border (they sit in the slim strip by design); left and right keep 8 px.
- **[D] Snippets and notes have a large editor over the chat** (owner, 2026-11-02): "Most editors have a large main-column editor; Snippets and Notes don't, at least not from a chat." The use case: other text is open in the side panel (a card's definitions, say) and a snippet or note is open in the centre column; the user switches characters in the side panel while the same snippet or note stays open for editing, and copies and pastes, or sends text to the snippet, from the side panel into it. A **Large editor** action on a snippet (Snippets panel and floating sheet) and on a note (the chat's notes, card notes, shared notes) opens it in the main column like the other large editors; it stays open while the side panel changes tab or character, has a close X, and edits through the same store as the side panel's copy (same live text, "edited" marker and save cluster). The side-panel editors keep full parity: they are the mobile editors; the large editor only adds the wide-screen layout. Its place restores on reload and Back/Forward. **Built, obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
  - **[P]** (notesnippetlarge, 2026-11-02) How: it is the chat workspace's overlay (`editor`, `focus=note` or `focus=snippet` in the URL), so it behaves as the preset, card and lorebook large editors do, including closing the panel sheet on a phone. A note's large view is its Notes-tab block, always open and filling the column (switch, rename, save cluster, ⋯, text, settings), rather than the library's note page, which lacks the chat's switch, card-named conditions and unlinking. A note's Large editor is in its ⋯ menu; a snippet's is an icon beside Copy. A shared note shown by a slot opens large as that slot; the shared note itself is a snippet and opens large from Snippets. Outside a chat, the floating sheet's Large editor opens the snippet on its library page in the same view.

#### 4.1a State the agent changes during a chat
- **[D]** Cards often hold things that change over a conversation, such as costumes. Today they stay at the top of the context, and the model works out the latest state by reading the chat. That usually works, because the chat is in causal order (owner, 2026-10-11).
- **[D]** In agentic RP, the agent should be able to change the card and the notes **for that chat**. **Mechanism superseded** (owner, 2026-11-03): tools that “change a card” create notes replacing prompts (§4.2), rather than timeline card edits.
- **[D]** These changes belong to the message and its history. They're tied to a particular swipe. Swiping resets them, and the next swipe may bring its own changes instead.
- **[D]** The user decides which items the LLM may and may not change.
- **[D]** Changing a card freely invalidates the cache on the next turn. Putting cards at the end of the context (§5.2) avoids that, but it's generally not wanted: cards are usually written as a scenario or premise for the start of the prompt. So in practice some card fields go later in the context, as ST's Character's Note does with its depth.
- **[P]** The mechanism, as deltas on what exists: **Partial foundation**: versioned state, note/card `agent_editable` data and ST depth-note mapping exist (ARCHITECTURE §3.8, §8, §14); autonomous tools and permission controls remain Phase 4 proposals (AGENTIC §4.3, §4.5), with the field-patch mechanism superseded by §4.2 (owner, 2026-11-03).
  - **Agent changes are chat-state effects** (§7.3), attached to the version that made them (§3.4). Swiping and branching then reset them with no extra machinery.
  - **Superseded** (owner, 2026-11-03; §4.2): the proposed `card.field.set` field patches on top of the bound card revision. Earlier rationale: avoid pinning a whole revision per swipe and keep upstream fixes arriving. The replacement is a note tied to the edited field, with the note's existing chat-state semantics.
  - **State that changes over the story belongs in notes or card modules, not the description.** A costume is a card-owned note at depth, not an edit to the top of the prompt. Card modules get the same placement options notes have (§5.2), so a card can carry several of them at depth.
  - **An "agent may edit" flag on each note, card module and card field.** It travels with the card, and a chat can override it with a state event. It's off by default. Making an item editable that's placed near the top of the context shows a warning, since each edit there rewrites the cached prefix (§5.5).
  - **ST's Character's Note** is one `depth_prompt` per card (`data.extensions.depth_prompt`: prompt, depth, role). The importer maps it to a card-owned note at that depth and role. Export writes one note there, and the others go in `extensions.rp_harness`, which ST ignores but which round-trips.

---

### 4.2 Notes that replace prompts

**Status:** Built 2026-11-05 (job noteoverrides): the full UI spec, cache display and assembly are in spec/NOTE-OVERRIDES.md, the implementation in ARCHITECTURE §12 and §15. **[D]** entries below are the owner's decisions of 2026-11-03 and 2026-11-05; **[P]** entries are proposals for how to realize them, not owner confirmations. Existing note progression, branching, revisions and per-chat activation (§3.4, §4.1, §4.1a) provide the starting point.

- **[D] DROPPED: timeline card edits** (owner, 2026-11-03), superseding the 2026-10-16 entry in §4. It “was too hard to work into a usable UX since it touches all these card surfaces and would have very non-obvious and potentially confusing semantics”. Editing cards breaks caching unless rare or appended at the end. The unresolved object semantics were: “What IS the card at chat state x, as an object?” Cards have many prompts that might need different semantics; “What if you modify the original card, what happens then?”
- **[D] The replacement:** “cards and presets are just bundles of prompts inserted in different parts of the conversation.” A note can replace (override) a specific preset prompt or a specific character card field wherever the note applies. This supersedes the 2026-11-02 **[O]** about overriding a preset field (§1.3). It covers almost every use of chat-specific presets without merging updated presets with chat changes. It cannot move structural things such as where chat history sits. Notes already have progression and branching semantics and dedicated UI, are single prompts, and are per chat by default.
- **[D] Placement:** by default the note's text goes where the replaced prompt would have gone. Optionally the note also overrides the position: a separate switch, with the position controls greyed until switched on, then prefilled with the replaced prompt's placement where that applies (a card's Description has no placement of its own). Even with its own position, this is still replacement: “prompt X for character Y is disabled” then lives in an object whose state is tracked in the chat.
- **[D] Visible from both ends:** a replaced card field or preset prompt must show that it is replaced and link to the note; the note links to what it replaces. Only one note can replace a given prompt. Trying to link a second shows an error offering to unlink the other.
- **[D] Starting from the current text:** getting the field's current text into the note must be easy, without always-visible affordances for this specific case.
  - **[P]** The field's existing menu gets **Replace with a note in this chat**, which creates a linked note prefilled with the current text and opens it. The note's ⋯ menu gets **Copy its current text** for the prompt it replaces. **Built** with one change: card fields have no menu, so **Replace with a note in this chat** is in the field box's own strip (shown on hover and focus) of card fields and preset prompts alike (spec/NOTE-OVERRIDES.md §2.2).
- **[D] On and off:** switching the note off brings the original prompt back. An empty note that is on means the prompt is switched off.
- **[D] Every kind of note can replace prompts**, including character (card) notes. A card note replacing preset prompts generalizes what cards already do for Main and post-history (§5.0).
  - **[P]** A preset prompt's **forbid overrides** applies to card notes as it does to card prompt overrides, and not to chat or branch notes, which are the user's own.
- **[D] Fork for this chat:** notes bound to the original card's fields must automatically apply to the new card fork.
  - **[P]** A card-field target is the character's place in the chat (“that character's Description in this chat”), resolved to whichever card fills that place on the viewed branch. Branches using the fork get the note on the fork; earlier branches keeping the original still get it on the original. No rebinding step.
- **[D] Preset updates:** targets must carry over when a preset is updated, including when the update is imported as a new preset.
  - **[P]** Target the prompt's identifier (ST's prompt `identifier`, kept across versions of a preset; built-in slots such as `main` have fixed ones), resolved in whichever preset is selected. If that preset has no prompt with the identifier, the note says **Its prompt isn't in the selected preset** and offers a picker to relink it. With its own position on, it is still inserted there.
- **[P] Original text:** the note's text may use `{{original}}` for the replaced prompt's text, as card prompt overrides do.
- **Overtaken [P] Replaced fields in the editors:** in a chat where a card field is replaced, show the card's own text with a line above, **Replaced in this chat by the note \<name>** (a link), and a distinct border. Lock the field in that chat, with an explicit **Edit the card's text** unlock for changing it everywhere. The preset editor does the same for replaced prompts. The owner's alternatives were: show the note's text in the field, with edits going to the note; tabs (**as-is / as replaced**); or make it not editable from that chat.
- **[D] The replaced field is changed itself** (owner, 2026-11-05, choosing between (a) the field shows the note's text, edits going to the note, and (b) tabs as-is / as replaced): “If we do this, the field itself has to be visually changed somehow. A line above it won't do it. I am thinking a glowing blue border, maybe, but it also can't be obnoxious and too eye-catching. Just obvious enough it cannot be ignored when editing the field.”
- **[D] Both, with tabs** (owner, 2026-11-05): “this isn't exclusive with the tabs. I simply don't see why we wouldn't have both. It's just about which tab is shown first and how to style this.”
  - **[P]** (coordinator, told to the owner): while a note replaces the field in the chat being viewed, the **As replaced** tab opens first, since that's what is sent; its field carries the subtle blue glow border and edits go to the note, with the note named and linked; the **Original** tab shows the card's or preset's own text, editable as usual. **Built** (spec/NOTE-OVERRIDES.md §2.1): the tabs at the end of the field's label row, the note named and linked under the box.
- **[D] Agents, Phase 4:** tools, built-in or from plugins, that “change a card” in a general way create these notes behind the scenes. They copy the modified text into a note tied to the edited field, set to go at the end of the context, optionally wrapped in a prompt that gives context. The cache is invalidated once per newly modified field, and everything else stays cached at the top. The change can be surgical: in the owner's costume example, a changed paragraph in Description becomes one note replacing Description without the costume, plus one note at the end of the context with the edited costume, wrapped with context. These remain note effects tied to the producing message version (§4.1a, §7.3). Phase 4 design: the `edit_card` tool over a `NoteTargets` interface the note-overrides work implements (AGENTIC §4.6, §8).
- **[P] Designed and built (2026-11-05):** the full UI spec for each surface (card editor fields, preset editor prompts, the note editor's **Replaces** control and position switch, the one-per-target error), the cache-breakpoint display and the assembler changes are in spec/NOTE-OVERRIDES.md (obsolete as a requirement; ARCHITECTURE §12, §15). Assembly is the registered `replacements` stage; the UI is the registered `note-overrides` feature (field view, note settings section, note menu item, chat provider).

---

## 5. Context assembly

### 5.0 Layout belongs to the preset (owner, 2026-10-26)
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-03, placeholdersource): placeholder source text is italic and muted; “drop ‘Filled from’” and state exactly where content comes from as a short noun phrase. Settings-dependent sources belong in the explanation. **[D]** (owner follow-up, 2026-11-03): “next to each placeholder's italic source line, add the app's standard ‘?’ help button”; its 2–3 short sentences explain insertion on every request, that the text is not editable in the preset, where to edit it, and settings-dependent details, replacing the separate hover on the line. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement:** ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D]** The context layout is the preset's, not a fixed one: presets are made by users, mostly for ST's layout, and their tail can be far longer than reference-harness's (whose single fixed layout is not a model to copy).
- **[D]** Everything after the chat history is the **tail prompt** (the satellite system prompt, for its system parts). It sits at the end so the model attends to it most and the history can't causally override its instructions (style, rules), and it can change every turn without breaking the cache. Presets put most of their weight there; so does the Author's Note.
- **[D]** Where the newest message goes is the preset's to control. Since a long tail can separate the newest message from the history, neither ST's placement (newest message at the end of the history, the tail after it) nor reference-harness's (tail, then the newest message last) is right for every preset. **[P]** A "Newest message" marker in the preset's prompt order, next to "Chat history": imported ST presets get it right after the history (ST's behaviour); a preset can move it after the tail. **Built [P] marker** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[D]** Every section of a preset can be system, user or assistant, as in ST's preset editor, which the app needs too.
- **[D]** Later, not now: modes where the card definitions move into the tail and are swapped every turn for the character speaking. The layout must allow it. **Superseded in part** (owner, 2026-11-06, §7.1): moving definitions to the tail is the preset's to do; the mode only rotates the speaker's card into the preset's positions.

- **[D]** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27) **Card definitions are filled the way the preset says**, globally, never per chat: ST's per-field markers and macros (`{{description}}`, Char Description, Char Personality, Scenario…) are filled field by field, each character's part wrapped in `<{{char}}>` … `</{{char}}>` by default (the owner's ST setting; ST's join prefix/suffix); the whole-card "Definitions" marker puts each card in whole, in a labelled block (§5.2). **[D]** There is never only one character (`{{user}}` is a cast card), so every cast card's definitions, the persona's included, are always wrapped as `<Name>…</Name>`, at the per-field markers and at the Definitions marker alike (owner, 2026-10-28). The build's hidden switch to ST-compatible assembly for one-character chats is removed.
- **[D]** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27) **A card's system prompt and post-history instructions** replace the preset's Main Prompt and post-history prompt, as in ST, with `{{original}}` for the replaced text, unless that prompt **forbids overrides**. These card prompts are usually an awkward attempt at a jailbreak, though not always; they must stay usable and not counterintuitive. **Built U11** (ARCHITECTURE §14): New prompt preset creates the ordered prompt document in the existing editor. **Forbidding overrides is on by default** for a new preset and for any new prompt it can apply to. Visible from both sides: the preset editor shows "replaced in this chat by Aria's system prompt" at that prompt, the card editor shows whether its prompt is used. On an impersonation turn the speaking card's prompts apply too; impersonating a persona without them, none (owner, 2026-10-28).
- **[D]** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27) **Card modules are card notes**: the 2026-10-09 "extended prompts attached to a card, swapped per chat or situation, possibly private" are what card notes now do (position, per-chat switch, conditions, private flag). One concept.
- **[D]** (owner confirmed 2026-10-27) **Choosing lorebooks**: a Lorebooks tab in the right panel, per chat (fork), listing each character's embedded book (on by default), books added from the library, and the global books, each with its switch; the "only below the history" setting (§1.3) sits there. Global books are **superseded** by the preset's books (owner, 2026-11-01, §1.3).

### 5.1 Pipeline [P]

**Built as registered, replaceable stages; obsolete as an implementation plan** (ARCHITECTURE §15). The sequence below is the original outline: ordinary RP now resolves independent cards and global preset selection (§4, §6.1), uses the implemented LCM layer (§8), and places direction in the end-of-request instruction (§2.2). Rich/compact display forms remain deferred (§10.2).
1. Take the path (§3.3).
2. Replay state events along it (§3.4).
3. Resolve participants, and cards through their layers (§4).
4. Resolve the preset through its scope chain and overlays (§6).
5. Expand macros according to where each piece of text came from (§5.6).
6. Project LCM: summary layer, compact tier, rich tier (§8).
7. Project roles (§5.4).
8. Add tail content: notes, the speaker directive, one-turn direction.
9. Place cache breakpoints.
10. Hand off to the provider adapter (pi-ai).

### 5.2 Card layout
- **[D]** ST concatenates cards *by field* and has no default boundaries between cards. Here:
  - **default:** all cards concatenated one after another, each in a clearly labelled block
  - **card-swap mode:** the active card's definition goes at the **tail**, which helps both caching and instruction-following. **Superseded in part** (owner, 2026-11-06, §7.1): card-swap is rotation only; the speaker's card fills the preset's definition positions and nothing moves to the tail, which is a preset edit (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §4).
- **Built** (cardswap, 2026-11-06): card-swap as rotation only, the owner's §7.1 [D]. As CHARACTER-CONTEXT §1 has it, card-swap is a card-layout setting (choice 3), with or without tools: the `cards.rotation { on }` state key and a "Card swap" switch in This chat; the `card-swap` mode keeps working by setting that layout for its replies until the modes leave the UI (ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts").
  - **[P]** As ST's swap mode (`getGroupCharacterCardsLazy` returns nothing in it, so `getCharacterCardFieldsLazy` reads the speaker's own fields; `getGroupDepthPrompts` returns none, so only the speaker's depth prompt is injected): only the speaker's description, personality, scenario and example messages, and only its own notes (its Character's Note among them). Its system prompt and post-history instructions are the speaker's, as in every group generation. The `<Name>` wrapper stays, as for a one-character chat here (§5.0). The inspector lists each left-out card under "Not sent this turn", sent only on its character's turn.
  - the user picks the layout, per RP and per model, and it must be easy to change
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: The owner’s §5.0 rule fills field markers field by field; Definitions is the separate whole-card marker. Both are built (ARCHITECTURE §15). Earlier wording: “A preset's `charDescription` and similar markers become the slot where the combined block goes.”
- **[P]** Placement is per piece, not only per card. The card's fixed definition stays where the layout puts it. Card modules and card-owned notes each have their own placement (top section, depth N or tail), so the parts that change during a chat can sit late in the context (§4.1a). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §15). Arbitrary agent-editable card fields remain Phase 4 work.

### 5.3 `{{user}}`, `{{char}}` and the speaker
- **[D]** In ST these rebind every turn (to the current speaker and the current persona). That breaks the cache and fights instructions like "don't write for `{{user}}`". Users need to control and pin these bindings, and to edit presets and cards to stop relying on them, which requires search and replace. **Binding controls built** (U09, 2026-10-28; ARCHITECTURE.md §15); cross-entry search and replace remains separate.
- **[D]** In preset text, `{{char}}` is every character in the chat except your persona, comma-joined, so the start of the request is the same whoever speaks; a card's own text uses that card's name, and the chat can pin other text in its place (owner, 2026-10-29). In a one-on-one chat it's the one name, as in ST; in a group ST uses the current speaker, which rebuilds the cache on most turns, and the ST fidelity gate lists this as a documented difference. `{{user}}` is in §2.2. The speaker for this turn is named by the end-of-request instruction (§2.2), which realises the earlier proposed "tail directive".

### 5.4 Role projection [P]

**Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal**: all three are registered strategies, with open persisted IDs (ARCHITECTURE §15).
These are strategies for mapping history to user and assistant roles:
- **by writer:** human → user, model → assistant (ST's behavior)
