- **strict alternation by position:** the owner's idea. It's stable as the chat grows (cache-friendly) and avoids long same-role runs that degrade models.
- **by perspective:** the speaker's own past turns become assistant, everyone else's user. It's natural for per-character agents.

### 5.5 Caching

Stepped history, dropped-message display and rewrite warnings without a TTL are implemented in ARCHITECTURE §12 and §15 (2026-10-13). LCM now supplies its summary cursor alongside stepped recent-history cuts (ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[D]** Preserving the KV cache is a near-top priority, second only to avoiding RCEs.
- **[D]** Depth injections are cache-friendly when breakpoints are placed correctly. A depth-0 author's note is the ideal place for information that changes, like reference-harness's tail prompt. The real cache-killers are changes near the top of the prompt, which the assembler should flag **[P]**. **Built** rewrite estimate (ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[P]** Breakpoints follow reference-harness's scheme (ARCHITECTURE.md "Cache control"): system/preset, summary layer, last verbatim timeline item. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6, §15).
- **[D]** **Cache-warmth indicator** near the composer, with an optional countdown (can be turned off).
  - The TTL comes from known provider defaults, or from a TTL the user sets on any model or connection. No TTL means no indicator.
  - **[P]** States: warm (verified from reported cache usage), warm (assumed), cold. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
  - **[P]** It tracks the prefix of the *next* generation and warns when an edit changed it ("next send rewrites ~N tokens"). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §15).
  - **[D]** That warning shows even when no TTL is known: a changed prefix is cold whatever the TTL (owner, 2026-10-13).
  - **[D]** Known provider defaults cover every provider that documents a cache lifetime, not just Anthropic (owner, 2026-10-13).
- **[D]** **History that doesn't fit is cut in steps, not one message at a time** (owner, 2026-10-13). ST drops just enough old messages to fit the maximum context, so once a chat reaches it, every new message pushes the oldest one out and the cached prefix changes on every send. Instead there are two thresholds, as in reference-harness: a **maximum** and a **target** context size. When the maximum is reached, enough old messages leave the context to get down to the target, and the start of the history stays put until the maximum is reached again, so the cache survives the sends in between.
  - **[D]** The target defaults to a fixed distance below the maximum, so a preset imported from ST, which has only a maximum, needs no translation (owner, 2026-10-13).
  - **[D]** Where the history starts is therefore state, not a formula of the messages and the budget (owner, 2026-10-13).
  - **[P]** Default target: 80% of the maximum. The start point is recorded with each generation's context and read back by the next one on the same path, so a rebuild reproduces it. It applies in every layout, ST-compatible mode included, where it's a documented difference from ST. LCM's summary cursor later takes this role (LCM.md). **Built** 80% non-LCM fallback and pinned history start (ARCHITECTURE §15); LCM now uses the owner’s explicit recent/summary pairs (§8; ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[D]** **Messages that dropped out of the context are shown as such** in the chat (owner, 2026-10-13): faded, or with another background or border shade (whichever fits the final design), and an icon. They must look different from messages the user hid on purpose, which ST marks with a ghost icon. It isn't shown as a note or issue on each reply.
- **[D]** Where the cached part ends is shown in the user's terms, not as "Cached part ends · prefix/pinned/history" (owner, 2026-11-02: "Cached part starts/cached part ends ??? This is confusing and it's not clear what it means."). The preset editor's and the inspector's lines say what's reused between replies above them and what an edit there costs (§6.2a). **Built** (promptmarkers; ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **[D]** **Parallel requests with a cold cache can cause N cache writes.** Stagger them deliberately per provider: start one, and release the others once its cache write is readable. When that happens differs by provider, so it must be tested and measured (cache-read token counts on the follow-ups) **[P]**. **Built** provider-specific staggering and measurement tooling (ARCHITECTURE §6, §15); no new paid provider measurement is claimed by this audit.

- **[D]** Remove the pinned-lore cache marker (owner’s 2026-11-02 cachestudy brief: "The owner decided to remove it"). **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (2026-11-03; ARCHITECTURE §15). Lore activation and prefix separation are unchanged.
- **[P]** Provider cache profiles belong in model/provider capabilities, selected by a connection/provider preset such as **Anthropic (AWS)**, rather than inferred from API compatibility or a model's vendor name. Profiles should specify matching/lookback, total marker budget including SDK markers, per-model cumulative minimum, TTL choices, cache prices, wire marker encoding, and raw usage normalization. A direct provider and a gateway may serve the same model with different contracts. The optional `cacheLayout` matching/encoding/budget flags and checkpoint placement are **built, obsolete as implementation proposals** (2026-11-03; ARCHITECTURE §6, §15). The selection is now the connection's provider (§9, providerchoice, **built**): its cache layout, lookback, marker budget, minimum and documented cache contract are the connection's defaults. Cache prices and usage normalization stay per kind and model.
- **[P]** Default direct Anthropic to lookback; conservatively choose exact-position repeats for AWS routes until the specific model/endpoint is verified. The reference explicitly measured exact-only Chat Completions, but does not establish no-lookback for all Anthropic or Responses AWS routes. Preserve the existing `explicit` gate, expose uncertain provenance, and never silently enable provider-specific wire extensions on an automatic-cache model.
- **[P]** Keep the current history checkpoint and repeat its previous identical prefix on exact-only routes; use the older-summary-tier split as a fallback when space permits. Preserve SDK final/system checkpoints, count tools against the budget, and remove the pinned-lore slot. Digest-based repeats and the optional tier split are **built, obsolete as implementation proposals** (2026-11-03; ARCHITECTURE §15). The measured synthetic tradeoffs and proposed discriminating probes are in the cachestudy report; named provider profiles still require owner confirmation.
- **[P]** Warn in the preset editor when an evaluated random/clock macro or other per-turn source lies before the reusable history, and quantify the downstream input that loses reuse. Assembly's volatility detection and issue are **built, obsolete as implementation proposals** (2026-11-03; ARCHITECTURE §15); editor-specific placement and wording remain proposed.

### 5.6 Macros

- **[D] Decided (owner, 2026-11-02, macrohighlight):** “Highlight using the real parser”, with comments dimmed, implemented value macros in one color and implemented directives in another, classified through the registry; unknown macros stay unstyled. Applies to every shared prompt-text field editor, including presets, cards, notes, lorebooks and snippets. “No hover popups or autocomplete: highlighting only.” Keep 5,000-line texts fast. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement:** ARCHITECTURE §10 and §14.6.
- **[P]** (macrohighlight, 2026-11-02, implementation choice): reuse the engine's scoped pairing, cache completed parser nodes before edits, and decorate only visible CodeMirror ranges; share the display macro registry. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal:** ARCHITECTURE §10.

**Built 2026-10-28 (U10; obsolete as requirements):** verbatim defaults for human/model text and opt-in output substitution are implemented in [ARCHITECTURE.md §10](../ARCHITECTURE.md#10-macros), alongside the bounded, approved display-only preset regex pipeline. Other proposals below retain their status.
- **[D]** In ST, `{{user}}` in greetings gets fixed when a card is opened, so changing persona before the first message keeps the old name. Substitution must stay dynamic until a message is sent.
- **[P]** Card-sourced text keeps its macros unresolved in storage. They're resolved when rendered or assembled, against the state at that message's position. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §10, §15).
- **[P]** Non-deterministic macros (`{{random}}`, `{{roll}}`, `{{time}}`) are evaluated once and frozen, for determinism and caching. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §10, §15).
- **[P]** A per-message toggle can switch expansion on. **Open/unbuilt**: the built output-expansion option is account-wide (ARCHITECTURE §10); Edit/Write has no per-message macro-mode control.
- **[P]** Expansion therefore depends on where text came from, and model text stays verbatim when it's sent back in later turns. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §10, §15).

---

## 6. Presets: scope and overlays

### 6.1 Scope
- **[D]** A preset isn't global, but it isn't obvious what it belongs to. **Overtaken** for ordinary RP by the owner’s global-default decision below (2026-10-27).
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: Ordinary RP uses the owner’s global model/preset defaults below; per-chat preset changes were rejected. Workshop/per-agent configuration remains Phase 6 work, and an explicit chat lock remains open. Earlier wording: “Inheritance chain: app default → default for the chat kind (RP or workshop) → chat → per-role override (narrator, character agent, summarizer) → card overlays and per-turn conditions.”
- **[P]** **Overtaken**: The built composer bar selects account-global defaults and shows their source (ARCHITECTURE §12, §15); ordinary preset selection is not a positional state event (§6.1 owner decisions). Earlier wording: “The composer strip shows the active preset and where it comes from. Changing it uses the same scope prompt as card edits. A preset switch is a state event.”

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-10-27) **A preset is global by default**: chats and forks don't own one. Which preset is right usually depends on the model.
- **[D]** **Presets and models (connections) are separate by default**: switching either is easy and doesn't touch the other. ST links them (switching model switches to whatever preset was last used or saved with it), which is extremely confusing: having switched or edited your preset, moving the same work from OpenRouter to the OpenAI API changes the preset without you noticing.
- **[D]** A connection can optionally **lock a preset**, one way: choosing that connection sets that preset, and the lock is visible in the connection's settings.
- **[D] Rejected:** per-chat changes to a preset (a chat's own prompt switches over the preset): too confusing to make sense. **Superseded for individual prompt customization** by notes replacing prompts (§4.2; owner, 2026-11-03); structural layout remains the preset's.
- **[O]** Locking a preset to a chat: potentially useful, never the default. The future agent chat (not agentic RP) will need a separate preset that works differently.
- **[D]** Models, presets and connections are fundamentally global (owner, 2026-10-27). Locking one to a chat is a specific, rare choice (the owner never made it in nearly four years of ST), never the default. The build's per-chat model and preset binding (`model.bind`/`preset.bind`, the This chat tab's "where each comes from") was never asked for: drift to remove, keeping at most an explicit lock.
- **[D]** The current preset and model are switched from the composer bar, one click each, their settings opening in the right panel; space in the bar is a concern with everything else there (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-01) **The side panel's Prompt preset tab switches the preset too**: a preset dropdown right at its top, the current preset selected, with the same search/select UI as the switcher above the composer. Both show under each preset, in un-emphasized text, when it was last modified, and sort by it (still filtered by the text). **Presets can be archived**: they often become outdated, but there's no reason to delete them for good. An archive icon button on each preset in these lists archives it. The views are Name (A–Z), Last modified, Last used and Archived; Archived, the last, shows only archived presets, with un-archive buttons where archive would be, always sorted by most recently archived. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5, §12, §15).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-01, relayed by the coordinator) **The Model tab has the composer's model selector at its top** in the same way, and the model selector gets the same improvements where they apply; **New preset** is in the Prompt preset tab's header; and the model selector links to where connections are made. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12, §15).
- **[P]** The details of both (presetpicker, 2026-11-01, not yet confirmed): the model list sorts by Name or Last used and has no archive (hiding a model already exists in its connection's list); New preset starts as a copy of the built-in default named "New preset", is chosen for the next reply and opens in the preset editor, and is also at the end of the preset list (the library's New prompt preset, which already existed, uses the same dialog); "Manage connections…" ends the model list ("Add a connection…" with none) and opens Settings → Connections in the same tab; the chosen sort is remembered per account on the server, Archived never (the list reopens on the last sort); the preset in use stays listed though archived, at the top, marked Archived.

- **[D]** Model settings move out of the preset into the model/connection settings: samplers and much more (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[P]** The split: **the model's settings** are what depends on the model or provider: samplers (temperature, top-p/k, min-p, penalties, seed, logit bias), context window and response length, reasoning or thinking effort, stop strings, streaming, caching options, provider routing, how roles go on the wire (system role support, squashing, names), the image limit, and whether prefill is supported. **The prompt preset** keeps what is written: its prompts, their order, roles and positions, the "Newest message" marker, the prompts used only in certain moments (impersonation, group nudge, continue), the prefill text, and its regex scripts. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6, §9, §14).
- **[D]** What ST calls a "preset" bundles three things the app keeps apart: the **prompt preset**, the **model (LLM) settings**, and **connection settings** with the model name (owner, 2026-10-27). An ST preset JSON (shared and imported by hand) can contain all three, so there are three import surfaces, each taking the parts it applies to.
- **[D]** The ST folder import takes the prompt presets (every chat-completion preset file; ST's selected one becomes the default) and **neither model settings nor connections** (owner, 2026-10-27). Keys are never read (`secrets.json`). Model settings can still be taken from one preset file by hand through the model-settings import surface.

### 6.2 Saving (ST's gripe #1)

- **[D] (owner, 2026-11-04):** “one shared Search and replace component, used by every surface”; the full-screen card editor must expand “inline” and the side-panel editor gets the same feature (“side-panel editors have full parity with the large editors”). “Search and replace must highlight matches in the actual text boxes on every surface … not only in the results list.” **Built; obsolete as implementation requirements** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P] (searchreplace, 2026-11-04):** Reuse the registered editors' search fields, worker and inline panel, with a shared pressed trigger. Publish preview ranges per editor store; CodeMirror decorations and native-field mirrors highlight matches without changing focus or selection. Clear stale ranges when the query or document changes. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).

- **[D]** In ST, some fields autosave, some have save buttons, and preset field edits lived in limbo: they survived restarts but vanished on a preset switch.
- **[D] Principle:** nothing is ever lost, and it's always clear which state you're in. (The earlier "autosave everything, no commits" proposal was rejected because it doesn't work for creating versions.)
- **[D]** That means **nothing is lost by accident**: edits are non-destructive, and input boxes can be trusted with your life, so nobody keeps a notepad on the side. It doesn't mean nothing can be deleted; deliberate deletion is allowed (owner, 2026-10-10). **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02): "Deleted unsent text never comes back on another device." Clearing or deleting leaves a server record so an older browser copy cannot put it back. **[P]** (fieldfixes, 2026-11-02) How: retain the last deletion's server row version and compare it with the browser copy's original base and any separately observed deletion, including on the writer; this avoids comparing device and server clocks. A copy from before an unobserved deletion stays available for explicit recovery rather than reopening automatically. Pending composers keep a synchronous, draft-addressed copy until acknowledged. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §§3.10, 12).
- **[D] Recycle bin** (owner, 2026-10-10). Deletes go to a global per-user bin and disappear from where they were; they can be restored from there or deleted permanently. This makes deleting fundamentally non-destructive.
  - **[D] Bin copy** (owner correction, 2026-11-02): “Delete explanations never mention Undo. Undo is for accidents; nobody plans to press it.” Use only “Moves this <thing> to the bin, from which it can be restored later.” Toasts say “<Thing> moved to the bin.”; the Undo action stays. Cancel/discard explanations stay short and name a bin section only when needed to find the text among several sections. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.11).
  - **Every delete shows a toast with a button that opens the bin,** so nobody has to search for it. Because of that, **deleting needs no confirmation anywhere except inside the bin**, which keeps deleting many things fast.
  - **The bin has two independent sections**, each with its own capacity, oldest evicted first. Overflow in one never evicts items from the other.
    - **Deleted items:** discrete things with an identity (chats, messages, swipes, branches, cards and other library entities), produced only by an explicit delete.
    - **Recovered text:** text from before a destructive change in a drafting session, saved when the draft is committed, and discarded drafts. It's produced passively during normal editing, so it **never shows a toast**.
  - **[D]** Deleting a single swipe (version), not the whole message, is the normal case, as in ST, and the most readily available delete in the UI. Deleting the whole message is the secondary action (owner, 2026-10-10). Deleting a message's only remaining version deletes the message.
  - **[D]** Messages, swipes and branches are restored in place: deleting marks them, so restoring puts them back exactly where they were, with their versions. The bin entry also records where an item came from and a preview, so its text can be copied out instead.
  - **[D]** Permanently deleting a chat or an entity removes it. Permanently deleting a message or branch erases its content but keeps an empty placeholder, so the conversation's structure and any summaries over it stay valid.
  - **[D]** Default capacities: 10,000 deleted items or 2 GB; 2,000 recovered texts or 200 MB. Large enough that most users never reach them; configurable. **Built U13:** account capacities and UI; ARCHITECTURE §3.11. (These three confirmed by the owner, 2026-10-10.)
  - **[P]** Eviction never deletes the item a delete just made. If deleting everything else that can go still wouldn't bring the section within its limits (one item bigger than the whole section, or old items that fail to delete), nothing is evicted: the delete goes through and the bin stays over capacity rather than one delete emptying it. Later deletes or emptying the bin bring it back within its limits. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.11).
- **[D]** **The composer belongs to a branch** (owner, 2026-10-10):
  - Each chat, and each branch within it, has its own composer.
  - A half-typed message waits where it was left, even months later, while other chats and branches show their own.
  - Starting a branch copies the current composer into it.
  - Switching between existing branches doesn't carry text along: moving text to another branch or chat is copy and paste, which is simpler than a composer that follows you around.
- **[P]** Anything appended at a branch's tip, such as a model reply, carries that branch's composer forward to the new tip, so text typed meanwhile stays in view. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.10, §12). Composers now belong to stable forks; append advances the tip without moving the draft.
- **[D]** Drafts and field-level undo history are **persisted on the server** for every text field. That protects against Ctrl-A plus a stray key and survives refreshes, closing the tab and switching devices. **Built for U02/U05**: both summarization instructions, non-secret connection/model forms, pending-chat prose and the short creation/rename forms; implementation and remaining live-subscription limit: ARCHITECTURE §3.10. The broader requirement still applies to other text surfaces.
- **[D]** The undo history only lives while a draft is being written. A commit ends it: the committed text is preserved in a version or revision, and history tables must not grow indefinitely (owner, 2026-10-10). **Superseded for library editors** (cards, presets, notes, lorebooks) by the edit history below (owner, 2026-11-01), and for message and summary edits (owner, 2026-11-02); it still holds for composers.
- **[D]** A bad save can lose hours of typing in any field (a card, a preset), whatever happened to the previous version. So when a draft is committed, the text from before **each** destructive change in that session goes to the bin's recovered-text section, and a discarded draft goes there too (owner, 2026-10-10). **Superseded for library editors** (owner, 2026-11-01) and for message and summary edits (owner, 2026-11-02): their edit history outlives revisions and saves, so that text stays a state of the edit history; it reaches the bin only when the retention below drops it. Cancelling a message's or summary's edit puts the saved text back as a new state, the cancelled text one step back, so nothing goes to the bin then either.
- **[D] Live text is what's sent** (owner, 2026-11-01). Anything that becomes prompt text (notes, cards, presets, lorebooks) applies as it's typed: a reply uses the current text, never a "last saved revision" behind it, as in ST, where prompt-manager edits, card fields and the Author's Note apply at once. ST's preset file is written only by "Update current preset", and edits are silently lost on a preset switch, "the nastiest UX issue in ST": here nothing is ever discarded by switching presets or chats. Before a reply is assembled, the client's pending typing is flushed, so what you see is what's sent. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.2a).
- **[D] Undo** (owner, 2026-11-01): the text box's own undo (Ctrl+Z) is what's normally used. No Redo of our own. The server-kept edit history exists for recovery (refreshes, other devices, accidents), not for normal undo. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] Edit history** (owner, 2026-11-01) is an explicit control on every text box that has it, clearly separate from revision History: it opens a list of the field's earlier states (from any device or session), browsable with their text; picking one puts it into the box as a **new** latest state (append-only: no travelling back, no branches, no redo). Retention: the last 200 states per field or 30 days, whichever keeps more; checkpoints never delete edit states. **Built** for library fields (ARCHITECTURE §3.10, §14.6), and for message and summary edits (owner, 2026-11-02: the same rule, kept across saves; reopening the editor lists the states from before the last save in "History of this edit", ARCHITECTURE §3.10). Versions (swipes), "Saved amendment history" and summary Revisions are unchanged.
- **[D] Revisions** (owner, 2026-11-01), permanent checkpoints, are cut only (a) when a generation uses text changed since the last revision, labelled with what caused it ("Used by a reply · <chat> · message 42", linking to that message), or (b) when the user presses Save. No idle or session checkpoints. A summary request is a generation too: it cuts the same checkpoint, "Used by a summary · <chat> · messages A–B" (coordinator, relaying the owner, 2026-11-01). A checkpoint squashes nothing away from the edit history. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.2a).
- **[D] Messages** (owner, 2026-11-01): a plain Save never asks anything; beside it a small "Save with a message…" opens an inline one-line field (Enter saves, Escape cancels); any revision's message, automatic ones too, can be added or edited later from History; the message is a label, not content. The same everywhere, chat-only notes included. A manual revision without a message shows as "Saved". **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] Revert** (owner, repeatedly; coordinator 2026-11-01): a visible discard beside Save in every editor, "Revert to revision N" (now an icon button named "Revert to the last save", §10.1), shown while something changed since the last revision; one click, no confirmation, since the text there stays in each field's edit history. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] Header state** (owner, 2026-11-01): an editor shows honestly when the live text has changed since its last revision ("Changed since revision N"), meaning not checkpointed, never "not sent". The wording is superseded by the save cluster (owner, 2026-11-02, §10.1): Save itself shows the state, with no revision number. No confirmation dialog on save; diffs are available on demand in History, human-readable for settings (never JSON). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] Settings** (selects, switches, numbers) are plain values (owner, 2026-11-01): they apply on change, have no per-setting Undo, History or Revert, never go to the bin as JSON; they're part of the revision like text, and History shows them readably. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10, §14.6).
- **[D]** Kept from the owner's earlier request (owner, 2026-11-01): reordering prompts in the Prompt preset tab and editor stays behind the floating unsaved bar (Save and Revert), because it's easy to do by accident. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6). The earlier [P] that only deleting a prompt was held too, adding one staying live, is **superseded** by the next item.
- **[D] Preset changes wait behind Save/Revert** (owner, 2026-11-02): every preset-level change that has no confirmation of its own waits behind the unsaved bar, as reordering does: deleting a prompt, adding one, attaching or removing a lorebook, and so on. The one exception is a prompt's on/off switch, which stays instant. The same in the Prompt preset tab and in the large preset editor. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6). How it's applied, for the owner to confirm:
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02: "Okay") A prompt's text, name and options (role, position, depth, condition, generation types) are not preset-level: they stay live text, as decided for every field (live text is what's sent). The preset-level settings outside the prompt list (reply sections, the end-of-request texts, prefill, utility prompts, regex scripts) stay live too: they're settings, which "apply on change" (above). Held: moving, deleting and adding a prompt (the character-definitions block included), and attaching or taking off a lorebook.
  - **[P]** A preset lorebook's on/off switch stays instant, as a prompt's does: it's the same kind of switch, and the chat's Lorebooks tab switches it in the preset at once (§1.3).
  - **[P]** A prompt added behind the bar can be typed into at once; its text is kept as live drafts, but nothing sends it until the bar's Save, and a reply's checkpoint leaves it out, so Revert takes it away entirely. Deleting a prompt that was only added behind the bar removes it at once (it was never saved or sent).
- **[P]** Creating versions or named revisions needs an explicit commit, and a commit is a clear action. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12, §14).
- **[P]** Every write carries the revision it was based on, so a stale tab can never silently overwrite newer state. **Built optimistic bases** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10, §14); compound forms retain documented snapshots and setting-save/draft-end operations are separate. This is not universal live field collaboration.
- **[D]** Search, and search and replace, everywhere: preset prompts, cards and chats.
  - **[D] Library-wide replace and unsaved edits** (owner, 2026-11-13: “Ok, skip and tell.”): when an entry has unsaved edits and a replace would touch it, the replace skips it and its result says plainly which entries it skipped and why, each with a way to open it. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (decisions1113, review-branchundo1; ARCHITECTURE §14.6 "Across the library"). Pending browser text is flagged at replace time, including another tab’s debounced edits. It replaces the earlier behaviour of saving the unsaved edits first as a revision of their own.
  - **[D] Regex scripts changed by the user's own replace** (owner, 2026-11-13: “Okay”, to the coordinator's “I recommend no, since you made the change”, i.e. no new approval after the user's own library-wide replace): a script that was approved stays approved after the replace; one that was not approved stays unapproved. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (decisions1113, review-branchundo1; ARCHITECTURE §14.6). The result names the changed scripts that stayed approved, including material pattern/flag changes. Undo restores the exact earlier approval while the replace revision is still current and no draft exists (review brief, 2026-11-13). Import, a card update from a source, ordinary revision restoration and the workshop assistant’s edits follow the existing approval rules (§11).
- **[D]** Text boxes work like a code editor (owner, 2026-10-16), and almost every text box needs it:
  - the left edge marks new, modified and unchanged lines
  - a diff view of the current changes against the last revision
  - a diff browser for every earlier revision

### 6.2a The prompt preset editor (owner, 2026-10-27)

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “This text is not very responsive”. Prompt rows give descriptions the content width without breaking words; titles take priority over secondary badges, counts and actions. Keep the wide layout. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P]** (promptrows, 2026-11-04): below a 36rem list container, use a grid for the handle, switch and wrapping title; secondary items wrap onto a following line. Apply the shared rule to every prompt kind in both editors.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “unique built-in prompts with fixed names that are already highlighted by their colour … shouldn't repeat their name in a coloured badge.” Keep badges for special prompts with customizable titles that are not otherwise highlighted. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “Rename it 'Notes from this chat' (if it actually means notes placed inside the history, say exactly that instead …). Link each listed note so the user can open it. Don't change how notes at depth > 1 are shown.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P]** (smallfixes5, 2026-11-04): keep kind badges for Main, Post-history, Auxiliary and Enhance definitions; omit placeholder, custom and unknown-kind badges. The positive-depth marker becomes “Notes in the chat history”; every listed note title opens its existing large editor, keeping depth and due annotations.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “It can't mean two things.” Depth 0 means after the last message; list a depth-0 note only under Notes after the newest message. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).


Notes replacing individual preset prompts, their links in both directions, and the proposed display and editing of a replaced prompt in a chat are specified in [§4.2](#42-notes-that-replace-prompts) (owner decisions and proposals, 2026-11-03; unbuilt). The full UI spec and cache-breakpoint display for these replacements remain open there.

- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, P1–P2; preseteditor brief): headers have separate name, editing and view rows. The panel keeps its picker + New row, then name + pencil and an expand icon with “Opens this preset in the large editor”; the large editor uses name + pencil + preset badge and the corner ×. Editing has Add prompt, Add definitions block, the unchanged save cluster, Search and replace (pressed while open, its panel below), then History and Make a variant together in the large editor. Narrow editing labels collapse in order: Search and replace, Add definitions block, Add prompt. Groups have smaller internal gaps than the gaps between groups.
- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, P3): “anything an action adds is scrolled fully into view (header included) and its box flashes a few times”; “Add prompt” and “Add definitions block” each explain what and where they add. Existing insertion places are retained, with their actual place in the hover.
- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, P4): “Auto-expand options”, remembered per user, replaces Show all options; hover “Opening a prompt also opens its options.” Turning on opens options in open prompts and in prompts subsequently opened; turning off closes shown options. A collapsed prompt’s options button opens both prompt and options. Individual options buttons remain usable.
- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, P5): “options + ⋯ buttons right after the title/pencil”; preview below the title, muted and truncated to one line. Read-only info is right-aligned, uncommon items first, placement next, role second to last, tokens always last with consistent gaps. The panel retains its layout and uses the same badge order. Placement is “in place” or “depth N”; every badge has a short factual hover.
- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, P6): custom prompts keep plain cards; built-in editable slots get uniquely tinted slot tags and explanations, with the card override explanation moved into the tag. Placeholders get tinted cards, distinct icons and kind tags, no text box or preview, and their muted “Filled from …” line. “Chat history” is the stronger full-width landmark band with a larger label. Summaries has a placeholder at the assembler’s fixed position just before history, without a drag handle.
- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, P7; preseteditor brief): show every assembler breakpoint for explicit-cache models, as thin “Cache breakpoint” lines qualified by prefix, summaries, history or end. Hover: “Prompt cache breakpoint: everything above it is cached between replies. Editing a prompt above it invalidates the cache from that prompt down. Not every provider supports caching.” Models without explicit caching show no lines or explanation.
- **[D] Decided** (owner, 2026-11-02, preseteditor brief S8): an enabled prompt above the first breakpoint containing registered volatile macros gets “Changes every reply”, with the actual macro names in the specified warning hover. Display does not change the assembler’s breakpoint logic.
- **[P]** (preseteditor, 2026-11-03): label-collapse thresholds use editor container widths 52/44/25rem in the large editor and 38/30/20rem in the panel; History/variant labels collapse under 35rem. Below 36rem a large prompt’s info occupies a separate right-aligned line. The view row uses one Expand all/Collapse all toggle. Auto-expansion is stored under the user’s local-storage key, with independent per-view options overrides. Existing definition insertion remains before the first per-field marker, else before history, else at the end. Pinned cache points use the qualifier “prefix”; multiple points projected to one row remain separate lines. Semantic tint hues and existing line icons identify the built-in kinds. A minimal local `ui/revealAdded.ts` reveals the block and flashes its border three times; the parallel lorebook job owns final shared-helper reconciliation. Summary placement follows the implemented assembler; the new row also appears with an empty layer. Unknown built-in kinds use the accent fallback. The inspector retains its existing reuse display.

- **[D]** Search, and search and replace, across the whole prompt preset. In ST it's impossible; the owner edits presets as JSON in an external editor to do it.
- **[D]** The biggest annoyance in ST's editor: twenty prompts in a row that can't be seen or edited as a whole. Finding something means opening each one, checking it, closing it, opening the next. The preset must be readable and editable as one document you scroll through.
- **[D] Rejected:** ST's separate pool of prompts. Adding a prompt means creating it with a button, then finding it in a dropdown and adding it to the list; disabled prompts may or may not be in the list. Here a new prompt goes straight into the list where you add it, and a prompt that's off stays in the list, off. (The build copied the pool: "prompts out of the order are listed to add back".)
- **[D]** The shape (owner confirmed 2026-10-27, from the coordinator's proposal):
  - The preset is one scrolling document. Each prompt is a block: a header (name, role, position or depth, an on/off switch, its token count) above its full text in the code-editor box, always visible. "Collapse all" gives an outline of headers; any block collapses on its own.
  - Markers are slim rows saying what fills them in the current chat ("Chat history (3,875 tokens)", "Definitions: Aria Vance, Rook"); dividers mark where the history starts, where the tail starts, and where the cached part ends. **Wording and look superseded** (owner, 2026-11-02; below).
  - Order by dragging a block's handle, or names in an outline column beside the document, which also jumps to a prompt. A "＋" between any two blocks inserts a new prompt there. **Superseded** (owner, 2026-11-02; below): New prompt in the toolbar.
  - The code-editor style search and replace bar (as in the chat: case, whole word, regex, preserve case, previews) sits at the top and **stays in view while scrolling**.
  - Explicit Save with the diff of every changed prompt; revisions. **Superseded** (owner, 2026-11-01): live text, Save without a dialog, diffs in History (§6.2).
  - **The editor overlays the centre column**, as ST's does: it never navigates to another page, so editing the preset while chatting stays convenient.
  - The right panel's Preset tab has the on/off switches and token counts, and **Edit on each block**, which opens the editor overlay scrolled to that prompt. A switch flipped there is an edit of the (global) preset and shows Save. **Superseded** by the next item (owner, 2026-11-01).
- **[D]** The side panel's Prompt preset tab holds everything needed to edit the preset (owner, 2026-11-01): each prompt expands in place into its text in a multiline editor, under a header that expands into the prompt's options; prompts reorder in the column by drag handles; nothing commits directly, and a hovering bar that follows the scrolling says what's unsaved, with Revert and Save, until either is used. The large editor stays, for more comfortable work, and opens at a given prompt. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14, Preset overlays). "Nothing commits directly" is **superseded** by live text (owner, 2026-11-01, §6.2): only reorders (and, proposed, deletions) stay behind the bar.
- **[D]** The large editor's blocks (owner, 2026-11-01): each prompt is a visibly bounded unit, a header (its state as chips) above its text; its options fold away per prompt by default, with a global show/hide for all of them; the name is renamed in place from a pencil beside it (field with save, Enter/Escape), with no name field in the options; the outline beside the document has a sensible default width and is resizable like the app's sidebars; Save and Revert sit together wherever Save is; actions carry hover/focus explanations so their labels stay short; the preset's name shows once, renamed the same way, with no Rename button. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **[D]** The lines between the prompt rows (owner, 2026-11-02, on the Prompt preset tab's "Cached part ends · prefix", "Cached part ends · pinned", "History starts", "At depth N: …", "Cached part ends · history" and "Tail starts"): "Cached part starts/cached part ends ??? This is confusing and it's not clear what it means. Also these intermissions are styled and sized too inconsistently with the UI they interrupt. They SHOULD be distinct and obviously separated - but this is just weird. They're too small and inconsistent." Each line says in the user's terms what it means for where a prompt goes, and is styled as a clearly separate element as wide as the rows. **Built** (promptmarkers; ARCHITECTURE §14).
  - **[P]** (promptmarkers) Labels: **The chat goes here**, **After the chat: sent last**, **Above this line: reused by every reply** and **Above this line: reused until old messages drop out**, each with an (i) button whose popover explains it (what's reused, that editing above the line makes the next reply resend everything after the edit once, which prompts belong where, that not every provider reuses text). Of the request's four cache points (prefix, pinned, summary, history) only one difference is something the user can act on: whether the part survives the chat's oldest messages dropping out. So the first kind shows as "every reply" and only the last of the others as "until old messages drop out", which covers the ones above it. A cache point inside the new chat's opening line shows just above "The chat goes here". The same reuse lines replace the inspector's "Cache breakpoint: …" lines.
  - **[P]** (promptmarkers) Look: a 2 px rule across the rows' full width, with the icon (in the rows' handle column), the label in semibold 14 px and the (i) under it; solid in the text colour for the chat lines, dashed in the accent colour for the reuse lines. Note places ("Notes inside the chat: Character's Note (depth 2)", "Notes after the chat", …) are dashed rows like a marker prompt's, with a note icon in the same column.
  - **[D]** Empty note places stay quiet (owner, 2026-11-02, coordinator's brief from the owner's screenshot of "Start of context notes: no active notes", "Before main prompt: no active notes", …): a note place shows only while it holds a note, in the large editor as in the tab. **Built** (promptmarkers).
- **[D]** Adding prompts (owner, 2026-11-02, on the large editor): "First issue: those damn + buttons. There's giant, unlimited width + buttons between ALL prompts. This is obviously colossal clutter and waste of space, it makes everything taller. The button isn't even really a button, it opens a dropdown for adding either a prompt or a 'character definition block'. Just make these both independent buttons shown on the header ONCE, the prompt is created and the user can drag it to wherever they want later." **Built** (promptmarkers; ARCHITECTURE §14).
  - **[P]** (promptmarkers) **New prompt** and **Definitions block** sit at the start of the list's toolbar, before Expand all, in the large editor; in the Prompt preset tab they end the toolbar, so the save cluster shares Expand all's row (§10.1 [D] One row; suitegreen2, 2026-11-02). A new prompt goes at the end of the list; the definitions block where the separate definition markers start (else just above the chat, else at the end). Either opens scrolled into view with its text focused, and waits behind the unsaved bar like any added prompt. With a definitions block already in the preset, its button is disabled and says so on hover and focus. A prompt's ⋯ menu keeps Insert a prompt above/below.
- **[D]** Wide rows (owner, 2026-11-02): "on this wide layout (and this IS the big editor meant to take up most of the screen) it's obvious how far away from the title all the chips and token counts and buttons end up going. Not sure how to fix this exactly, but this is harder to parse due to the huge distance between titles and what they refer to. Also there's less information shown at a glance compared to the sidebar version of this, which shows the first line of the prompt in each header." **Built** (promptmarkers).
  - **[P]** (promptmarkers) In the large editor a row's name (at most 28rem wide) is followed directly by its chips, its token count, the options toggle and ⋯; a closed prompt's first line fills the rest of the header in muted text. Nothing is pinned to the far end of the row.

- **[O]** Token counts per prompt for the current chat: ST updates them only after a message is sent with the preset. Whether to update them continuously is open. **Partial**: raw text estimates update while typing and expanded/marker counts refresh from the committed context without a send (ARCHITECTURE §14, §15). Debounced assembly of unsaved preset drafts remains open/unbuilt.

### 6.3 Per-card preset overlays
- **[P], superseded for prompt replacements** by notes (§4.2; owner, 2026-11-03), including card notes. Earlier proposal: prompt toggles or replacements attached to a card (or a card within one chat), applied when present or speaking; the owner's example was “you may impersonate” for a narrator card. Existing prompt conditions and Main/post-history overrides remain in §2.2 and §5.0. A separate general per-card overlay editor is no longer the proposed replacement mechanism; broader agent/card policies remain Phase 4/6 work.

---

## 7. Agents and rollouts

Tools that “change a card” during RP create notes replacing its fields, including the owner's surgical costume example, in [§4.2](#42-notes-that-replace-prompts) (owner, 2026-11-03; Phase 4, unbuilt). This supersedes timeline card edits and the field-patch proposal in §4.1a; version-attached note effects still follow §7.3.

**Phase 4 design** (2026-11-05): [spec/AGENTIC.md](AGENTIC.md), as deltas from reference-harness `latest`; its build units are its §11. Entries below marked unbuilt Phase 4 point to its sections.

### 7.1 Timeline vs. rollouts
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, on “Narrator with tools”) “That's just how things work by default?” The modes leave This chat in favour of independent Context, Tools, Cards and Who replies settings. **Built** for shared context (chatsettings); ARCHITECTURE §16. Separate contexts remain unbuilt.
- **[D]** The chat timeline exists independently of agent rollouts. A rollout forks from the timeline, produces output, and ends. Different agents with separate contexts can contribute to the same chat, and agentic and non-agentic chatting share one representation.
- **[D]** Orchestration modes are configurable, and there will be more than two:
  - **a single agent per turn with all cards loaded** (reference-harness's way, the most cache-friendly)
  - **one agent per character**, with its own system prompt and only its own card. It speaks only for itself and sees history only for the periods it was present. This turns the model from a narrator into an agent acting for its character, and scales to many characters. It's not the owner's usual choice.
  - plain ST-like non-agentic generation
- **[P]** A mode decides three things: who runs on a turn, what each agent sees, and how output is committed. **Partial**: execution/planning/commit registries host plain replies and summary strategies (ARCHITECTURE §6, §15, §16); general agent orchestration remains Phase 4 work, designed as the `orchestrationModes` registry (AGENTIC §6). **Superseded as user-facing choices** (2026-11-06, below): the modes become four independent chat settings; the registry stays internal.
- **[D]** Per-agent private state (notes, memories) hidden from other agents **[L]**. Getting agents to actually use such tools is the hard part. Follow reference-harness's rule that how an agent is prompted to use a tool is designed as carefully as the tool.
- **[P]** With presence-limited views, summary chunks are cut at presence boundaries so they can be shared wherever views overlap. **Unbuilt Phase 4 proposal**; deferred with the optional per-character mode (AGENTIC §6, §10).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, relayed by the coordinator) An agent per character is valuable mainly because each has its own definitions; with every card's definitions always in context, descriptions mix and influence each other. Some definitions must stay shared (physical descriptions are needed for others' turns). The candidate: "internal" definitions as card notes at the end of the context, sent only for the current speaker, also in single-model chats; its costs are recency (they can override changes made in the story) and caching. Design: [CHARACTER-CONTEXT.md](CHARACTER-CONTEXT.md) (Proposed; revised the same day by the corrections below; its §7.2 lists what's open). The relayed "knowledge scopes", converging at the summary layer and scene scopes are superseded by the corrections below.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "Card fields have nothing to do with this. You can't split this unless you do something like edit the card with an LLM. Usually 'description' contains everything. We could invent a concept of a card having a public and a private part of its def, but it would be non-standard. This requires the user to adapt the card either way."
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "The default would be as it stands, with every character at once. In most cases that's going to be the right choice. It costs the least and the LLM has full context of everything. It doesn't scale as well with too many characters and doesn't separate them too well, which is why *sometimes* you might want to separate them." Own-turn notes are an opt-in way of running a chat, not the plan for every chat (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §1).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "This was already decided. The only way to do it is notes. The other surface doesn't work. There's no public/private part in the spec. If we were to use card extensions, we'd use them to specify the card notes and their configuration, since that's actually general purpose." Own-turn card notes are the mechanism; no public/private card extension. An LLM-suggested split stays as a later helper that proposes notes (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) On a fixed place for the own-turn block: "Why would this be predetermined? This is what the prompt preset is literally for." Own-turn notes go where note positions and the preset put them. **[P]** The preset gets an **Own-turn notes** marker, like its other markers, holding the speaker's own-turn notes; "Move to an own-turn note" places the new note there. The built-in preset (and an imported ST preset, which has no such marker) puts it at the start of the tail, so the cached prefix doesn't change with the speaker in the default all-at-once layout, matching the original idea of card notes at the end of the context. Users move the marker in the preset; a single note can still take any ordinary position. Costs per place are information for the cache estimate (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.1, §4.1).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "This doesn't really solve the issue of interoperability. The user will be editing notes and not the card, so exporting it will show the original description. The UI probably will help make it clear enough so they're not surprised, but this isn't really 'solving' it. ST cards just don't have this functionality." ST gets the original Description. **[P]** Every Export… shows a notice for cards whose fields notes replace (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §3).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "Swap mode still shows the persona on ST. With this configuration as described the LLM would quite literally never see it. … If a user wants card defs to be shown at the end, this can be done by editing the prompt preset. It's the rotation that can't be expressed that way… Why do we have to go out of our way to seemingly override the user's preset choices." Card-swap is rotation only: the preset's card-definition positions hold only the speaker's card, as in ST's "Swap character cards"; the persona and everything else are unchanged; nothing moves to the tail. The cache cost of a speaker-dependent start is the user's choice, shown by the cache estimate. ST's swap mode, cited from its public source, and the target behaviour: CHARACTER-CONTEXT §4 (built by the `cardswap` job).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) On whether "narrator mode" sees everything: "This is not even a question." On plain mode with scenes or labels: "What?". One shared context has no scenes, labels or views.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "When a message is generated, a certain set of characters is present. That's what the presence marker originally referred to. Those characters can see the message. The story doesn't have to be branching, since a character could be 'gone' without their actions being narrated in a specific branch. If there are different branches of conversation, however, logically different characters may be part of them. A merged in chat only had those characters present." The knowers of a message are the characters present when it was generated (§3.5; the version's recorded cast). **[P]** What presence can't express yet: a character gone while still in the cast (an "away" mark recorded with the version's cast), and later, one-message audiences (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.1). Away and Mute are separate marks, following from §3.5 [D]: Mute means "doesn't talk", presence means "is there and sees"; a muted character still knows what's said.
- **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-06: settled, following from the 2026-11-06 [D] above) Physical descriptions are needed even when writing other characters' turns, so some definitions stay shared. So a reply in a separate context sees the other cast cards' shared text (a Description after its replacement note) and an unsplit card whole. This refines "only its own card" above (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.5).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "With per-character agents, context is different for different agents. You essentially have a parallel chat context for each of them. So each of them would get different summaries, if their chats diverge. This could be optimized in such a way that summaries are shared up to the point where there's a shared prefix. The idea of sharing a summary for everyone isn't stupid but the issue is that if this is fine then why separate the chats in the first place? You can do the part of showing parts of character defs at the end only for some characters without any of this." Each separate context gets its own summaries where it diverges, shared up to the common prefix. Own-turn notes are independent of knowledge separation (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.3).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) On gap lines: "Yes, but I am not sure this wording is always what we want. One important case: two entirely separate storylines, that are 1 on 1 chats, merge. What do we say then?" **[P]** A gap line only where the character was present before and after a stretch of its own storyline; a merged-in storyline it was never part of is someone else's past and nothing is said by default; the wording is preset text with macros (absence count, names), plus an optional join line at a merge point, empty by default (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.2).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, confirming the gap-line proposal above: "Ok") A gap line appears only where a character was present before and after a stretch within its own storyline. Nothing is said by default about a merged-in storyline the character was never part of. The wording is preset text with macros, plus an optional join line at a merge point, empty by default.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "the information asymmetry and diverging storyline stuff could very well work with non-agentic RP and non-agentic models, since it's not inherently about tool calling. Keep in mind I am still not sure agentic RP even works well at all." **[P]** A chat has four independent choices, each defaulting to today: context per speaker (one shared context, or separate contexts built from what each character was present for), tools (none, or agent tools), card layout (the preset's, card-swap rotation, and own-turn notes with either) and turn picking (Auto, Auto helped by a decision model, No one). Per-character agents are separate contexts plus agent tools, not a mode of their own; every combination works with plain models (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §1).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "What does that even mean? What mode? A narrator would be a *character* not a mode. An omniscient narrator would see every message and not have a presence value at all. The only issue is that it would need a way to render converging storylines in its context, and that may be difficult." Narrator is a cast role **[P]**: no presence value, sees every message. **[O]** How a narrator's context shows storylines that diverged and converged; options sketched in CHARACTER-CONTEXT §6.
  - **[P]** (owner, 2026-11-08) Proposal put to the owner: within a chat, the diverged stretches appear as labelled sections per storyline (e.g. "Mira and Tobin, 41–54" and "Wren, 41–54"), the way a chat merge shows two chats. Owner: "Okay, although I feel like both aren't really solved or fully settled yet." This direction is the working default; the question stays **[O]**.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, relayed by the coordinator) "One model with tools writes every turn, with all cards loaded" is just the default. **[P]** The orchestration modes go away as user-facing choices: `narrator` differed from `plain` only by tools and agent instructions, and `card-swap` was `narrator` plus a card layout. Tools are a per-chat setting, card-swap a card-layout setting, a separate context per character the context setting; This chat shows these instead of a Reply mode select. The `orchestrationModes` registry stays internal, as the plugins' extension point, with one built-in entry planning replies from the settings. `orchestration.mode` is removed with no migration (nothing real is stored yet; dev data is seeded again) (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §1).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) "Parallel context for each agent is the obvious default… Having common summaries, and diverging and converging recent history, however, isn't really that absurd. It would mean a common cache prefix for everyone, and giving common context for older history to every agent could lead to a more coherent narrative, while allowing short term information asymmetry… The issues here are two: this would need to be its own specific way of doing things that is different from the default, and the way it works is slightly arbitrary. Depending on context size, summary settings, the conversation, etc, the boundary between what has divergent storylines and the converged past is pretty arbitrary… One benefit is that it allows for temporary separate storylines to not make a chat more expensive forever. I also earlier mentioned this (merging summaries, but keeping recent chat separated) as a way to merge external chats in. I think it's not obvious how all of these options and alternative ways of doing things would be worked into a single coherent system/UI without becoming really hard to understand." The default is parallel contexts with their own summaries. **[O]** A "shared past" alternative; **[P]** idea: its boundary is an explicit story event (a merge, or the user marking "Storylines rejoin here"), everything before it summarized into one shared layer, separate recent history after it; the same mechanism covers merging an external chat (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §2.4).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) No one under Next means the next reply isn't automatic: you press ▶ on a card in the Cast list to get one, "the same as setting everyone muted, except without having to do so".
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06) On turn picking: "That's only true for agents. Non-agentic RP doesn't have tools… but making the auto turn picker smarter *is* useful… it isn't especially dangerous if that decision is wrong, but… the way it works in ST without any kind of real context feels stupid. Two characters cannot talk without a third one who left the scene 3 messages before not butting in. Perhaps the decision model should just choose who speaks next, although the 'who has left' decision and state is useful since it can be decided more 'locally' based on a specific message, at least in theory." **[P]** Both opt-in decision points: Who is in the scene (built; local, per message, sets away) and Who replies next (being added). With one shared context away only affects turn picking; with separate contexts it also decides who sees what, so a decision-set away shows as a timeline row with Undo. Characters can still leave through their tool with agent tools on (CHARACTER-CONTEXT §5). This replaces the earlier open item on who marks a character gone.

### 7.2 Output contracts
- **[D]** Support both: capturing the final assistant text, and an explicit `send_message` call. Agentic models produce intermediate blocks that aren't the final output, and weaker models don't use `send_message` reliably.
- **[D]** One internal commit pipeline, so hooks and checks apply the same way to both.
- **[D]** RP produces one message per turn, as a hard rule.
- **[D] Shelved:** treating intermediate output blocks as real messages.
- **[D] The agent instructions are an editable preset prompt** (owner, 2026-11-17, on making them one): "Yes, although we need to make sure they're delivered when you have a preset that wasn't created for agentic RP". **Built** (sendmsg1; ARCHITECTURE §9 "Agent instructions"): the preset prompt "Agent instructions", held, edited and placed like any prompt, sent only when a reply uses tools; a preset without it (every ST import, older presets) still sends the default text at the end of the request.
  - **[P]** (sendmsg1) Empty text sends the app's default, which still follows the chat's tools and reply contract; last among the order's prompts that are on, it joins the end-of-request message (where the default always went), anywhere else it's its own message there; turned off, nothing is sent. The built-in preset holds it last and empty, so its requests are byte-identical to before. A workshop never sends it: its own text is the existing Workshop instructions override (`workshopTurn`).
- **[D] `send_message` is to be the default story contract, with reference-harness's nudge** (owner, 2026-11-17, on a suggestion to tell models not to narrate their tool use): "The problem is they will do the latter anyway. Models output regular output blocks in between tool calls to update progress, they're trained to do this. This is why reference-harness forces send_message. It also prevents the model from doing something annoying, which is to replicate the metadata XML tags around reference-harness messages: many models see those in context and think they have to output them with their message. This never happens with send_message. Of course reference-harness has the (very essential) nudge mechanic to force models to behave, whenever they try to output instead of tool call. Our harness should probably have reference-harness's behaviour on this as the default". This supersedes `both` as the default contract (the first item above decided supporting both, and the code defaulted to `both`; both contracts stay as choices).
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-17, on a quick port of the nudge) "Do you understand how reference-harness nudges work? And how the whole end session mechanic works? There's a lot to it that makes it functional". Not built yet: the study and the delta design are [REPLY-CONTRACT.md](REPLY-CONTRACT.md) (Proposed), with its milestones.
- **[D] Failure after the nudges is visible** (owner, 2026-11-17, on spec/REPLY-CONTRACT.md §3.4): "It has to fail visibly. Not sure what staying silent would even mean here." The design already does this; answered.
- **[D] No redo on another model** (owner, 2026-11-17, on the checks gate redoing a turn on a different model): "This is not an autonomous agent. The user can switch a model and swipe themselves. Moreover, we don't have the affordance for choosing a secondary model" The gate's model-switching redo is rejected, not deferred, and is not in the delta design (REPLY-CONTRACT §3.6).
- **[D] Stories get `no_reply`; no progress messages** (owner, 2026-11-17, "ok" to the coordinator's proposal, after the owner's point: "Why would the automated reply order know better than the actual LLM? It's the same with the decision model"): the replying character may pass. A pass is never a failure and never invisible: the chat shows a small line "<Name> stays silent", swipeable like a message to get an actual reply. In a group or round the next character in the round then replies; with no round, the turn returns to the user; in 1:1 the same line appears and the turn returns to the user. Progress messages (`final: false`) are not used in stories: one reply is one message, and anything written along the way shows in Steps; workshops keep theirs. A model that neither sends nor passes after 3 nudges still fails visibly. (REPLY-CONTRACT §3.1.)
- **[D] Progress messages are not needed in stories** (owner, 2026-11-17, on REPLY-CONTRACT §3.1): "The user can literally expand the agentic rollout and read it. Just like you can do it in this harness."
- **Open design item: multi-speaker turns, needs its own spec (after 2026-11-19)** (owner, 2026-11-17, REPLY-CONTRACT §3.1): "The only legitimate usage is allowing an agent to write as multiple characters. This is perfectly sensible - however it requires a different conception of what a 'turn' is since the rollout is *internal* to a turn-message currently (also this doesn't work when context varies per character, obviously) So this would need some design work and we have to decide when it's allowed. I think it actually solves a problem, which is that models tend to combine different character reactions in the same message when they're happening close by, not respecting who the speaker is. But so far, everything assumes there is ONE speaker per turn." Until then, one speaker per turn and no progress messages in stories.
- **[D] One agent reply is one rollout of model requests, capped per chat** (owner, 2026-11-17, on "12 turns"): "That is a single agentic rollout with 12 requests, or 12 steps, not 12 turns. This should be configurable, however." User-facing text says model requests or steps, never turns (a turn is a message). **Built** (sendmsg1; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts"): This chat → Tools, "Model requests per reply", 1 to 100, default 12, a branch-owned setting frozen with each run, so Resume keeps the run's own cap.
- **[D] A workshop run has no request cap** (owner, 2026-11-17): "What about the workshop agent? It cannot be limited, much like how claude code wouldn't be". **Built** (sendmsg1): the workshop planner sets none (it was 40); the run goes on until the model ends it or the user stops it, the long-run compaction and elision keeping its context in bounds. **[P]** No workshop setting for one either: Stop already ends a run, and nothing found a need for an optional limit.

### 7.3 Rollouts as versions
- **[D]** Because the rollout is separate from the timeline, regenerating creates a new rollout and therefore a new version.
- **[P]** Tool effect classes (reference-harness has a version of this in `tools/side-effects.ts`): **Unbuilt Phase 4 toolset** (AGENTIC §4.1, §4.5); version-attached state is a built foundation (ARCHITECTURE §3.8).
  - **read-only:** lore lookup, summary expansion, search. Freely regenerable.
  - **chat state:** notes, presence, private memory. State events attached to the version, so swiping reverses them.
  - **chat state** also covers notes replacing card fields and other note edits, within what the user marked editable (§4.1a, §4.2). The earlier wording, “edits to this chat's copy of a card”, is superseded by the owner's 2026-11-03 note mechanism.
  - **external or global:** library card edits (the card everywhere), settings, plugin edits. Regenerating warns first, and these tools aren't offered in RP turns.
- **[P]** (phase4perm, 2026-11-06) Where the user says what the model may change (AGENTIC §4.3). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts", "In the client"):
  - In a chat: "Model may change", in This chat right below Reply mode's Tools, shown only for a mode that uses tools. One checkbox per chat note, per field a note can replace on each cast card, and per card note, grouped "Chat notes", "<Name>'s card", "<Name>'s notes". Each starts as the note's or card's own setting and a change applies from the next reply on this branch. Boxes whose tool isn't ticked under Tools stay in place, disabled, with one line under the group's heading saying which tool to tick; a linked note's box is always disabled, saying it's a shared note (only its own editor changes it). A card's six fields sit side by side in equal columns. **Overtaken** (next item): "There's no 'back to the card's own' control: once ticked or unticked in the chat, the chat's choice holds."
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, relayed by the coordinator) A per-box way back to following the note's or card's own setting: "shown only when the chat overrides the box", with "a short label and a hover explanation", visible, "not behind a key or a hidden gesture", in the side panel and the large editor alike. **[P]** A "Reset" button at the end of the box's row (each card field in its own column, so nothing moves when it comes or goes), explained on hover ("Goes back to …'s own setting: the model may / may not change it."); it writes `agent.permission` with `on: null`, which drops the chat's choice from that position on. **Built** (cardswap; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts").
  - On a card: "Model may change", six checkboxes at the end of the Definition tab (both editors, from one registered section), saved with the card. Like the note's "The agent may edit it", only the user's own edit sets it: a restore, merge or replace never adds a field.
  - The boxes' "Click to …" explanations are hover-only, not repeated under each box on a phone; the section's "?" explains them there.
  - The note editor's "The agent may edit it" hint no longer says agents aren't built.
- **[D]** A note the model writes belongs to the reply that wrote it (owner, 2026-11-06, on seeing such a note listed on every branch: "What...?!"). It must not appear at all where that reply isn't on the path, not even as an inactive note: it appears where the reply is shown and disappears with a swipe away or on another branch. The server's listing for a fork leaves it out, not only the panel. **Built** (phase4perm; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent-written notes"): `ChatNoteEntries` (the Notes panel, its large view and note targets through `ListChatNotes`, the model's `list_notes` and other note tools, "Model may change") and the one-note-per-target check all skip it there, so a same-titled or same-target note can be written on that branch. The inspector and context only ever had it where it's on.
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, relayed by the coordinator) The Library's note list leaves out model-written notes from every branch, while the chat's Notes panel stays as is. **Built** (cardswap): `ListLibrary` for notes skips a note whose first revision is a reply's (origin `generation`); the note editor's switcher reads it.

### 7.4 Display

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05; modellabel, implemented in ARCHITECTURE §5) "Remove the cap: the label uses the header's actual free width (up to the toolbar)"; "Only when the header truly runs out of room does the all-or-nothing rule apply (connection hidden entirely, then the model name cut)."
- **[P]** (modellabel, implemented) Measure the full text and available label width with ResizeObserver; retain intrinsic sizing independently of the visible connection to avoid a sizing loop or streaming layout jump.
- **[D]** Thinking tag-pair boxes (owner, 2026-11-03 follow-up): "the same treatment as the History edits Search and replace boxes". Keep both tag inputs and Match case/Treat as a regex within each bordered box; replace Remove pair with a corner X ("Remove this pair"), always available because zero pairs are allowed. Replace Add a pair with a + beside the section heading ("Add another tag pair"), revealing and flashing additions through shared `revealAdded`. A neighbouring ⋯ holds "Add the default pair" ("Adds <think> and </think>"), retaining restoration of missing defaults. The single-line fields and switch-before-send-back ordering remain. **Built (thinkingsection follow-up); obsolete as an implementation requirement**; ARCHITECTURE §15 "Reply sections". **[D]** No ⋯ menu for restoring the default pair (owner, 2026-11-03: "What even is the point of this? Just remove it"; a dedicated menu just to add <think></think> was pointless); removed.
- **[D]** Thinking settings (owner, 2026-11-03): the top switch "looks like it enables the entire thinking feature". Move "Move tagged thinking into its own panel" below the tag pairs and their add row, directly above "Send thinking back for the last replies"; keep the remaining order. Opening and Closing tag fields become single-line inputs, capped by the shared 40rem rule, side by side when room permits and wrapping otherwise. **Built (thinkingsection); obsolete as an implementation requirement**; see ARCHITECTURE §15 "Reply sections".
  - **[P]** (thinkingsection, 2026-11-03) A content-width grid keeps the pair row within 40rem and wraps below two 14rem columns. **Built**; ARCHITECTURE §15.
- **[D]** Reasoning, steps and intermediate output stream **live and expanded by default**, then collapse when the final message arrives. If the user collapses or expands them during a run, that becomes the sticky preference (stored per user **[P]**). **Built**: streamed reasoning and per-account preference (ARCHITECTURE §12, §14); an agent reply's steps the same way, with their own per-account preference (`steps`) (phase4ui, ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts").
- **[P]** (owner request 2026-10-31; coordinator's design, [spec/REPLY-SECTIONS.md](REPLY-SECTIONS.md)) **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §12 and §15; review-thinkhide1). Tagged sections at the start of a reply (SillyTavern's reasoning Auto-parse): the prompt preset holds a list of opening/closing tag pairs (default `<think>` / `</think>` and `<thinking>` / `</thinking>`, literal, case-insensitive, on; the tags as the following decision has them).
  - **[D]** The tags are **literal text by default** (owner, 2026-11-02: "The text currently suggests it is regex unconditionally, which is bad especially given that imported presets did not assume it would be (ST does not even support regex there)"). A tag matches exactly as written, as ST's prefix and suffix do; values imported from ST are always literal. Regex is an explicit opt-in per pair, "Treat as a regex", off by default, with an explanation. Built (thinkstream, 2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §15 "Reply sections").
  - **[D]** While a reply streams (owner, 2026-11-02: "we should show everything as in-thinking once the opening tag matches (fully), while text is still streaming. But the thinking should be shown if the thinking block is expanded"): once the opening tag has fully arrived, everything after it goes to the thinking panel, not the body, until the closing tag; then the text goes to the body. The panel shows while streaming, open or folded as the streaming preference above says, streams live when open, and its header says "Thinking…" until the body starts. Built (thinkstream; ARCHITECTURE §15 "Reply sections").
  - **[P]** (thinkstream, 2026-11-02) A reply that ends without the closing tag keeps the text in the panel and an empty body, as ST's streaming does (`ReasoningHandler.finish` leaves it as reasoning); the panel says it was never closed, and "Show as reply text" puts it back. The saved result is the same as parsing the whole reply at once. A partial opening tag is held back rather than shown briefly in the body: exactly while it still begins a literal tag, so `<b>` goes through at once. Leading sections, after whitespace, move out of the body into a second collapsible panel, "Thinking in the reply", below the provider's "Reasoning", whether or not the provider sent reasoning; tags later in the text stay. The exact tag text is kept, so the original reply is reconstructable. Not sent back by default; a preset option sends it on the last N replies in its original tags. "Show as reply text" undoes a wrong parse as a new edited version.
- **[D]** Rollout steps expand **inline**, as a collapsed "N steps" element inside the message version they belong to, the way coding harnesses do it. (A side-panel rollout timeline was rejected for space reasons.) **Built** (phase4ui; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts").
  - **[P]** (phase4ui, 2026-11-05) The element sits above the version's reasoning, since the steps came before the final turn's reasoning and text. A tool row's result, and a thinking or text step's full text, open under it; the element links to the run in the inspector.
- **[D]** **Run inspector** (side panel or full view): the exact assembled context of *that specific run*, presented readably from system blocks through summaries to the chat, as in reference-harness's console.
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-10-28; PHASE1-DECISIONS §1) From any generated message or swipe, old ones included, a button opens the exact request that produced it. What was sent is stored, never reconstructed from references. Every part records what it is and where it came from (preset prompt, card and field, note, lorebook and entry, history message and version, the added closing user turn, summary blocks). One block per turn, titled boxes for the parts, all prompt text verbatim and the inspector's own labels unmistakable from it, readable at 1440 and 390, light and dark; prompts and notes skipped by their condition show as "not sent".
- **[P]** A badge on the message header shows the step count and time, and nothing for plain generations. Rollouts attach to versions, not to positions between messages. **Built** (phase4ui; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts"): "N steps · T s" (N tool calls), a toggle that opens the steps in the message.
- **[P]** Rollouts that produce no message (summaries, jobs, decision calls) appear in an **activity panel** with cost and token use. **Built** (phase4ui; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts"): the chat panel tab **Activity**.
  - **[P]** (phase4ui, 2026-11-05) Activity reads the usage ledger, one row per rollout and purpose, so a refusal judgment (a `decision` request riding on a reply's rollout) is listed beside summaries and condensations. A summary row links to its request; a judgment's to the reply.
