### 7.5 Resuming

- **[P]** (review-resume1, 2026-11-13) **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal:** removing a model binding resolves the current default before Resume, Continue or a new version; a model switch checks the kept run against the new context budget; a removed tool's committed receipt still supplies its result; provider identity controls replayed thinking; stopped plain replies have visible Resume. See AGENTIC §12.8 and ARCHITECTURE §16. Decision statuses above and below are unchanged.
- **[D]** Resume a stopped or broken rollout mid-way **without sending another turn and without the model being able to tell**. reference-harness does this; most coding harnesses don't. **Built** (resumeui, 2026-11-12; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Agent rollouts", "In the client"): Resume among the message's actions on an agent reply that stopped or failed.
- **[P]** As in reference-harness (`src/agent/recovery.ts`): **Built; obsolete as a proposal** (2026-11-05; ARCHITECTURE §16, "Agent rollouts": `ResumeVersion`, `agent/resume.ts`).
  - a frozen context snapshot is taken when the rollout starts
  - the transcript is flushed as each turn completes
  - on failure, synthetic error messages and partial turns are stripped and a fresh agent is reseeded
- **[P]** Interruption policies: **Built** (2026-11-05; ARCHITECTURE §16).
  - after a tool call but before its result: re-run the tool
  - midway through a tool call: discard it and resample
  - midway through text: continue it through prefill where supported, otherwise discard it
- **[P]** Any step of a rollout can be edited before resuming. **Built** (server 2026-11-05, `ResumeFromStep`; the inspector's Edit and Resume from here, resumeui 2026-11-12; ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[D]** Anything stopped midway can be resumed without a new message: a crash mid-rollout, a model error. The request that failed is sent when the user resumes, to a new model if the user chose one (owner, 2026-11-12). Design and audit: AGENTIC §12. **Built** (resume1, 2026-11-12; ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[P]** (resume1) The turn being written is saved while it streams, so a crash loses at most its last 2 s; a restart sends nothing until the user resumes.
- **[P]** (resume1) Resume, Continue and a new version use the model the composer shows with the reply last. With the same model the frozen request is sent byte for byte; with another, the messages and transcript are kept, the new model's settings and cache layout apply, and other models' thinking and signatures are left out.
- **[P]** (resume1) Resume offers the tools the chat has now; a removed tool isn't offered, finished calls keep their results. When the request can't be built from the chat now, Resume says why and offers to send the saved request unchanged, never silently.
- **[P]** (resume1) A provider that sends nothing for 300 s fails the request ("no_response", not retried); each connection can change the wait. Any byte, keep-alives included, restarts the clock.
- **[P]** (resume1) Failed and interrupted replies show Resume beside their status: an agent run goes on; a plain reply is continued, or written again when it has no text.

### 7.6 Decision models
- **[D]** Jev and similar decision models are integrated as in reference-harness (`spec/DECISION-MODEL.md`), but are **never mandatory**. Each decision point has a heuristic fallback. Phase 4 design: AGENTIC §7 (points `presence` and `lore`, opt-in per connection). **Built** (phase4decide, 2026-11-05; ARCHITECTURE §16 "Decision models").
- **[P]** (phase4decide) A decision connection (kind `system-one`) answers decisions only: it's left out of model pickers and refused for replies. Its Decisions card picks one of its models and turns each point on, with a confidence slider; nothing is sent until a point is on. Several connections with the same point on: the first by name answers.
- **[P]** (phase4decide) `presence` decides who is in the scene after each reply (two or more characters), and away is Mute, so it governs who Auto picks to reply next; the reply policy still picks among those present. It never overrides the user's own Mute or the agent's `set_presence`, and never sends everyone away.
- **[P]** (phase4decide) `lore` asks only about entries that carry a question for the decision model (the entry's `decision_hint`, set in its Matching settings), at most 32 per reply, over the last six messages; a yes includes the entry as if its keys had matched.

### 7.7 Agent use ("workshop")
- **[D] The workshop beside a story is a chat from the start** (owner, 2026-11-17, on the Workshop tab showing only its explanation and a "Start a workshop" button): “Why would this interface look like that? It should be a chat UI already open with the composer. Yes, this needs a message to explain what this is and what is going on to the user - a message that can disappear after the chat actually starts, but the chat UI can't be hidden before that. Making 'start the chat' its own action is bad UX. The chat is created when the first message is sent.” **Built** (workshopstart1; ARCHITECTURE §16, “The workshop beside a story”): the tab always shows the message area and the composer, focused, with the strip's model and preset; an intro stands in the message area until the first send, which makes the workshop and sends the message in one action. Start a workshop is gone; New workshop and Talk about this chat open a new one's composer and make nothing.
- **[D]** Long workshop sessions require LCM summaries. Owner, 2026-11-13: “the agent NEEDS LCM summaries. It was INVENTED FOR CODING AGENTS.” This closes WORKSHOP §10 question 4 and its Later item. Summaries cover messages and tool steps; originals remain recoverable. **Built worklcm1 (2026-11-13)**; this requirement is obsolete as an implementation plan. See ARCHITECTURE §16, “Workshop transcript summaries”.

- **Design (proposed, 2026-11-12):** [WORKSHOP.md](WORKSHOP.md) designs everything in this section, the Phase 6 parts of §7.3 and §10.3, and the plugin loader, as deltas from the build and reference-harness, with build units in its §9 and open questions in its §10. It promotes nothing here. [WORKSHOP-CONTEXT.md](WORKSHOP-CONTEXT.md) (proposed, 2026-11-12) designs the workshop's context window, long runs, history tools, preset structure, card and persona; built parts are in ARCHITECTURE §16, "Workshop context". It promotes nothing here either.
- **[D]** Agentic work outside the RP itself: creating cards with tools, editing and cloning them, changing settings, setting up chat modes, eventually writing plugins.
- **[D]** It needs a **clear UX distinction** within one shared UI, and it must be hard to slip into "Claude Code mode" from RP by accident. It ships preconfigured with an agent preset and a default assistant card the user can swap for any of their own.
- **[P]** Technically it's a chat configuration made of: **Unbuilt workshop configuration (Phase 6)**; registry/state groundwork is not a running workshop.
  - an output contract that allows several messages
  - **rollout continuity**: fork fresh per turn, or **resume the previous rollout** **[D]** (the timeline stays separate either way)
  - the meta toolset
- **[P]** The chat kind (RP or Workshop) is chosen at creation and can't be toggled afterwards. Workshops have a distinct accent, a badge and a capability strip. **Unbuilt Phase 6 proposal.**
- **[P]** An out-of-character side channel attached to an RP chat: a small workshop docked in a panel that can read the story but writes to its own timeline. **Unbuilt Phase 6 proposal.**
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-17, on the built rule that each side-chat message reads whatever branch the tab beside it shows) "This seems incoherent. How would this work given the workshop can be opened in its own pane, or even as the only chat in the center column? It's no longer in the sidebar, so it can no longer be considered to be coherently connected to any 'currently open chat'". Design: [WORKSHOP-BRANCHES.md](WORKSHOP-BRANCHES.md).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-17) "once the workshop is opened in the center-column in any way, there's no way to go from Workshop -> branch or chat it is connected to *at all*". The link back is job workshoppop1; WORKSHOP-BRANCHES §8 gives it the stored branch to name.
- **[D]** A side workshop is a parallel thread of one story branch (owner, 2026-11-17): "My proposal was that it follows the chat it's connected to, as if it was a parallel thread part of it. So, when you branch a chat, you branch its workshop, and the new branch is attached to the new branch. This way they evolve independently. if you go back to the original branch, the workshop is *as it was*, because it never was changed, even if you messaged its branch." Each workshop branch is paired with one story branch, stored; a message reads its paired branch up to its newest message when sent, wherever the workshop is shown.
- **[D]** A branch made at an earlier message gets the workshop as it was then by default (owner, 2026-11-17): "We have established that branching from an earlier point by default means that connected data is branched from the state it had at that point - so the workshop would be branched from its own earlier state, in this case. That said, I am not certain this is the correct/expected behaviour in practice." The workshop ends at its newest message sent while the story stood at or before the fork point; branching at the newest message copies it whole.
- **[D]** The user chooses in the fork dialog, as for notes (owner, 2026-11-17): "With author's notes, we explicitly let the user choose. I think we should consider asking for the workshop chat as well, maybe in the same UI (only if there is a workshop chat, and it changed since after the forking point, of course)". Branch from here's note dialog gets a row per attached workshop with later messages: As it was then (default) / As it is now, covered by the set-all switch; paths that never ask use the default (WORKSHOP-BRANCHES §4).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-17, answer "Ok." to the coordinator's recommendation) A workshop appears only beside the story branch it was started on and the branches made from that branch later; the story's existing sibling branches don't list it. Starting to talk on a sibling starts a separate workshop there.
- **[P]** (workshopbranchspec, 2026-11-17) One fork row per attached workshop per story branch at fork time, no copied rows (a lazily made branch can't match because fork labels fix what a fork inherits when its row is made); `workshop.paired` and `workshop.story_at` state events; the server resolves the story position and the browser's `storyAt` goes away; deleting a story branch bins its workshop branches with it; a branch switcher on the workshop, limited to the shown story branch's workshop branches in the side panel. WORKSHOP-BRANCHES §§2–8.
- **[O]** What a side workshop knows of the story without a tool call. Owner, 2026-11-17: "I think the user would expect it to know where the story is, just like the user can see it. We decided not to just dump all context into it… But I also don't want it to have to sort of, eat soup with a fork in order to reconstruct the story as it is. It's also kind of an issue if it has to constantly call tools to get story pieces in because I feel like it'd end up redundantly getting some of the same stuff in turn by turn whenever the story changes and you message it again. Not sure". On append-only story updates: "The problem is, I don't see how this is anything but 'dumping the entire story (as the RP model sees it) into the agent context' regardless. So I would hold off on this before we make a decision." Options listed neutrally in WORKSHOP-BRANCHES §9.
- **[P]** Permissions: **Unbuilt tool/plugin consent UX (Phase 6)**; imported data already cannot grant executable capabilities.
  - tools with external effects need approval
  - installing a plugin always needs confirmation
  - **[D]** Content the user loads (a card, a preset) is effectively their own prompt. If it steers the agent, that's user error, not our vulnerability. Agent capabilities don't depend on where content came from, because defending against the user's own prompt would hamstring the agent (owner, 2026-10-11).
  - **[P]** Import formats never carry capability grants: a card or preset file's own fields can't enable tools, plugins or permissions (Marinara's bug, §11). The user turns those on. **Built boundary** (ARCHITECTURE §8–§11); workshop capability/approval controls remain Phase 6 work.
- **[D]** The in-app coding agent isn't needed for daily use, since it's new compared to ST, "but I want to have it soon. This is going to be a core feature." (owner, 2026-11-07)
- **[P]** Every UI action is a typed server operation, so that later it can be exposed as an agent tool. **Built authenticated typed operation foundation** (ARCHITECTURE §2); uploads and cookie login also use HTTP. Exposing these as workshop tools is Phase 6 work.

---

- **[D] Decided (owner, 2026-11-13, calltool1):** for providers without cache-safe native loading, asked "build the dispatcher for those providers, or leave them on every tool from the start?", the owner answered "Ok, build it." TOOL-LOADING §11.1 option (b) is Decided; the rest retains its existing markings. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal:** fixed `call_tool`, canonical transcripts and validation, measured defaults and an untested per-connection override (ARCHITECTURE §16, TOOL-LOADING §15).

### 7.8 MCP servers

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16, relayed by the coordinator) MCP support is essential, for both agentic RP (story chats with tools) and the workshop, and each MCP connection can be enabled separately for each. Nothing of it is built yet.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16, corrections to the first MCP draft, relayed by the coordinator) No read-only restriction for stories and no gating by the server's annotations, which are self-declared hints and not trustworthy: enabling a connection for Stories or Workshops is the consent, and every allowed tool of an enabled connection is offered there. Per-tool allow lists stay; annotations are shown only as information. An interrupted call is never re-run silently. Every connection can be turned on or off per chat as an override, in story chats and workshops alike, whatever the account default. Build order: remote HTTP first, then sign-in (OAuth), local programs (stdio) last, keeping stdio admin-only behind the startup switch.
- **[P]** (mcpspec, 2026-11-16) The design is [MCP.md](MCP.md), as deltas from reference-harness `latest` (`src/mcp/`, `src/tools/side-effects.ts`) and from the built agent machinery: per-user connections in Settings → MCP servers, optionally shared by an admin; Streamable HTTP and SSE, local programs (stdio) for admins only where the instance allows them from outside the app, keys as host-bound credentials and sign-in with the server; account defaults for Stories and Workshops, overridden either way per branch in This chat and per workshop in the capability chip, with a per-tool allow list; nothing sent to a server before a use the user enabled; one `mcp` effect class for every MCP tool, offered in both kinds once enabled, with no approval for a fresh call; a receipt for every call and a prompt before re-running an interrupted one; results through the data-only boundary; a new MCP call ledger. It promotes nothing here beyond the owner's statements above.

### 7.9 Files and the agent's environment

All owner statements below are from 2026-11-17 unless dated otherwise, relayed by the coordinator. The design is [FILES-AND-ENVIRONMENT.md](FILES-AND-ENVIRONMENT.md) (proposed, filesspec). It promotes nothing beyond these entries. Nothing here is built.

- **[D]** No per-session setup: "We can't have that you need to do a bunch of manual work for every session for basic things. I don't open a claude code session and enable internet access and reset it to an empty folder with nothing else." Tools are available, the network is available by default, and the work persists.
- **[D]** Persistent user files: "Users must be able to put documents and files in the agent's environment that the agent can read, edit and modify. This doesn't just mean chat attachments; those are a crude way to give a few files at a time to the agent that has no notion of folders and organization."
- **[D]** Attachments: "Message attachments (which can be of any type, and both text files and images and videos, must have native support - allowing you to open them when you attach them) are part of it. For a workshop agent with file access, it would have to be able to see these as files too - not just as blocks passed to it (only images would generally do that) with a path in its folders." And: "the agent needs to see what folder they're in, and have automatic access to them. reference-harness does have that too."
- **[D]** "The issue with one sandbox per workshop chat is that there's no way to upload files and folders that can be seen repeatedly, or to pick up work from them later."
- **[D]** The in-app coding agent "is going to be a core feature" (2026-11-07, §7.7). The workshop agent is a coding agent and a general-purpose agent (2026-11-13; relayed as a paraphrase, not a verbatim quote).
- **[D]** The working folder: "we would probably have its top folder by default. I don't see how else this works. Then the user can at any time change its working directory. This part may be unlike claude code."
- **[D]** Its picker: "it may make sense just to adopt the same UX. Why can't we have another thing that opens at the bottom, where you select the working directory? It's how coding agents work. we have space next to model and preset."
- **[D]** Remembered per card: "this would be remembered per-character card. So the same character gets the same folder preselected."
- **[D]** No folder wall unless justified: "I am not sure if we need to specifically restrict agents from operating outside their assigned folder, I think this needs to be justified, since the environment as a whole is sandboxed." Separation between different users' files stays required. The earlier statement it supersedes as the working rule: "there's still a use case for segregating folders somehow, but I am not sure how to do it. Claude Code *can* be opened in specific folders per-project too, and can be made not to look into other folders."
- **[D]** Open WebUI as a reference for its shell feature only: "OWUI's agentic features are, in general, an absolute disaster that should not be imitated. But OWUI's shell feature does have a few neat points". The points taken are switching it on or off in any chat, and a right side panel to explore the folders the agent can access, see paths, open, delete, upload and rename files.
- **[D]** Later: "a user should also be able to open the folder on their system if the harness is running on their PC, but that would be an exception, not the rule (and something we can work out later when we have a desktop packaged version of the app)".
- **[D]** The UI doesn't block the rest: "file management can work from the web UI (although, a file management web UI is obviously a followup or parallel work to having folder/sandbox support in general; the point is it can't slow down or block it or anything else)".
- **[D]** The sandbox is cross-platform: "My main concern is how do we sandbox? Remember this isn't like reference-harness where it can rely on docker and compose. We will package this as a linux/windows/macos desktop app too with something like electron, and it has to 'just work', not just a linux server app. This is the first platform-specific component."
- **[D]** Tools are the app's own: "This cannot be a requirement. We can just ship whatever we need ourselves and/or have it be retrieved during setup." (on the agent only having host-installed tools)
- **[D]** No bundled Linux VM: "This is never going to be acceptable. Forget about it". That rules out WSL2, Lima and Virtualization.framework, Firecracker and krun, and gVisor as a VM.
- **[D]** Host folders and running without the sandbox should be considered: "do we let users manually select folders outside our designated sandbox directory? This would only be possible in some circumstances: desktop packaged app; admin on server version; etc. But why not allow selecting any folder on the host? Whether or not this makes sense would depend on the sandbox, but we should consider allowing users to not use the sandbox in these cases if they don't want to. Then it just becomes like Claude Code."
- **[P]** (filesspec) The design: per-account Files on disk with versions, bin and sharing; a branch-owned working folder, picked by a strip chip after Model and Preset and remembered per card; OS-native sandboxes (bubblewrap and Seatbelt through Anthropic's sandbox-runtime, AppContainer and job objects on Windows) behind a replaceable `commandSandboxes` registry; a pinned toolchain the app ships or fetches; network on by default to public addresses only; host folders and an unsandboxed mode for desktop users and permitted server admins, never for regular server accounts; any-type attachments in workshops saved into the working folder; one additive migration. It replaces WORKSHOP §1.2's "No shell".

### 7.10 Subagents

All owner statements below are from 2026-11-17, relayed by the coordinator. The design is [SUBAGENTS.md](SUBAGENTS.md) (proposed, subagentspec). It promotes nothing beyond these entries. Nothing here is built.

- **[D]** "Another thing we haven't designed yet, is subagents."
- **[D]** "reference-harness never got subagents, because every session already starts from a single message, in parallel with others, within the same chat."
- **[D]** "Claude code can also message its agents continuously."
- **[D]** "the agent needs to be able to choose a different model for subagents if needed, but I am not sure if we want every single added model to be available."
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-17, on S2, a read-only child checking a draft reply against lore, notes and summaries before it is sent) "Yes this is an obvious use case. Although the main use case for subagent is workshop chats, which use it like claude code." S2 is accepted; the later entry below folds it and S3 into one story helper and drops S1.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-17, "Ok." to the coordinator's recommendation) S2 and S3 become one story helper: a read-only child on a cheap model that the story agent can call either to look something up (S3) or to check a draft against lore, notes and summaries (S2). S1 is dropped: with separate contexts, a character querying another's context would leak knowledge it shouldn't have (metagaming), and narrators and decision models already see everything.
- **[P]** (subagentspec) A subagent is a child rollout started by the parent run's `agent` tool: its own context (the task, or a fork of the parent's), tools never beyond the parent's, one level deep, a model chosen by alias from a list the user allows (default: the chat's model, plus its summary model where the chat already sends to it; allowing a model is the consent for its connection), spend caps per subagent and per run, and its report returned as quoted tool data. Shown as a collapsed row in the parent's Steps with Stop and its own inspector; nested Stop and Resume never re-run an effect silently. Parent and subagent message each other (`message_agent`, `report_progress`); a follow-up to a finished subagent carries its run on. Recommended over agent-started visible threads, which stay a later "Open as thread". Workshops first; three story uses are proposals for the owner. One additive migration (three `rollouts` columns).

## 8. LCM (hierarchical summarization)

- **[D], 2026-11-03:** “summarization should be able to set its own preset, in addition to being able to set its own model.” (owner). **Built (summarypreset; obsolete as an implementation requirement):** account defaults and per-chat overrides select the current preset, a specific preset, or instruction and messages only; unavailable specific presets fall back to the current one with a warning. ARCHITECTURE §16 records the implementation.

- **[D]** This is the main feature: chats of unlimited length with a fixed, predictable, cache-friendly context budget, keeping all original messages and every intermediate summary. The agent can drill down and search.
- **[D] Baseline:** reference-harness ARCHITECTURE.md §9b:
  - summarization sessions reuse the agent factory, model, persona and rendered prefix
  - rich, compact and summary tiers, with target/max hysteresis
  - eager generation threshold below the compact maximum
  - use / wait / omit
  - condensation cascade
  - failure placeholders
  - lineage tables, `expand_summary`, summary search
- **[D]** The owner's manual ST workflow already works:
  1. hide messages up to the end of the last summary
  2. keep the summaries in context
  3. ask the model to summarize the chat since then, with the full preset loaded

  Some frontier models took the summary request for a prompt injection until a line acknowledging summary instructions was added to the preset. That's acceptable under the compatibility contract.
- **[D]** Normally LCM runs in response to user messages, and it doesn't have to run immediately on large imported chats.
- **[D]** **LCM also works in the plain, non-agentic chat mode:** the drop-in replacement for ST's chatting, with LCM added. The difference from agentic mode is that there's no drilling down into summaries and no search of the original text. The model sees only the top-level summaries in the context (owner, 2026-10-12).
- **[D]** **Summarizing through the user's own preset** must be supported. reference-harness's summarizer is a model for ours and may be the default. But, as with ST's summarization, running the summary as a normal conversation turn is likely to work very well, and chat-mode models may have no tool support (owner, 2026-10-12):
  - the conversation is cut off at the point to summarize to
  - a summarization instruction is appended as a system message, where the provider supports a system message mid-chat (Anthropic, among others), and as a user message otherwise
  - the model's reply is the summary
  - it shares the cache prefix with the normal conversation, as reference-harness's does
- **[D]** **Refusal detection** is needed on this surface, so a refusal is never saved as a summary. **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02): "A refused summary keeps its own ‘Retry with…’ model picker." The removal of message retry pickers (§3.1) does not apply to summaries. reference-harness has one, which may need refining for our cases later. Refusal detection is useful for every message, not only summaries (owner, 2026-10-12).

- **[D]** The summary divider and the summary panel have a toggle for what the timeline shows before the summary line: the original messages, or the summaries themselves in the chat. It can be switched at any time, including while previewing a search and replace (owner, 2026-10-26). It is saved per account: the toggle belongs to the chat viewer itself, not to the chat or anything about it (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[P]** "Summaries in the chat" shows the layer the model actually reads, in path order, each in place of the messages it covers, with its level and range; each expands to its messages (a level above 1 to the summaries it condenses). So the timeline can show exactly what the model sees. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) A message a summary covers shows the summary icon, not the word "Summarized" (in a long chat the word repeated on hundreds of messages). The icon stays, because it carries the hover explanation. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02) When a redo of a summary the user edited fails, the user's text stays in use, so the failure doesn't count on the chat's divider (it showed "1 failed" for good): it's listed only in the Summaries tab under Failed, with Retry. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §16).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-10-27) The owner's actual ST method is the "Sum2" Quick Reply: pick a message range, hide everything outside it, generate **with the full preset** and an instruction ("Pause your roleplay. Context usage has reached a critical level… If there is already a summary, your new summary should start where the previous one stopped…"), unhide, post the result. ST's Summarize extension offers the same choice: "Classic" (full context plus the instruction, optionally without lorebook and Author's Note) or "Raw" (the instruction as system prompt plus the messages).
- **[D]** Catching up a chat that has no summaries is done up front; the main cost is reading the context (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** Summarizing must hit the chat's cache prefix: breakpoints set carefully where applicable, working as well for Anthropic and OpenAI models called natively as through OpenRouter or AWS (reference-harness is set up for this). Continuous summarizing should then be cheap and relatively rare (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** A separate summarization model may make sense in some cases (the owner doesn't usually use one: for a first catch-up you can switch model, summarize, switch back). How to configure it is open and can be deferred if its UX is too hard (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D] Rejected:** `{{summary}}` silently reading the imported summary whatever its note's switch (the build's behaviour): ad-hoc logic the user can't see or turn off. The summary is a note like any other (owner, 2026-10-27).
- **[D]** The summarization settings must be designed properly (owner, 2026-10-27).

- **[D]** The summarization settings (owner confirmed 2026-10-27), per fork, with an account default for new chats, in the right panel's Chat tab:
  - on/off;
  - with the full preset (default; the owner's Sum2) or without it (instruction plus messages only);
  - the instruction, editable (default close to the owner's Sum2 wording, neutral);
  - **recent messages kept as written: a maximum and a target** (above the maximum, the oldest are summarized until the recent part is back at the target), reference-harness's hysteresis as already decided above and built as stepped history;
  - **the summary block: a maximum and a target** (above the maximum, the oldest summaries are condensed into higher levels until it's back at the target);
  - the total context limit stays with the model's settings; the section shows when the pairs don't fit inside it;
  - when it runs: with the next reply, or right after one while the cache is warm;
  - under Advanced: chunk size and summary length.
  - Rejected: a manual "summarize up to here". The point is automation, and it can't work with several levels.
- **[P]** Imported ST summaries are plain notes: "Summary (from ST)", switched on if ST was sending it (injected or through `{{summary}}` in the active preset), controlled only by its switch. The preset editor marks each `{{summary}}` with what it fills in, and the note says the preset includes it. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §13, §16).
- **[D]** Default sizes (owner, 2026-10-28): recent messages target 12,000 and maximum 20,000 tokens; the summaries target 14,000 and maximum 18,000. Summaries cover unbounded history and recent chat grows faster, so recent messages get the wider gap. Chats that never changed the sizes they copied follow the new defaults; sizes a user set stay.
- **[D]** Summaries are **separate blocks placed like the chat history**, usually just before it, so a new summary never invalidates the ones before it. `{{summary}}` exists only for an imported ST chat's single summary note text: it inserts the chat's switched-on summary notes, never the summaries (owner, 2026-10-28, correcting the build, where `{{summary}}` held the summaries).
- **[D]** A summary request doesn't send the chat's notes: the current notes may describe events after the summarized range. A setting "include notes in summary requests", per chat over the account default, is **off by default**. A snapshot of the notes at the cutoff may come later (owner, 2026-10-28).
- **[D]** Switching summarization on in a chat with an imported ST summary note switches the note off in the same step, visibly on the note; the user can switch it back on, and the note then says it's also sent beside the summaries (owner, 2026-10-28).
- **[P]** The summary length is set in tokens and the instruction asks for it in words, about 0.75 words per token: models follow word counts more reliably (PHASE1-DECISIONS §5 default). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[P]** An ST chat whose Summarize extension was disabled imports its summary note switched off, since ST sent nothing (PHASE1-DECISIONS §5 default: keep). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §13, §16).
- **[P]** Lowering a maximum to its target or below lowers the target with it, at the same share of the maximum, and says so (PHASE1-DECISIONS §7.33). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §16).

### 8.1 Imported summaries
- **[D]** Importing a chat also imports its summary, if it has one. **This is required before the owner starts using the harness** (owner, 2026-10-12).
- **[D]** The custom single-field summary is kept in its own field, even where LCM makes it technically obsolete. A chat that already has a summary keeps it.
- **[D]** When a chat has a run of messages hidden continuously from message 0, and a summary covering them, the summary can act as an L1 summary over those hidden messages.
- **[D]** LCM is **not enabled by default** on imported chats, but enabling it is easy and obvious. On enabling it, the user chooses: keep the summary as the L1 covering that range, or redo LCM from scratch. Either way, the summary stays in its own field.
- **[O]** Details to work out (owner, 2026-10-12): **Answered/built** (ARCHITECTURE §13, §16): `extra.memory` and extension settings, continuous-hidden-run coverage, note placement, enable/adopt/backfill UI; obsolete as an implementation question.
  - where ST keeps the summary (its Summarize extension stores it on messages, `extra.memory`, with injection settings in `extension_settings.memory`)
  - how "covering the hidden run" is detected
  - how the summary is placed in the context before LCM is enabled (proposed: as ST placed it, so an imported chat sends what ST sent)
  - what the UI shows
- **[D]** **Semi-background idle-time jobs:** run LCM work in the gap after a reply, while the cache is still warm, which also refreshes it (depending on settings and provider).
- **[P]** Summaries cover **path segments**, so threads and branches share summaries up to their fork. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §16). Shared immutable material has per-fork selections, freshness and jobs; multi-parent thread UX is still later work.
- **[P]** Editing a message already covered by a summary marks that summary stale and re-summarizes it lazily. Mechanical edits such as renames are applied to the summaries directly. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.13, §16).
  - **[D]** Re-summarizing is never triggered by the edit itself: an edit or swipe must not start a generation. If it's automatic, it runs with the next reply (owner, 2026-10-13).
  - **[O]** How branches, forks and edits interact with shared summaries has to be worked out from what the user sees and expects, not only from what's correct (owner, 2026-10-13). So does how the UI shows which summary covers which messages, and where "re-summarize" lives. **Answered by later owner rules** (§3.4, §10.3) and built per-fork summary selections, revisions, range/rail views and rerun controls (ARCHITECTURE §16); owner UX acceptance remains unverified.
- **[D]** A summary that fails (for example, the model keeps refusing) leaves the original messages in the context for as long as they fit; no placeholder replaces them until there's no room (owner, 2026-10-13).
- **[D]** LCM is on by default for new chats (owner, 2026-10-13). Imported chats stay off by default, as above.
- **[D]** Both summarizer styles are supported: the reply itself is the summary (direct output, which chat-only models without tool calling need), or the model writes it through tools as reference-harness does (owner, 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-13).
- **[P]** Summaries are **editable**, with versions. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §16).
- **[D]** Memory retrieval of *some* sort will be a feature. LCM is already a form of RAG (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** Assume vector search will exist somewhere eventually. Plain embedding search over all messages is of doubtful value on its own (owner, 2026-10-10). sqlite-vec already covers vector search, but only by brute force; an indexed ANN search is a benefit of alternatives such as Turso, not a requirement.
- **[P]** Search tools combine full-text and vector results with reciprocal rank fusion (RRF). reference-harness's memory retrieval (`spec/MEMORY-RETRIEVAL.md`) and its RRF evaluations are the baseline. **Unbuilt** vector/embedding/RRF retrieval proposal; FTS history search is built (ARCHITECTURE §3.13), not this Phase 4 retrieval tool.
- **[D]** The UI shows where verbatim context ends and which summaries stand in for older history. **[P]** Three layers: **Built** all three proposed surfaces (ARCHITECTURE §12, §15, §16).
  1. an always-on **boundary divider** in the stream, which opens the current summaries
  2. an optional **coverage rail** next to the messages, with bars per summary by level, stale marks, and click to open
  3. the **context view**: the exact assembled request with token counts and cache breakpoints, plus "the context this message was generated from" for each message

---

## 9. Connections and models

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, modelpicker): "When you open it to select a model, you want to be able to find specific models quickly, and to switch between a set of models you've been using - potentially from different connections. Connections can on their own add *thousands* of models - OpenRouter for example - so the model selector must work in a way as to not make one connection with thousands of models compromise its utility or make it hard to find models, for example offering ordering by last used, showing pinned favourites at the top or in their own tab, or allowing easy filtering by connection (a useful thing in general), etc."
- **[P]** (modelpicker, implementation choices): one registered model picker across reply, account, fallback summarizer, first-run and decision selections; pins first across connections, recent selections and reply use, an always-visible connection filter, punctuation-tolerant fuzzy search over id/name/prefix, connection groups and measured windowing. Pins, recent selections and sort live in account settings. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal:** ARCHITECTURE §15.

- **[D]** No ST-style connection profiles. **A connection is one wire type, one provider and one set of settings.** You switch models within a connection; otherwise you switch connections.
- **[D]** Every provider type has an **overridable base URL**. No special "proxy" concept: a provider's native model picker stays whatever the address.
- **[D]** Model lists are fetched from the configured upstream. Users can add and edit models for every provider type.
- **[P]** User-added and edited models have capability fields: context length, reasoning, prefill support, cache mode, TTL (like reference-harness's `compat` flags). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6).
- **[D]** No coupling between presets and connections, except an optional one-way "preset override" field on a connection.
- **[P]** Model choice is a (connection, model) pair, inherited through the same kind of chain as presets but completely separate from them. The summarizer can have its own model. **Partial/overtaken**: pair and independent selection built (ARCHITECTURE §6, §15); the inherited chain is superseded by §6.1’s global defaults. A dedicated steady summarizer-model control remains open, explicitly deferrable under §8; retry/fallback is built.
- **[P]** Several connections of the same type are allowed. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6).
- **[P]** API keys are write-only: stored on the server and never sent back to the browser. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.12, §6).
- **[D]** Keys are stored once as credentials that connections share, and are sent only to the hosts they were entered for. Moving a key to a new host takes typing it again. Keys are protected more than chats because they have economic value: easy key theft makes the frontend worth attacking, as with ST (owner, 2026-10-11).
- **[D]** Most settings are configurable from inside the app, so the user's agent can change them. Security settings are the exception, changeable only from outside (environment or config); host binding for keys is the first (owner, 2026-10-11).
- **[D]** A provider kind is an upstream API as that upstream defines it, endpoints included. The base URL moves the whole API; there are no per-endpoint URLs (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** Day-one kinds include OpenAI Chat Completions and OpenAI Responses, Anthropic, and DeepSeek at minimum (its reasoning has agentic quirks). OpenAI Chat Completions is the default, listed first, then Anthropic (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[P]** (providerchoice) A kind is a provider's API type. Chat Completions is the first and default API type wherever a provider offers it (OpenAI, OpenAI-compatible). A connection made by kind alone, as by the ST import, gets the provider its kind and address imply (Chat Completions: OpenAI without an address, a custom server with one).
- **[D]** **A new connection starts from its provider** (owner, 2026-11-02: "Users should just be able to select something like 'Anthropic (AWS)' and know everything is being done according to what that needs, for caching and everything else (eg, token accounting quirks)."; 2026-11-07: "Isn't this just a provider and API type? Why wouldn't this be the first choice when setting up a new connection? This is as basic as anything gets."). The provider is the form's first field, followed by the fields that provider needs. It sets the API type, the default address, how the key authenticates, the caching layout and capabilities (lookback, marker budget, minimum cacheable size, TTL, `prompt_cache_key`), token accounting and compatibility shims. Each derived setting is a default the user can override in an advanced section. A connection stores its provider id and the user's overrides only. Providers are a registry, replaceable by id; shims and connections key off it. **Built** (providerchoice; ARCHITECTURE §3.12, §6 "Providers").
- **[P]** (providerchoice; vertexbedrock 2026-11-07) The built-in providers, in picker order: OpenAI (Chat Completions, the default API type, or Responses), Anthropic, Anthropic (AWS Bedrock), Anthropic (Google Vertex), OpenAI (AWS Bedrock), OpenRouter, DeepSeek, Google AI Studio, OpenAI-compatible (custom: the address is required, the key optional, and no `prompt_cache_key` unless turned on), then the decision model. Anthropic defaults to automatic markers with lookback. Bedrock defaults to markers at each section without lookback, so the previous history marker is repeated (reference-harness's measured exact-position matching), and takes a Bedrock API key or an AWS access key (SigV4). Its Region sets the address. OpenAI sends the chat's `prompt_cache_key`. The default selection is the first provider, OpenAI with Chat Completions, following the 2026-10-10 [D] above. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §6).
- **[P]** (vertexbedrock, owner 2026-11-07: "OpenAI and OpenAI (AWS) have different cache semantics, for example, and other differences … There's also Anthropic (GCS)") **OpenAI (AWS Bedrock)**: OpenAI's Responses (first and default) or Chat Completions API as Bedrock's runtime serves it at `bedrock-runtime.{region}.amazonaws.com/openai/v1`, with the Anthropic Bedrock provider's two keys and SigV4 code. pi-ai's OpenAI adapters build the request; a transport in the controlled fetch signs it or sends the Bedrock API key, and turns AWS's errors into OpenAI's. Models are pi-ai's Bedrock catalog's OpenAI IDs by inference profile (`us.`, `global.`; base IDs of closed models aren't served on the runtime), and GPT OSS on Chat Completions only. **Built against recorded shapes only; not yet checked live** (ARCHITECTURE §6 "OpenAI on AWS Bedrock").
- **[P]** (vertexbedrock) **Anthropic (Google Vertex)**: Anthropic's Messages API at `…/projects/{project}/locations/{region}/publishers/anthropic/models/{model}:streamRawPredict`, the body without `model` and with `anthropic_version: "vertex-2023-10-16"`. The Region (global by default; us, eu or one region) and the project (the key file's by default) build the address. The key is a service account's JSON key file, stored encrypted as other keys are; the server signs a JWT with it, exchanges it at Google's fixed token endpoint for an access token and keeps that in memory until a minute before it expires. The key itself is never sent. Model IDs are Anthropic's catalog's with `@` before the date. **Built against recorded shapes only; not yet checked live** (ARCHITECTURE §6 "Anthropic on Google Vertex").
- **[P]** (vertexbedrock) Each provider's cache rule and its source. "Unverified" means neither reference-harness nor the provider documents it, so the default is the conservative one.

  | Provider | Cache rule (the connection's defaults) | Source |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | OpenAI | Automatic, best effort; no markers; the chat's `prompt_cache_key`; 1,024-token minimum; 5 minutes, 30 for GPT-5.5 and later | platform.openai.com/docs/guides/prompt-caching; liveapi F8 |
  | OpenAI (AWS Bedrock), GPT-5.6 and later on Responses | `prompt_cache_breakpoint: {mode: "explicit"}` on `input_text` blocks at each section; no lookback (a request "reads the cache only if it starts with an entire earlier request"), so the previous history marker is repeated; four writes per request, one left for the automatic marker on the latest message; 1,024 cumulative tokens per marker; at least 30 minutes; writes 1.25× input; `prompt_cache_key` sent | docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html, …/model-card-openai-gpt-56-luna.html; reference-harness ARCHITECTURE.md "Cache control" |
  | OpenAI (AWS Bedrock), GPT-5.5 and earlier, GPT OSS, and all of Chat Completions | Automatic only, best effort: markers stripped (shims), the estimate calibrated from reported reads; 1,024 minimum; no write fee | docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html. **Unverified:** whether markers on Chat Completions are refused or ignored, and the TTL for earlier models |
  | Anthropic | `cache_control` markers, automatic layout, lookback about 20 blocks; four markers; 5 minutes or 1 hour | platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching |
  | Anthropic (AWS Bedrock) | Markers at each section, no lookback (previous history marker repeated); four markers; 5 minutes or 1 hour where the model offers it | reference-harness spec/LATE-INPUT.md "Cache probes" (measured), ARCHITECTURE.md "Cache control"; docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html |
  | Anthropic (Google Vertex) | As Anthropic: automatic layout with lookback; four markers; 5 minutes or 1 hour (not Claude 3.x); writes 1.25× (2× for 1 hour), reads 0.1× | docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/partner-models/claude/prompt-caching ("behaves as described in the Anthropic documentation"); platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/claude-on-vertex-ai. **Unverified:** lookback on Vertex (documented only by that reference, not measured) |
  | OpenRouter | Per upstream: Claude and Gemini `cache_control` markers; OpenAI automatic | openrouter.ai/docs/features/prompt-caching; liveapi F3 to F5 |
- **[P]** (providerchoice) Bedrock speaks the Messages API through InvokeModelWithResponseStream: pi-ai's Anthropic adapter builds the request, and a transport in the controlled fetch moves it to Bedrock's address, body and signing, then turns AWS's event stream back into Anthropic's events. pi-ai's own Bedrock adapter (Converse, through the AWS SDK) isn't used, because it takes no fetch and reads ambient AWS configuration. Bedrock's models are pi-ai's Bedrock catalog's Claude IDs; listing Bedrock's models is another AWS service the key isn't sent to. **Built against recorded shapes only; not yet checked live** (ARCHITECTURE §6 "Anthropic on AWS Bedrock").
- **[D]** The server secret that encrypts keys is generated automatically; setup must be trivial (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** An instance-wide outbound proxy (HTTP or SOCKS) for every request the harness makes, LLM calls included, with a bypass list of IP ranges that contains the local ranges by default, and a per-connection override as a collapsed advanced option. Local addresses aren't blocked by default: local models are normal (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** An admin can force the instance proxy on everyone, so non-admins can't send requests direct from the admin's machine and IP (owner, 2026-10-11).
- **[D]** Every LLM request is tracked: uncached input, cache reads, cache writes and output, normalized across providers' reporting quirks. Cost is computed from that by formulas: built in for every supported provider, overridable by users, conditional on model, upstream and context size, with a currency label that's only for display (owner, 2026-10-10). **Built (U12, 2026-10-28):** per-user declarative rates, conditions, context tiers and currency labels; implementation in ARCHITECTURE.md §6.
- **[D]** Later, and deliberately small: connections an admin shares with all or chosen users. Their URL, key and proxy stay hidden; the admin can optionally restrict their models; per-user cost limits are optional. Connections are per user by default (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** Nothing goes to a provider the user didn't choose (§11, owner, 2026-11-02): a connection receives a chat only when the user switched to it and submitted; switching, opening a chat or editing sends nothing. Token counting through a provider, its own or another connection's, is a per-connection choice, off by default (§4).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-07, liveapi) Every provider detail is checked against the real APIs, Anthropic and OpenAI first party especially, caching above all, with the owner's own keys: "RP is not against ToS for OAI or Anthropic, so long as the content is SFW." Content is synthetic test fixtures only, never the owner's data; spend stays small (cheap models, OpenRouter capped). "Most people who use Claude for this use Bedrock or OpenRouter," so routes are checked too. Prompt shapes that normal features and preset settings produce must never trip a provider's role rules (Gemini is strict); where one does, specific models on specific providers get their own compatibility shims.
- **[P]** (liveapi) The checks are a suite, `npm run test:live`, outside `npm run check`; it drives the app's own server through its RPCs, with a recording pass-through in front of each provider's API. **Built:** ARCHITECTURE §1. The shims for what it found are proposals in its report, not built.
- **[P]** (livefix, caching) Default layouts cache what the next request repeats: Anthropic's automatic layout moves pi-ai's last marker to the assembly's endpoint before the end-of-request instruction; OpenRouter Claude and Gemini get `cache_control` markers in both layouts (Gemini one, at the deepest point, since OpenRouter uses only its last); OpenAI-kind connections always send the chat's `prompt_cache_key` (since providerchoice: the OpenAI provider does by default, a custom server only when turned on). The estimate claims nothing warm below the model's documented minimum cacheable size, and nothing for OpenRouter Claude without markers; for OpenAI's best-effort cache it is calibrated from the reads the provider reports (no more than the last read; a change re-sends everything). **Built:** ARCHITECTURE §6 "Default cache markers", §15.
- **[P]** (livefix, compatibility) What a provider refuses or needs for some of its models is a registered compatibility shim per provider (since providerchoice; per provider kind before), optionally API type, and model pattern (shims.ts), replaceable by id: it can set and fix capabilities and rewrite the request. Built-ins for the live suite's F7, F9, F10 (Chat Completions GPT-5.4 with tools sends effort none, as OpenAI's error suggests; the model page says a Responses connection keeps reasoning), F11 and F13 (OpenRouter Gemini's prefill fixed off). A model added by id gets the kind catalog's price and flags. A reply with no text after its prefill fails, on every provider, so the error shows and Retry is offered. **Built:** ARCHITECTURE §6 "Compatibility shims".
- **[P]** (liveapi2) Only DeepSeek's own host (`api.deepseek.com`) is held to its `/beta` address for a prefill; another host is a router or compatible server that decides where a `prefix: true` request goes, so Continue sends the prefix there. An OpenRouter model's routing change (`provider`) reads as a changed prefix in the cache estimate, since each provider caches on its own. **Built:** ARCHITECTURE §15 "Continue", warmth.ts.
- **[P]** (liveapi2) Shims for one OpenRouter upstream: a compatibility shim may also name OpenRouter provider slugs, and applies when the model's routing pins (`only`) to those. Live, the same model differed by upstream: DeepSeek V4.1 Flash took a prefill on DeepInfra and Fireworks, but on Together the reply came back as reasoning with empty text; MiMo V2.6 Pro took one on DeepInfra, but restarted the reply on Xiaomi and StreamLake. First users would be "no prefill" for those pairs, so Continue uses the nudge. Capability shims apply when models are listed, but routing is a model setting resolved in the assembly worker, so this needs the routing in the shim lookup. **Built:** ARCHITECTURE §6 "Compatibility shims" (route rules): DeepSeek via Together and MiMo via StreamLake take no prefill, MiMo via Xiaomi is unreliable with one; the model page lists the pinned route's rules, and for an unpinned route says where prefill fails.
- **[P]** (liveapi2) Pinned pricing: a pinned route's cost uses that provider's own price from OpenRouter's public endpoint list (`GET /models/{author}/{slug}/endpoints`, per-endpoint `pricing`: prompt, completion, cache read and write, discount, prompt-length overrides) rather than the model's headline price (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash lists $0.051/M input; pinned to Together or Fireworks, turns billed 1.4 to 3.7 times the listed price × usage, and pinned to DeepInfra 0.7 to 1.3 times it). An earlier note that the listing was unavailable was wrong for the public API. A failed fetch falls back to the model's general price with a visible note. **Built:** ARCHITECTURE §6 "Pinned pricing".
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) The usage ledger stores, with each priced request, the rates it was priced at (input, cache read, cache write, long cache write, output, per million tokens) and the network route it took (direct, or which proxy, and why). A closed request's cost can be recomputed only when it was priced from rates (a price table, an override or a price endpoint), never one the provider reported. Prices fetched from a provider or price endpoint will live where model prices live now, with no new table. **Built** (schemafinal; ARCHITECTURE §3.12).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16, pricespec) The app knows the prices of the main models on the providers we support; users override only in specific cases, and Settings → Costs stays the override place.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) A past request keeps the price it was charged at: prices of open models on aggregators change by provider and over time. Correcting a wrong built-in or override price may replace estimates on past rows priced from the table or overrides, never provider-reported charges. A per-request snapshot of the rates used is added by its own schema job.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) We keep known prices current ourselves: built-in tables updated with releases, and providers' public price data queried where an API exists (OpenRouter's model-list pricing and per-endpoint pricing).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) A generic price endpoint extension: a connection can opt in, per connection, to fetch prices from its base URL; nothing is sent without that opt-in. Fetched prices then win over our known prices for that connection, allowing dynamic price adjustment (mainly for people running a gateway for downstream users). We define the format and publish it, since nobody has standardized one.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) Which models need a built-in price: "For the major closed source labs, we need prices for *every model that is still available for inference* at the very minimum."
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) Precedence: "Different connections can have totally different prices." A price the user sets on a specific connection beats that connection's fetched prices. An account-wide or per-connection-type price never overrides a connection's own fetched prices. Fetched prices are per connection.
- **[P]** (pricespec) The format (`GET {base}/v1/prices`), detection, refresh policy, formula order and ledger recording: [PRICES.md](PRICES.md).
- **[P]** The provider layer's design is in [PROVIDERS.md](PROVIDERS.md). **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6). General agent-loop integration remains Phase 4 work.

---

## 10. Web app, platform and stack

### 10.1 Web app
- **[D]** (owner brief, 2026-11-06) “The left panel's, Home's and Add characters' card-list sorts move from browser storage into the per-account settings (the `display` setting, like the switchers' sorts), so every list's sort is remembered on the server.” **Built** (newflows, ARCHITECTURE §5). Existing URL choices still describe a particular view; an unqualified visit starts from the account's saved sort.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-06, settingsdb): "Having five settings tables is insane." Consolidate account, app and device preferences in one `settings(scope, scope_id, key, value_json, revision, updated_at)` table, with each setting's scope and schema declared in a registered, replaceable registry that plugins can extend. Writes validate through the registry and use the common revision for stale replacements. Workbench layout uses its device id; per-chat/per-fork state belongs to chat state. Built; implementation details and the rule prohibiting new settings tables are in ARCHITECTURE §3.7.

- **[D] Split view with tabs: the workbench** (owner, 2026-11-05): “There's currently no way to show two chats side by side. Many other things *can* be done side by side, such as two presets or lorebooks, as a simple side effect of having more or less independent sidebar and main column editors. ... it's a big complaint with ST that copying a few preset prompts from one preset to another is genuinely a lot of work that usually involves notepad in the middle. Same with cards and other things.” “currently opening something in the center column stacks it on top of the chat, and there's no system to manage it, I believe it just works by always replacing what's on top of the chat, and essentially having two layers.” “the system that solves all of these UI actions is essentially that of a modern code editor like VS code, with split view with tabs. The two columns on the side would continue to exist as they currently do, just like code editors have side columns, and for now the image-viewer would also open in its own extra column as it does until we figure out how to properly incorporate it. But the center view (and potentially the entire view depending on what's open) would be splittable into different frames that hold tabs for anything you open. A tab for a chat, a full size editor of something, etc. And then a couple buttons on the top header for splitting along a vertical line (and optionally a horizontal one). Tabs can be dragged between frames, frames are resizable by hand, tabs can be closed and clicked, etc.” “It would also maintain the semantics of editors where if you open a file it first gets opened in a sort of temporary tab that is replaced by the next tab you open, unless you either double click that tab or click again where you opened that item (not sure about the exact semantics in VS code, but you know what I mean). This is to avoid opening 10 times resulting in 10 stray tabs to manually close.”
- **[D] The chat header stays** (owner, 2026-11-05), on the center header (multi-character portrait, cast subtitle, rename in the title, branch control, pin branch, the chat's ⋯ menu): “I like the way it looks, it has just the right amount of information ... it's one of the main surfaces of the app.”
- **[D] Openers are links** (owner, 2026-11-05): “most UI elements that open centre column things (eg, chat/branch/character buttons on the sidebar/homepage) should really be links - so they can be opened in new tabs directly, which using them is the whole point of having browser navigation”
- **[D] Build it** (owner, 2026-11-05): “If we can make it look more or less how it does now by default, maybe with an extra few buttons to start the split and tab stuff, and slightly different behaviours to accommodate a somewhat different model, I don't see why we shouldn't do it. Even if I don't like some design aspects, I'll just ask to change them. I have given a pretty specific reference for how it should behave and feel.”
- **[P]** Direction (coordinator, agreed with the owner in chat, 2026-11-05; built by workbench): one item and no split looks as before plus the split buttons in the header; the tab strip appears once a frame has two or more tabs, or the view is split. Tabs are a slim strip (small portrait or icon, title, close); each tab's own header (the chat header, an editor's) stays inside the frame below it and compacts in a narrow frame as it does at narrow widths. The side panels stay and follow the frame focused last; each chat tab keeps its own composer, scroll and draft. Preview tabs follow VS Code: a single click opens one (title in italics) that the next open replaces; double-clicking the tab or the opener, or editing (typing in an editor or a composer), keeps it; opening an item already open focuses its tab. Phones (390) have no splits and switch tabs from a strip of every open tab. The layout persists per account and survives reload (superseded 2026-11-07: per browser tab, below); the browser URL is the focused tab's, so links, Back/Forward and new browser tabs keep working. Every opener is an `<a href>`. Tab kinds are a registry. Moving a tab has a visible control besides dragging.
- **[P]** How (workbench, 2026-11-05; implemented, ARCHITECTURE §5 "The workbench"): one tab per item (a chat with all its branches, a library entry, a page path; Settings and Import are one tab each); a second view of the same chat isn't possible yet. Split right / Split down split the frame they're in: the new frame takes the shown tab when the frame has others, else it starts empty with a hint, waiting for the next open. The frame's ⋯ at the strip's end holds Keep tab open, Move to the frame left/right/above/below, Move to a new frame right/below, Close other tabs and Close frame; a middle click on a tab closes it. Opening from a chat (Large editor, Full editor) keeps the chat's tab open and opens the editor as a preview tab beside it, bound to that chat (its unsaved text, its preset context, Close returns to it); opening from the sidebar or a page follows the preview rule, so Home → a chat replaces Home as before. The side panel shows beside the frames for the chat focused last, or for the chat an editor tab was opened from; a library entry opened on its own has none (the shown-tab condition was dropped on 2026-11-07, below). Closing the last tab opens Home. A new browser tab (middle click) opens the saved layout with that item focused (superseded 2026-11-07: it opens just its URL, below). Typing keeps a preview tab only in a chat, a new chat or an editor: searching, filters and a page's forms (a new chat's title, settings) don't. A preview tab is reused in place, so switching chats in it keeps the outgoing chat on screen until the next is ready, as before. Back in a tab opened from another goes back to the one it was opened from. An editor tab opened from a chat scrolls on its own and keeps the chat's portrait column. A dialog or popover of a tab behind another closes while that tab is hidden and opens again when it's shown. An old link to a chat's large editor (`?editor=`) opens the editor's tab over the chat's. VS Code parity (owner, 2026-11-06: "the pane/tab system semantics don't match the way code editors work"; gap list workbench-gaps.md): Split right/down show the shown tab in the new frame too, so one chat can show in two frames, each with its own scroll, composer and panel; an opener focuses the focused frame's tab of the item, else another frame's (VS Code's reveal if open), so the sidebar never duplicates an item by accident. A tab dropped on a frame's outer quarter splits that side, the overlay showing the half it takes. **Open beside** in a chat row's right-click menu (with Open and Open in a new browser tab; the row's corner actions stay as they are), and Alt-click on a link open the item (character tiles' own Open beside button was removed, owner 2026-11-06, below) in the frame to the right, adding one if none; middle and Ctrl-click stay a new browser tab. Tabs have a right-click menu (owner, 2026-11-06: the native-menu rule covers editable and message text only; tabs aren't text) with the ⋯ menu's items for that tab: Pin/Unpin (pinned tabs first, a pin in place of ×, kept by Close others, to the right, saved and all), Keep open, Split right/down, the moves, Maximize frame/Restore frames (also a strip button while split), Close, Close others, Close to the right, Close saved (tabs with nothing unsaved), Close all, Reopen closed tab (the last 20 of this page) and Close frame. A tab whose editor has changes not yet saved as a revision shows a dot in place of × until hovered; closing it asks nothing, because typed text is kept as drafts with the item. No keyboard shortcuts were added (visible controls first). A link inside a frame opens in that frame (owner, 2026-11-06: a character tile in a Characters tab scrolled the chat in the other frame): reveal-if-open applies to the sidebar's openers only, so a frame never moves because of a click in another; a tile still opens its character's latest chat, now in the frame it was clicked in. Character tiles have a chat row's right-click menu (Open, Open beside, Open in a new browser tab). Buttons over a picture (a tile's star and archive, a group tile's pin; Open beside until its removal) keep their backing while hovered and while their tooltip is open (owner, 2026-11-06: Open beside vanished on a dark portrait once its tooltip opened). The Move to the frame … items show a frame with an arrow pointing their way (owner, 2026-11-06: a right chevron on Move left read as wrong, and as a submenu). ~~The layout is kept per account and device (coordinator, 2026-11-06): each browser makes and keeps its own id, so a phone and a desktop don't overwrite each other's layout; a new device starts at one tab, and a browser that can't keep an id shares one layout with others like it.~~ Superseded by the per-browser-tab layout (owner, 2026-11-07, below).
- **[D]** Each browser tab's address decides what it shows (owner, 2026-11-07, on the root URL opening a chat beside Home): "Why does the homepage open to this now?" "This effectively removes the homepage entirely. It can't even be navigated to." "It also means the URL no longer represents what's open and navigation breaks" "how can this possibly work even in principle for an app that is designed to be used from multiple tabs?" No layout is saved per account or device. A browser tab's frames and tabs are its own, kept in that tab's session storage so its reload restores them with the URL's item focused (an item the stored layout lacks opens in the focused frame). A new browser tab, a pasted URL or a middle click opens exactly its URL as one tab, never another tab's layout; the root URL with nothing stored for the tab opens Home. The URL always shows the focused frame's tab; Back and Forward go between the places navigated to. **[P]** (homeopen, 2026-11-07) A page load restores the stored layout only when it is a reload or a Back/Forward return; any other load (typed, pasted, a link opened in a new tab, a login) starts at its URL alone.
- **[D]** Portraits with frames (owner, 2026-11-07): "Opening portraits is broken now with the tabs and frames." (the picture viewer opened inside the left frame and ran over the right one) "Also it should be possible to drag the image viewers into frames, as tabs, but they should still open in their column by default." **[P]** (portraitframes) The column is the shell's own, left of the frames (the owner's 2026-11-05 "its own extra column", like the side columns), not inside the frame it was opened from: it never covers a frame, a narrow frame doesn't squeeze it, and with one frame it looks as before frames. It showed the pictures of the shown tab focused last among those with pictures open (replaced below). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "The workbench", "Portrait column"). **[P]** (portraitframes) Pictures as tabs: the viewer's header dragged onto a frame's strip opens the picture there as a tab at that place; onto a frame's outer quarter, in a new frame on that side, as a dragged tab does; the column closes it. The viewer's ⋯ menu has Open as a tab (in the chat's frame) as the visible control. The tab is an ordinary one: close, move, split, its right-click menu, kept after reload; it names the picture (image, name, cutout), so it shows that image even if the card's portrait changes later. Phones have no frames, so their full-screen viewer offers neither. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Pictures as tabs").
- **[D]** One image column (owner, 2026-11-07): "the image viewer column still looks broken with the multi-pane view. It seems to have independent instances of itself for each source pane. If I open two images from one chat pane, then click on a portrait from a different pane, it replaces both of them, and vice versa". **[P]** (portraitcol) One column for the whole workbench: a picture opened from any frame goes on top with the same rules as a second one from the same frame (Keep one image open keeps one in the whole column), never replacing another frame's; closing one leaves the others; Open as a tab and a drag open it beside the view it came from. Each view still keeps its own pictures in its tab's URL, so they come back after a reload; pictures of a tab behind another in its frame stay in the column. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Portrait column").
- **[D]** Dividers stay put (owner, 2026-11-07): "Why does dragging an image between panes resize other panes? ... The divider between panes moved as a result." **[P]** (portraitcol) A closed frame's room goes to its sibling in the same split, as before; when the side panel shows, hides or is resized, the frames beside it take the change and no divider moves (the cause here was the panel hiding once a picture tab got focus, which made every frame wider in proportion). New splits still share the visible width evenly. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Side panel").
- **[D]** The side panel stays with its chat (owner, 2026-11-07): "This is obviously a UX issue. You can't have that opening a new tab on top of one changes the layout like this, making the sidebar disappear. Why can't it remain attached to the chat it was on before?" The panel shows the chat focused last until another chat is focused, even once that chat's tab is behind another in its frame; a picture, a library entry, Home or any other tab never hides or switches it; an editor tab opened from a chat shows that chat's, as before. Closing its chat moves it to the chat tab focused most recently. **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-07) "I don't want unexpected layout shifts just because chats close." **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-07; the owner may switch to keeping the closed chat's panel) With no chat left open, the panel keeps its place and width and says "Open a chat to see its settings here." until a chat opens (which shows its panel there) or the user hides it; it hides only when the user hides it, and shows only once a chat has shown it in this browser tab. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Side panel").
- **[D]** The bars' buttons in tab mode (owner, 2026-11-07): "You can almost soft-lock yourself if you hide both sidebars and have only tabs open that aren't chats. The chat header currently carries the sidebar open button for both (the close button for the left bar is on the bar itself)." "This can only happen when the chat switches from single-pane to tab mode, though. The header can then loose its one button on the right for this (the left button only shows up when the sidebar closes, but I guess it'd also be removed when inside a tab). Then on the top tab strip, on the right, there's already buttons. On the left, you'd have to move the first tab a bit to the right to make space whenever the left tab is closed, but it doesn't need anything there otherwise." Single-pane mode (no strip) keeps both buttons in the chat header. In tab mode the headers have neither; the side panel's toggle goes with the strip's buttons at the right end of the top-right frame's strip, and the hidden sidebar's Show button at the start of the top-left frame's strip, the first tab moved right to make room (nothing there while the sidebar shows). Same icons and hover explanations; nothing key-only. **[P]** (sidetoggles) Tab mode is the strips showing on a wide screen (a frame with two tabs, or a split); phones keep their header buttons (their panel is a sheet opened from the header; their sidebar is the menu). Every page header loses the Show button in tab mode, not only the chat's, since the strip carries it. The panel's toggle is the strip's last button, after Maximize frame and ⋯, because it comes and goes with a chat; it is the toggle of the chat whose panel shows (the panel's chat-attachment rule above), there even while that chat's tab is behind another, and absent with no chat open (the placeholder keeps its own Hide panel). A maximized frame's strip carries both. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "The workbench", "Bars' buttons in tab mode").  **[P]** (coordinator, sidetoggles review, 2026-11-07) A frame too narrow for all its strip buttons: the tabs give way first (shrink, then scroll); then Maximize leaves the strip for the ⋯ menu's own Maximize frame item; the ⋯ menu and the panel's toggle always stay inside the frame. **Built**.
- **[D]** Portraits dragged onto frames (owner, 2026-11-07): "Dragging a portrait from the chat into a pane results in the composer displaying this" (an attachment error). **[P]** (portraitcol) A message or Cast portrait dragged onto a frame opens its picture there as a tab, as a dragged viewer does (onto the strip at that place, onto an outer quarter in a new frame); the chat's drop zones never take the app's own portraits as attachments, while files from outside still attach. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Pictures as tabs").
- **[D]** Dragging characters and chats onto frames (owner, 2026-11-07): "When you have a chat open, and you're dragging the portrait, I'd say opening the image itself in the pane you drop it is reasonable… The same is true for the portrait in the header of a character def, sidebar or main view: the defs are literally already there. But from other places? homepage, library, left sidebar, 'Add character' in the left sidebar, characters list in the left sidebar… the most correct action is to open their big definition editor in that pane. On the other hand, dragging and dropping chats, opens those chats in the pane (same as clicking them)." **[D]** (coordinator's brief from that, 2026-11-07) Portraits in a chat (messages, Cast) and in a character definition's header (the side panel's character tab and the full-size editor) open their picture tab where they're dropped. A character from Home's grid, the library, the left sidebar's characters or the Cast's Add characters opens that card's full-size editor as a tab there; a chat row or tile (the sidebar's rows, group tiles, the Chats list) opens that chat there, as a click does. A drop goes where a dragged tab's does: into a strip at that place, or into a body's outer quarter, which splits. A click does what it did. Nothing dragged from inside the app becomes a composer attachment; a drop a frame doesn't take does nothing; the drop preview shows only for what a frame takes; files from outside still attach. Phones need no drag: a long press stays the row's own (its app menu where it has one), and nothing new is added there. One payload kind each for a picture, a card and a chat, registered, so a plugin adds its own. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Dragging onto frames").
- **[D]** No Open beside button on tiles (owner, 2026-11-06): "Remove the 'open in a frame on the right' button. I never requested it to begin with, and now it's redundant with the context menu." Character tiles lose their hover button; Open beside stays in tile and row right-click menus, with Alt-click and middle click. No other tile or row had the button. **Built** (archivebtn; ARCHITECTURE §5 "The workbench").
- **[D]** Opening a character (owner, 2026-11-06): "It opens the character. Not their definitions. In our interface, navigating to the character means showing their chats on the sidebar." A character tile, in the sidebar or a Characters tab in any frame, opens the character: the sidebar shows their chats (All chats › name, New chat with name, their chat rows) and the frame opens their latest chat with their card's panel, else a new chat with them, as before the workbench. Open beside, Alt-click, the tile menu's Open and a new browser tab open the character the same way; never the card editor.
- **[D]** A workshop opened from a story's side panel never replaces the story (owner, 2026-11-17, about the panel's "open in a tab of its own" button replacing the story in a single-pane center): "it contradicts what the message says, it's also obviously not what the user wants. What I expected was for it to split-pane on its own and put itself as one of the panes; or to add itself as an actual tab in the main pane (which would have to be converted to the tab-layout like when you split)".
  - **[P]** (workshoppop1, coordinator's choice between the owner's two options) The button (now "Open beside the story": "Opens this workshop beside the story, in its own pane.") splits a single pane and opens the workshop in the new pane on the right, the story staying in its pane; with a split, it opens as a kept tab in the pane next to the story's (right first, then left, below, above), never replacing that pane's preview tab; a workshop already open anywhere is focused instead of opened again. On a phone (one pane) it opens the workshop on its own page and Back returns to the story. The side chat's "open a panel tab in its own tab" uses the same rule. Built (ARCHITECTURE §5 "Workshop side chat", "Opening beside the story").
