- **[D]** A workshop links back to its story (owner, 2026-11-17): "once the workshop is opened in the center-column in any way, there's no way to go from Workshop -> branch or chat it is connected to *at all*. It's referenced in the title. It's not actually linked anywhere. So I lost the original chat entirely once it replaced the center column".
  - **[P]** (workshoppop1) An attached workshop's header has "About “<story title>”" under its title, wherever it's shown as a chat, opening the story by the same rule (focused where it's open, else beside the workshop; a phone navigates); a deleted story reads "About a story that was deleted (deleted)", with no link. The chat list and the sidebar's rows show "about “<story title>”" beside a workshop's badge. The branch it reads isn't named yet: the side chat reads whichever branch the story's tab shows, so a workshop alone has no branch of its own to name.
- **[D] A message jumped to flashes, then is unmarked** (owner, 2026-11-06, about opening a chat from a character): "when you open a chat from a character, you get a message highlighted like this. I think it's dubiously useful to begin with, but my bigger issue is that there's no obviously way to even make the highlight disappear? This seems to not have been thought through at all?! It should flash a couple times like when scrolling to a specific old message on a chat app, not remain highlighted." **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 "Message jumps").
  - **[P]** (msgflash, 2026-11-06) Every jump to a message flashes it with the same flash as an added item (§1.3 "Adding an item reveals and flashes it": the accent ring three times; with reduced motion one steady highlight that fades), then leaves it unmarked: opening a character's chat at the line they last spoke, a message link, History edits' matches, a summary's range, the branch menu's "Go to where this branch starts", the bin's branch links, a card revision's "used in" links, the context view's sources, switching to another branch at a message, and a message just inserted. The URL keeps naming the message, so shared links, reloads and back/forward still open there; the history entry remembers that its jump has flashed, so a reload or coming back to it doesn't flash again, while choosing the same jump again does. Selection and keyboard focus rings are unchanged.
  - **[P]** (msgflash, 2026-11-06) Opening a character's chat at their last line stays for now; it is one switch (`OPEN_AT_LAST_LINE` in the client's character links) that opens at the chat's end instead.
- **[D]** Sidebar sizing history (splitfix, 2026-11-05): the earlier content-fitting, percentage caps and reserved-slot/stable-position rules are **superseded by the owner's flexible-Chats rule below**. Split-offs keep ordinary Chats-row styling and timestamps.
- **[D]** Flexible Chats and responsive foot sections (owner, 2026-11-05): “The Chats list is the flexible part (flex-1) and always gets all remaining height”; foot sections retain their chosen/default sizes, default two full rows each. When three or more Chats rows and the foot sections cannot fit, shrink the foot sections, never below one complete row; then collapse them to headers, split first. Responsive constraints are driven by column height and never overwrite saved preferences. “A header-only or absent split section gives its space to the Chats list.” Parent-only split sections remain one From-link header without a body or handle; split-off lists retain their chevron and Chats-row presentation. Merge origin/master first and keep d67a8cf8's rename pencil immediately after the branch name. Capture only Journeys, Harbor and Hills at 1440×1000 dark, plus Journeys at 390×844 dark. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** Click wins over automatic footer collapse (owner, 2026-11-05): “clicking an auto-collapsed header opens that section (the user's click wins over the height rule): the Chats list then gives up space down to one full row, and the other foot section stays collapsed. Clicking again collapses it. Never disable the toggle.” Cover both Branches and Split off at 390×844 and capture each open in dark mode. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P]** Implementation (splitfix flexible layout, built 2026-11-05): the registered footer contributions occupy only their actual boxes. Measure the first three main-list rows including headings to calculate available footer space; apply minimum expanded heights before temporary collapse. Presence is a list/header/absent distinction, never a reserved empty slot. Keep the existing pixel size and manual-collapse preference keys.

- **[D]** Chat-switch flashes (owner, 2026-11-05): “"first time" for each chat, not for the page. Also that's only for the sidebar. For the chat view itself it seems to flash every time a chat is switched”. Investigate both sidebar and centre in Vite and built clients, including owner-sized synthetic data.
- **[P]** How (chatflash, built 2026-11-05; obsolete as an implementation proposal, see ARCHITECTURE §12): retain the outgoing chat until the incoming session's first window is ready, and retain branch/split sections until their replacement reads finish; retained controls are inert during the handover. Keep the existing virtualizer landing and restoration logic.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, splitlink follow-up) “The title line of a branch row never wraps: the title truncates with an ellipsis, and the pencil and the 'opens here' chip stay on that one line after it, at every panel width including phone. Row height must be identical with and without the pencil/chip.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.


- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, splitlink) “Every row that shows a last-message preview (branch rows, chat rows, the header's branch list) uses the latest message on that branch that has text, skipping empty and failed ones; only when the branch has no message with text, show ‘No messages yet’ in the same two-line slot, so row height never changes.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, splitlink) “While renaming, the field takes the full width of the title line (✓ and ✕ stay at its end) at the row's normal title text size; it is prefilled with the branch's own name when it has one and empty otherwise, with the faint placeholder ‘Branch name’; the row's height does not change.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P]** (splitlink, 2026-11-05; implemented) Infer missing imported navigation targets from shared display-path ranges; use existing reader operations and compact InlineRename. Summary format 7 invalidates cached previews.


- **[D] Branch and chat pins** (owner, 2026-11-04): “Currently you can pin any branch, and it becomes listed among pinned chats. This feels somewhat odd, you go straight from a branch being one of many under a chat, to it being pinned at the global top level among a few chats.” “Usually, you'd want to pin a branch *above others* somehow, because it's one of the main branches of conversation you want to remember/continue and separate it from the rest, which could be a long list kept for historical reasons.” (Owner, 2026-11-05, approving branch-row pins within their chat and chat-row pins globally): “Yes, this should obviously be done.” Pinned branches precede named branches, then the rest, each by last message. Global Pinned lists chats once, opening their current branch. Existing pinned branches retain their local pin and pin their chat globally. Both pins explain their action on hover/focus. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P]** (pinsplit, 2026-11-05; implemented) Keep branch pin storage; migration 0004 rebuilds the unused chat pin table with one row per user/chat and carries existing branch pins over. Existing registered shell rows select the corresponding pin operation.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05, branchrows) “If we put the pencil, the button has to be far smaller than it normally is to fit the layout and the title text size. It CANNOT make the card taller. Moreover, I am thinking about whether we want it on every branch, or just the selected one. Probably the latter.” Only the selected left-panel branch row has a small pencil after its name, opening the shared inline rename with Enter/Escape and confirm/cancel, without changing row height. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P]** (branchrows, 2026-11-05; implemented) The existing registered branch-list surface uses compact `InlineRename`, the `NameFork` RPC and existing `SetForkName` worker operation. An invisible copy preserves the title's measured height while the editor replaces it.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-05): “Change the character order.” In chat rows and home/group tiles sharing shell fork rows, characters follow their latest message on that branch, then silent members in join order; card swaps retain character identity and personas stay excluded. Header faces and Cast keep join order.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04; faceplus4): "the + cards are invisible on a selected chat". Keep the +N tile visibly a tile in every row state and both themes, including the header and other occurrences. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12). **[P]** Give the shared tile an edge using the existing semantic border token.

- **[D] Chat portrait overflow** (owner, 2026-11-04): "I've noticed that the sidebar only ever shows 2 character portraits, with no indication of more, regardless of how many there are. We should fix this. Two are the most that fit side by side realistically on most sizes, but we need to make it clear there's more." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D] Superseded by faceplus3** (owner, 2026-11-04; faceplus2): "We have this + card on the header, why make the chat list inconsistent with it?" Use the header’s shared +N portrait tile everywhere faceplus added a badge, scaled to the portraits; reserve a portrait slot in tight stacked rows and retain every name on hover/focus. Supersedes the faceplus badge proposal below. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04; faceplus3): "Being able to see two fully side by side is good. But there's never a benefit in showing a card + the full size \"+ card\" side by side." "Two full cards like before, but the second card is stacked over a + card that is only partially showing (instead of 3 in a line)". Never sacrifice a fully visible portrait that fits; tuck the shared +N tile partly behind the last visible portrait with its text fully legible. Keep wider groups showing more portraits, and retain all names on hover/focus. Supersedes faceplus2’s portrait-slot rule. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12). **[P]** Rows keep two full portraits and move the pair above the text below 17.8125rem; only the count tile overlaps, with content-sized exposed text.
- **[P] Superseded by faceplus2** (faceplus, 2026-11-04): Overlay a semantic-token `+N` badge at the portrait group's bottom-right without allocating space; hover/focus lists every character name. Reuse the badge in the three-portrait chat list. Header faces and group tiles already indicate overflow. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §12).

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (navtiles account/recovery):** “what to do with the footer It's fairly odd. It shows the account name usually, plus the "unsaved text button", which is completely unrelated.” Approved the proposal to move account name and Log out to the top of Settings, remove the footer, and show browser recovery only when copies exist as a counted warning row above the navigation tiles: “Yes to your decision. I think not showing the account name is probably a positive overall anyway.” The count updates as copies are added, saved or deleted; the existing dialog and hover explanation stay. **[P]** Register the warning and Settings account row in replaceable slots; notify recovery views after committed browser-copy mutations, including other tabs. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (navtiles Account header correction):** “The rest is fine, but the account headering here at the top is terrible”. Remove the top account box entirely; Name / Handle / Role remain on the Account page. Log out belongs in the Account card and appears only for real password sessions. Settings starts with its navigation and content top-aligned, as before the footer change. This supersedes the earlier top-of-Settings account-row placement. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (footerport):** “These are fine.” Port the approved account and counted browser-recovery changes to master. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.

- **[D] Action explanations** (owner, 2026-11-01/02; implemented 2026-11-04, obsolete as an implementation proposal): action buttons and menu items “need popover explanations”; avoid “giving the user pointless implementation details”; “ST shouldn’t be mentioned in explanatory text unless the text is actually relevant to ST interop”; “Assume every user looking at a preset editor knows what a prompt cache prefix is.” Explanations state the action and at most one clarifying fact, without recovery instructions; standing guidance belongs in adjacent question-mark help. Implementation is recorded in ARCHITECTURE.md.

- **[D] New chat from a character’s chat list** (owner, 2026-11-04): “But how do I go from there to "new chat with this character"? "Start a chat" at the top header makes you find the character again.” On the proposed right-panel control: “That button intentionally doesn’t exist anymore”. Left panel only. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (charnewchat; ARCHITECTURE §12). **[P]** A list action row above one named character’s chats opens the existing pending chat with that character chosen; omit it from All chats, groups and branch scopes.

- **[D] (owner, 2026-11-04, headerswap)**: “these two buttons on the chat header should swap places: the sidepanel thing needs to be next tot he sidepanel”. The ⋯ menu precedes the side-panel toggle, leaving the toggle last beside the panel, including on phones and any center header with that toggle. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §5).

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (navtilesport):** “This looks fine now, proceed with the other changes”. Port the approved Home / Library / Import / Settings / Bin tiles to master. “One issue. I'd like them to all be the same width.” **[P]** Use equal columns sized to fit the widest label, retaining balanced wrapping and the same width on incomplete rows. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, picturecrop): "Two non-destructive cuts stored as settings on the kept original: (1) Picture = free-shape crop (presets Free/2:3/1:1) used by large view, viewer and export (server renders the cut PNG, re-encodes, embeds card data); (2) Portrait = existing 2:3 cutout, chosen inside the cut picture, for small portraits. Defaults: picture = whole image. “Reset to the original”. Picture window gets a Picture | Portrait segmented switch at top; Picture tab previews: large view + export; Portrait tab: Message/Cast/Tile previews. Replace picture… → Picture → Portrait. ST imports unchanged (already cropped). Personas too." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**; ARCHITECTURE §14.5c.
- **[P]** (picturecrop, 2026-11-04): keep the Picture rectangle inside the existing image-bound `avatar_crop` settings object as optional `picture`; portrait coordinates stay relative to the original. Existing summary projections carry both cuts without changing the database baseline. Existing writer actions save both together. PNG export rebases the portrait to the rendered picture for round trips.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (interfacetab):** “The Display tab should be renamed to "Interface"”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12. **[P]** Use `/settings/interface` and `?panel=interface`, redirecting old links and preserving internal display identifiers.

- **[D] (owner, 2026-11-04)**: “prompt preset main editor vs lorebook main editor vs main chat view. The latter has a clear top header that connects to the two columns, the former two do not. From now on I want all center-column views to have such a header.”
- **[D] (owner, 2026-11-04)**: “Moreover: through the header, it must be possible to switch what specific document the center column editor is editing: so a selector for lorebooks and presets for each respective editor makes sense. Chats already have this in the form of the branch switcher.”
- **[D] (owner, 2026-11-06, switchers)**, about the switcher above the large prompt-preset editor: “It's literally just a plain select. This is awful - we have an entire UI to switch prompt presets, it isn't being used, this is unusable with any large number of them. Same issue with lorebooks and presumably cards too - audit these header switchers. For prompt presets, the switcher in the *sidebar editor* is the complete, good one, which makes this even more inconsistent.” Every header switcher uses its kind's complete picker, the large and side-panel editors the same component; a kind without a good picker gets one on the preset picker's pattern. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6, “Header switchers”).
- **[P] (switchers, 2026-11-06)**: The preset editor's header opens the side panel's registered picker body; choosing there switches the document the editor shows, as the 2026-11-04 selector did, and doesn't change the preset the next reply uses; Default (built-in) isn't listed, since it can't be edited. Cards and personas get the card lists' filter, sort with direction and Include personas, opening on Personas for a persona; lorebooks, notes and snippets get a filter, Name / Modified and their edit times. Archive stays a preset feature (owner, 2026-11-01), not extended to other kinds. Every picker list above 60 rows mounts only the rows in view; Down from the filter enters the list, Enter takes the first row.
  - **[P] (switchers fix round, 2026-11-06)**: The preset editor's header never sets the preset the next reply uses: the large editor isn't tied to a chat, so its header only switches what's edited. The lorebook, note, snippet and card switchers' sorts are account choices on the server, as the preset lists' sort is, not browser memory. Card rows show their portraits, as the speaker switcher's rows do. Lists end with the kind's existing way to make one: **New card…** (the library's New card form; the card opens in the editor), **New snippet** (the Snippets panel's empty snippet) and, over a chat, **New note** (the Notes tab's new note for this chat, on from the latest message). Lorebooks and library notes have no way to make one, so their lists have no New row. The card switcher lists the cards the left panel and Add characters list (`GetShellCards` through `browseCards`), so a card the card lists leave out (archived ones, once card archiving lands) leaves it too.

- **[P] (centerheader, 2026-11-04)**: Reuse the chat height token through one registered header frame; contribute title/actions from each view and navigate with shared same-kind selectors and existing draft guards.

**Built (centerheader, 2026-11-04):** These decisions are implemented and obsolete as implementation requirements; see ARCHITECTURE §5 and §14.6.

**Built follow-up (cardheader1, 2026-11-16):** The full card editor bounds its version within the existing identity row so the display-name switcher retains readable text on phones, including an empty version’s add prompt and long versions. Browser glyph-width checks and the center-identity layout audit cover 1440/390 in both themes; see ARCHITECTURE §14.6. This correction is obsolete as an implementation requirement; no Proposed/Open item changes status.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (snippettitle):** “After renaming a snippet, its History dialog/label must show the new title”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (navtiles Home and Library copy):** “The tool tip for library implies the library doesn't have characters but it literally opens on them. I think "characters" in this footer menu should probably become "home", it's the home page.” Rename the footer tile Home with a home icon; describe Library's actual contents, including characters. **[P]** Home: “Open the home page.” Library: “Browse characters, personas, lorebooks and presets.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (navtiles trial):** “turn it into a series of buttons/tabs with icon+text under, styled similarly to our right-sidebar tabs”; “on one row if space permits, but split into multiple rows (they all need to always be visible) responsively.” Initially trialled on `ui/next`; approved for master above. **[P]** Reuse panel-tool styling on route links, balance wrapping by measured label widths, and show browser recovery above the tiles when copies exist. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyport approval):** “The history UI is fine now.” Port the approved History redesign to master, retaining side-panel History access. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui add and editor controls):** “The add message button had its style changed and now has no message anymore, which is not what I asked. The message can remain removed from the edit one because it's redundant. But the add message one needs the text and should be styled like before, height equivalence notwithstanding, moreover, the spacing between the text and the pencil to edit it is zero”. On editor controls: “Why do the x and tick buttons go all the way there”. Restore the original ghost-style pencil-and-text Add message button for empty messages; keep Edit icon-only with the shared title-pencil gap. Confirm/cancel belong directly after the input, using the shared inline editor’s gap. Preserve equal row heights. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui inline messages):** “Move edit/add message to the row with the message text, and make it work like the title edit buttons, where the message turns into the edit box inline. Make sure that with or without message, the height of that row is the same. Messages that would span two rows, when rendered, get truncated with ellipsis. Hovering over them shows the full message in a popover, clicking the ellipsis also expands the message text into potentially multiple lines.” Use the shared pencil rename flow with Enter/Escape and confirm/cancel, a fixed-height message row, a full-text hover preview and an ellipsis toggle to expand/collapse the full message. **[P]** Keep a 32 px control strip and show expanded text below it so the ellipsis stays stationary. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui message and insets):** “1. The message has to be moved to above the button cluster, because we can't have it be randomly in between compare and the panel it opens. 2. Padding between the modal edges and the stuff inside the modal is uneven 3. see screenshot: no fields differ should get a bit more padding so its position matches where an accordion's text would be.” Put the revision message below the name/badges and above the actions; Compare's panel follows its action row. History has equal visible insets on all four edges, including when scrolling. “No fields differ.” starts at the same left offset as an accordion title. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui comparison toggle and spacing):** “why is the X next to the tabs and not to the right? It looks like it's attached to a tab. It needs to look like it's closing the box. Honestly, just remove it, the compare button toggles it anyway. But the spacing is uneven, the top of the tabs has way more space than below.” Remove the comparison X; Compare toggles the comparison, stays pressed while open, and explains the click action. Use equal space above the tabs row and below it before the first field. This supersedes the earlier X-beside-tabs decision. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui action order and close):** “1. Put restore before compare. 2. the layout now has a pointless empty row above the comparison tabs. Remove it and move the x to be next tot he tabs.” Entry action order is Restore to revision N, Compare, then the message action. The former close-X placement at the end of the tabs row is **superseded by the later comparison-toggle decision above**; the action order remains in force. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui revision actions):** “The position of that restore button makes no sense, and there's no reason for it to be buried. It should be next to the compare button, and say something like "Restore to revision x"”. On the origin line: “Can it have other values? Otherwise, saved seems redundant, and imported should just be a badge next to the revision name.” Restore belongs beside Compare on each entry and works without opening the diff; keep the latest action disabled with an explanation. **[P]** Omit user/Saved; badges: import → Imported, merge → Updated, agent → Agent edit, changeset → Changed. Generation usage and its chat link stay on a line, as does branch-change context. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui diff headings):** “This just says before/after which isn't helpful. It communicates nothing. The title of each panel should say what state it refers to: for example Revision 1 / Revision 2 Or "Revision 1 / Current State"”. Previous-revision comparisons name the parent and selected revision, with “Empty” for a first revision; current-state comparisons name the selected revision and “Current state”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

**[D] 2026-11-04 (owner):** "I just realized I can't find a way to see a card's past revisions"; "side-panel editors must have full parity with the large editors (they are the mobile editors)." Built: History access for card/persona, lorebook and preset panels; obsolete as an implementation requirement, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

**[P] 2026-11-04:** Place History beside Search and replace through a replaceable panel action slot; retain the existing note and snippet History actions.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04, castgap): “Why is there so much space above the cast row of the sidebar tabs?” Match the spacing above and below the Cast row. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12. **[P]** Reduce the host strip’s top padding to match its existing 4px bottom inset.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (viewertop approval):** “The header size change does look slightly off, but it's better than not having it, and I think it won't bother most users. We're keeping it”. Keep the top side viewer's header aligned with the chat header; lower viewers retain compact headers. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.5a.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (viewertop trial):** “matching only the top viewer's header to the chat header's height. Stacked viewers below it keep the compact height.” “I wonder if it'd look bad to have them uneven. We can try it though”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.5a. **[P]** Reuse the shell-header height token only for the first side viewer; at narrow column widths its two control rows share that height.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui follow-up):** “I would've never guessed that's what it does. It's a radio button, which is a form type control, under the restore button. I thought it somehow affected "restore". This is quite awful. Why not turn this into two tabs for the diff view? One is "Changes from previous revision" the other "Changes from current state" or something similar”. Use those two labels on shared diff tabs, with hover explanations and an empty-document comparison for the first revision; Restore is a separate revision action. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-04 (historyui):** “I want a redesign for the history UI. This looks sloppy.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §14.6. **[P]** Keep all library revision surfaces on the shared browser: bordered entries, aligned metadata, labeled actions and comparisons directly below their entry, with Restore before the changed fields.

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): Field help wrapping below the label is “obviously totally incorrect, broken and sloppy looking and it's a regression I didn't ask for.” The ? stays inline immediately after the label. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §5).

- **[D] Avatar initials fit their tile** (owner brief, 2026-11-02): “Make initials fit any tile size: scale the font with the tile (container units) and use at most 2 characters, so they never overflow.” **Built 2026-11-04 (groupinitials)**; obsolete as an implementation requirement. See ARCHITECTURE §14.5.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-03 (chatrownarrow):** “We need a way to be more responsive to smaller column widths for the chat listings in the sidebar that have two or more images especially. The images end up taking so much space that the text is unreadable.” Each shared chat row chooses its layout from its own width: side-by-side portraits when the text retains 9rem, overlapping portraits when that retains 9rem, otherwise small overlapping portraits above full-width text. Keep at most two portraits, the first on top, a 2px row-background ring, two-line titles/previews with normal title word boundaries, the date at the bottom and pin/branches in their corners. The Chats filter keeps the entire option name; counts hide first and the sort/direction group wraps right-aligned as needed. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12. **[P]** Implementation uses measured rem container thresholds and the existing shared row and registered browse slot.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-03 (helpalign):** “wherever a label (switch, checkbox, radio, field label) carries the ‘?’ help button, the ‘?’ sits inline immediately after the label's last word (the normal small gap), vertically centred on the label's FIRST line, at the same size everywhere.” Captions do not move it; wrapped labels keep help after the last word, without a separate column. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5. **[P]** For wrapped labels, inline flow centres help on the final text line: staying after the final word and on the first line cannot both hold when that word is on a later line.

- **[D] Owner, 2026-11-03 (personafilter):** “a toggle's tooltip says what clicking does, as "Click to <action>"”. Include personas uses a normal-colour Lucide user-x while off and a pressed/accent plain user while on; its name stays Include personas and its tooltips are exactly “Click to show personas” / “Click to hide personas”. Other icon toggles retain icons and behaviour. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §5.
**Built 2026-11-03 (chatrowcorners):** **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-03: “Upper right: the pin button, top-aligned with the title’s first line”; “Lower right: the branch-count button, bottom-aligned with the date line”; “the column for the buttons is only as wide as the wider of the two buttons.” Shared chat rows retain the two-line title clamp, existing controls and empty lower corner without branches. This requirement is obsolete as an implementation plan; see ARCHITECTURE.md, Chat row corners.

- **[D] Stacked chat rows** (owner, 2026-11-04, chatrows; §10.1): “this layout is far too tall vertically to make sense and obviously wastes a ton of space”; “The date, where it currently is, has far too much space above it, below the text. IT obviously makes no sense.” “The second one is worse, because it looks like the forks button should be on the same row as the date, but isn't.” “Ideally, we can squeeze date, images, buttons on one row, and then have title and subtitle below. But I wouldn't do this. The date is potentially too long. So it remains at the bottom on its own row, and we need to fit the fork button at the top instead.” Stacked rows show faces, forks when present, then a fixed upper-right pin; title and preview follow, then the date directly below with the same small row gap, including without a preview. Wide rows and list column counts stay unchanged. “However, we'll probably end up using the space differently anyway” defers column-count changes. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, see ARCHITECTURE §12. **[P]** The existing container queries use a 4px text-row gap and an 8px gap between the top controls.

- **[D], built (homecontrols, owner, 2026-11-03):** the home page's controls get a new arrangement, chosen from the controls' container width with container queries (not viewport breakpoints). **Home page only:** the left panel's grid keeps its own compact controls (owner, 2026-11-03: applying it there "was NEVER requested. And it doesn't even fit."; the coordinator's brief had wrongly extended it). Wide: filters left and sort group right, bounded search below left. Medium: filters above search left and sort right. Narrow: horizontally scrolling, unbroken filter segments above right-aligned sort, then full-width search. “Find the thresholds by measuring the controls' real widths, not by hard-coding pixel guesses, so it adapts when labels or counts change.” Implemented and obsolete as a proposal; see ARCHITECTURE §12.

- **[D] Left-panel name filter** (owner, 2026-11-04): “left panel: I think the filter by name should be on the same row when there's enough space. When it wraps, chats/characters should expand to fill their row so there isn't empty space next to them”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (leftsearch; ARCHITECTURE §12). **[P]** Use a 26rem content-width threshold, an 11rem switch with equal segments and at least 14rem for the field; hover and focus never change their geometry.
- **[D] Column header alignment** (owner, 2026-11-03): "the left column header has a lot of space around it and ends up looking misaligned with the centre column's header, which is less tall." The left header, centre chat header and right panel's top tab bar share one height token and continuous bottom borders wherever they sit side by side; left title/actions are vertically centred with the chat's controls, with matching vertical padding. Chats / Characters stays below that divider, spaced like the centre's content. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12, "Column header alignment").
  - **[P]** (headeralign, 2026-11-03) How: retain the centre header's portrait-bearing height, 65 px (48 px portrait + 8 px above/below + 1 px border), at every width through `--spacing-shell-header`. The sidebar's tabs and the panel's cast strip start 16 px below the common divider, matching the message list's leading inset.

- **[D] Chat-list / branch-viewer divider** (owner, 2026-11-02): “The divider between the upper list and the lower branch viewer is draggable”: reuse the panel resizer's handle and behaviour; arrow-key resizing with `aria-valuenow`; double-click resets; remember the size per account; minimum/maximum heights keep both halves usable. On phones follow the column's existing behaviour. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (chatswith; ARCHITECTURE §12).
  - **[P]** (chatswith, 2026-11-03): Keep the preferred lower height per account in this browser, like column widths; clamp the rendered height to the available room without rewriting that preference. Default to 45% of the browser's height, keep 112 px for branches and reserve 224 px for the upper list and its controls where the window allows. Use the same split inside the phone's existing navigation drawer.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, newcarddialog follow-up): "Remove every standing explanatory sentence under the title of every create, new or rename dialog"; "Where a fact really matters for a choice in the form, it goes in a '?' beside that field, and only then." Applies to preset, card, variant, fork, user, model, key, note, snippet, persona and other creation/naming surfaces. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10).

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, New card): "What is going on here? Why is there a random undo?" Creation and rename forms have no detached history button. New card has no standing sentence below its title; its third label is **Version**, with a question-mark tooltip, "Shown as the card's subtitle." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §3.10).
- **[P]** (newcarddialog, 2026-11-02) Shared form status shows history only when an existing settings editor explicitly requests it. Draft persistence and recovery notices continue on creation and naming forms.

- **[D]** Right-panel close control (owner, 2026-11-02): “Remove the × from the right panel's tab bar on layouts where the chat header's panel toggle is visible while the panel is open”; “Keep a way to close the panel where the panel covers the chat header.” The tab bar reclaims the space and the header toggle shows a pressed state. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (panelclose; ARCHITECTURE §12). **[P], built:** keep the phone sheet's existing top-right `WindowClose`, matching the left navigation drawer.
- **[D] Tab order** (owner, 2026-11-17, on the chat panel's tools): "You should be able to drag these tabs to change their order. This is important because most of the time, at least a few end up being pushed off to the side into the extra menu. However, that UI needs to somehow account for that extra menu's behaviour, and interact with it. One concern is when one of those tabs is open, it's always visible within the order, but it's not in its 'real' position. How do we handle this?"
  - **[P]** (coordinator, 2026-11-17; built by tabdrag1) **One ordered list.** The row shows as many tools as fit, in that order; More holds the rest, in the same order. The order is an account display preference (`panelTabOrder`); empty means the usual order, and new or plugin tools follow the ones it names. **The open tool while it's under More** shows as a guest just before More, after a thin divider, in the selected style, so it never looks like it's at its real place; its entry in More stays at its real place, marked "Shown above". Closing it or opening another removes the guest. The guest's room is kept before the row's tools are counted, so the row can push one more tool into More but never flips between layouts. **Dragging**: within the row; onto More (to the end; held there, More opens to drop at a place inside); inside the open More; from More up into the row; the guest drops at its new real place. A drop mark shows where it lands and the tools slide to their new places; on touch a long press starts the drag (a long press without moving opens the tool's menu). **Without a drag**: each tool and each More entry has an app context menu (right-click or long press): Move left / Move right (Move up / Move down in More), Move to start, Move to More (Move to end when nothing overflows) or Move out of More, Arrange tabs…, Reset tab order, each explained on hover. More ends with **Arrange tabs…**, a list of every tool with move buttons and drag handles (the shared reorder list), marking those under More, with Reset tab order. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5 "The chat panel's tool order").
- **[D] Decided (owner, 2026-11-02, macrohighlight):** tabs left open across a deploy show “A new version is available.” with “Reload” when the server reports a different client build. “Reloading must never lose typed text.” “Don't auto-reload.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement:** ARCHITECTURE §5 and §7.
- **[P]** (macrohighlight, 2026-11-02, implementation choice): emit a unique client build stamp, report it on the existing health route, and check after RPC reconnect, focus, online and every 30 seconds. Register the notice in the app banner slot. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal:** ARCHITECTURE §5 and §7.
- **[D] Bin size units** (owner, 2026-11-02, on the raw byte limits): “This should NOT be in bytes.” **Built**: bin capacities use MB/GB, grouped item counts, adjacent Save buttons and the standard disclosure; other UI file and import sizes share the formatter. Implementation is recorded in ARCHITECTURE §3.11 and §5.
- **[P]** (binunits, 2026-11-03, implemented): retain binary multiples under the existing MB/GB labels; prefer GB when one decimal converts back to the same whole-byte count, otherwise MB, retaining full precision for older arbitrary limits. Round edited fractional bytes to the nearest byte and reject nonpositive or unsafe values inline. Read-only sizes round to at most one decimal.

- **[D] Decided (owner, 2026-11-02):** “Every modal that can be dismissed without a decision (closing it loses nothing, or simply cancels) must have a visible close button: an X in the top-right corner. Clicking outside still closes it too.” “Modals that demand a specific resolution (confirm/cancel choices such as ‘Discard changes?’, destructive confirmations, anything where outside-click is already disabled) don't get the X; their buttons are the way out.” **Built, obsolete as an implementation requirement:** shared dialog, sheet and popover behavior is documented in ARCHITECTURE §5; portrait/picture viewers are reserved for the separate portraitview work.

- **[D] Avatar overflow tiles** (owner, 2026-11-03): "The round thing in the header is still there." The +N overflow indicator must have the same shape, size and radius as the avatar tiles, stay vertically centred inside the stack, use muted token colours and show hidden characters' names on hover. Check chat headers, chat lists, home/library and Cast for remaining circular avatar styling in both themes. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (overflowchip; ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
- **[D] Small portrait shape and pending greeting clicks** (owner, 2026-11-02): "remove the stray element or style, so only the image (with its intended corner radius) shows"; clicking the pending greeting portrait and a saved message portrait "must behave the same." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (portraitfix; ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
- **[D]** Tile favourites (owner, 2026-11-02): "Favourite buttons should only appear when the image is hovered, although toggled-on favourite stars stay on regardless. There's no need to show 1000 turned off buttons." Off stars appear on tile hover or keyboard focus within it; on stars remain visible. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §5).
- **[D]** Tile favourite hover (owner, 2026-11-02, starhover): "The star must stay visible and clickable whenever the tile or the star is hovered or focused, on every surface. Clicking it must never open the card." On touch all stars show. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §5).
- **[P]** (hovertoggles, 2026-11-02, implementation choice) Tile pins and message-image context toggles follow the same rule. Opacity preserves layout and tab order, including explanations on focus; coarse-pointer or no-hover devices keep toggles visible because tapping the tile already opens it. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §5).
- **[D]** Sidebar branch widths (owner, 2026-11-02): "a max width was given to chat listings in the left-hand sidebar, but never applied to branch listings." Apply the chat list's 40rem cap to branch listings and the other sidebar row lists. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** Chat row titles (owner, 2026-11-02): "A chat row's title shows at most **2 lines**, then an ellipsis. The full title is in the hover." Use normal word boundaries and clip a long unbroken token instead of splitting it across lines; keep avatars, branch count and pin centered without shrinking. Applies to all sidebar chat/branch variants and the same home lists. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** Side panel editors stand on their own (owner, 2026-11-02): "This also reminds me that creating new prompts doesn't seem available in the sidebar. The new version of it should be. In general, the sidebar editors should stand on their own, it should be possible for a user to only use those, and they should generally double as mobile UI editors for the thing they're doing. The main view editor is just a wider-screen version that is designed for wider, larger layouts and takes advantage of them where it makes sense, like here with the side column showing only the prompt titles at a glance." Applies to every side-panel/large-editor pair (prompt presets, lorebooks, cards, …): the side panel's version can do everything, and at 390 px it is the phone editor; the large editor adds only what a wide screen makes possible. **Built for the prompt preset** (promptmarkers). Queued card/lorebook gaps are built (parity, 2026-11-04; ARCHITECTURE §14): shared book settings, lorebook variants and usage, card fork actions and favourite; History, Search and replace, embedded book naming and card version import were already present. This queued subset is obsolete as an implementation requirement.
- **[D]** Layout review (owner, 2026-11-02): "There's a spacing issue with the character note switches. In general I want new UIs to have space issues like these detected before they're approved." The Notes switches need an inset, and new UI must be checked for spacing, clipping, alignment and overlap before approval. **Layout audit and shared spacing fixes built** (layoutlint, 2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §5); this portion is obsolete as an implementation requirement. An audit failure remains a defect to resolve or explicitly report for a design job.
  - **[P]** (layoutfixes, 2026-11-02) The chat header narrows by the chat column's own width, not the screen's: below 40rem (a phone, or the chat at its 440 px minimum beside a widened panel) it takes the phone's layout, leaving out the faces (the side panel has the cast), the pin (the chat lists' rows have it) and the rename pencil (⋯ has Rename; the name itself still renames on click). The name keeps at least 6rem; the branch control is the one thing that gives up room, cutting its name short (its full name is in its accessible label and its list). Nothing wraps or moves between widths except at that one threshold. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 "The header").
  - **[P]** (layoutfixes, 2026-11-02) A row of tabs wraps onto another row rather than run out of its column, everywhere (`ui/Tabs.tsx`): the phone's card editor tabs wrap after Lorebook. Wrapping was chosen over sideways scrolling because every tab stays visible with no scroll affordance to find. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §5).
  - **[P]** (layoutfixes, 2026-11-02) A toolbar's separator takes the toolbar's own gap, no extra margin, so the gaps between its controls are even (the lorebook editor's All entries row). A segmented choice's explanation line (shown under it where nothing hovers) wraps at the choices' width instead of widening the row past the screen (the home sort on a phone). A preset prompt's chips, count and actions wrap within the header on a phone instead of pushing ⋯ past the block's edge.
  - **[P]** (layoutfixes, 2026-11-02) Where the layout audit's 8 px fixes met later master changes, the 8 px inset wins: block headers (preset prompts, notes, snippets) have 8 px above and below their buttons (loreentry had raised it to 4), as do the sidebar's chat and branch rows, open lorebook entry rows and the tile and row pin and favourite toggles.
  - **[P]** (layoutfixes, 2026-11-02) The composer takes the same standard 8 px inset: its header, text and control rows are padded 8 px inside the 1 px border (controls 9 px from the box's outer edge), and the next-reply strip under it moves to 9 px so the pickers still line up with the box's controls. Reason: consistent spacing is the owner's rule (the layout review [D] above), and composerrow's owner request was even gaps, which are unchanged (6 px); its 4 px padding was an implementation choice. `e2e/composerRow.spec.ts` now expects 9 px and keeps its equal-gap and alignment checks.
- **[D] One set of picture controls** (owner, 2026-11-02, on the enlarge button showing on some portraits and not others, and a black dot in the middle of a hovered portrait): every portrait that allows editing shows the same controls in the same place. A click on the portrait opens its whole image in the side viewer; a click no longer replaces the picture, and the separate enlarge button goes. Exactly one overlay button, in the portrait's **lower-right** corner, opens one **Picture** interface that both adjusts the cutout and replaces the picture; replacing goes straight to choosing the new picture's cutout, since the two always go together. The button stays visible and usable on touch, with a short label and a hover explanation. The black dot goes. The persona portrait's badge "You play" becomes **"You"**. This supersedes the 2026-11-02 [P]s above on Adjust cutout / View full image / Replace over the top-right corner and a click replacing the picture. **Built** (portraitview; ARCHITECTURE §14.5c).
  - **[P] Picture-dialog layout** (picturedialog, owner requested a redesign, 2026-11-02; not yet confirmed): “What's going on with this UI? What is smaller/larger for?” Keep “<name>'s picture” and the corner X. Remove the standing explanation and gesture caption. Centre one compact row under the image: **Cutout size**, the viewer's shared toolbar slider (minimum through the largest fitting 2:3 box), reset icon (“Reset the cutout”), then a small ? whose hover says “Drag the box to move it, or a corner to resize it. Arrow keys move it; + and − resize it.” Corner drags and +/− keep the slider in sync. The right column holds only **Previews**, labelled **Message**, **Cast**, **Tile**, at actual portrait sizes, updating live; at 390 they form one row below the image. **Replace picture…** stays left, secondary like **Cancel**; Cancel and **Save cutout** stay right. Implementation choices: linear slider, resize about the box’s centre with edge clamping, existing help and reset icons, tile preview at the responsive library grid’s 134 × 201 px image area inside the minimum 136 px bordered tile, phone image capped at 36dvh to leave room for previews, a wrapping footer keeping Cancel/Save together, and a saving spinner over the label’s retained space so Save cannot widen and wrap that footer. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5c).
  - **[D]** (owner follow-up, 2026-11-02): "Make every portrait click behave the same: if the viewer is showing that portrait's image, the click closes it; otherwise it opens or switches to it." Editable header and Metadata portraits use the same click toggle as chat-message and pending-greeting portraits. Library tiles keep their navigation when they do not open a viewer. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (viewerbar follow-up; ARCHITECTURE §14.5a and §14.5c).
  - **[P]** (portraitview, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) How: the button is an icon (a picture) named "Picture", its explanation "Picture: adjust the cutout small portraits show, or replace the picture" (on a portrait without one: "Picture: add one", then the formats). The Picture interface is the cutout chooser's dialog, titled "<name>'s picture", with **Replace picture…** beside Cancel and Save cutout; a new image opens it again as "<name>'s new picture" with Save picture. On a portrait with no image the button picks a file directly and the portrait itself is not a button. The full editor's Metadata picture with no image keeps its dashed **Add a picture** box instead of the button. On the full editor's own page (no chat beside it) a click opens the full-screen viewer. Dropping an image on a portrait still replaces it, through the same cutout step.
  - **[P]** (layoutfixes, 2026-11-02) Overlays and compact chips declare their intent to the audit at their source rather than being reshaped: the portrait's corner toolbar (Adjust cutout, View full image, Replace) is an overlay whose buttons are its frame's segments; the card editor's sticky tabs and save row cover the fields scrolling under them (`Tabs` `rowOverlay`); a one-line or multi-line field's text fills its own bordered frame; the new-tag chip's field and + are part of the chip. The card name's favourite star takes the name row's own gap (no extra padding), so the row's gaps are even, and a multi-line field's control strip keeps 8 px from the field's border.
- **[D]** Direct add actions (owner, 2026-11-02), on the Notes “Add a note ▾” menu: "This is absurd, a huge button with text that takes up the space of 3 icon buttons just to serve as a dropdown with the main thing everyone is trying to do [and] a niche alternative feature. Just make these two icon buttons next to each other." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6): New note and Pick a shared note are adjacent icon buttons.
- **[D]** Row-action spacing (owner, 2026-11-02): "The archive button for presets touches the edge of the box it's in. This is such a basic UI visual mistake." Trailing actions in scrolling lists keep at least the left content inset before the box edge and scrollbar, at every width, with or without scrolling; tooltips use shared placement and stay inside the viewport. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §5).

**Built (fieldwidth, 2026-11-02):** the short-field width requirement below is implemented and obsolete as a plan; see ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** Accessible from a browser and usable remotely (the owner runs it in Docker behind Authelia on their own domain). It must work well on phones for a real release, though not perfectly from day one.
- **[D]** Every view has a URL. Refresh and the back button work.
- **[D]** Refresh restoration (owner, 2026-11-01): "refreshing should generally result in reopening to the same item you were looking at before." (2026-11-02 urlstate brief): "Sort and filter changes replace the history entry; switching places pushes one."
- **[P]** (urlstate, 2026-11-02, implementation choice) Keep chat/item/settings identities in existing paths; put the viewed fork, panel/editor/book/message destinations and browsing choices in queries, following home's default omission and device-seeding convention. Keep reading offsets in browser history, portrait weights and existing note/lore-entry expansions per device, and action confirmations transient. **Built except saved-amendment/original-text dialog destinations, deferred after two failed phone attempts; obsolete as an implementation proposal for the built portions** (ARCHITECTURE §5, §12).
- **[D]** Home state (owner, 2026-11-02): "the homepage's tabs/sorting are not driven by url or query parameters." The tabs, sort and direction, filters and search must restore from the URL. The owner's earlier rule (2026-11-01): "refreshing should generally result in reopening to the same item you were looking at before."
- **[P]** (homeurl, 2026-11-02, implementation choice) Home's existing Show tabs push history; sorts, direction and search replace it. Query defaults are omitted, unknown values fall back, and the existing browser sort memory seeds an unqualified visit only. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** **Multiple tabs work from day one**, e.g. comparing an old branch in another tab. In-app side-by-side panels come later.
- **[D]** Any number of generations can run at once across chats. You can read or send in another chat while one is generating.
- **[D]** **Nothing is locked during generation.** (ST can't even show group members' cards while generating.)
- **[P]** A running generation uses the context snapshot taken when it started. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §15).
- **[D]** Notifications when something finishes elsewhere, like a chat client: badges, plus the tab title and favicon, with optional sound. **Built U14:** browser-local opt-in completion sound; ARCHITECTURE §12. **[P]** Web push for phones.
- **[P]** The server owns all state. Clients subscribe over a WebSocket. Writes are per entity with a version check, and no client ever writes back a large settings blob. **Built foundation** (ARCHITECTURE §2, §3.10, §12); live subscriptions do not cover every open authored-setting draft and compound forms retain their documented snapshot limits.
- **[D]** **Separate users**, each with their own library, chats, connections and settings, as in ST's multi-user mode.
- **[P]** Authentication: works behind a reverse proxy that handles auth, such as Authelia, plus built-in login. Proxy identities map to users. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §4).
- **[D]** Built-in login (owner, 2026-10-11):
  - **No sign-up:** accounts are created for people, not by them.
  - **No account, no login:** on loopback, the app works without setting up an account. Most people will use it this way.
  - **Once an account is configured,** loopback requires login too. A setting turns that off; it's on by default, for security. As a security setting, it's set only outside the app (§11).
- **[D]** The details of built-in login (owner, 2026-10-11):
  - **What counts as configured:** a user with a password.
  - **Loopback without login, once accounts exist** (with the setting off), acts as the admin.
  - **Before any account exists,** allowlisted addresses (`ALLOWED_IPS`) work without login too, like loopback. Docker needs this, because there the host's browser connects from the bridge gateway, not loopback.
  - **The first password** can be set from inside the app, over that passwordless access. (The owner first said there'd be no separate bootstrap mechanism. Their headless-server requirement, below, needs one.)
- **[D]** No cap on how long a proxy-mode connection keeps its identity: logging out of the proxy needn't end an open tab, because the app isn't used on shared computers (owner, 2026-10-11).
- **[D]** Headless servers (owner, 2026-10-11): an instance on a server, for example in Docker, must be configurable without browser access to its loopback, and without an SSH tunnel. The typical user still just starts it and uses it on localhost, with nothing to configure.
- **[P]** For that, an environment variable sets the first admin's password: `ADMIN_PASSWORD_FILE` (a Docker secret) or `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, with an optional `ADMIN_USER`. It only applies while no enabled admin has a password, so a password changed in the app is never overwritten, and an instance whose admins can't log in can be recovered. The server then counts as protected, and can listen beyond loopback without an allowlist. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §4).
- **[D]** **Explanations say what the user gets, not how it works, and don't mention ST** (owner, 2026-11-02, on the home card list's Created sort, then explained as "By when the card was made: for a SillyTavern import, the date ST gives it (its create_date); otherwise when it was added here": "This explanation for Created is absurd. For one, it overexplains giving the user pointless implementation details, second, it mentions ST. The fact that something follows ST in some way is an irrelevant design detail. This is an entirely independent product. ST shouldn't be mentioned in explanatory text unless the text is actually relevant to ST interop. We don't explain things relative to ST otherwise and don't assume users come from it. In general, there's some explanatory text in various places that was added recently that overexplains while mainly giving internal details that don't matter."). The rule that every action, menu item and setting has a hover/focus explanation and a short label stands; the explanations get better, not fewer. Each says, plainly and briefly, what the control does or what the user gets, without field names, storage, workers, caches as mechanism, the assembler, or how something is computed unless the user acts on it. ST is named only where the text is about interop: importing from or exporting to ST, ST file formats, an import report.
- **[D] Image settings** (owner, 2026-11-02): "Remove the ⋯ Image settings button and its popup entirely." "Message portrait size moves to the chat panel's Display tab", beside the other message-appearance settings, with the same account persistence. The existing toggle keeps switching Show ↔ the chosen hiding mode, per device; its explanation is "Shows or hides images on this device (Alt+Shift+B). Right-click to choose blur or hide." Right-click or touch long-press opens two radio choices, "Blur hidden images" and "Remove hidden images"; choosing changes the concealment immediately if images are hidden, and otherwise leaves them shown. Blurred images stay blurred on hover and tap, with no standing note. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a). Supersedes the Images popover placement in the earlier copy proposal below.
- **[D]** The same holds for option names and labels (owner, 2026-11-02, on Settings' image section, whose "Message portrait size (account)" read "SillyTavern default (60 × 90)": "This is also absurd. Why is it named 'SillyTavern default'?"). Options are named for what the user gets; labels lose internal qualifiers such as "(account)", and where a setting applies is said in plain words only where the user needs it, at most once per section; help text drops what doesn't help the choice.
- **[P] Message spacing** (reading1, 2026-11-15; ST-COMPARISON "Less story per screen"; not yet confirmed). **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 "Message spacing"). A per-account choice under Chat width in the Interface settings: **Comfortable** (the look as it was, the default) and **Compact**, which tightens the gaps between and inside messages and the text's line and paragraph spacing, slims the name row, and shows a message's version arrows only when it's pointed at, focused or (on a phone) tapped; the newest message always shows them. Two steps, no third. Text size is unchanged: there is no text-size setting, and none was added here. Switching keeps the text being read where it is on screen.
- **[D]** The composer's "?" Keyboard shortcuts popover goes (owner, 2026-11-02, on its list of command names with no keys and a stray guidance line: "What the hell is this, what does it mean, what is it even for?"). Shortcuts show only in their own control's explanation; the guidance line belongs to the guidance toggle.
- **[D]** The chat's "Advanced: names in prompts" paragraph says only what the names are for and that Keep this name stops one following the cast, without branches or re-reading the prompt (owner, 2026-11-02, via the coordinator).
- **[D]** No standing lines about when or where edits take effect, or about the app's structure (owner, 2026-11-02, on a character tab's "What you change is used for Shannon as you type, in every chat where you play them.": "Is also out of place. It sounds like the text on the tooltip before, it's badly phrased and confusing, but it really just doesn't even belong there at all. It answers a question nobody asked."; on "The card's Character's Note is one of its notes, in the Notes tab, as are its other notes.": "What the fuck?"; on the Notes tab's "Notes are sent with the chat where each says, as you type…" and "The card's own: what you change here changes them in every chat with…": "another dogshit pointless sentence"). They're dropped, not moved into tooltips. Only where the scope changes the user's choice does a short note stay, in that control's own explanation (a chat on an earlier revision of its card still says editing won't reach it).
- **[D]** Standing description lines under section headings and settings go (owner, 2026-11-02, on the card's greetings sections: "We don't really need to explain 'alternate greetings' at all. Group greetings maybe, but that should also be a tooltip for a ? button next to it."). Where the heading says it all the line is dropped; where the user needs it to choose correctly, a short version is in a quiet "?" beside the heading (`ui/AboutHint.tsx`, named "About <heading>"), shown on hover, focus or tap.
- **[D]** Explanations are goal-first: what the user can do or get, in their terms, before anything about how (owner, 2026-11-02, on the lorebook's Select toggle, "A checkbox on each entry, to change one setting on the selected ones at once.": "This is one of the worst explanations of the ability to select and modify multiple items at a time I've ever read.").
- **[D]** The chat's message roles setting names the roles plainly and says what each option does (owner, 2026-11-02, on "Advanced: who each message is sent as": "It's very unclearly worded. Given it's advanced, it should be straightforward with what this is. Don't try to explain user/assistant turns. Explain *what these do*."). **[P]** (copy) Heading "Advanced: message roles", no intro; options By author, Alternating (roles alternate by position whoever wrote each, nothing merged) and By perspective.
- **[P]** (copy, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) **Built (settingscopy, 2026-11-02); obsolete as an implementation proposal.** Settings' field explanations use the existing "?" beside each label, following the owner’s standing-description rule above; state, errors and warnings stay visible. See ARCHITECTURE §5. This records the implementation choice, not owner confirmation.
- **[P]** (copy, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) How: data that came from an import says "imported" rather than naming ST (an imported chat "keeps the day it started", a summary badge reads Imported, a message "came hidden with the import"); a setting kept only so it survives a round trip says "Kept for export"; the portrait sizes are Smallest (48 × 72), Small (60 × 90, the default), Medium (72 × 108) and Large (84 × 126), under "Message portrait size" in a popover titled Images whose one line says show/blur/hide applies on this device and portrait size on all the user's devices; a model's Prefill choice "Through a tool schema" is "Yes, the OpenAI way"; a card book entry's "Name (card books)" and "Priority (card books)" are "Name in exports" and "Priority in exports". A unit test (`packages/client/test/stWording.test.ts`) fails on "SillyTavern" or "ST" as a word in any client string outside the import, export and connection-import surfaces it allowlists. The ST import's own pages, reports and the server's format diagnostics keep naming ST.
- **[D]** **A text box's controls are inside it** (owner, 2026-11-02; refines 2026-11-01's [D] that text actions live in the editor's own chrome, never in a custom context menu: the browser's own menu stays over text). On a screenshot of "Send text to snippet", the edit history's clock and Open large appearing around a focused box: "This is EXTREMELY annoying. It appears below every single textbox when I click in it, making layout shift below the textbox in the corner of my eye. This and other textbox-related controls should appear *inside* the textbox, as an overlay attached to a border, semi-transparent and inconspicuous, so they don't affect anything around the textbox itself." And: "It should also only show up when you're in the text field or hovering over the text field to begin with. So 10 text fields don't show their controls all the time for example, making the UI much less cluttered." So: focusing, typing in, changing or leaving a box never moves anything around it; its controls are hidden until the pointer is over the box or focus is in it, then semi-transparent, opaque while the pointer is on them; on a touch screen they show while the box has focus; hiding and showing moves nothing; they stay reachable by keyboard and touch, each with its explanation. And (same day): "It seems that note text fields don't have the 'Open this larger in a modal that fills the center of the screen' button. This should be part of text fields' internal UI as I mentioned before and it should be available for all these versioned fields in the sidebar, including notes, not just character defs." So Open large is one of the box's own controls on every multi-line versioned field (notes, the card's fields, preset prompts, lorebook entries, snippets, summaries), the box over the page editing the same text with the same history and saving, focus back in the field when it closes. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.2, "A text box's own controls").
- **[P]** (fieldchrome, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) How: one strip inside the box along a border, in the shared `ui/FieldFrame.tsx`, that always keeps its room (the box is that much taller or narrower for good, whether the strip shows or not): under a multi-line box's text, at a one-line box's end, so text can never run under a control. Its start holds what the box's text is now ("edited", the token count, which moved there from the label line); its end the controls, as icons (the text actions, edit history, Open large), after the box in the focus order. Hidden, at 60% with the pointer or focus in the box, opaque with the pointer or focus on the strip. **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02): "the token count shows on hover/focus; the ‘edited’ marker stays visible until a revision is saved; multi-line fields’ extra height is accepted." The marker stays in the strip's reserved space, visible while the field differs from its last saved revision; the token count and controls retain the hover/focus visibility above. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.2). Revert beside Save keeps its room while hidden, since its appearing on the first key typed moved the whole form down. A message being edited in the chat has no Open large: it's edited in place, as wide as the message. Send selection to snippet got its own icon (brackets around a text cursor) so it reads apart from Send text to snippet's.
- **[D]** **Wide columns** (owner, 2026-11-02): the side panel's tabs, like the sidebar, use all the width they're dragged to, where it helps: "text fields like these should expand all the way", and grids such as Cast's Add characters add columns; "some elements may need to be limited in width for UX and aesthetic consistency", so nothing spreads out to the far edges without purpose.
- **[D] Stable unavailable controls** (owner, 2026-11-02): "a button that sits between other elements never disappears." When unavailable it stays in place, disabled and greyed, with a hover saying why (for example, "No edit history yet."). "Only a control at the very end of a row may appear or disappear, and only if nothing else moves." Controls that never apply to an item's kind may remain absent. The Send selection to snippet button retains its existing placement because its appearance moves no other control. This supersedes the empty Edit history and hidden Revert placeholders described in the earlier fieldchrome/savecluster implementation choices. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §5).

- **[D]** **Short fields** (owner, 2026-11-02): "I don't like it when textboxes that clearly don't need it stretch indefinitely with the UI like this. It just looks bad." Single-line short values (names, titles, numbers, keys, URLs, model ids, words and phrases) stay at most 40rem (`max-w-controls`), narrower for always-short values such as numbers. Multi-line descriptions, prompts, notes, message editors and code editors keep the whole width. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[P]** (panelwidth, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) How: paragraphs at most 80 characters wide; multi-line text fields and editors the whole width; single-line fields capped at 40rem, numbers narrower, with an explicit opt-in for a single-line field that needs the whole width; a row of controls that belong together at most 40rem (`max-w-controls`), dropdowns included; search and filter fields count as controls, not text fields, so they stay 40rem with their filter bar; the sidebar's chat list stays one column of rows at most 40rem wide (rather than chats in several columns); a bordered box keeps its actions at its own corners however wide it is. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12).
- **[D]** **Stacked portraits** (owner, 2026-11-02): "This is fine, but currently it replaces the image if you click on another portrait. That's reasonable but I don't want that. I want it to instead split the column, opening the new portrait above the current one. Just like the column width, the boundary between the two image viewers stacked on top of each other should be draggable. This continues indefinitely, and they can be individually closed. A toggle icon-button on the header for each viewer can be toggled on in order to switch back to current behaviour. Should say something like 'only keep one image open at a time'. It would close any others, and keep that one, switching to the current behaviour from then onwards." **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a "The portrait viewer").
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04, tabportrait): "Clicking on a character's tab itself in the sidebar when you already have the image viewer open, opens that character's image in the image viewer. Needless to say, this is a bug". Clicking a cast-strip chip only switches the tab; it never opens, switches or closes a viewer image, whether a viewer is open or closed. Portrait clicks keep their behaviour. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
- **[P]** (portraitstack, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) How: a new viewer takes an equal share of the column's height (1 / (n + 1)), the others giving up theirs in proportion, so their relative sizes stay; dragging a boundary changes only the two viewers beside it; a double-click on a boundary evens those two. Each viewer is at least 180 px high, its header included; when they don't fit at that, the column scrolls. The column's automatic width is the widest need among them: each image's width when fitted to its own box's height (so a wide image under a tall one sets it, and the tall one shows narrower with room beside it); a width the user dragged stays as it is. **View full image** never closes a portrait. Escape closes the top viewer. In a narrow viewer (under 17rem) the scale shown is left out of the header so the name keeps its room. The toggle is the account's `DisplayPrefs.singlePortrait` (default off), so it follows the user across tabs and devices; turned on in another tab, the column there collapses to one at its next portrait click. Phones keep one full-screen viewer, a click replacing it, whatever the toggle.
- **[D] Image-viewer controls** (owner, 2026-11-02, on the side image viewer): proper image-viewer controls for mouse and touch. The mouse wheel zooms, centred on the cursor, and doesn't scroll the page; click and drag pans; no scrollbars, the image moving inside a fixed viewport; on touch, a pinch zooms around the pinch centre and one finger pans. Double-click (and double-tap) keeps switching between 100% and fit. Panning is clamped so the image can't be dragged entirely out of view, and the cursor shows grab/grabbing when panning is possible. The top bar's two floating percentages and "1"-looking button, unclear in meaning, become one aligned toolbar row: a small zoom slider with the current percentage beside it, a Fit button and a 100% button, each with a hover explanation, plus the existing close button. Zoom goes at least from fit (or 10%) to 800%. A small self-written pointer-events implementation is preferred over a new dependency. **Built** (portraitview; ARCHITECTURE §14.5b).
  - **[P]** (portraitview, 2026-11-02, not yet confirmed) How: the zoom runs from the fit (or 100% when the fit enlarges a small image) to 800%; the slider is logarithmic, so each stretch of it zooms alike; zooming out to the fit snaps to Fit. Panning stops where the image's edge meets the box's; an image narrower than the box stays centred on that axis. The percentage's explanation names the image's own size in pixels (what the old small percentage's tooltip held). The "1" button is the existing "Only keep one image open at a time" toggle and stays, beside Close. In a narrow column (under 22rem) the zoom controls take their own row under the name, still one aligned row.
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02, viewerbar): "Fit and 100% are two options of one control, but they don't look grouped, and Fit sits closer to the slider than to 100%." Group the slider and a fixed-width, tabular percentage; use the lorebook's segmented control for **Fit | 100%**, selecting the current preset or neither at another zoom; keep the **1** toggle visibly pressed when on and separate Close slightly at the end. Gaps within a group are smaller than equal gaps between groups, with controls vertically centred. Every control has a short explanation; the toggle's is "Keep one image open: opening another replaces this one." **Tooltip wording superseded by the owner's merge follow-up:** toggle explanations say "Click to …"; this toggle says "Click to keep only one image open at a time." when off and "Click to allow multiple images open at a time." when on. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
  - **[P]** (viewerbar, 2026-11-03) How: 4 px within the zoom group, 12 px between groups and 20 px before Close. Keep the existing responsive second zoom row under 22rem; the slider shrinks within that row. Preset explanations remain tooltips and accessible descriptions without adding a second line on phones; both preset labels keep the same weight so selection does not move controls.

- **[D] Save controls belong only to title rows** (owner, 2026-11-03, preset screenshots): icons between “Add definitions block” and “Search and replace” “interrupts identically styled buttons and doesn't look right. This shouldn't be done. Move them to the right.” Preset and lorebook editors, panel and large, keep the cluster at the right end of the title row with the name ✎ and kind tag. In the panel it is immediately before ⤢, which stays rightmost. Toolbars hold only their existing same-styled actions (Add prompt, Add definitions block, Search and replace, History, Make a variant), with no save controls or reserved gaps. This confirms the 2026-11-02 placement and supersedes all earlier toolbar/editing-row placement proposals.
