The card cluster belongs to row one of the four-row header (§2.2), superseding saveflow's separate row above the names. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] Header save cluster, option B** (owner, 2026-11-02; saveflow brief): "one compact save cluster in a fixed spot, the top-right corner of every editor's header, never in tab rows or toolbars". Cards use the shared header beside the portrait; presets and lorebooks use the title row, in panel and large views. Notes and snippets keep their per-item header cluster. "[Save] [Save with message…] [Revert], in that order, the same component everywhere"; all three stay visible, greyed when clean. Save explains "Save a revision: a version of this you can go back to from History.", Save with message explains "Save a revision with a note saying what changed.", Revert explains "Revert to the last revision.", and a clean button explains "No changes since the last revision." Saving briefly says "Revision saved." inline. Every tab holding unsaved changes has a small accent dot in its top-right corner, explaining "Unsaved changes"; nested tabs propagate to their parents. Every multiline field gets a small "Revert to last revision" icon in its existing chrome, greyed when equal to the last revision; it restores only that field through draft/edit history. All other save/revert controls in editing, tab and toolbar rows are removed. This supersedes the older placement, replacement-message mode and held-only Revert descriptions below and in §6.2a. **Built; obsolete as implementation requirements** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P]** (saveflow, 2026-11-03) Keep the existing three 32px icons and their accessible names. The message field opens directly below the unchanged cluster, with a pressed message button and corner ×, following the standing rule that controls never disappear or move. The brief confirmation also sits below the buttons without changing the header's dimensions. The held-changes description stays where it was, with all saving and reverting handled by the one header cluster.
- **[D] The save cluster** (owner, 2026-11-02, about the "Saved as revision 1", Save, "Save with a message…" row wherever something is saved as revisions): "It's horrible, just incredibly poorly designed. It's both cluttered, confusing, and too large and verbose. Why do we say 'Saved as revision 1'? what useful things does this communicate to the user? Why can't we have a good icon for save, and save with a message, instead of typing it out, with the popover (that already exists) filling in for the ambiguity? This cluster needs to be redesigned. It needs to be compact, less cluttered looking, while still being extremely unambiguous to users, and providing the right visual feedback on the current state, but only giving users the information they *actually need*. The tooltips themselves seem fine to me. The same cluster is used in a ton of places." Then, of Save's explanation ("Saves revision 2: a checkpoint in History to come back to. Replies already use what you type as you type it."): "Actually nevermind. 'Replies already use what you type as you type it'? Who the hell wrote this? This doesn't communicate the point at all." (coordinator, relaying the owner, 2026-11-02: the explanations say plainly that Save keeps this version to return to from History, that edits are already in effect without saving, and that Save with a message does the same with a note; no revision numbers or internals). Supersedes the **Header state** item's "Changed since revision N" (§6) and the "Revert to revision N" label. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P]** (savecluster, 2026-11-02) How: one shared `ui/SaveControls` everywhere (the library page's toolbar, the chat's character, Prompt preset and Lorebooks tabs, every note, every snippet, and the preset's unsaved-order bar). Three icon buttons of one size: Save (a floppy disk), Save with a message (the disk, smaller, with a speech bubble at its corner) and Revert (the undo arrow); accessible names stay the words ("Save", "Save with a message…", "Revert to the last save"). No state text. The states are shown on Save: **nothing to save**, a faint disk, not actionable but still focusable so its explanation says why; **unsaved**, Save filled in the accent colour; **saving**, a spinner in it; **just saved**, a green tick for under two seconds, then quiet again; **failed**, Save filled red with a warning mark, the reason in its explanation, and saving again retries. Screen readers hear "Saved" or "Couldn't save: …". The cluster never changes size: Revert's place is kept, empty, while nothing changed. **Superseded** (owner, 2026-11-02, §4.1): Revert is always shown, disabled and greyed out while nothing changed.
- **[P]** (savecluster, 2026-11-02) With nothing to save, Save with a message stays in place, faint and not actionable like Save, rather than hidden: hiding it would leave a gap or change the cluster's size, and keeping it shows where the option is. **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-02): "‘Save with a message’: the note field keeps replacing the save cluster. No popup." Pressing it opens the one-line field in place (the 2026-11-01 **Messages** item), which is a mode, so the cluster's size changes only there. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[D] One row** (owner, 2026-11-02, of the Prompt preset tab, the save cluster on its own row on the left and Expand all / Collapse all / Show all options on the next, on the right): "The save cluster is left aligned and the expand cluster is right aligned. Why aren't they on the same row? It just creates empty space." **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.6).
- **[P]** (savecluster, 2026-11-02) How: the cluster joins the row of the surface's own toolbar, Save on the left of a list's toolbar and at the end of an actions row: the Prompt preset tab's Expand all row (Save left, the list's buttons right), a panel lorebook's entries toolbar (the same), the character tab's row of card tabs (Definition, Greetings… left, Save right; that row now stays in sight while scrolling, as Save's own row did: the actions row above wraps to three rows on a phone, too tall to keep in sight), an open note's header (between its name and its ⋯ menu), and a snippet's row beside All chats. The library page and the large preset editor already have Save in their sticky toolbar with Search and History, so they keep it there. When a row is too narrow, the toolbar's buttons wrap under it; there is no row with Save alone.
- **[P]** (savecluster, 2026-11-02) The library page's "Saved a revision." notice is gone, Save's tick says it; the notice stays only when a save did more (variants that took the change or have conflicts, text moved to the bin). The page's own "Keeping your changes…" line goes with the state line: typing is kept as it's typed, and a failure to keep it still shows its alert. No save shortcut exists to show in Save's explanation.

- **[D] Viewer stack order and options** (owner, 2026-11-03, gallery brief; corrected 2026-11-04): “The whole image header is the drag handle”; “newly opened images go on top.” The column has fixed height and does not scroll. The previous live-reordering and right-click/long-press menu wording was not the owner's request and is superseded by the decisions below. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “Dragging them seems broken now. It's twitchy/glitchy, and moves them right away instead of on-release, which would let you preview where they'd fall like before.” Dragging previews the destination with a drop indicator, keeping viewers stationary until release. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “Move up/down/top were supposed to go into the three dot menu. They're somehow in a right-click context menu that was never meant to be built.” Remove the custom header context menu, including long-press, restore the browser menu, put Move up, Move down and Move to top in ⋯, and correct the “right-click for more” explanation. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
  - **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): “The three dot menu doesn't have the icon we had for Keep one image open, *and* it's misaligned with an empty space in its place.” Reuse the old keep-one toggle's icon in the menu's icon column, without an empty gap. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).
- **[P]** (gallery, 2026-11-03): Alt+↑/Alt+↓ move one place and Alt+Home moves to the top; order follows the viewer's existing URL state and height weights stay with each image. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §14.5a).

#### Split branches shown as chats (splitchat, 2026-11-05)

- **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-05: “A separated branch gets shown as a top-level chat with its own branches. Nothing changes in the timeline, the timeline can still step to the original chat's branches at the branch point.”
- **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-05: “If we have an independent, resizeable split branches section, then the parent branch should just go in its header. Of course, most of the time a branch would only have either a parent or split children, but it could have both. Make sure the test UI has both for me to see.” The section sits below the open chat's branches, has an independent divider and collapse control, and shows ordinary chat rows.
- **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-05: “But the parent would also appear as a header in the section we just clicked, so it'd be easier to go back.” Opening a split child changes the main view and the branch/split sections to that child's group.
- **[P] (splitchat)** Coordinator's final redesign, 2026-11-05, supersedes the earlier move/migration/baseline proposals: store only a split-root marker in the existing account key-value settings. Never move chat/fork rows or alter the graph, triggers, baseline or migrations. A group's branches descend from its marked root, excluding deeper marked subtrees; a root's parent is the group containing its parent fork. Derive current branch, pins, title and preview within each group. Original chat groups exclude split groups. No compatibility migration or fresh-database deployment is needed.
- **[P] (splitchat)** The chat menu's “Make this branch its own chat” acts on the shown branch, absent on its group's root; the standard toast offers Undo. The split section header reads “Split off (N)” and, when applicable, “From “<parent title>””; the parent truncates before the count. Parent-only groups have just the parent link. Each control has a brief hover/focus explanation. State and section height persist across navigation, including returning through that same parent link; linked navigation selects and reveals the target's ordinary Chats row. The section and divider wording is superseded by splitsection below.
- **[P] (splitchat)** A thin timeline divider after the split message links the groups: “Split into its own chat here” for the parent, “Split from “<parent title>” here” for the child. The same sections appear in the phone drawer. Synthetic review data includes a group with a parent and children, a parent with several children, and a parent-only child; fixtures use public app operations.

- **[P] (splitchat)** Coordinator, 2026-11-05: row reveal waits for the selected Chats row and its scroll viewport to be measured. Observe row/viewport resizing and row rendering; scroll only when the entire row is outside the visible bounds or clipped, and stop following after the user moves the list.
- **[P] (splitchat)** Coordinator, 2026-11-05: Delete chat removes only the viewed group, including when it is the original group. The confirmation counts the group's branches; the writer rechecks that exact set. Deeper split groups stay live and link to their nearest surviving ancestor group, or have no parent when none survives. The group is one existing bin item so Undo restores all its branches together. Shared history used by surviving or separately binned groups remains intact, including when a group is purged. Keep the physical chat while any group may be restored; no baseline or migration changes.

- **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-05 (splitconfirm): “there's no confirmation. It's just done on a click. Given that it's creating an entirely new object at the top level, that it has very non-obvious consequences and meaning, and that it isn't a common thing to do, it should have a confirmation modal explaining exactly what this is and what it is for, and possibly letting the user do something like title the chat/change the chat title (prefilled with the original chat title or the fork's title, if one exists, with the latter obviously having priority)”. Every entry point goes through the modal; confirming applies the title. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-05 (splitconfirm): “this feature is fairly inaccessible, and this is the type of feature that, if it isn't obvious how to do, will never be discovered by users. We need a new surface for it. I propose a button in the corner of branch items on the sidebar. Regular top level chats have a pin icon and a browse forks button on the right ... so we have a free spot. Since this action isn't something you'd casually do on many branches, it should only appear while that branch is selected. And of course the button needs to still require confirmation via the explanatory modal.” **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P] (splitconfirm)** The modal is titled “Make this branch its own chat?”, says what leaves, that nothing is copied or deleted, that the new chat starts with the shared messages and that the two chats link to each other, and what it is for. The “Chat title” field may be emptied for the automatic title (the original chat's name and the date). Undo also restores the branch's previous name. The corner button (split icon) sits at the lower right of the selected branch row, below the pin, in the foot list and in a chat's branch list in the panel; the group's first branch has none, as the menu has no entry for it. The owner asked “why is the pin icon on the opposite side on branches compared to top level chats?”: no owner decision placed it at the lower right (splitlink's agent put it in the preview's padding to give the title the full width), so branch pins now sit at the upper right as chat pins do, and the branch title line ends before that column.
- **[D]** Owner, 2026-11-05 (splitsection), about the “Split off (N)” section: “this UI is bad. It's incredibly confusing, even more so for someone who has no idea how this works. 1. It's not clear at all what it represents, in general 2. It literally says "Split off 'From [chatname]". Technically this is true referring to the current chat, but it refers to both what's below and the one next to it - the relationship with the items below and the one split off from is entirely unclear, as is the difference. We need to redesign it”. Supersedes the splitchat header wording above (“Split off (N)” / “From “<parent title>””) and the parent-only header-only section. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P] (splitsection)** Coordinator's redesign, 2026-11-05: the section is “Related chats (N)”, shown only when there is a relation, with a “?” help: chats that share their beginning with this one because a branch was made into its own chat. Inside, two captioned groups of ordinary chat rows: “This chat was split from” (the source chat's row) and “Split off from this chat” (one row per chat split from this one). Each row has one relation line under its preview, “Shares messages #1–14 · split 4 Nov, 19:13”. Collapsing, sizing and auto-collapse keep the splitfix foot-section rules. Timeline dividers read “This chat was split from “<title>” here” and “Split off from this chat here · <title>”. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement**, ARCHITECTURE §12.
- **[P] (splitsection)** The count covers both groups. The relation line uses the split chat's first branch's split point, the same one the branch labels use: the number of messages it shares and the time of its first own message. A source-only chat now has a one-row list instead of a link in the header; an open section is never taller than its rows, so a short list leaves the rest to Chats. **Built**, ARCHITECTURE §12.

### 10.2 Rendering

- **[D]** (owner brief, 2026-11-07): "Add one client copy helper with a fallback (a hidden textarea plus document.execCommand('copy')) used by both" message/reasoning/snippet copy and recovered-text copy. "Copy must still copy the raw text." **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (followups-ac, ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **[D]** HTML rendering must be possible (ST's HTML "games"). Interactivity will be expanded later, once the harness is agentic.
- **[D]** Per-turn HTML makes the context explode, so recent and older messages need to be shown differently.
- **[D]** Message text renders as ST renders it (owner, 2026-10-29; ST 1.19 `messageFormatting`): text in double quotes ("…", “…”, «…», 「…」, 『…』, ＂…＂, not inside code) in the quote colour, `*italics*` in the italics colour (italics inside a quote keep the quote colour), `__text__` underlined in the underline colour, single line breaks kept as line breaks, and unclosed Markdown fixed on every message. The four colours (main text, italics, underline, quote) are theme colours with ST's defaults (adjusted to read well in the light theme), and the user can change them in display settings. **Built W3** (ARCHITECTURE §14); HTML remains deferred below.
- **[D]** Copying selected message text copies the raw text underneath, not the rendered form (owner, 2026-10-30): selecting a rendered italic or quoted passage and copying it gives its `*…*` and quotes as written, so it pastes back as the same message text. The copy is exactly the stored text from where the selection starts to where it ends: a selection that starts inside a span and stops before its end copies the opening mark without a closing one. **Built fcopy** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **[P]** HTML in messages is deferred, not dropped: it's required, but it wasn't part of the pre-Phase-4 fixing (coordinator, 2026-10-29; earlier recorded as the owner's words, which the owner says they weren't). Until it's built raw HTML shows as text.
- **[D]** Existing HTML-outputting cards must work about as in ST; how to do model-driven UI well is a separate, later discussion (owner, 2026-11-07: "It was deferred once like 1 month ago at the start of the project, and then never explored. Start by looking at what ST does before proposing a model for us. Realistically, we need to support something similar since there's existing cards that output HTML. The other issue is finding a *good* way of doing model driven UIs and that's a much bigger separate discussion for later.").
- **[P]** The model for HTML in messages, as a delta from ST 1.19 and Tavern Helper: [HTML-MESSAGES.md](HTML-MESSAGES.md) (htmlmsg, 2026-11-07). Inline HTML sanitized as ST does, with five fixes; full-document blocks in opaque-origin frames; the model's side stays ST's regex depths; nothing stored in messages changes.
- **[D]** Card JavaScript is supported, sandboxed, and runs only once the card or preset is approved; HTML is not trimmed from older turns by default, as in ST (owner, 2026-11-07: "Okay.", to HTML-MESSAGES.md's two defaults).
- **[D]** Card media is saved on this server (owner, 2026-11-12): "I actually think we should just download and rehost everything when we save a card, or at least try." Transparently: "we can't change the card contents. We just save the files and fetch them from local storage instead of opening the links. The links internally stay as they are." Only from approved sites: "the user should be asked with a dialog to approve specific domains he's not seen before. cardex can be pre-approved on the whitelist (but removable as any other)."
- **[P]** How: [SAVED-MEDIA.md](SAVED-MEDIA.md) (rehost1, 2026-11-12): an account list of approved sites (exact host, or with subdomains), one approval dialog per import batch, a per-revision map from address to saved file, served from `/media/saved/` and looked up when a message renders. **Built** (ARCHITECTURE §12 "Saved card media").
- **[P]** Model-generated HTML renders only in a **sandboxed iframe**: no same-origin access, ideally a separate origin, a strict CSP, and a narrow `postMessage` bridge to the harness. This follows AIRI's CVE-2025-59053, where card text rendered as HTML led to RCE through Tauri IPC.
- **[P]** Ordinary messages get sanitized markdown. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **[P]** Each version has a **display form** and a **context form**: recent turns send the rich form, older ones the compact form (reference-harness's rich/compact split).
- **[L]** Template-bound UI: a persistent "stage" or widget whose state the model updates through tools, so only small state changes enter the context.

### 10.2a Image input (vision)

**Built (U15/U16/U22; obsolete as an implementation proposal):** ARCHITECTURE §§12, 16 describe version-owned uploads, direct attachment input, server-backed attachment drafts, thumbnails/full-size viewing, reply and summary transport and text-only refusal. Image token and cache estimates are built (ftok, 2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6, §15). The [P] choices below retain their status.
- **[D], built (U15/U16)** Users can attach images to their messages, as in any chat interface, and vision models see them (owner, 2026-10-10). It's rarely used in RP, but it must work well and feel integrated. The owner's case: sending drawings to a card-making chat as the source for character and costume designs.
- **[D], built (U16)** Attaching is direct: drag and drop onto the composer or the chat, paste from the clipboard, and a picker button. ST's "find the right button in a submenu" is the anti-pattern.
- **[D], built (U15)** No captioning by another model for text-only models, for now. There's no clear reason to send an image to a text-only model in this app.
- **[P], built (U15/U16, completed by fimg; ARCHITECTURE §12)** Attachments belong to the version, so editing or writing a new version can keep, add or remove them. Thumbnails show inline in the message, and a click opens a full-size viewer.
- **[P], built (U15/U16)** When the chosen model has no vision capability, the composer and the request preview say so before sending, so images are never silently dropped ("Nothing changes silently").
- **[D]** Images are context like anything else, so their token cost and cache effect show in the context view, and the estimate is accurate on every provider (owner, 2026-10-30, non-negotiable; replaces the [P]): image tokens are part of the prompt. **Built ftok** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §6, §15): counted/formula/calibrated estimates and explicit unknowns, not a claim of verified exact counts on every API.
- **[D]** Editing a message can keep, add or remove its images (owner, 2026-10-30). Outside the editor, hovering an image shows a button in its corner that takes it out of the context or puts it back; an image taken out keeps that button visible, in its toggled state. An image the app leaves out by a rule (the last-N limit, a model without vision, a summarized range) shows the same button toggled and disabled, and hovering the image says it isn't sent to the model and why. **Built fimg** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §12): version-owned image editing and fork-local context toggles, with disabled rule exclusions and reasons.
- **[D], built (U22)** Older images: the summarizer sees the images in the range it summarizes, so the summary can describe them, and they leave the context together with the messages that range covers (owner, 2026-10-13).
- **[D], built (U15/U22)** **Every request carries at most the last N images**, N configurable, because many models have a hard limit (Xiaomi MiMo: 4). This applies everywhere images go to a model: replies, summaries and anything else (owner, 2026-10-13). **[P]** N is set per model, with a default from the model catalog where it states one. **Built [P]** per-model cap/catalog defaults (ARCHITECTURE §6, §12, §16).

- **[D]** An exact provider count of an image only through the connection's Token counts choice (§11, §4; owner, 2026-11-02). With the default (local), images use the formula, calibration or an explicit unknown, never a zero. **Built** (providersend).
- **[P], built ftok (2026-10-30)** Builder choices: prefer an exact provider count, then a registered model/provider formula, then usage-ledger calibration, then an explicit unknown. With no recorded dimensions, calibration can be used directly. Unknown images reserve a documented bound or 1,024 tokens for budgeting; unknown is never presented as zero. Exact counting runs asynchronously after a send or model change for images in the open chat’s next context, rather than on attachment; sending does not wait (ARCHITECTURE §6, §15). These choices remain proposed, not owner-confirmed.

### 10.3 Chat-wide edits

- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-04): "look in cluster has no spacing". Give the Searches panel's Look in toggles the same spacing as its other toggle/button rows. **Built (lookin); obsolete as an implementation requirement**; ARCHITECTURE §3.13. **[P], built:** retain the shared `Segmented` control and use the panel's existing 4 px button gap; the other button rows already have that gap.

Implementation: fork independence unit 8 (ARCHITECTURE §3.13) builds message/swipe/summary search and replace, history diffs and undo, state changesets, and the rename-history RPCs. The proposed notes/card-override and cross-thread scope extensions remain unimplemented. The decided search panel and U18 card-name Save/fork rename offers are implemented (ARCHITECTURE §3.13); these portions are obsolete as proposals. No UI decision in this section blocks them.
- **[D]** Large edits across a whole conversation, summaries included, such as search and replace to rename a character, and agentic editing to instructions.
- **[P]** A **changeset** is one atomic batch of new versions across slots and summaries, with a title and one entry in the chat's history. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.13).
- **[D]** It's applied directly, with no review gate since everything is reversible. Afterwards you can inspect the diff and revert all of it or individual messages. (The earlier "draft, then review" proposal was rejected.)
- **[P]** Scope options: the current path, all branches, or chosen threads; messages, summaries, notes, and card overrides. Drastic rewrites can be applied as a new chat. **Partial/overtaken**: current-fork messages/swipes/summaries are built (ARCHITECTURE §3.13). The owner’s flow below restricts today’s panel to this chat; card overrides are replaced by fork-and-swap. Notes, chosen threads and new-chat transformations remain later Phase 5/7 proposals.
- **[D]** Nothing changes that the user doesn't see: an edit to history applies to the chat it's made in, and a fork made afterwards inherits it (forking from an earlier point must never undo it, or the fork would show something different from what was in front of the user). Each fork is effectively its own chat, connected to its siblings only by shared history (owner, 2026-10-26). A chat's current fork is the one last chatted in or created by forking; named or pinned forks are its peers (owner, 2026-10-26, UX-REWORK §3). Built throughout the navigation shell in U01 (2026-10-28; ARCHITECTURE §12), including checkpoints and same-tip peers. **[P], overtaken** by the owner-confirmed “Always this chat only” flow below; implemented in ARCHITECTURE §3.13.
- **[D]** Renaming a card asks, in the owner's wording (2026-10-26):
  > "Aria" will be renamed to "Ari"
  > Replace "Aria" with "Ari" in past messages?
  > - No. Past messages will keep "Aria".
  > - Yes, only in this chat. (Does not touch any forks of this chat or any other chats)
  > - Yes, in all 37 chats with Aria
  "All chats with Aria" counts forks as chats.
- **[P]** A history edit in a chat includes the swipes of its messages (owner leaning yes, 2026-10-26). A swipe shared with an existing fork then reads differently there, since that fork isn't touched. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.13). Existing siblings retain their own effective versions.
- **[P]** After Fork for this chat, the character is new: replacing the old name in that chat's past messages is offered for this chat only, or not at all. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3.13).
- **[D]** Search and replace layout (owner, 2026-11-02, historyedits brief): “The name is "Search and replace" everywhere”; “Each search/replace pair is its own bordered box” reusing the editors' shared UI, with equal bounded fields, compact match toggles, Previous/Next beside Find and Replace/Replace all beside Replace. “Look in: Messages · Swipes · Summaries” is a per-box multi-select segmented row that never wraps. A “+” beside the section header has “Add another search and replace” on hover, reveals and flashes the added box, and boxes get “Remove this search and replace” corner × controls when more than one exists. The separate list below is “Applied edits”, with “No edits applied yet.” The side panel tab stays **History edits** (owner, 2026-11-03: "Why was this renamed from "History Edits". It's way too long now and the name doesn't even fit the icon"): the "everywhere" rule names the search feature, not the tab that holds it and the applied edits. when empty, and never repeats the tab name. **Built; obsolete as an implementation requirement** (2026-11-03; ARCHITECTURE §3.13).
- **[D]** Search and replace over a chat works like a code editor's: case-sensitive or not, whole word, regular expression (with capture groups in the replacement), and every match is previewable, matches in summaries included (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **[D]** The flow (owner confirmed, 2026-10-26):
  - Opened from the chat's menu, the chat's list of history edits, or Ctrl+H, in the right panel (a sheet on phones) so the chat stays in view.
  - One Find and one Replace field with code-editor toggles: **Aa** match case, **ab** whole word, **.\*** regular expression (invalid patterns say why, live), and **preserve case** (Aria → Ari also turns ARIA into ARI and aria into ari). Enter / Shift+Enter step through matches; ＋ adds another Find/Replace pair to the same set.
  - Where to look: messages, their swipes, summaries (each a toggle). Always this chat only; forks and other chats are never touched, and the panel says so.
  - Matches as you type: a count by kind ("42 in 17 messages, 3 in swipes, 2 in summaries"); message matches highlighted in the chat with before → after shown inline (the replacement expanded, capture groups included); a list grouped Messages / Swipes / Summaries. A summary match opens that summary in the panel, with its level, the messages it covers and before → after highlighted, since summaries aren't otherwise in the stream. Each match can be skipped.
  - What it costs: the line "The model will re-read N messages and M summaries on the next reply (cache: ~X tokens)".
  - **Replace** (the current match) and **Replace all**; everything replaced while the panel is open is one changeset, applied at once, with Undo in a toast and an entry in the chat's history edits (before/after per message and summary, undo per item or all).
  - The pattern runs in a worker with a time limit, as the card editor's search does (ARCHITECTURE §14.6), so a runaway regular expression stops with a message.
- **[D]** Wide-range edits reach beyond one chat: replacing strings and names across chat history, for example when a card is renamed or a renamed fork is used where the old name appears (owner, 2026-10-26; §4).

### 10.4 Stack reasoning
- **[D]** TypeScript. Rust was considered (smaller memory footprint, no Node, faster startup) but isn't viable today:
  - the Rust LLM crates either own their loop and change often (rig) or are chat-only clients (genai)
  - hot-loading plugins written by an agent is far easier in TS
  - one language lets the macro engine and preset parser run in both browser and server
- **[D]** The provider layer is pi-ai 1.x (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[P]** pi-agent-core 1.x for the agent loop, with pi-ai underneath (Pi reached 1.0 on 2026-10-01), as in reference-harness, which is on 0.84.2 with a pi-ai patch. Pi's experimental durable-execution package isn't needed: surviving a disconnect only requires running the loop on the server.
- **[D]** Not building on DSH as the host:
  - it's about two months old, with weekly breaking alphas
  - its rule that "model-visible means logged" and its core-service context assembly conflict with separating the timeline from rollouts
  - it has no documented auth
- **[D]** No Cordis (owner, 2026-10-11; evaluation in [CORDIS.md](CORDIS.md)). The plugin threat model and structure are in [PLUGINS.md](PLUGINS.md).
- **[P]** SQLite with FTS5 for the timeline, versions, summaries, rollouts and jobs. JSON files can't handle the version and branch structures. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §3).
- **[D]** Performance on chats of any length is an absolute priority: very smooth, never buggy, no corners cut. Looks rank below that but matter a lot (screenshots) (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** TanStack libraries, Query where it applies (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[P]** Effect 4 across the server and the client data layer: [EFFECT.md](EFFECT.md). The owner asked for it to be strongly considered. **Built; obsolete as an implementation proposal** (2026-10-30; ARCHITECTURE §1–§6).
- **[D]** Client framework: **React** (owner, 2026-10-10). This is one of the main stack decisions, because UI quality for real users matters most; reference-harness's console stack is not the default. Evaluation, and the proposed stack around React: [CLIENT-FRAMEWORK.md](CLIENT-FRAMEWORK.md).

### 10.5 Desktop app and updates
- **[D]** A desktop app for Linux, Windows and macOS, beside the server: "We will package this as a linux/windows/macos desktop app too with something like electron, and it has to 'just work', not just a linux server app" (owner, 2026-11-17, §7.9). Bundled Linux VMs are rejected (§7.9). The server (Docker) version stays.
- **[D]** Updating is a first-class part of the design: "Actually, how to update it is important" (owner, 2026-11-17).
- **[D]** The desktop app checks for updates by default. Asked "Should the desktop app check for updates by default?", the owner answered: "Yes." (owner, 2026-11-17). The server's default (off until an admin turns it on) is still Proposed.
- **[D]** Self-signed, updates from GitHub: "For now we self-sign. We're not going to have any authenticated signatures. Also updates should come from github." (owner, 2026-11-17). No Apple Developer ID, notarization or paid Windows certificate. What self-signing means per platform, and the integrity checks that replace paid signing, are in [DESKTOP.md](DESKTOP.md) §6.2 and §7 (Proposed).
- **[P]** (desktopspec) The design is [DESKTOP.md](DESKTOP.md): an Electron shell that runs the unchanged server on the official Node binary it ships (measured: the server crashes on Electron 45's own Node); a `desktop` auth mode, loopback only, with windows signed in by a one-time launch token; LAN use opt-in from the shell's own settings, with a password; updates through electron-updater (stable and beta, signed, differential), and for the server an admin notice with the operator's command; a backup before every database upgrade, one transaction per upgrade, and a `min_app_version` marker so older versions open data that additive migrations changed; plugins checked against the new version and suspended with a notice when they fail; desktop and server differences as registered parts. Not built.

---

## 11. Security

- **[D]** Avoiding RCEs is the top priority.
- **[D]** Running a plugin is the user's conscious choice, and a plugin written by the user's own agent is the user's plugin. Our responsibility is that content (cards, presets, lorebooks, chats, model output) never becomes code. The three lines of attack to weigh: credentials, the user's system, and exfiltrated chats (owner, 2026-10-11; [PLUGINS.md](PLUGINS.md)).
- **[D] Nothing goes to a provider the user didn't choose** (owner, 2026-11-02: "NEVER send anything to providers users didn't actually choose to. Users choose to send a chat to a provider by switching to it *and submitting*. Editing a card for example cannot do that, neither should switching a provider on its own or opening a chat. This is a matter of ToS, retention policies, privacy, etc." And: "It should also be possible to specify a separate token counting endpoint for a connection."). What may reach a provider without a further choice: the user's submit (send, reply, regenerate, swipe, continue…) to the chat's chosen model, and work the user explicitly started or set up for a named connection (an approved LCM backfill, the summarizer or refusal judge they chose, a connection test or model list, which carry no user content). Everything else is a per-connection opt-in, off by default, or doesn't exist. **Built** (providersend, 2026-11-02; ARCHITECTURE §6): image token counts, which went to the chat provider's counting endpoint when a chat opened, an image was attached or the model changed, now follow the connection's Token counts choice like text (§4); `test/providerSend.test.ts` checks that with defaults nothing reaches a provider before the submit.
  - **[P]** (providersend) Reading of "chose": the summarizer's model (falling back to the chat's own) and the refusal judge are models the user picked for that role, and their requests follow a submit (or an approved backfill, or the user's own Summarize/Retry), so they stay as they are; see the job's audit (work-folder/2026-11-02/providersend.md) for the full list.
- **[D]** Users are never assumed trusted. One user must never be able to see another user's chats, cards, connections or keys, and that has to be certain (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** It must be hard to expose an instance by accident. Without a password, an IP allowlist or an auth proxy, the server doesn't listen beyond localhost (owner, 2026-10-10).
- **[D]** The exposure rules (owner, 2026-10-11; built, ARCHITECTURE.md §4):
  - Listening on loopback is always allowed.
  - Beyond loopback, the server starts only with protection: an IP allowlist; proxy mode with named trusted proxies, the only addresses that may then connect, so nobody else can send the identity header; or, once built-in login exists, a password on every user. An allowlist alone is enough, without a password.
  - **[P]** How that's read (built, ARCHITECTURE.md §4): beyond loopback is allowed once an enabled user has a password. Everyone beyond loopback then has to log in, and a user without a password can't log in at all, so no user is reachable from there without one.
  - Peers are checked by each connection's source address, never by a forwarded header, so the check holds in Docker, where the host's requests come from the bridge gateway.
  - The override, `ALLOW_INSECURE_EXPOSURE`, is set only outside the app, like every security setting.
- **[P]** Cross-site requests (built, ARCHITECTURE.md §4; found 2026-10-11): browsers let any page open a WebSocket to the user's own loopback, so before this, any site the owner visited could call every RPC of a local instance. Requests are refused when `Origin` names another site, or when `Host` is a name that isn't the instance's (DNS rebinding). Proxy mode with named trusted proxies skips the `Host` check, since only the proxies can connect. Instances served under a domain name list it in `PUBLIC_ORIGINS`.
- **[P]** Rules: **Partial**: non-executable imports, write-only keys and literal/sanitized rendering are built (ARCHITECTURE §4, §6, §8–§11, §14). Sandboxed HTML and tool/plugin approvals belong to the deferred features, not current enforcement of an agent runtime.
  - imported cards, presets and lorebooks never execute code, and their fields never grant capabilities (a file can't enable tools or plugins by itself)
  - model and card HTML renders only in a sandbox
  - API keys are write-only
  - tools with external effects need approval
  - plugin installation is always confirmed
- Reference incidents:
  - **AIRI** (CVE-2025-59053): card text rendered as HTML, leading to RCE through Tauri IPC.
  - **Marinara Engine** v2.0.9–v2.3.3: undisclosed. The fix removed its extension system and stopped imported agent files from granting themselves tool access.
  - **ST's 2026 CVEs**: in its CORS proxy and SearXNG proxy (SSRF/XSS) and extension deletion. Don't ship open proxies.

---

## 12. Later / meta

- **[L]** Agent-driven configuration: the agent edits or imports presets, sets up chats and modes, writes plugins.
- **[L]** Plugin system, and the harness extending itself from within.
- **[L]** Template-bound interactive UI (§10.2).
- **[L]** Per-character agent memory and private state (§7.1).
- **[L]** Internal side-by-side panels.
- **[L]** **Card archive integration** (owner, 2026-10-26). Testing a card today is a slow loop: browse an archive, download the file into the right folder, find it in the app, try it, and a folder of files isn't how the app represents cards anyway. Instead, a card archive is searched as if it were another section of the user's local cards, seamlessly but deliberately: the two are never mixed. A card from the archive opens with the same UI as a local card (the chat with its greeting, the definitions) without being added. It becomes one of the user's cards only when they edit it and save, add it with an explicit action, or, the main way, send the first message. Until then the user can move on to the next card, so many cards can be tried without setting any of them up.
- **[L]** **Blind roleplay ("Russian roulette")** (owner, 2026-10-26): the app presents a chat with a greeting from a random card, from the user's own cards or from the archive. The user passes, or starts the chat, and can still pass to the next one afterwards. A setting decides whether the character's name and picture stay hidden too, to spoil even less. The estimated cost of the next message is shown as usual, so a hidden card doesn't hide its cost.
- **[D]** The archive integrated with is cardex, through its public API, with no privileged access (owner, 2026-10-26). **[O]** Whether other archives are supported too. **[P]** (cardsource, 2026-11-06) Sources are a registry on both sides, cardex the first and only built-in entry, so a plugin can add another archive ([CARD-SOURCES.md](CARD-SOURCES.md) §2); whether more ship built in stays open.
- **[D] Cardex integration** (owner, 2026-11-06): "Cardex integration. We need to start designing this, especially our end of things, and it's going to become a major selling point of the UI. It seems to both be seamless, and also not invasive. The basic idea is that the archive becomes another tab on the homepage for cards, and another select option for cards in the sidebar, the filter bar directly searches the archive (plus tags and every other criterion cardex supports also need to be filterable here), and we show results in our UI like they're local cards (but only when the cardex tab/option is the one open). Clicking them, and this is important, doesn't add anything to your collection, and yet they open like any other card you've never used, with defs and such in the sidebar and a prompt to start a new chat. Certain actions, like sending the first message, would lock them in, adding them to your collection. Modifying the card too. We'd have to decide the exact UX for this, and how not to make it confusing. Moreover, we'd want additional actions to actually intentionally add them to your collection, not having to do anything like editing or sending a message." Designed in [spec/CARD-SOURCES.md](CARD-SOURCES.md) (Proposed, unbuilt).
- **[D] Standalone lorebooks from the archive** (owner, 2026-11-12): "In the lorebook editor, I guess. They can be returned with characters. You can filter them." They come back in the same results as characters with a type filter, and open in the existing lorebook editor. **[P]** (cardex3) Opening one adds it to the user's lorebooks; updates follow its origin as a card's do ([CARD-SOURCES.md](CARD-SOURCES.md) §3.9). Built (cardex3, 2026-11-12; ARCHITECTURE §11.5, "Standalone lorebooks").
- **[D]** The source's tab is labelled by the source's name, **"cardex"**, never "Archive", since "Archived" is the view of cards the user has archived; another source gets its own name (owner, 2026-11-06: "Ok", via the coordinator).
- **[P]** (cardsource, 2026-11-06) The whole design is [CARD-SOURCES.md](CARD-SOURCES.md): cardex is the last **Show** option on home and in the sidebar's select; the search, a generated Filters panel and source sorts query it; remote cards open as the existing pending chat from a server cache, never in the library; Send, Save and Add to my cards add them in one transaction each; the server proxies every archive request. Its §9 lists four decisions for the owner with the defaults used (D5, what an update offers, was settled by the owner on 2026-11-06).
- **[P]** (cardsrc1, 2026-11-07; the owner chose the spec's defaults for D1–D4) Phase 1 is built (ARCHITECTURE §11.5) with these choices of its own: a source starts on, and nothing is sent until its option is opened (D1). With an admin's switch off, a user's "Show cardex" stays in place, disabled, saying an admin turned it off, rather than being hidden. Settings → Card sources sits in a "Cards" settings group of its own. Image keys are HMAC-signed by the server, so nothing is stored per key and a key works only for the user it was issued to. The cache is files with an in-memory index, not database rows, so the baseline is unchanged. Redirects are followed only within the source's origin, stricter than "within hosts" (a source whose thumbnails sit on a CDN host will need its hosts' redirects allowed). Dev registers the fake archive under the id `cardex` until a real adapter exists.
- **[P]** (cardsrc1, 2026-11-07) Phase 2 (browsing, ARCHITECTURE §11.5) simplifies the spec in these places: the Filters panel opens inline below its button at every width (no bottom sheet on phones), like the other panels; every loaded tile stays mounted, kept cheap with `content-visibility`, instead of dropping tiles past 600; a card opens on a minimal page of its own (picture, fields, counts, Open on cardex) until phase 3 brings the pending chat; tiles have no hover actions until phase 3 gives them something to do; cardex's search modes show as a "Match" choice in the Filters panel (Words or Meaning).
- **[P]** (cardsrc1, 2026-11-07) Phase 4 (the cardex adapter, ARCHITECTURE §11.5) makes these choices: the fake archive serves cardex v1's own shapes and the real adapter runs against it, so dev and tests exercise the production mapping and the fake replaces cardex rather than standing beside it; the rating starts at cardex's own anonymous default (`include`, shown as Everything), as CARD-SOURCES §5.3 says; only characters are listed (`kind=character`), since lorebooks have nowhere to go yet; 48 cards a page; the sorts offered are Popular, Newest (reversible to oldest), Name, Tokens (reversible) and Best match, which falls back to Popular with no words; the other v1 filters (dates, picture, Chub's flags, deleted from source, covers) aren't offered yet; a card is fetched as cardex's mirrored PNG (card and portrait in one file), falling back to its JSON (no portrait) for a CHARX card, which has no mirrored file, as cardex advised, and picked from the summary's `download_modes` when listed; a card cardex lists no download for says so ("cardex doesn't allow downloading this card.") and nothing is tried; a cursor cardex refuses as `invalid_cursor` restarts the search from page 1.
- **[P]** (cardsrc1, 2026-11-07) Phase 3's first part (adding, held cards; ARCHITECTURE §11.5) differs from CARD-SOURCES §3.7 and §4.5 in these places: revision 1's `origin_ref` stays as `ImportFile` writes it, and the source, remote id, version and hash are in the `card_origins` row instead; a card is recognized as held by its origin or by the same file's hash, not yet by an adapter's `recognize()` over imported cards' `source` entries; a held card whose family has several live cards opens the one recorded, not the newest live fork when that one is in the bin (such a card simply isn't held, and adding it again makes a new card); "Added <name> to your cards." is a toast with Show it, which opens the card's page; a held tile's menu adds Show on <name>, so the source's page stays reachable.
- **[P]** (cardsrc1, 2026-11-07) Phase 3's draft chat (ARCHITECTURE §11.5) differs from CARD-SOURCES §3.3, §4.2 and §4.4 in these places: local tiles no longer have hover actions, so a remote tile's Inspect and Add to my cards are in its context menu only (with Open, which opens the draft chat as a click does); the pending chat has no context estimate or inspector yet, so there's no assembler source for a cached document to give it; a draft chat opened for a card the user already has opens their card's pending chat.
- **[P]** (cardsrc1, 2026-11-07) Phase 3's card editor (ARCHITECTURE §11.5) differs from CARD-SOURCES §4.3 and §4.5 in these places: the drafts' target kind is `remote_card` (the spec's `remote-card`, in the style of the other kinds); the cached copy isn't pinned while it has drafts, so an evicted card is fetched again when its editor opens; the portrait and gallery controls, Export, Use as persona and the other card actions wait until the card is added (Export shows, disabled, saying so) rather than adding it themselves; the full editor isn't offered for a card not added yet, only the panel's; a card whose name and version match one of the user's own is held to the same name check as any card before Save adds it.
- **[P]** (cardsrc2, 2026-11-07) Phase 5 (updates, ARCHITECTURE §11.5) makes these choices: the notice has Review and merge and **More choices…**, which opens the new-version dialog on the newer file (Replace, Merge…, Add as a separate card) rather than putting all three on the notice; a version counts as taken once Review and merge has staged it on the import line (the merge notice then offers it until merged), or after Replace or Add as a separate card; the card added separately is an ordinary import with the cardex link in its Sources and no origin of its own, so later updates are still offered on the first card; results read from the cache don't record versions, so a held tile's badge can lag until the next fresh page or Check; cards recognized only by their file's hash get no update notices; the library list's "update available" covers a newer cardex version too; with cardex turned off the notice and Check are hidden.
- **[D]** (owner, 2026-11-16) Card source tracking: a card linked to its archive entry (by the user, or by import) is recognized as that entry even without a known version; after **Add as a separate card**, both cards track the source (the new copy at the version it was added from); each card has **Ignore future updates**, on the update notice and in its Sources, which stops the notice and badges until turned back on. **Built** (schemafinal; ARCHITECTURE §11.5); this supersedes the cardsrc2 choices above that the separate card has no origin and that hash-only recognition gets no notices only where they conflict. Builder choice, **[P]**: a linked card with no known version shows an update as soon as the source reports any version.
- **[P]** (cardsrc2, 2026-11-07) Phase 6 ("Only tried", ARCHITECTURE §11.5) makes these choices: "your messages" are the user's own messages (each counted once, whatever its edits) in every chat where the card was present, not only those addressed to it; the criterion lives in the shared registry beside the other two, because the server's query and the writer's recheck use the same predicates, and it's marked for card sources so the dialog offers it only while one is on; its count beside it excludes favourites, as the dialog does; N is at least 1 (the dialog's number fields' minimum), so at the default a card with no messages and one with a single message are both caught.
- **[O]** Open for archive integration (2026-10-26). [CARD-SOURCES.md](CARD-SOURCES.md) proposes an answer to each (search §3.2, untrusted content §4.6, unsaved cards §4.2, tried cards §9 D3, duplicates §3.7, updates §3.8 (decided 2026-11-06, §9), archive forks §10 item 9, blind roleplay §7):
  - How much of an archive's own search (tags, filters, sorts, semantic search, "cards like this") the section offers, and how archive search shows next to local cards in the top-level layout.
  - Archive cards are untrusted and opened casually: greetings with HTML/CSS, cards with regex scripts and other extensions. Rendering must be safe, and nothing in an unsaved card turns itself on.
  - What works on an unsaved card: swiping its greetings, choosing persona and preset, its embedded lorebook. Fork, Fork for this chat and attaching lorebooks need a saved card: they save it (as an edit's Save does) or wait until it is saved.
  - The library filling with cards that were only tried, each with a one-message chat: a "tried" state, easy bulk removal, or another answer.
  - Duplicates: a card already held (an earlier download, an ST import) is recognized by the archive's id where the card carries one, and offered instead.
  - The author updates the card: show that a newer version exists. **Superseded** (owner, 2026-11-06: "Ignore that older design note."): the earlier "take it as a separate card or a fork, never merged into the user's copy (§4)" no longer holds. **[D]** An update to an added card offers Review and merge, Replace, or Add as a separate card ([CARD-SOURCES.md](CARD-SOURCES.md) §3.8, §9).
  - An archive card that is a fork of another archive card: whether the local copy records that relationship or starts its own lineage.
  - Blind roleplay: the greeting usually names the character, so hiding means the UI hides name, picture and definitions, not the story; a Reveal action; no repeats of passed cards; a way back to one passed by mistake; filters for the pick (tags, content ratings, own cards only or the archive too, "like the cards I liked").
- **[L]** A native app as a thin client to the user's server (a wrapper around the same frontend, not a standalone app). Low priority, and it must not constrain the design (owner, 2026-10-10).
